# 11 — Flubsound Pro — Post-construction Enhancement Report

This report lists what Flubsound Pro still needs, measured against what music listeners and gamers hear and against the tools they would otherwise use (Voicemeeter, Equalizer APO + Peace/AutoEQ, Boom 3D, FxSound, SteelSeries Sonar, Nahimic, Dolby Atmos for Headphones, Windows Sonic, DTS Headphone:X, Razer THX Spatial, Turtle Beach Superhuman Hearing / Audio Hub, Waves Nx / MaxxBass, iZotope Ozone, FabFilter Pro-Q / Pro-L, Sonarworks SoundID). It records defects and gaps only. What already works is documented in [01](01-architecture.md)–[10](10-headset-compatibility.md) and [TRACEABILITY][TR-sum].

> **Method.** Thirteen area auditors (bass, loudness, clarity, spatial, gaming, music, headsets, adaptive, latency, platform, stability, UX, future-proofing) read the code and docs and measured the already-built binaries (the round-7 review build of `flubsound-cli`: `params`, `presets`, `process`, `analyze`) and scratch harnesses. Their findings were merged into the 60 enhancements below. Each group was then scored for benefit (1–5), difficulty and effort, and an adversarial skeptic re-checked every claim, re-ran the measurements it doubted and corrected the item. The corrected values are the ones reported here; where the skeptic overturned a finding, the item says so. No repository file other than this one was changed.
>
> **Snapshot and provenance.** Two trees are involved, and the report says which one each figure comes from. (a) *Measurements* were taken on the round-7 review build of `flubsound-cli` (built 07:26 on 2026-09-27 from the round-7 working tree; the nearest commit is `88c13af` at 08:06, so that hash identifies the build approximately, not exactly). (b) *Code citations* refer to the tree at **`d096a4d`** ("Hardening wave A", committed 10:33), which landed during the audit; the report was saved at 10:51. `d096a4d` changed the `GatedLoudness` / AutoLevel / AutoDrive / LoudnessMatch release behaviour, took the governor's limiter-GR input from a fixed 10 ms window, and made a dropped NaN/Inf block reset only the signal path (`ProcessingChain::resetSignalState()`, new in that commit). Claims that touch those areas were therefore re-checked on a build that contains `d096a4d`'s sources (`fa-review`, built 10:15, `LoudnessMaximizer.cpp.o` newer than its source), by the post-report review and by this revision, with these results. **Unchanged:** the E37 bypass gap (2.9 LU on dense −16 LUFS pink at `-b 100`, post-report review; 3.10 LU on this report's own stimulus); the E10 1e30 spike (Signature on −20 dBFS pink: 100 ms of silence, then +13.0 / +7.7 / +3.5 dB over 3.1–3.5 / 3.5–4 / 4–5 s and −2.1 dB at 5–6 s, output identical on both builds); the E42 latencies (1352 samples for Warm Vinyl in Quality, 1152 samples at 8 kHz, post-report review); the E06 block-size dependence (identical outputs on both builds: −7.30 to −7.38 LUFS over blocks 64–4096 on a 20 s −18 dBFS-RMS pink with Boost 100 + Loudness 100, and −7.59 to −7.94 LUFS, a 0.35 LU spread, on the post-report review's stimulus); the Competitive FPS bed lift (+5.0 LU on −50 dBFS and +9.8 LU on −60 dBFS pink). **Changed:** Night Mode's post-event recovery (on the post-report review's ambush scene the bed at +5–7.5 s after the event moved from +5.6 to +6.8 dB against input). **Not re-checked after `d096a4d`:** the E10 NaN-burst figure (240 ms, measured on the round-7 build, while its explanation cites `resetSignalState()`, which only `d096a4d` introduced) and the E21 ambush-scene numbers (+16.6 dB bed lift, 6.6 dB hole), which are pre-`d096a4d` values. On a second scene (−50 dBFS pink bed, 4 s of 40 ms bursts at −12 dBFS peak) both builds lift the bed +12 to +16 dB and leave no post-event hole, so the hole depends on the event's energy and duration, which is why E59 must pin the stimulus. Test counts quoted from [TRACEABILITY][TR-sum] are "as recorded there at `88c13af`"; at `d096a4d` the tree holds 519 `TEST_CASE`s in `tests/*.cpp` and 48 in `tests/app/` (the `d096a4d` message reports gcc 518/518 and RTSan 519/519).
>
> **Limits.** All figures are objective measurements (CLI renders, analytic filter responses, code reading). Every perceptual claim in this report ("sounds boomy", "externalises", "fatiguing") is a hypothesis until it passes listening tests, and every device, latency and platform claim needs validation on real hardware. Nothing here has been heard by a listening panel, and none of the Windows or macOS platform code has run. Line numbers were taken from the tree at `d096a4d`. Other workflows are editing the tree, so they drift; the function and constant names are the stable reference.
>
> **Work in flight elsewhere (treated as "being built, not verified"):** automatic per-game profile switching by foreground app, the click-free crossfaded engine swap on structural changes, and UI scale with a high-contrast theme. Many items below depend on them.

---

## 1. Executive summary

**Current state.** [TRACEABILITY][TR-sum] records 20 of 30 requirements as Implemented and tested, 1 as build-verified only (R4.1) and 9 as Partially implemented (R1.1 latency, R1.2 backends, R1.5 buffer management, R3.5 THD protection, R4.4 tray and hotkeys, R4.5 per-app routing, R4.6 virtual device, R5.3 neural, R6.1 headsets). As recorded in TRACEABILITY at `88c13af`, 503/503 `flub_tests` and 34/34 `flub_app_tests` passed; the tree at `d096a4d` has 519 core and 48 app test cases, and that commit reports gcc 518/518 and RTSan 519/519 (see the provenance note above). Those tests prove safety and invariants: the ceiling holds, nothing allocates, nothing goes NaN. They do not test sound quality, and every defect below passed CI. The product's actual state for its two target audiences is:

1. **Most users get no processed audio, or doubled audio.** Windows, the main gaming platform, has no first-party way to get system audio into Flubsound Pro: no virtual endpoint and no APO ([driver README][DRV]: "design, not implemented"). In a default build, Automatic routing chooses endpoint moves that always fail. `WinAppAudioRouter::isSupported()` returns true ([PlatformServices_win.cpp][PSW] :1071), but `setAppEndpoint` fails without `FLUB_ENABLE_UNDOCUMENTED_ROUTING`. The only per-app path that works, process loopback, plays the dry app and the processed copy together about 12.75 ms apart, which is comb filtering. macOS has no path at all (R4.5), and Linux needs a script and manual wiring (R4.6).
2. **The 7.1 Game strip rarely receives 7.1, and mishandles it when it does.** Games render to stereo endpoints, so the virtualiser folds a stereo signal carried in an 8-channel container through two 30° virtual speakers (10.8 dB channel separation instead of full separation; a −15.4 dB notch at 1418 Hz on centred sources). When real 7.1 arrives, the LFE is discarded on the non-virtualised path (`downmixToStereo()`, "LFE dropped"; measured about −93 to −240 dBFS output) and under-calibrated on the virtualised one (0 dB instead of the +10 dB convention). The renderer is a spherical head whose only front/back cue is a 4 dB rear shelf; it has no elevation. Measured HRIRs cannot be loaded, and no output is taller than 7.1 or wider than stereo.
3. **The gaming features mostly fail on real dynamics.** Footsteps lifts 20–80 ms steps by only +0.3 to +1.1 dB and does nothing (0 dB) on normally mastered mixes, because thresholds are absolute dBFS and the gain rises at the release rate. Impact boosts rumble (+3.3 dB) more than explosions (+1.3 dB) and cancels against Footsteps. In Night Mode, AutoLevel raises the ambience bed +16.6 dB, lets the event through, then leaves a 6.6 dB hole that has not recovered 6 s later (round-7 build; after `d096a4d` the hole persists with a changed shape, [E21](#e21)). Nothing tames loud events, nothing ducks game audio under voice chat, and neither the Chat strip nor the microphone gets speech processing.
4. **Loudness and protection are tuned for safety, not for sound.** Loudness 100 in Music holds −21.8 dB MTND with no back-off for the whole 24 s measured ([E06](#e06)), because the governor budget is an unweighted −30 dB THD+N that sees only clipper and saturator THD, not limiter IMD. The soft clipper is a static waveshaper (THD+N −16 dB at 12 dB of drive), and the wideband limiter ducks a 2 kHz tone by up to 22.6 dB on every kick. The governor makes level sag 4.7 dB and hunt for 45 s. Presets push into the limiter because there is no headroom management, and base drive settings are never governed (R3.5 open part).
5. **There is no headphone correction.** There is no per-device correction layer, no AutoEQ/Peace import and no target curves. The only EQ is 10 bands shared with taste presets, and every preset load wipes it. All 8 device profiles (Turtle Beach and Xbox families only, all `labVerified:false`) carry advice text and no sound data, and every one recommends the same two presets.
6. **Stereo music on headphones gets little headphone-specific benefit.** The virtualiser never runs on stereo strips (`binaural = config.inputChannels > 2 && ...`). Crossfeed is a 700 Hz M/S low-frequency side cut with no ITD. "Space" puts up to 9.3 dB of interaural difference on centred vocals. Nothing compensates for listening level: the app never reads endpoint volume, and there is no ISO 226 contour. The "Late Night" contour is a static +1.7 dB.
7. **Latency, the core competitive claim, has never been measured end to end, and every path that exists misses 10 ms.** The Low Latency profile saves about 2 ms but leaves 12.75 ms (Windows loopback FIFO) or about 21 ms (default PipeWire quantum) of buffering in place ([01 §5.3][01-53]). Presets silently override the latency profile, and MixEngine pads every strip to the slowest one, so a Quality music preset moves the Game strip from 4.00 ms to 28.17 ms. A second master look-ahead is stacked on top of the per-strip limiters.
8. **The tests prove safety, not quality.** There are no multi-tone IMD, kick-alignment, pumping, sibilance, localisation, burst-footstep or scenario tests, and no listening-panel or real-game data (R2.9 "real-game efficacy" untested). There is no soak test, glitch detector, coverage-guided fuzzing or pluginval run, the plug-in has no tests, and about 2800 lines of Windows and macOS platform code have never executed.
9. **The product cannot ship or be supported yet.** It has no installer, signing, update channel, logging, crash reporting or diagnostics export, and no LICENSE or SBOM, while the JUCE AGPLv3 obligation remains ([02 §6][02-6]). Preset and state formats have no migration: a version 1.1 preset is refused, stored state is sparse and tied to current defaults, and a corrupt settings file silently falls back to defaults. The headline controls are Boost and Loudness, yet there is no hearing-safety feature, and "Tournament Clean" calls a −1 dBTP limiter "hearing protection".

**Conclusion.** Three things decide whether Flubsound Pro is worth using, and all three come before any new DSP: (a) get audio into the engine honestly: stop the failing Automatic route and the dry+wet doubling now ([E47](#e47)), and build the Windows endpoint path ([E46](#e46)); (b) run the Windows code on a real PC and measure what is heard, with a quality regression suite ([E53](#e53), [E59](#e59)); (c) fix the defects that make the product worse than bypass on common content: the stereo-in-8-channel fold, the preset latency override, the fake "loudness-matched" bypass, the Night Mode hole and the Footsteps law ([E27](#e27), [E40](#e40), [E37](#e37), [E21](#e21), [E19](#e19)). Several of these have one-day slices ([§3](#3-quick-win-enhancements)).

## 2. Prioritized enhancement list

Priority is the skeptic-adjusted rank: **High** means it fixes a defect users hear or hit today, or it gates the credibility of other work. **Medium** means real value with a narrower audience or a dependency that is not ready. **Low** means small or speculative value this cycle. Within each group, items are ordered by benefit score, then ID. Benefit is on a 1–5 scale for music listening and gaming; difficulty is Low / Medium / High / Very high; effort is in engineer-days or engineer-weeks for the team in [07 §1][07-1]. "Quick win" and "Ambitious" tags refer to [§3](#3-quick-win-enhancements) and [§4](#4-ambitious--advanced-enhancements).

<a id="e05"></a>
#### E05 · Loudness maximizer redesign: transient-aware clipping, LF-safe limiter envelope, multiband mode, transient preservation

*Audio quality · benefit 5 · difficulty Very high · 8–11 engineer-weeks · Ambitious*

- **Status (Phase 2 batch 1).** Stage 1 partially done in `LoudnessMaximizer.*` and `TruePeakLimiter.*`, judged on [E59](#e59)'s `flubsound-cli quality` suite (before → after; maximizer alone at 12 dB drive unless stated). (1) Crest-gated, depth-capped clipper: threshold raised to 6 dB over a two-stage 5 ms RMS follower (`MaximizerParams::clipCrestDb`), no sample loses more than 3 dB (`clipMaxDepthDb`, C1 blend). (2) LF-safe limiter envelope (`LimiterEnvelope`, maximizer only): a period hold (the recent peak spacing + 1/8, ≤ 25 ms, none for isolated peaks) in place of the proposed fixed 10 / 25 ms hold, which cost 1–2 LU on kick-heavy programme and lengthened every duck; a triangular attack over the same support; a 150 / 800 ms program envelope, output gain min(fast, program). Done-when rows: THD+N of −6 dBFS sines 60 Hz / 1 kHz −16.3 / −16.2 → −131 / −111 dB (≤ −30 met; `KnownGap closed: 60 Hz and 1 kHz THD+N ...`); 40 Hz at 7 dB GR, limiter alone, Balanced −32.5 → −296 dB (≤ −45 met); 2 kHz under kicks max dip 8.9 → 3.7 dB at fixed drive (≤ 6 met; p95 − p5 6.3 → 4.6, 2 Hz modulation 2.07 → 1.35 dB, the same on 1–8 kHz probes), at Music Boost 100 2.4 → 5.4 dB (still ≤ 6: the old clipper's THD+N had held the governor at a mean scale of 0.64, now 1.0); true-peak ceiling on the 11-rate matrix 8–192 kHz for the strip (met; the master keeps the plain envelope); Boost 100 kick onset / body −1.80 → −1.18 dB (≥ 0 not met); pink at Boost 100 −8.02 → −8.71 LUFS (within 1.5 LU met). Also: bass-third IMD −15.3 → −33.5 dB, SMPTE −5.1 → −45.2 dB, MTND −23.6 / −13.4 → −30.1 / −21.6 dB at −18 / −12 dBFS in (−7.8 / −4.4 → −8.3 / −7.7 LUFS out); DC after the maximizer at 24 dB drive about −141 dBFS in every profile ([E10](#e10)'s Low Latency and limiter-alone rows closed). Costs: less loudness per dB of drive (Loudness 100 on a kick programme +6.0 → +4.5 LU over the input), the kick's decay lifted against its onset (centroid shift 5.8 → 8.6 ms), render time +3–4 % (CLI, 60 s pink, maximizer alone and Signature). Preset render diff: 4 presets move beyond 0.1 dB, all on the kick programmes, most Club Loud (tone-kicks −0.92 LU, limiter time > 1 dB 8 → 42 %, THD+N max −29.9 → −33.2 dB), the others ≤ 0.23 dB; `tests/golden/preset-render-baseline.json` re-recorded for it. Tests re-based for the intended change: `test_distortion.cpp` (the clipper-reading tests use the plain curve; the clip-floor chain test a pulse), `test_protection_gaps.cpp` (governed-clipping test on a pulse; drives of the GR-window, NaN-block, +24 dBFS and segment tests), `test_neural_slot.cpp` (+12 dB model: +0.7 dB louder, was > 1), `test_maximizer.cpp` click bound 12 → 14×, the KnownGap pumping / kick-onset / DC rows. Open: `clip.maxDb` and the crest as user parameters and the named styles (steps 1, 4: `Parameters.*`, presets), 3–5 ms Quality look-ahead (3), the LF-de-emphasised detector with an LF-only limiter in the glue path (5, for ≤ 3 dB ducking), Boost-to-attack coupling (6, for onset / body ≥ 0), the master limiter's envelope, the loud-end MUSHRA.
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2).** Steps (1) and (4) done in `Parameters.*`, `ProcessingChain.cpp` and `LoudnessMaximizer.*` ([03 §11.4](03-dsp-design.md#114-parameters)). (1) The crest gate and the depth cap are parameters: `max.clipCrest` (0–24 dB, 0 = off) and `max.clipMaxDb` (0.5–24 dB, 24 = uncapped; the Approach's `clip.maxDb`, keyed under the maximizer's `max.` prefix), defaults 6 / 3 dB, the stage 1 constants. Moving them now glides over 50 ms (linear in gain) where it used to take effect at once: a hard-clipped 100 Hz tone with crest 0 ↔ 6 dB and depth 3 ↔ 12 dB every 200 ms, second difference 1.23 → 1.04 × the held settings'. (4) `max.style`: Custom (default: the six controls keep their own values), Transparent, Punchy, Aggressive, Safe; a style sets `max.clip`, `max.clipKnee`, `max.clipCrest`, `max.clipMaxDb`, `max.release`, `max.autoRelease` (published as effective values; the stored ones return under Custom), and a preset carries it as `"max.style": "Punchy"`. `flubsound-cli quality`, maximizer alone at 12 dB drive, Custom → style: Transparent MTND at −12 dBFS −21.6 → −27.2 dB, 2 kHz dip under kicks 3.7 → 2.9 dB, pink −6.9 → −7.9 LUFS; Punchy kick onset − body −0.5 → −0.1 dB, pink −5.9 LUFS, dip 5.3 dB, sine THD+N ≤ −55 dB; Aggressive −5.5 LUFS, dip 6.0 dB, 40 Hz THD+N −31.7 dB (its 3 dB gate clips steady tones); Safe (limiter only) MTND −29.6 dB, −8.7 LUFS. Migration: a preset without the keys resolves to Custom and the stage 1 values, i.e. today's sound: the preset render diff is unchanged (125 renders) and 100 CLI renders of every factory preset are sample-identical. `contentHash` does move for every preset, because it hashes every parameter of the layout, defaults included, so any new parameter changes it; `tests/golden/parameter-defaults.json` and `factory-presets.json` were re-recorded for that alone in review (the five keys in `parameter-defaults.json`; all 25 `contentHash` values). No factory preset selects a style yet (they store only non-default values; choosing one is a re-voicing). Open: steps (3), (5), (6), the master limiter's envelope, the loud-end MUSHRA, a style control among the app's main maximizer controls (today only the card's full parameter view shows the three new keys).
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2, steps 3 and 5).** Step (5) done in `LoudnessMaximizer.*`, `MacroMap.cpp` and `Parameters.*` ([03 §11.3.2](03-dsp-design.md#1132-glue)); step (3) measured and not adopted. (5) The **LF-first limiter** `max.lfLimit` (0–1, default 0, layout version 3): inside the glue path's band split, the low band alone is limited 3 dB under the ceiling before the bands are summed, from its own detector (the band's held peak over half a period of 30 Hz, 1 ms attack, 250 ms release), so a kick is taken down in the low band instead of by the wideband limiter ducking everything with it. Music Boost raises it from 0 at 50 % to 1 at 100 % (ungoverned; no factory preset goes past Boost 45 %). Tuned on the E59 suite at Boost 100 (threshold 0 / −1 / −2 / −3 / −6 dB: kick onset − body −0.55 / −0.29 / −0.02 / +0.21 / +0.50 dB, probe dip 3.81 / 3.39 / 2.91 / 2.55 / 2.05 dB; release 60 / 120 / 250 ms: bass IMD at 12 dB drive −27.1 / −31.7 / −37.2 dB). Done-when rows at Music Boost 100: kick onset / body −1.18 → **+0.21 dB (≥ 0 met)**; 2 kHz probe under kicks, max dip 5.37 → **2.55 dB** (quality suite; p95 − p5 4.99 → 1.09 dB) and on the pumping scene 5.52 → 2.49 dB (spread 4.86 → 1.25 dB, time > 1 dB down 14.0 → 6.0 %), i.e. under the ≤ 3 dB this item expected only from a multiband mode (`KnownGap closed: pumping ...`, `KnownGap closed: Music Boost 100 on the E59 quality suite ...`); pink at Boost 100 −8.71 LUFS unchanged. Maximizer alone at 12 dB drive with `max.lfLimit` 1: dip 3.74 → 2.18 dB, −6 dBFS sine THD+N at 40 / 60 / 1000 Hz −70 / −59 / −99 dB (≤ −30 met), bass IMD −33.5 → −37.2 dB. Costs: bass weight at the loud end (pumping scene at Boost 100: below 150 Hz +4.0 → +1.3 dB, the 2 kHz tone +5.8 → +7.5 dB, integrated −10.19 → −9.35 LUFS), MTND at −18 dBFS with it fully on −30.1 → −25.6 dB (no look-ahead: the 1 ms attack modulates the multitone's low tones), and Boost 100 + Loudness 100 on the kick-heavy governed-macros scene −11.13 → −8.57 LUFS (the wideband limiter reduces less, so the governor's GR budget no longer backs Boost off; block-size spread 0.01 → 0.00 LU). (3) Quality look-ahead 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 ms (`quality --profile quality`, before step 5): IMD −33.7 / −34.3 / −34.8 / −35.3 dB and MTND −30.1 / −30.3 / −30.4 / −30.5 dB gain a little, but the probe dip grows 3.84 / 4.00 / 4.13 / 4.25 dB and Boost 100 kick onset − body falls −1.22 / −1.34 / −1.44 / −1.56 dB (the longer attack ducks before the kick's first peak): not worth 1–3 ms of latency, so Quality stays at 2 ms (latency unchanged, [03 §11.6](03-dsp-design.md#116-latency--cpu)). Also: the bypass reference's limiter has the LF-safe envelope ([E10](#e10)). Preset render diff: no audio value moves (every factory preset is below Boost 50 %); only `thdnMaxDb` moves, from [E06](#e06)'s window alignment. Open: step (6) Boost-to-attack coupling; the MixEngine master limiter's envelope; `max.lfLimit` in Gaming (its splitter is not armed there, and arming it would move every gaming preset), in the named styles and as an app control; the loud-end MUSHRA.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, step 6 and the master envelope).** Partially done in `ProcessingChain.*`, `MacroMap.cpp` and `MixEngine.cpp` ([03 §14.2](03-dsp-design.md#142-boost-intensity-staging), [§11.3.2](03-dsp-design.md#1132-glue)). How Boost drives the maximizer across its range, measured on the E59 quality suite (Music, Boost 0–100): (a) the LF-first limiter's row now reaches 1 at Boost 75 % instead of 100 % (still from 50 %, ungoverned); (b) step (6), Boost's **transient coupling**: once per governor tick the limiter's deepest GR over its 1 s programme GR (the transient GR, held about 1 s) adds up to +1 dB of Clarity attack, times smoothstep(50 %, 100 %) of Boost and the SafetyGovernor's scale (governed), slewed at 4 dB/s, Music only; a steady tone's GR is its own programme, so steady material gets nothing, and `reset()` restores the uncoupled attack. Before → after at Boost 70 / 80 / 90 / 100: kick onset − body −2.14 / −0.89 / −0.05 / +0.21 → −1.29 / **+0.23 / +0.20 / +0.30 dB** (≥ 0 now from 80 %; (a) alone gave −1.46 / −0.30 / +0.16 / +0.21), 2 kHz probe dip 1.94 / 2.69 / 2.74 / 2.55 → 1.37 / 1.70 / 1.94 / 2.23 dB, pink loudness unchanged (−10.13 / −9.00 / −8.89 / −8.71 LUFS), 1 kHz sine THD+N unchanged (the Done-when row −94.8 dB at 100 %). KnownGaps: pumping scene at Boost 100 dip 2.49 → 2.10 dB (spread 1.25 → 0.99, time > 1 dB down 6.0 → 4.0 %, lift 0.97 → 1.46 dB), the quality-suite Boost 100 row dip 2.55 → 2.23 dB, onset − body +0.21 → +0.30 dB, and a new Boost 80 row in the kick-onset case (−0.89 → +0.23 dB); Boost 100 + Loudness 100 on the governed-macros scene −8.57 → −8.73 LUFS (block-size spread 0.00 LU). Costs: the kick's 40–60 ms at Boost 100 +2.39 → +1.53 dB over the input, its energy centroid −2.8 → −5.0 ms, the limiter's deepest GR 3.55 → 3.97 dB, the bass two-tone IMD at Boost 70 / 80 / 90 / 100 −12.6 / −9.2 / −6.5 / −5.6 → −11.6 / −7.7 / −6.0 / −5.5 dB (the LF-first limiter's 1 ms attack on the low band, now fully on from 75 %; the chain's bass harmonics and saturation dominate these readings); a 2 dB cap reached +0.47 dB but cost 1.7 dB of that window (centroid −6.8 ms), so the cap is 1 dB. No factory preset reaches Boost 50 %, so no preset render moves from (a) or (b). Styles across Boost's range: measured and not coupled. At Boost 70 / 100 the named styles trade kick onset − body against dip and loudness (Custom −2.14 / +0.21 dB, dip 1.94 / 2.55 dB, −10.13 / −8.71 LUFS; Transparent −1.85 / +0.52, 2.96 / 3.54, −10.14 / −9.03; Punchy −2.38 / −0.06, 1.76 / 2.25, −10.12 / −8.07; Aggressive −2.59 / −0.57, 1.75 / 1.77, −10.12 / −7.90; Safe −1.74 / +0.98, 3.14 / 4.13, −10.15 / −9.29) and none is better than Custom on all three, so Boost keeps Custom's values. The **MixEngine master limiter** now has the LF-safe envelope (latency unchanged): two strips summing 40 Hz 6 / 10 dB over the ceiling, THD+N −32.9 / −29.2 → below −300 dB; the ceiling holds (`tests/test_limiter.cpp`; the CLI has no master, so no render moves). Found, not changed: below Boost 60 % Music kick onset − body is about −2.9 dB at every Boost above 0 (Boost 1 % −3.28 dB against +0.11 dB at 0; Gaming +0.1 … +0.5 dB from 0 to 60 %), because Boost arms the glue's band split from its first percent (the floor that avoids a comb-filtered crossfade at 40 %) and the 120 Hz LR4 sum is an all-pass with about 4 ms of group delay under 80 Hz; arming it only with glue, engaged at parked corners the way the bass engine's stages are, would remove that from the 8 Music and device presets at Boost 15–35 % that set no glue, a re-voicing, and a delay that size at 50 Hz is likely inaudible. Open: that decision, the look-ahead (step 3, not adopted), `max.lfLimit` in Gaming and as an app control, a style control in the app, the loud-end MUSHRA. *Review (Phase 3 batch 2):* the master envelope's cost in the app: a mix pushed about 11 dB over full scale now leaves its loudest sample 0.62 dB under the headset ceiling cap (0.07 dB with the plain envelope), so `tests/app/test_app_headset_cap.cpp`'s bound went from 0.6 to 0.7 dB; the quality ratchet's Boost 100 kick onset and centroid rows now meet their targets and lost their `recorded` values (+0.30 dB, −4.95 ms).
- **Status (p3b3, app controls).** The app-control remainders are closed in `ui/ModuleCard.*` and `ui/ModuleRack.*` (the ModuleDescriptor table): the Maximizer card's main controls are Drive · Ceiling · **Style** (`max.style` combo: Custom / Transparent / Punchy / Aggressive / Safe) · Clipper · Glue · Release · **LF Limit** (`max.lfLimit`, 0–100 %); under a named style the Clipper and Release knobs, which the style sets (published as effective values; the stored ones return under Custom), dim to 42 % and the card's note reads *Style Punchy sets Clipper and Release*. Before: the three keys only in the expanded card's generic grid. Both write the selected strip's parameters (defaults Custom / 0), so no sound, golden or parameter moves and no parameter was added. Tested in `tests/app/test_app_ui_guards.cpp` (*the Maximizer card has Style and LF Limit ...*, plain and under Xvfb); screenshot state `module-readings`. Still open (DSP, unchanged here): `max.lfLimit` in Gaming and in the named styles, the glue-split arming decision (a re-voicing), the look-ahead, the loud-end MUSHRA.
- **What.** Gate the soft clipper on crest factor against a 5–10 ms envelope and cap its depth at about 3 dB (user-adjustable), so extra drive becomes limiter gain reduction (GR) rather than clip depth. Give the limiter an adaptive gain hold (at least half a period of the lowest frequency present), a two-stage fast + program envelope, a smoothed (cascaded-box or Hann) attack, 3–5 ms look-ahead in Quality only, and named styles (Transparent, Punchy, Aggressive, Safe). Couple Boost's attack amount to measured GR so transients survive heavy limiting. Multiband limiting comes last, only if cheaper fixes fail.
- **Why.** Every listener hears this stage once Boost passes about 25 % (Boost engages `MaximizerOn` at 0.25–0.27). Music: at loud-end drives the output is grossly distorted, sustained bass ripples, and every kick ducks vocals and pads. Gaming: explosions pull footsteps and positional cues down through the wideband limiter.
- **Expected benefit.** The most direct "cheap versus pro" lever at the loud end. Today: THD+N −16.0 dB for a −6 dBFS sine at 12 dB drive (limiter alone −37.7 dB); −32.5 dB on 40 Hz at about 7 dB GR (−30.3 dB in Low Latency); a 2 kHz tone ducked 22.6 dB under 55 Hz kicks and more than 1 dB down 36.4 % of the time; Boost 100 lowers kick onset/body by 3.9 dB. The clip cap alone only moves distortion into the limiter's LF ripple, so cap and envelope must ship together. The multiband mode is the least certain part and stays out of the first release.
- **Approach.** (1) `LoudnessMaximizer` `updateClipThreshold()` / `softClip()`: a 5–10 ms RMS follower, crest-gated clip, `clip.maxDb` default 3 dB, excess routed to GR. (2) `TruePeakLimiter` already has run-based release blending (`kRunGapMs`, `kBlendStartMs`/`kBlendEndMs`) but only an 8-sample true-peak hold. Add a 10 ms (25 ms when < 50 Hz energy is present) gain hold after the look-ahead minimum, a triangular or Hann attack in place of the single `boxRing` (keeping Kh ≤ L/3), and a 300–800 ms program envelope, taking min(fast, program). (3) Look-ahead 3–5 ms in Quality only; Balanced stays at 1.5 ms (R1.1). (4) Styles as parameter bundles. (5) Before any multiband mode, try an LF-de-emphasised detector plus an LF-only limiter in the existing `ThreeBandSplitter` glue path. (6) Boost coupling in `ProcessingChain::applyParameters()`, hard-capped and slew-limited, because it is a positive-feedback loop.
- **Depends on.** The [E06](#e06) quick slice first, so the loop being tuned is stable. [E04](#e04) for attack coupling. Ship the LF envelope with or before [E01](#e01)'s +10 dB LFE default. The MixEngine master limiter shares `TruePeakLimiter`, so the master bus changes too.
- **Done when.** Sine at 12 dB drive: THD+N ≤ −30 dB at 60 Hz and 1 kHz. 40 Hz at about 7 dB GR: ≤ −45 dB in Balanced. 2 kHz under kicks: max dip ≤ 6 dB with the LF-de-emphasised detector (≤ 3 dB only with multiband). True-peak ceiling holds on the 11-rate matrix for strip and master. Boost 100 kick onset/body ≥ 0 dB. Integrated LUFS at max Boost within 1.5 LU of today. Loud-end MUSHRA on 10 masters against Pro-L 2 and Ozone at matched LUFS.
- **Evidence.** [LoudnessMaximizer.cpp][LM] `updateClipThreshold()`, `softClip()`; [TruePeakLimiter.cpp][TPL] single `boxRing`, `kTruePeakHold = 8`; [ProcessingChain.cpp][PC] `prepare()` (2 ms Quality look-ahead); [MacroMap.cpp][MM] Boost rows; [03 §4.9][03-49]; CLI measurements above.

<a id="e15"></a>
#### E15 · Per-output-device correction layer with AutoEQ/Peace import and target curves

*Headsets · benefit 5 · difficulty High · MVP 3–4 weeks, total 8–13 engineer-weeks · Ambitious*

- **Status (Phase 2 batch 1).** MVP partially done. Before: no correction stage (`MixEngine::process` summed the strips straight into the master limiter), no import, a hand-built correction was wiped on every preset load. After: `DeviceCorrection` (`core/include/flub/dsp/DeviceCorrection.h`, [03 §14.10](03-dsp-design.md#1410-device-correction-and-the-headroom-predictor-desktop-app)) runs between the strip sum and `master.process` (`MixEngine::getDeviceCorrection()`): up to 16 RBJ-exact SVF sections per channel, per-channel filters and gain, the automatic preamp from [E11](#e11)'s predictor, curves handed over through an SPSC ring and crossfaded over 20 ms (equal-gain raised cosine), off = bit-exact passthrough, Compare = filters off with the broadband gain kept. Parser `core/include/flub/io/ParametricEqText.h`: AutoEQ `ParametricEQ.txt` and the APO subset of Approach step 3 (Preamp; PK / LSC / HSC / LS / HS with Q; slope-form shelves → RBJ Q; LP / HP / BP / NO / AP; BW Oct; Channel; Device ignored), refusing GraphicEQ / Include / Convolution / Stage / scripting and the corner-frequency shelves with the line and a reason; `format()` round-trips exactly. App: one curve per output endpoint in `AppSettings` (`device.corrections`, APO text), outside `ParameterStore`, applied by `EngineController` on every device change and carried into every rebuilt engine by `AudioEngineHost`; Settings > Correction imports, switches, compares and removes it ([06 §6.11](06-gui.md#611-settingsdialog)). Measured (`tests/test_device_correction.cpp`, synthetic curves only): rendered 1 s impulse response against an independent cookbook-biquad reference at 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz, 20 Hz – 20 kHz, 24 points/oct: worst 0.0003 dB (Done-when 0.1 dB); 0 dBFS-peak pink through a 10-filter curve without its file preamp: −2.69 dBFS out (automatic preamp −5.67 dB; Done-when < 0 dBFS); a curve change during a 1 kHz + 60 Hz programme stays within −64 dBFS of the ideal crossfade, with no sample step above the steady signal's largest. `tests/app/test_app_device_correction.cpp`: preset loads, A/B, BypassAll, an automatic profile applied and restored, a strip-layout rebuild and a restart leave it untouched; an endpoint change swaps it (to flat for an endpoint without one); rendered through the engine a +6 dB bell's preamp takes 6.0 dB off 200 Hz. Preset render diff: 125 renders within 0.1 dB of the committed baseline (the CLI has no correction stage). Review: `DeviceCorrection::process` / `reset` joined the `[[clang::nonblocking]]` `static_assert`s in `tests/test_rtsan.cpp` (they were annotated but not checked), and the parser now refuses Preamp lines that add up beyond ±30 dB per channel (each line was bounded, their sum was not). Open: the Done-when's check over 20+ real AutoEQ files against a numpy reference (blocked on the licence review, Approach step 5; only synthetic curves are tested), stable endpoint IDs (the key is the output device name, [E51](#e51)), GraphicEQ (needs the convolver), targets, amount, bass / tilt preferences, boost caps, the per-model database and [E16](#e16)'s suggestion, a curve display, the plug-in and CLI, running the Windows / macOS app, and the lab and blind tests.
- **Status (p3b4, endpoint key).** The stable-endpoint-ID item of the Open list is done ([E51](#e51)'s p3b4 Status line has the details): each correction is stored with the endpoint's id and hardware id beside its name and found by the endpoint's identity, so a curve saved on *Headset Earphone (Stealth 700 Gen 2)* is applied after a re-plug into another USB port as *(2- Stealth 700 Gen 2)*, and follows a rename; files keyed by the name load and gain the ids the first time the endpoint is seen with them (`tests/app/test_app_device_safety.cpp`). Without platform ids (Linux, macOS) two identical headsets under one name still share a curve. Not run on Windows yet; the rest of the Open list stands. No golden or baseline entry moves.
- **What.** A post-sum "Device correction" stage before the master limiter: 16-band minimum-phase PEQ with per-channel bands (unit imbalance) and an automatic preamp computed from `responseDb`. It is keyed to the output endpoint and kept out of presets, A/B and auto-profile restores. Import AutoEQ `ParametricEQ.txt` and Equalizer APO/Peace syntax. Later: GraphicEQ, target selector (Harman OE 2018 / IE 2019, diffuse field, a mild "competitive" 2–5 kHz lift), amount 0–100 %, bass/tilt preferences, boost caps, and a licence-checked per-model database including Turtle Beach models.
- **Why.** For both music fidelity and competitive gaming, the largest audible error is the headphone's own response (typically ±5–10 dB with 5–10 kHz peaks). Every preset and footstep boost lands on top of it, and a hand-built correction is wiped on every preset load. Peace/AutoEQ, Sonar and SoundID all offer this.
- **Expected benefit.** The highest-value item for both audiences. Most of it comes from importing a per-model `ParametricEQ.txt` (AutoEQ has already fitted it to a Harman target) into a per-endpoint stage. The classic SVF bell and Q-shelf are algebraically the RBJ cookbook responses, so imports match without [E09](#e09). Target selection and tilt need raw measurements that users rarely have and that may not be redistributable.
- **Approach.** (1) `DeviceCorrection` between the strip sum and `master.process` in `MixEngine::process`: two mono `ParametricEq` instances (Classic response, 16 bands), preamp = −max(0, max responseDb over 20 Hz–20 kHz), coefficient sets swapped atomically with a 20 ms crossfade. (2) A `CorrectionStore` outside `ParameterStore`, so `PresetIO::applyToStore`, A/B banks and the auto-profile restore points never touch it, keyed on a stable endpoint ID (`IMMDevice::GetId`, CoreAudio UID, PipeWire node.name + serial). Never auto-apply from the family-level `DeviceProfiles` name match; only suggest. (3) Parsers in `core/src/io/` for AutoEQ and the APO subset (Preamp; Filter PK/LSC/HSC/LS/HS; Channel; slope-form shelves converted to RBJ Q); refuse Include/Convolution/Stage with a clear message. (4) Targets only when raw measurements exist. (5) Check each measurement source's licence separately (AutoEQ code is MIT; oratory1990, crinacle and Rtings data have their own terms).
- **Depends on.** [E11](#e11)'s static-boost predictor for the preamp. [E16](#e16) only for automatic suggestion. The partitioned convolver (shared with [E12](#e12) Phase C, [E29](#e29)) for GraphicEQ FIRs. The device lab ([10 §5][10-5]) for in-house corrections.
- **Done when.** A rendered log sweep matches the imported curve within 0.1 dB from 20 Hz to 20 kHz, checked against an independent numpy RBJ reference over 20+ real AutoEQ files. 0 dBFS pink input stays below 0 dBFS after the preamp. Preset load, A/B swap and auto-profile switch leave the correction untouched. An endpoint change swaps it without a click. Lab: residual against target ≤ ±2 dB from 100 Hz to 8 kHz on a Turtle Beach headset; blind preference test.
- **Evidence.** [MixEngine.cpp][ME] `process()` (sum → master limiter only); [DeviceProfiles.h][DPh] `Profile` has no EQ, sensitivity or target fields; [device-profiles.json][DPJ] 8 entries, no sound data, all suggesting Flubsound Signature / Competitive FPS; [PresetIO.cpp][PIO] `applyToStore`; [PresetManager.cpp][PM] `loadIntoBank` keeps only `BypassAll`; [Svf.h][SVF] `SvfCoeffs::make()`; roadmap item 3.8 in [07][07]; [09 §2][09-2].

<a id="e19"></a>
#### E19 · Redesign the footstep/detail enhancer: programme-relative, onset-gated, and fast on real steps

*Gaming · benefit 5 · difficulty High · 4–6 engineer-weeks, interim fix < 1 day · Ambitious*

- **Status (Phase 1 batch).** Interim (0) done in `ProcessingChain.cpp` `configureModeBands` (bands 4 / 5 threshold −42 / −45 → −6 dBFS); 20 ms steps under a bed at −24 / −40 / −50 LUFS lift 0.00 / −0.01 / +3.81 → +5.76 dB (≥ 90 % of the steady +5.70 dB, contrast change within 0.5 dB of a static bell's), shots +0.6 to +1.1 dB. Open (KNOWN_GAP): −14 LUFS (+3.81 / +2.30 dB), steps out of digital silence (+0.52 vs +5.71 dB), Competitive FPS bed +4.37 dB and contrast −1.66 dB at −50 LUFS; the redesign.
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 1).** Redesign partially done. (1) The `CueEnhancer` is `DynEqMode::CueLift` in `DynamicEq.*`, run by Gaming mode bands 4 / 5 (`ProcessingChain.cpp` `configureModeBands`): the band's mean square (2.5 ms, or four periods at low frequencies) against its own background (rises ≤ 5 dB/s, falls with 400 ms, never under the −75 dB hiss floor, learnt over the first 300 ms), onset gate 2 → 4.5 dB over it, 30 ms hold, 1 / 40 ms attack / release, and a loud cap on the band peak 26 → 36 dB over max(background, −55 dBFS) and −6 → −1 dBFS absolute that withdraws the lift within 0.5 ms. (2) By "leave the gaming table": Footsteps and Boost no longer drive the broadband upward compressor in `MacroMap.cpp` (Detail does). Band 6 decoupled ([E20](#e20)); the 9 gaming presets retuned (below). Measured on the [E60](#e60) stage 1 scenes (`tests/test_scenes.cpp`) at −14 / −24 / −40 LUFS: module (Footsteps 100) step / bed contrast −2.04 / +0.77 / +0.77 → +5.86 / +6.04 / +6.04 dB, bed +1.98 / +2.44 / +2.44 → +0.01 / +0.07 / +0.07 dB; Competitive FPS contrast −1.50 / +0.50 / +0.35 → +4.69 / +4.98 / +5.07 dB, bed +1.89 / +2.50 / +3.63 → +0.03 / +0.40 / +0.85 dB; Battle Royale contrast −1.45 / +0.06 / +0.31 → +3.70 / +4.35 / +4.83 dB, bed +0.84 / +3.92 / +5.48 → −2.46 / +0.16 / +0.78 dB. On the E59 scenes (`tests/test_known_gaps.cpp`): isolated −60 dBFS steps out of digital silence 0.52 / 0.93 / 1.65 → 5.34 / 5.63 / 5.67 dB against 5.68 dB steady (KnownGap closed); 20 / 40 / 80 ms steps under a bed 5.24 / 6.03 / 6.53 dB at every level from −14 to −50 LUFS (interim 3.81 / 3.18 / 2.57 at −14 LUFS, 5.76 / 5.77 / 5.82 below), contrast change at 40 ms −1.44 (−14 LUFS) / +0.76 → +5.99 dB (a static +7 dB bell: +0.77); Competitive FPS bed −0.06 / +0.66 / +1.66 / +4.37 → −1.86 / −1.30 / −0.94 / +0.69 dB and contrast (20 / 40 / 80 ms) ≥ +4.39 dB at every level (was −1.67 … +0.83); gunfire in the 3.2 kHz band (Competitive FPS / Battle Royale / Night Mode) +1.13 / +0.94 / +0.58 → +0.22 / +0.25 / +0.05 dB; −50 / −60 dBFS pink beds through Competitive FPS +5.16 / +9.79 → +2.57 / +2.52 LU (CLI). A stationary sound is its own background and is not lifted: a steady −60 dBFS 3.2 kHz tone 6.43 → 0.00 dB over 1 kHz at 48 kHz; the last 500 ms of a 1 s burst under a bed 5.70 → 1.00 dB. Preset retune (render diff: only these five and the E04 pair moved): Competitive FPS Voice & Score 0.35 → 0.2; Battle Royale Footsteps 0.6 → 0.75, Voice & Score 0.4 → 0.1, Boost 0.4 → 0.35, `comp.makeup` 2 → 0 dB (it lifted the bed with everything else); Competitive FPS, Battle Royale, Night Mode, Horror Detail and 7.1 Headphone Surround carry the anti-masking band as user band `dyneq.0`; golden renders re-recorded for them (FPS −1.82, BR −3.23, Horror −0.74, 7.1 −0.53, Night −0.33 LU on the golden programme, whose steady pink the old bells lifted). The Done-when's bed ≤ +1 dB, contrast ≥ +3 dB, ≥ 80 % on 20–50 ms bursts and lift within ±1 dB across levels hold for the module, Competitive FPS and Battle Royale (asserted in `tests/test_scenes.cpp` and `tests/test_known_gaps.cpp`). Open: approach step (2) proper, a background-relative floor for Detail's upward compressor in `Compressor.cpp` (its fixed −75 dBFS floor is unchanged); (3) the maximizer's bed-lift budget; (4) referencing the tracker and cap to the pre-AutoLevel signal (a chain-order change) and running after [E15](#e15)'s correction; a spectral-flux term in the gate (level over background only today); ABX on real captures; Night Mode's bed lift (+11.13 dB in the E21 ambush scene, 11.98 before) is [E21](#e21)'s retune. [E36](#e36)'s "First Run – Game" (Competitive FPS at Boost 0.20, Footsteps 0.30, Detail 0.15) now lifts the −50 / −60 dBFS pink beds +0.97 / +0.93 LU (+2.69 / +2.58 LU before, same tree, CLI), so its cap is due for review there; the shipped Competitive FPS now passes the same ≤ 3 LU check (−60 dBFS bed +9.7 → +2.5 LU through the app engine), and `App: First Run - Game ...` in `tests/app/test_app_first_run.cpp`, which asserted it still failed (≥ 6 LU), was re-based in review.
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2).** Approach step (2) done: in Gaming the broadband upward compressor's floor follows the programme's background (`Compressor` `upRelativeFloor`, set in `ProcessingChain.cpp` for Gaming mode; Music keeps the fixed floor). The background is `BackgroundTracker` (`core/include/flub/dsp/BackgroundTracker.h`, the CueLift law: ≤ 5 dB/s up, 400 ms down, never under `comp.upFloor`, 300 ms learning), stepped every 16 samples on the detector's held peak; the lift is scaled 0 → 1 from 3 to 9 dB over it, the switch glides over 20 ms and the output stays block-size exact (`tests/test_compressor.cpp`: a −60 dBFS pink bed +9.99 → +0.00 dB, a −40 dBFS tone over it +4.14 dB either way). On the [E60](#e60) scenes at −14 / −24 / −40 LUFS nothing moves: the gaming presets' upward thresholds (−36 … −45 dBFS) sit under those beds, and the upward section added 0.00 dB of bed lift in every preset before too. The bed lift left there (Horror Detail +2.04 / +2.68, 7.1 +2.18 / +3.16, Cinematic +3.11 / +4.02, MOBA +2.83 / +4.24, Racing +3.29 / +4.16 dB at −24 / −40 LUFS) is static and contrast-neutral: the compressor make-up Detail engages, Detail's air and Boost's maximizer drive, i.e. step (3). Where the upward section works, on quiet material (the core scene at −50 / −60 LUFS, `tests/test_scenes.cpp` *Detail's upward compressor does not lift the bed of the quiet scene*): bed lift Competitive FPS +2.27 / +2.50 → +0.86 / +0.77 dB (the upward share 1.41 / 1.73 → 0.00 dB), Horror Detail +4.73 / +7.73 → +2.75 / +2.62, Battle Royale +1.22 / +3.77 → +0.77 / +0.73, Night Mode +9.72 / +13.99 → +8.43 / +6.76 dB; step / bed contrast Competitive FPS +4.40 / +5.20 → +5.26 / +5.28, Horror Detail +2.33 / +3.49 → +3.53 / +3.60 dB; hole ≤ 0.05 dB (up to 0.28 dB before). E59 *KnownGap closed: step/bed contrast* at −50 LUFS: Competitive FPS bed +0.69 → −0.93 dB, contrast 20 / 40 / 80 ms +4.55 / +4.72 / +5.21 → +4.63 / +5.35 / +5.81 dB. −50 / −60 dBFS pink beds (CLI): Competitive FPS +2.57 / +2.52 → +0.85 / +0.80 LU, Horror Detail +4.47 / +7.90 → +2.84 / +2.77, Battle Royale +0.73 / +4.04 → +0.70 / +0.67, Night Mode +11.97 / +14.72 → +11.85 / +7.13 LU; [E36](#e36)'s First Run – Game +0.97 / +0.93 → +0.49 / +0.44 LU. [E21](#e21)'s Night Mode ambush: the lift with Auto Level off 6.72 → 5.10 dB; with it the bed stays at +11.1 dB (Auto Level makes up to its +6 dB cap). Preset render diff: only Competitive FPS, Horror Detail, Night Mode and Battle Royale move, on the game-quiet and ambush programmes (re-recorded in `tests/golden/preset-render-baseline.json` in review) (the quiet bed −1.0 to −1.9 dB, `compUpwardMaxDb` −0.2 to −1.8 dB); no Music preset moves. No gaming preset retuned: with the upward share at 0 dB everywhere, no bed-lift or contrast metric calls for one; what is left is step (3). Step (4) not done. It needs no chain reorder but a hook: `ProcessingChain::process` passing `AutoLevel::getGainDb()` each block to the compressor and the dynamic EQ (a new `setReferenceOffsetDb`), which subtract it from the level they feed their background trackers and loud cap (exact for AutoLevel's broadband gain). That touches `ProcessingChain.cpp` and `DynamicEq.*`. Open: steps (3) and (4), the spectral-flux term, ABX on real captures.
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2, step 3).** The maximizer's **bed-lift budget** `max.bedLift` (0–24 dB, 24 = none, the default; layout version 3) in `LoudnessMaximizer.*` and `ProcessingChain.*` ([03 §11.3.1](03-dsp-design.md#1131-drive)): on programme that stays well clear of the ceiling the drive becomes D − w · max (0, U + D − B), where U is the lift ahead of the maximizer (EQ, compressor make-up, bass and presence boosts) and w goes from 1 (6 dB of spare headroom over the drive) to 0 (the drive reaches the ceiling) on the input's peak (400 ms release); loud programme keeps the full drive, the cap glides 20 ms and trims at most 12 dB. The chain measures U as the lift of the bed: the background (`BackgroundTracker`, the step (1) law) of a 50 ms K-weighted level at the maximizer's input minus the chain input's, stepped only on the bed (not during an event, a 400 ms loudness 6 LU over the input's background, nor loud programme, nor 2 s after either). A first version on 3 s loudness counted the steps' lift and the compressor's reaction to combat and trimmed beds up to 2.3 dB under their input at −14 LUFS; with the background trackers −14 LUFS does not move at all. Tested: a −40 dBFS bed through +4 dB of EQ and 3 dB of drive +7.0 → +1.0 dB with a 1 dB budget, a −8 dBFS programme unchanged; the maximizer alone 6 → 1 dB on quiet pink, loud pink within 0.05 dB. **Not adopted by the gaming presets in this change**: their E60 scene values are pinned in `tests/test_scenes.cpp`, which this change does not own. Measured with `"max.bedLift": 1` in every gaming preset (the scene matrix, −24 / −40 LUFS, bed lift before → after): Horror Detail +2.04 / +2.68 → +0.43 / +0.22 dB, 7.1 Headphone Surround +2.18 / +3.16 → +0.73 / +0.30, Cinematic Adventure +3.11 / +4.02 → +0.93 / +0.68, MOBA +2.83 / +4.24 → +1.16 / +0.63, Racing +3.29 / +4.16 → +1.11 / +0.80; contrast within 0.3 dB (MOBA −2.25 → −2.51 dB at −24 LUFS) or higher (Horror +3.20 / +3.22 → +3.36 / +3.40), holes of these five within 0.35 dB, recovery 0 s; −14 LUFS unchanged. The trim also applies to the combat where it stays clear of the ceiling (event change at −40 LUFS: Racing +4.09 → +0.65, Cinematic +4.51 → +1.16 dB). Night Mode is no candidate (−40 LUFS bed +9.79 → +3.74 but −24 LUFS hole +0.79 → +1.72 dB, recovery 1.4 → 2.8 s; its AutoLevel and 6 dB make-up are [E21](#e21)'s retune); Competitive FPS and Battle Royale already meet the ≤ +1 dB. Review: a budget switched on mid-stream, after the drive had moved while it was off, started its cap from the value it had released at, so the drive stepped at once (3 dB cap under a 12 dB drive: one sample moved 0.65 of the level); the cap now starts from the drive it replaces and glides (largest step 0.0013 of the level; *LoudnessMaximizer: switching the bed-lift budget on after the drive moved glides from that drive*). Open: adoption (the re-pin of those rows), E19 step (4), the spectral-flux term, ABX.
- **Status (p3b4, step 4 + onset flux).** Step (4) done without a chain reorder: `DynamicEq::setReferenceOffsetDb` and `Compressor::setReferenceOffsetDb` (`FLUB_NONBLOCKING`, in `tests/test_rtsan.cpp`), which `ProcessingChain::process` calls with `AutoLevel::getGainDb()` every block. The CueLift background and the relative term of its loud cap (the −55 dBFS reference included), and the upward compressor's `BackgroundTracker`, run on the level minus that gain (their floors moved the other way); the gain computers (onset gate, hiss taper, the −6 … −1 dBFS roll-off, the compressor curve) read the level as it is. It is exact for Auto Level's broadband gain: with a ±10 dB upstream step handed over, the Footsteps 100 bands' steps and bed and the relative upward section's bed deviate 0.00 dB from the run without the step; not handed over, +10 dB lifts the bed +4.88 dB (steps +1.04) and gives the upward section 4.90 dB of lift on a stationary bed (bed +3.19 dB), and −10 dB costs the steps 1.40 dB (`tests/test_scenes.cpp`, *an upstream gain step does not move …*). Auto Level itself moves at most 3 / 4 dB/s, inside the tracker's 5 dB/s / 400 ms, so on the [E60](#e60) scenes the effect is small: the cue enhancer's own lift in Night Mode (cue SNR gain as shipped minus with Footsteps 0) 2.08 / 2.11 / 2.37 → 2.08 / 2.08 / 2.33 dB at −14 / −24 / −40 LUFS, in First Run – Game with Auto Level on (−14 LUFS) 4.90 / 5.14 / 5.21 → 4.91 / 5.08 / 5.14 dB, the module with Auto Level on 6.01 / 6.06 / 6.06 → 6.01 / 5.98 / 5.98 dB: the Done-when's ±1 dB holds for all three (asserted, *the cue enhancer's own lift is the same …*). First Run – Game as shipped has no Auto Level (4.89 / 4.97 / 5.06 dB, bit-identical). Night Mode's whole-preset cue SNR gain still spreads 1.73 / 2.00 / 3.83 dB (was 1.73 / 2.03 / 3.87; 1.73 / 2.66 / 4.21 with Auto Level off): its 3:1 compressor squeezes the steps of the loud programme, an [E21](#e21) retune, not the tracker. Presets without Auto Level and the Music ones with it (Late Night, Podcast & Voice, Voice Chat: the relative floor and the CueLift bands are Gaming only) are bit-identical: the render diff at 0.00 dB tolerance moves only Night Mode, by ≤ 0.09 dB (ambush `compGrMaxDb` −8.31 → −8.40, true peak −15.18 → −15.10 dBTP), all 125 renders within the baseline's 0.1 dB (the p3b4 review confirmed it: 36 values of Night Mode's five programmes move at 0.00 dB, nothing else, and re-recorded them with the baseline; Night Mode's golden render −22.354 → −22.352 LUFS, largest third-octave move 0.018 dB, within the 0.05 dB gate and re-recorded with `factory-presets.json`); the scene matrix's Night Mode rows move ≤ 0.04 dB (inside their pins), the module, Competitive FPS and Battle Royale rows not at all (bed ≤ +1 dB, contrast ≥ +3 dB unchanged). The **spectral-flux term** is in, opt-in and off everywhere: `DynEqBandParams::cueOnsetFlux` keys the CueLift gate on how fast the level rises out of the background (from 2 to 4.5 dB over it within 15 ms of crossing 2 dB after 30 ms under it; the event then stays an onset until the level has sat under 2 dB for 30 ms). On the Footsteps 100 bands a 3.2 kHz swell rising out of the bed at 20 dB/s is lifted 2.81 → 0.20 dB (largest applied gain 7.00 → 1.84 dB, the first 15 ms), steady tones stay unlifted, and it costs no footstep sensitivity: the core scene's steps at −14 / −24 / −40 LUFS 6.13 dB cue SNR gain either way, −60 dBFS 20 / 40 / 80 ms bursts out of silence 5.05 / 5.67 / 5.64 dB either way (*the cue gate's onset-flux key …*). Not switched on in the mode bands: that re-voices every gaming preset (the unit keeps presets without Auto Level bit-identical), so it waits for an owner decision and `configureModeBands`. Open: adopting the flux key, [E15](#e15)-ordering of the cue band, ABX on real captures.
- **What.** A dedicated `CueEnhancer`: the lift is engaged from a slow (300–500 ms) background estimate, so a 20–40 ms step gets its lift from the first millisecond. Thresholds are programme-relative (per-band percentile floor plus offset, CutAbove from the recent median or gated LUFS, an absolute hiss floor), and the lift is gated on onset / spectral-flux likelihood in 1–6 kHz so stationary beds (rain, wind, room tone) are not raised. A minimum-statistics noise estimate replaces the fixed −75 dBFS upward-compression floor. After [E15](#e15), the cue band runs after device correction.
- **Why.** The flagship competitive control lifts real footsteps by +0.3 / +0.6 / +1.1 dB on 20 / 40 / 80 ms bursts (+3.4 dB only on steady tones), does nothing (−0.00 dB) on content at −27.5 LUFS, and on quiet material (−51.5 LUFS) raises the ambience bed while contrast barely improves. A static +6 dB bell (Superhuman Hearing, Sonar) currently beats it.
- **Expected benefit.** The largest gaming-audio defect. Measured contrast change: Footsteps 100 −4.2 dB; Competitive FPS −6.0 dB with the bed +10.9 dB. The skeptic found that about 60 % of the preset's bed lift comes from outside bands 4/5: Competitive FPS still lifts the bed +6.3 dB with Footsteps at 0 and +10.7 dB with Detail at 0, via Boost's `CompUpMaxGainDb` 5 dB, M1's 3 dB, M4's 8 dB and maximizer drive. The broadband upward compressor must therefore become background-relative too, or leave the gaming table.
- **Approach.** (0) Interim, separate commit: set bands 4/5's threshold to about −6 dBFS in `configureModeBands`, so BoostBelow acts as a static bell with a hiss taper and loud roll-off (contrast-neutral, full lift from the first sample). Ship only with a preset check that the loud cap still protects gunfire, and only after the [E59](#e59) slice's burst-contrast test exists (its Done-when below needs that metric). Ship it in the same Phase 1 batch as [E17](#e17)'s band-4 clamp, because a static bell applies the band-4 lift at every sample rate, including 8–16 kHz hands-free links. (1) `CueEnhancer` with background-relative expansion, onset gate, 30–60 ms hold, loud cap relative to the slow gated level. (2) The broadband upward compressor uses the same minimum-statistics floor. (3) Budget maximizer drive's bed lift per preset. (4) Reference the background tracker and loud cap to the pre-AutoLevel signal.
- **Depends on.** Interim (0): the [E59](#e59) slice only. Full redesign: the [E21](#e21) *slice* (AutoLevel moves the reference the cue band is measured against); E21's later Startle Guard must exclude the cue band from its sidechain, which constrains E21 and does not block this item. The [E60](#e60) stage 1 burst and ambush scenarios, which supply the contrast metrics in Done-when. [E20](#e20)'s band-6 decoupling (band 6 stops following Footsteps) ships inside this redesign; keying band 6 to E21's Tame amount waits for E21. Rewriting all 9 gaming presets, [test_factory_presets.cpp][T-fp] and [test_modes.cpp][T-modes].
- **Done when.** Module and Competitive FPS / Battle Royale presets: bed ≤ +1 dB and step/bed contrast ≥ +3 dB (today +10.9 dB and −6.0 dB). 20–50 ms bursts at −45 to −60 dBFS get ≥ 80 % of the steady law. Lift within ±1 dB across −14 / −24 / −40 LUFS. Interim config: contrast change within ±0.5 dB and ≥ 90 % of steady lift on 20 ms bursts. ABX on real captures against bypass and a static +6 dB bell, loudness-matched.
- **Evidence.** [DynamicEq.cpp][DEQ] `isExpander()` (a rising gain counts as attack only for BoostAbove/CutBelow, so band 4 rises at its 120 ms release); [ProcessingChain.cpp][PC] `configureModeBands` absolute −42 / −45 / −36 dBFS thresholds; [MacroMap.cpp][MM] `kGamingTable`; [test_modes.cpp][T-modes] steady sines only; [08 C5][08-C5].

<a id="e27"></a>
#### E27 · Game-strip input awareness: channel-layout verification, active-channel detection, native-HRTF bypass and stereo upmix

*Gaming · benefit 5 · difficulty Medium · Phase 1 1.5–2 weeks plus the Linux channel-order check 0.5–2.5 days, total 6–7 engineer-weeks (upmix split out) · Quick win (Phase 1)*

- **Status (Phase 1 batch).** Phase 1 steps (1)–(3) done in `core/*/dsp/ActiveChannelDetector.h`, `ProcessingChain.*`, `Parameters.*` (`virt.input`, `virt.ownHrtf`) and `MeterBus.h`; FL / FR-only 8-channel input: −16.78 dB notch and 10.74 dB separation → 0.00 dB and exact, switch ≤ 2.5 s, back to surround ≤ 300 ms. The passthrough ramp was made RMS-compensated in review (centred pink swelled 1.2–1.5 dB above both folds mid-ramp → none). Open: step (4), the Linux tone check and ALSA order (`AudioEngineHost`), the UI indicator.
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2, step 4 on Linux).** Done in `AudioEngineHost.*`: the per-channel tone test (`tests/app/test_app_host_io.cpp`, a fake 8-input device of JUCE type "ALSA", a −20 dBFS tone on one device input at a time, the Game chain's `activeChannelMask` naming the engine channel it reached) found the order wrong, so this was the P0: a JUCE ALSA device (the `default`, `pipewire` or `pulse` PCM, which remap the `flubsound_game` sink by position) delivers ALSA's FL FR RL RR FC LFE SL SR, and 4 of the 8 channels landed in the wrong engine slot (FC in BL, so dialogue in the rear-left virtual speaker; LFE in BR; RL / RR in FC / LFE). Channels identified 4 / 8 → 8 / 8 on ALSA; JACK (PipeWire links the monitor ports in the sink's own order, the engine's) 8 / 8 before and after. The fix is the fixed ALSA permutation for 5.1 / 7.1 strips (`AudioEngineHost::deviceInputOrder`, applied while the device inputs are gathered; chosen from the device type at each device start). Open: reading the backend's channel map (`snd_pcm_get_chmap`; a raw `hw:` USB-class device whose driver reports the WAVE order is permuted the same way today), CoreAudio / WASAPI layouts, the round-trip check, the UI indicator.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 1, step 4 remainder on Linux).** Partially done in `AudioEngineHost.*` and `PlatformServices_linux.cpp`: at each device start the host reads which speaker each device input carries, from the backend's channel map (an ALSA card PCM's capture chmap, `snd_pcm_query_chmaps_from_hw` with libasound loaded at run time, `platform::AudioChannelMaps`) or else from the channel names (JACK / PipeWire-JACK ports such as `monitor_FL`), and permutes any 2.0 / 5.1 / 7.1 strip generically (`AudioEngineHost::orderFromPositions`); without positions the fixed ALSA order (plug-in PCMs `default`, `pipewire`, `pulse`) or the identity stays. `EngineStatus::inputChannelMap` names the source. Round-trip channel check with fake 8-input devices (`tests/app/test_app_host_io.cpp`), channels identified before → after: JACK ports in ALSA order 4 / 8 → 8 / 8, scrambled ports 0 / 8 → 8 / 8, engine-order ports 8 / 8 → 8 / 8; an ALSA HW device whose chmap is WAVE order (USB audio class) 4 / 8 → 8 / 8, a scrambled chmap 1 / 8 → 8 / 8; a plug-in PCM without a map 8 / 8 unchanged. No render or KnownGap metric moves (device-input path only). Open: running it on a real sound card with a chmap (none here; the libasound lookup is tested on its pure parts and to find nothing without cards), the one-click in-app round-trip check (a tone into each channel of the Game sink), CoreAudio / WASAPI layouts, the UI indicator. The PipeWire router still links monitor port *k* to input *first + k*, the order JUCE's own JACK connections use, so the channel names describe both.
- **What.** Detect energy in channels 2–7 with hysteresis and show "receiving 2 / 6 / 8 channels". When only FL/FR carry content, crossfade to a new stereo-passthrough fold (unity, loudness-matched). A manual "game renders its own HRTF" switch (later driven by the title database and auto-profile rules) forces virtualiser off, width 1, focus 0 and crossfeed 0. Read the backend channel layout (WAVEFORMATEXTENSIBLE mask for JUCE device inputs, ALSA/JACK channel map, CoreAudio `AudioChannelLayout`) and permute into the strip. Add a one-click round-trip channel check.
- **Why.** Windows process loopback asks for an 8-channel container, and a game rendering for a stereo headset fills only FL/FR. `virt.on` defaults to true and 8 of 9 gaming presets leave it on. So stereo games and in-game-HRTF titles (CS2, Valorant, Fortnite 3D Headphones) get a second HRTF that pulls hard-left cues back to 30°. On Linux, a mis-ordered ALSA layout would put dialogue in a rear virtual speaker.
- **Expected benefit.** This fixes the mainstream Windows gaming case. Measured, fold only: hard-left noise in FL of an 8-channel file comes out at L −27.2 / R −38.0 dBFS (10.8 dB separation; ILD 0.1 dB at 200–300 Hz), where a 2-channel file gives total separation. A centred source gets a −15.4 dB notch at 1418 Hz and +2.6 dB at 250 Hz, colouring voice and footsteps in the band-7 region. Layout reading closes a plausible Linux bug: `AudioEngineHost.cpp` hard-codes `FL FR FC LFE BL BR SL SR`, but ALSA's 8-channel order puts FC at index 4.
- **Approach.** (1) Per-channel `MeanSquareFollower` after `autoLevel.process(in)`; surround when channels 2–7 exceed −50 dB relative to max(FL, FR) for 300 ms, stereo below −60 dB for 2 s. Publish `activeChannelMask` via `MeterBus`, shown next to the existing `virtualizerNeedsSurround` warning. Once surround is confirmed, latch it until the device or the routed process changes, with a manual Auto / Force surround / Force stereo override, so sparse rear content cannot flip the fold mid-match ([§6](#6-updated-risk-assessment-for-the-proposed-enhancements) T14). (2) Do not just set virtMix to 0: `downmixToStereo()` applies BS.775 with a further −3 dB, so FL/FR-only content is 3.01 dB low, and the virt path is 0.84 dB below passthrough on uncorrelated content. Add a third fold state and a 300–500 ms loudness-matched ramp. (3) The `binaural` flag in `applyParameters()` must follow the detector. (4) Linux: verify first with a per-channel tone through `flubsound_game`; if the order is wrong this is a P0 bug fix. Then read the channel map and permute pointers in `AudioEngineHost::processBlock`. (5) Split the stereo-to-7.1 upmix into a separate Low item: it fabricates direction and is wrong for competitive play.
- **Scope by phase.** Phase 1 is steps (1)–(3) (7–10 days) plus the first half of step (4): the per-channel tone check through `flubsound_game` (about 0.5 day), and if it fails, a fixed permutation for the ALSA order in `AudioEngineHost::processBlock` as a P0 fix (1–2 days), 0.5–2.5 days in all. The rest of step (4), reading the backend channel map on each OS and permuting generically, goes to the Phase 3 macOS/Linux lane (Linux) and rides on [E46](#e46) M1's channel-ID Done-when (Windows). The round-trip channel check goes with them. Step (5), the upmix, is a separate Low item listed in [§5.5](#55-later-or-gated-on-evidence). The title-database flag waits for [E26](#e26).
- **Depends on.** Nothing for Phase 1, including the in-flight engine swap: the passthrough fold is an in-chain crossfade like the existing virtualiser toggle (`virtMix` is a smoothed target in `applyParameters()`), and `ProcessingChain::needsReprepare()` treats only `latency.profile` as structural, so no Phase 1 step re-prepares the engine. The layout read happens at device open, which already re-prepares today. The database-driven flag needs [E26](#e26) and the in-flight auto profiles. The Windows round-trip check needs [E46](#e46) or a third-party cable. [E25](#e25), [E28](#e28) and [E30](#e30) reuse the detector and level matching.
- **Done when.** 8-channel FL/FR-only input switches to passthrough within 2.5 s, equals a 2-channel render within 0.1 dB, with no level step above 1 dB during the switch; rear content switches back within 300 ms; −45 dB ambience does not trigger it; a 7.1 capture with a 30 s rear-silent stretch stays in surround once surround has been confirmed. CLI: FL-only gives full separation (today 10.8 dB) and FL = FR has no notch deeper than 0.5 dB (today −15.4 dB). Linux per-channel tone test identifies all 8 channels. Listening: a hard-left CS2 cue stays hard left with the flag set.
- **Evidence.** [test_virtualizer.cpp][T-virt] "7.1 fed only FL/FR equals the stereo layout exactly"; [AudioEngineHost.cpp][AEH] hard-coded channel string (~L30); [ProcessingChain.cpp][PC] `downmixToStereo()`, `binaural = config.inputChannels > 2 && ...`; [PlatformServices_win.cpp][PSW] `channelMaskFor(8)`; [Parameters.cpp][PAR] `virt.on` default; [DeviceProfiles.cpp][DP] `adviceFor` (double-HRTF warning only for headset surround); [08 C4][08-C4]; [09 §2][09-2].

<a id="e46"></a>
#### E46 · First-party Windows system-wide path: a virtual Game 7.1, System, Chat and Mic endpoint driver, or APO Lite

*Stability & platform · benefit 5 · difficulty Very high · APO Lite 8–12 weeks; driver M1 4–6 months, M2 +3–4 months (driver total 7–10 months) · Ambitious*

- **What.** Two products tracked separately. **E46a APO Lite** ([02 §4.3][02-43]): allocation-free `flub_core` hosted in audiodg as a single-strip, per-endpoint System profile, with no driver signing and no capture FIFO. **E46b driver**: an attestation-signed fork of SimpleAudioSample exposing "Flubsound Game" (7.1) and "Flubsound System" render endpoints read through WASAPI loopback (M1), then Chat render, processed-mic and Mic endpoints and a zero-copy shared ring with sample-clock slaving (M2).
- **Why.** Without it no Windows user gets whole-system processing without VB-Cable or Voicemeeter, which Flubsound Pro may not redistribute. Games never deliver 7.1, and per-app strips cannot work. Every competitor ships its own endpoint or APO.
- **Expected benefit.** The largest competitive gap on the main gaming platform. The two tracks serve different people: APO Lite gives music and system audio processing but no 7.1 bed, no Game/Chat/Music separation, and it double-processes if the engine outputs to the same endpoint. The virtual 7.1 endpoint is what gaming needs (channel beds only, not ISpatialAudioClient objects). The latency gain arrives only at M2, since M1 still reads through loopback and keeps the 612-frame FIFO.
- **Approach.** APO Lite: SFX/MFX APO, versioned shared-memory parameters, latency via `IAudioProcessingObject::GetLatency`, a heartbeat counter the app checks, mutual exclusion with the engine output on the same endpoint, vendor-APO detection. Driver: SimpleAudioSample fork (MIT) with `KSAUDIO_SPEAKER_7POINT1_SURROUND` 48 kHz float, captured into the existing capture slots at M1; the `FlubVirtualAudioShared.h` IOCTL ring at M2. The installer must set the Game endpoint's speaker configuration to 7.1, or games open it as stereo. Driver Verifier and CodeQL for drivers, `pnputil` installer with clean uninstall. Hire or contract a Windows audio driver engineer: [07 §1][07-1] budgets a 1-FTE "Systems / driver engineer (Windows)", but that role is not filled, so [§5](#5-recommended-implementation-order) runs this item in a separate lane that starts only once someone is in the seat.
- **Depends on.** [E47](#e47) first (honest, recoverable routing whose journal covers driver endpoints). [E42](#e42) to measure M2. The in-flight engine swap for format changes. Legal review, EV certificate, Partner Center onboarding (2–6 calendar weeks).
- **Done when.** 72 h Driver Verifier under render + loopback stress. A 7.1 channel-ID file and the Windows speaker test reach the correct Game strip channels. CS2 and Apex show 8 active channels. The games launch and play with Vanguard, FACEIT and EAC present. M2: loopback-measured ≤ 12 ms end to end. APO Lite: null test confirms processing; the heartbeat survives reboot, a feature update and an OEM driver update; the double-processing guard fires.
- **Evidence.** [driver README][DRV] ("design, not implemented", ABI header only); [TRACEABILITY Known gaps][TR-gaps] R4.5, R4.6; [EngineController.cpp][EC] `looksLikeLoopbackDevice` (matches "cable output", "vb-audio", "voicemeeter"); [02 §4.3][02-43]; [01 §5.3][01-53].

<a id="e53"></a>
#### E53 · Verification of the real-time and platform layers: soak, glitch detection, fuzzing, pluginval, real Windows and macOS execution

*Stability & platform · benefit 5 · difficulty High · 8–12 engineer-weeks*

- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2).** Linux soak slice done, offline (no devices): step 4's discontinuity detector, driven by step 2's kind of automation. `DiscontinuityDetector` (`core/include/flub/analysis/Discontinuity.h`; streaming, allocation-free, `FLUB_NONBLOCKING`) reports clicks (a spike of the 4th-order difference 24 dB over the residual on both sides and above −50 dBFS, i.e. a −60 dBFS step; a jump in value by cubic fits on either side; not recurring within 25 ms except at 1 or 2 blocks, so the corners of clipped waves and a buzzy voice through saturation are set aside while zipper noise at the block rate is not), dropouts (≥ 0.5 ms of exact zeros that start abruptly after programme), NaN / Inf runs and DC steps (the 2 Hz low-pass moving ≥ −30 dBFS within 250 ms). `flubsound-cli soak` runs the chain on a seeded, streaming programme (music, game, speech-like, a loud section, a fade into 1 s of digital silence) with one host action every 250 ms ± 50 % (Boost and macros; gains, EQ, bass, clarity, width, compressor, drive; module toggles, mode, factory presets, bypass, A/B; `--automation all` sets any non-structural parameter), watches its input (a self-check) and output, and reports each detection with the action before it and whether the global bypass was engaged (`[bypassed]`, JSON `bypassed`), the peak and the block times against the budget; exit 1 on a detection. `analyze --glitches` runs the detector over a file (a loopback capture). Tests (`tests/test_soak.cpp`): clean tones 40 Hz – 16 kHz, tonal music, pink noise, onsets and fades read nothing; a skipped or repeated sample, a −60 dBFS impulse and a −50 dBFS step read once within 4 samples on tones and tonal music; a 10 s chain soak under automation in CI (about 0.7 s). `tools/scripts/soak.py` runs a 6-row matrix (Music, Gaming, Competitive FPS, Late Night at Low Latency / 128 samples, 44.1 kHz with protection Normal, the parameter fuzz) for N minutes each (1 h nightly, 8 h per release). First findings, 1 min per row: 0 dropouts, NaN or DC steps; every click of the five user rows (4–7 per row) comes 1.4 ms after `bypass -> on`: the dry reference's true-peak limiter starts cold when the global bypass engages and outputs silence for its 67-sample latency, so the dry path enters the 30 ms crossfade as a step of about 4.7 % of the signal (−37.8 dBFS of residual on a −10 dBFS 110 Hz tone). Fixed in review, in `ProcessingChain` step 7: after a cold start of that limiter the crossfade waits its latency (1.4 ms) before it moves; 1-minute rows (`soak --seconds 60`, defaults / Gaming / Competitive FPS / Late Night, seeds 1–4) 7 / 6 / 5 / 9 → 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 clicks, and the 10 s CI soak now asserts 0 clicks (*KnownGap closed: engaging the global bypass is click-free …*; `soak.py --known` has no default any more). The fuzz row read nothing in 1 min (seed 6) and 66 clicks in 10 min before that fix: 8 were that bypass-engage click (the fuzz writes `bypass = on`, now grouped with it), 22 come while the bypass is engaged with `input.gain` at +4.7 to +22.7 dB, and 36 while processing under extreme random settings, in pairs (both channels) spread over 25 different last actions, not yet triaged. Triaged: `soak --automation all --interval 100 --seed 5` clicks 8 times within 0.5 s at 52 s (−41 dBFS) because an action at 51.82 s set `input.gain` +13 dB while bypassed, and the bypass reference's true-peak limiter (1 ms look-ahead) then pulls about 13 dB off the loud scene: its gain ramp breaks the waveform at 1 % of the signal per sample (stopping the automation after that action keeps all 8; turning the bypass off at 51 s removes them; the same limiter alone on the soak programme reads 0 clicks in 60 s up to +6 dB over, 37 at +13 dB and 97 at +20 dB). That is heavy limiting of a reference driven far over the ceiling, which the detector does not tell from a glitch; the soak report marks such detections and `soak.py` counts them apart ("while bypassed"; they still fail the row). An injected 1-sample skip is flagged at 11 of 32 places in the chain's music output (KNOWN_GAP): elsewhere the waveform barely moves over one sample and the skip stays under the floor, which is what the Done-when's phase-locked tone predictor fixes. Open: steps 0, 1, 3 and 5; the real-time soak (PipeWire null sink, the 997 Hz predictor, xrun counts) and the nightly CI job.
- **Status (Phase 3, step 1: fuzzing).** Partially done: the core parsers are fuzzed; readWav, the JUCE-side parsers, pluginval and the nightly run are open. Four libFuzzer targets in `tests/fuzz` (Clang 20, `-fsanitize=fuzzer,address,undefined`; CMake option `FLUB_BUILD_FUZZERS`, off by default; they link their own instrumented copy of the core). `fuzz_json` covers `json::parse` / `write`: accepted text must write, parse back and write to the same text. `fuzz_preset` covers `preset::migrate` + `fromJson`: accepted presets stay in range, round-trip through `toJson` (sparse and full) with the same values, uuid, suggested profile and `contentHash`, and through a `ParameterStore`. `fuzz_state` covers a strip's saved A/B state as `EngineController` restores it, user device-profile files (`Database::load`, `match`, `adviceFor`) and plug-in state recall (`resolveSavedState`). `fuzz_eqtext` covers `eqtext::parse`: the limits hold, a failed parse leaves the curve untouched, and `parse (format (c)) == c`. Seeds: `tests/fuzz/corpus`, the factory presets and the device-profile database; `tests/fuzz/run-fuzzers.sh <build> <seconds>` runs the four in parallel; the CI job `fuzz` runs each for 30 s (about 75 s locally from a fresh build). Found and fixed, each with its reproducer in the seed corpus: (a) a user device-profile file with `"specificity": 1e12` cast an out-of-range double to `int` (UB, and `match()` multiplies it by 1000 in an `int`) → specificity is clamped to ±1000 and `ceilingDbTp` to −12..0 dBTP before the casts (`DeviceProfiles.cpp`); (b) a number for a choice or toggle (`"mode": 0.4`) was kept as 0.4 but saved as `"Music"`, so saving and reloading changed the preset's values and `contentHash` → such numbers are read as the chain reads them, the nearest index and on at ≥ 0.5 (`PresetIO.cpp`; factory presets use labels, so no preset, golden or render moves); (c) a correction file whose commands were all ignored (only `Channel: C LFE` lines) or a flat `Preamp: 0 dB` imported as a flat curve, but `format()` wrote no command, so the text the app stores failed to parse on the next start → a flat curve is written as `Preamp: 0 dB` (`ParametricEqText.cpp`). After the fixes, 5 min per target found nothing (2.8 M / 1.6 M / 1.8 M / 4.6 M executions). pluginval (step 3) could not be obtained offline here and was not run. Open: `readWav` (it reads from a path and needs an in-memory entry point); the app's settings XML and the plug-in's ValueTree (JUCE's parsers, which need a JUCE fuzz build); replacing the fixed-seed loops in `test_json.cpp` and `test_wav.cpp`; the 1 h nightly job; steps 0, 3 and 5; the real-time soak.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, pluginval and the nightly fuzz).** Partially done in CI. Step 3: the `app` job downloads pluginval's latest release on all three OSes and validates the Flubsound FX VST3 at strictness 5, in process, editor tests included (Linux under Xvfb); a failed test fails the job, after the test build is uploaded, and the log is kept as an artifact. It could not be run by the batch (no GitHub release download then), so reading the plug-in for what pluginval checks at that level (bus layouts, state save / restore, parameter ranges and text, processing at several rates and block sizes, thread use of the re-prepare timer) found nothing to change, and neither did a stand-in run here through JUCE's own VST3 hosting (not in the repository): mono, stereo, 5.1 and 7.1 inputs accepted, 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz × 64–1024-sample blocks and 200 blocks of random automation with no NaN, Inf or subnormal output, every continuous parameter restored exactly from saved state, state saved and loaded from a background thread. Step 1: `.github/workflows/nightly.yml` runs the four fuzzers for 1 h each every night (by hand with any duration), starting from the corpus earlier nights grew (Actions cache); before, only the 30 s per-commit smoke. *Review (Phase 3 batch 2):* pluginval 1.0.4 (the latest release, as the CI step downloads it) ran here on the GCC Release VST3 under `xvfb-run -a`, exactly as the step runs it: strictness 5 passed (every test group: open cold / warm, info, programs, editor, editor while processing, audio processing at 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz × 64–1024-sample blocks, state, automation, editor automation, automatable parameters, the bus tests), and so did strictness 10 (13 s); Windows and macOS remain for the first CI run. Open: strictness 10 in CI and plug-in state round-trip tests in the repository, `readWav` and the JUCE-side parsers, the nightly soak job, steps 0 and 5.
- **Status (p3b4, strictness 10 and the state round-trip).** Step 3 done in the repository; strictness 10 on Windows and macOS waits for the first CI run. (a) CI: batch 2's run [36575196651](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/36575196651) passed pluginval at strictness 5 on all three OSes, so the `app` job's pluginval step now runs strictness 10 (in process, editor included, Linux under Xvfb, still blocking). pluginval 1.0.4 at strictness 10 passes here on the GCC Release VST3 under `xvfb-run -a` (every group, the fuzzed parameters included, 14 s), after the fixes below. (b) State round-trip tests in the repository: `tests/app/test_plugin_state.cpp` (4 cases, 0.4 s) compiles the plug-in's processor into `flub_app_tests` when `FLUB_BUILD_PLUGIN` is on (`tests/app/CMakeLists.txt`), so it runs in the CI `app` job on all three OSes. Every parameter at four settings (minimum, maximum and normalised 0.3 / 0.77 as a host sets them; toggles both ways; every choice entry, the latency profile included), a new instance, and all **30** factory presets imported as a user does: the reopened instance holds the same values bit for bit, saves the same bytes and processes 4 × 512 samples to the same samples at the same latency; a new instance holds the core's defaults and an imported preset the preset's own values, bit for bit. Found and fixed in `plugin/Source/PluginProcessor.*`: (1) a loaded state, saved again, was different bytes in every case (0 / 4 cases passed): `setStateInformation` rebuilt the PARAMs in layout order, while an instance writes them in JUCE's internal parameter order; it now keeps the saved state's own order and appends what the state lacks (values were already equal). (2) Float dust through the host's normalised 0..1: a new instance and an imported preset played values beside the core's (the *Acoustic & Singer-Songwriter* import: 33 values, `comp.makeup` −9.5e−7 dB for 0, `dyneq.0.freq` 89.9999 Hz for 90, at most 0.001 off; a new instance: every skewed default, `gate.attack` 5.0000005 ms), and a saved project stored that dust; the defaults, an imported preset and a loaded state now set the raw values and the state tree exactly (`snapRawValues`: only values within 1e−5 of the range, never on the audio thread; a host's own automation is never changed), so the plug-in plays the same values as the app → 0 values off. No core code, golden file, render baseline or `contentHash` moves; the plug-in's own output moves by that dust only.
