no integrated loudness moves by 0.1 LU. `tests/test_scenes.cpp` pins (Night Mode and Late Night only, plus the guard rows): the E60 matrix Night Mode at −14 LUFS (event change −9.03 → −8.97, onset jump 0.65 → 0.57, others ≤ 0.03), at −24 (event change −7.44 → −7.12, onset jump 1.80 → 1.20, hole −1.14 → −1.19, step lift after 0.78 → 1.05 dB), at −40 (event change −0.05 → −0.29, onset jump −0.07 → 0.28); Late Night at −24 (onset jump 1.89 → 1.76, hole −1.12 → −0.89, recovery 2.08 → 2.43 s, step after 0.19 → −0.06) and −40 (onset jump 2.72 → 2.56, hole −0.46 → 0.07); dialogue at −14 Night Mode SNR −5.75 → −5.30 dB; speech → music → silence Night Mode at −14 (silence lift 8.37 → 8.12, speech return 0.01 → 0.11), Late Night silence lift 6.30 / 6.75 → **5.81** / 6.53 dB (−14 LUFS now ≤ 6: the review cleared its `silenceGap` flag); track change Night Mode −14 (step −4.66 → −5.39, overshoot −0.16 → 0.45) and −24 (step −0.88 → −2.32, overshoot −0.95 → 0.36, settle 0.94 → 0 s), Late Night −14 (overshoot 0.88 → 0.98) and **−24 settle 0 → 1.88 s** (the review set its `knownGap` flag at −24: the upper gate now holds the whole loud track and its compressor takes the 15 LU step; the 3 s measure's leak used to take part of it); the guard rows' onset jumps (0.23 / 0.47 / 0.09 / 0.35 / 0.41 → 0.02 / 0.07 / −0.28 / −0.27 / −0.21 dB) and event changes (+0.06 to +0.27 dB). `tests/test_known_gaps.cpp`'s Night Mode ambush event −10.31 → −10.28 dB stays inside its pin. The KnownGap ratchet and every other preset's value do not move.
  - **Open:** Night Mode's first combat event at −24 LUFS (the compressor attack, owner), the combat row of the 10-minute run (a fight at a loud ambience is levelled like programme), Late Night's −24 LUFS track-change settle, and the guard as a `TruePeakLimiter` sidechain (not needed for latency: none added).
- **What.** (1) A slow leveller with an upper gate: blocks more than about 8 LU above the slow measure are left out of integration, quiet-context upward gain is capped (+6 dB), and downward adaptation freezes during short events. (2) A Startle Guard: a momentary-loudness cap at "slow reference + N LU" (Shield +6, Balanced +10, Off) implemented as a sidechain gain computer inside `TruePeakLimiter` so it adds no latency, with a sidechain weighting that does not duck the 2–5 kHz cue band. (3) A user "Dynamic range" control (10/15/20 LU), a multiband night stage, and corrected "hearing protection" wording in Tournament Clean.
- **Why.** In Night Mode an explosion arrives far above ambience and footsteps then sit several dB down for seconds. A night mode that startles defeats its purpose. Music: Late Night misses its −20 LUFS target (−22.1 / −23.1 LUFS).
- **Expected benefit.** The defect is confined to presets that enable Auto Level (Night Mode, Late Night, Podcast & Voice; the parameter default is off). Measured on an ambush scene (12 s of −50 dBFS ambience, then −12 dBFS gunfire): Competitive FPS has no gap (+3.0 dB before and after); Night Mode lifts the bed +16.6 dB, drops the event −5.2 dB, then leaves a 6.6 dB hole not recovered after 6 s. These are round-7 (pre-`d096a4d`) values. `d096a4d` changed the gated-loudness release that AutoLevel uses; on a `d096a4d` build the post-report review measured the bed at +5–7.5 s after the event at +6.8 dB against input (+5.6 dB before), still far below the pre-event lift, so the hole persists but has a different shape. On a scene with a shorter, lower-energy event (4 s of 40 ms bursts at −12 dBFS peak over a −50 dBFS pink bed) both builds lift the bed +12 to +16 dB and leave no hole. The E59 slice must re-baseline the ambush metric on the current tree with a pinned scene. On competitive presets the full 38 dB contrast passes through (event peak −1.1 dBFS), so the guard is a comfort feature there, as in Dolby Volume, Sonar and Nahimic.
- **Approach.** Slice in AutoLevel only: a 400 ms K-weighted momentary measure beside the 3 s one; freeze integration while momentary > slow + 8 LU; cap upward gain at +6 dB (today ±12); faster upward slew after a freeze (e.g. 3 dB/s for 2 s; today 1 dB/s); retune Night Mode's target. Then the guard in the limiter sidechain, AutoDrive and AutoLevel time constants separated ≥ 5x or merged, the multiband night stage and the Dynamic range control (which absorbs [E20](#e20)'s Tame). Run per strip before the MixEngine sum.
- **Depends on.** Slice (AutoLevel only): the [E59](#e59) slice's ambush metric, nothing else; it retunes Night Mode's target. Guard and Dynamic range control: [E19](#e19)'s redesign (the cue band it must exclude from the sidechain is defined there), and [E20](#e20)'s Tame keying lands with this control. Per-strip placement is decided here, before the MixEngine sum, and [E22](#e22)'s strip-priority master builds on it later; E22 is not a prerequisite. Night Mode, Late Night and Podcast & Voice need retuning. The `TruePeakLimiter` API change touches every strip and the master.
- **Done when.** Night Mode: ambience lift ≤ +6 dB and ambience 1 s after the event within 1 dB of its pre-event value. Competitive presets with the guard on: event minus ambience ≤ N + 1 LU, step band within 1 dB of its pre-event gain after 1 s. First combat event after 10 s of quiet ≤ steady-state events + 1 dB. Late Night within ±1 LU of −20 LUFS. Low Latency chain latency unchanged. 10 minutes of mixed programme with no leveller oscillation above 1 dB.
- **Evidence.** [Protection.cpp][PR] `AutoLevel::process` (`slew(gainDb, desired, 1.0f, 4.0f, dt)`, ±12 dB clamp, 3000 ms gated loudness, open loop on the input; the `GatedLoudness` restart and cold-start changes from `d096a4d`); [Parameters.cpp][PAR] Auto Level default off; `gaming-night-mode.json`, `music-late-night-low-volume.json`, `gaming-tournament-clean.json` in [presets/factory][FAC]; [08 C5][08-C5].

<a id="e32"></a>
#### E32 · Listening-level awareness: endpoint volume and device sensitivity driving ISO 226 loudness compensation and a hearing guard

*Adaptive & intelligent · benefit 4 · difficulty High · wording fix hours; relative contour 4–5 weeks; absolute SPL + dosimeter 5–6 weeks*

- **Status (Phase 1 batch).** Wording slice done in `music-late-night-low-volume.json`, `gaming-tournament-clean.json` and `presets/README.md`; claims "adaptive bass lift" / "hearing protection" → a fixed contour / limiter-only, 0 render values moved. The relative contour and hearing guard are open.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 1).** Relative contour partially done: the DSP, the budget and the endpoint-volume API, not the app wiring. `LoudnessContour` (`core/*/dsp/LoudnessContour.*`, [03 §14.12](03-dsp-design.md#1412-loudness-contour-chain)) after the automatic preamp: ISO 226:2023 Formula (1) (1 kHz exact, the T_f row exact at 2.4 phon, an independent evaluation within 0.001 dB), G(f) = Lp(f, P + L) − Lp(f, P) − L fitted by two low shelves, a 3 kHz bell and a 10 kHz shelf (≤ 0.80 dB, ≤ 0.48 dB at 50 Hz, 0 to −40 dB). New parameters `contour.on` (off), `contour.reference` (80 phon), `contour.level` (0 dB), `contour.maxLift` (18 dB), layout version 4; `ProcessingChain::setListeningLevelDb()` adds the host's endpoint offset. Budget: the programme-weighted lift above 3 dB comes off the level as the stage's own trim (−9.9 dB at −30 dB); at Normal / Strict the allowance is scaled by the governor (E06); the automatic preamp's model counts the net lift (E11); above 4 dB of lift at 50 Hz the maximizer's LF-first limiter (E05 step 5) is armed at lift / 12 dB (hot kick programme at −30 dB: limiter GR 1.99 → 1.09 dB with it). Done-when rows: 50 Hz tracks the level within 1 dB from 0 to −40 dB (worst 0.78 dB: 0.00 / 3.98 / 7.94 / 11.87 / 15.18 dB against ISO 0.00 / 4.08 / 8.12 / 12.10 / 15.95); a 1 dB-per-25 ms ramp glides (≤ 0.39 dB per 20 ms period, HF residue −77 dB re the tone); pink + contour at −30 dB: limiter GR 0 / 0 / −0.72 dB at −20 / −14 / −10 dBFS RMS (without the contour 0 / 0 / −2.24). Off by default and bit-exact: no measured value of the core suite moved (837), no preset render moved. `platform::AudioEndpoints::queryOutputVolume` (`app/Source/platform/PlatformServices*`): Linux through `pactl get-sink-volume` / `get-sink-mute` (the default sink for JUCE's ALSA device names; tested against a fake `pactl`), Windows `IAudioEndpointVolume` and macOS `kAudioDevicePropertyVolumeDecibels` compiled only (Windows checked with MinGW). Open: the app's endpoint poll and "this is my reference volume" control feeding `setListeningLevelDb`, a contour UI, Windows / macOS runs, the ISO 226 check against the printed tables, the 10-listener panel against Late Night (whose static contour is unchanged), and (c): absolute SPL, a sensitivity field, the dosimeter and the listening-level cap.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, app).** The app wiring of the relative contour is done in `engine/EngineController.*`, `settings/AppSettings.*` and `ui/SettingsDialog.*` ([06 §6.11](06-gui.md#611-settingsdialog)). Before: the app never read the OS volume, so the contour followed `contour.level` alone and "this is my reference volume" did not exist. After: Settings › Processing › *Listening level* has *Loudness contour on the <strip> strip* (`contour.on`, per preset, off), *Follow the system volume* (an app setting, `contour.followVolume`, **off by default**), *Reference volume* (`contour.referenceVolumeDb`, −60 … +12 dB) with **Use current volume**, and a live line (*System volume now −35.0 dB, reference −20.0 dB: the contour plays −15.0 dB re the reference.*). While following, a background thread reads `AudioEndpoints::queryOutputVolume` for the open output every 250 ms (never on the audio thread; the message thread only applies the reading) and every strip's chain gets volume − reference through `ProcessingChain::setListeningLevelDb` (one relaxed atomic; re-applied to every engine the host builds). Switched on without a reference, the volume at that moment becomes it, so switching on changes nothing until the volume moves; a failed read holds the last volume of the same output, a new output starts from 0 dB; off hands every chain 0 dB. Measured through the whole engine on a Music strip with `contour.on`, fake endpoint 30 dB below a −6 dB reference: 50 Hz re 1 kHz −0.02 dB at the reference → +11.90 dB (ISO 226: +11.87 dB), the chain's applied lift at 50 Hz 11.71 dB with its headroom trim −9.90 dB, every strip's chain at −30 dB, a strip without `contour.on` unchanged. `tests/app/test_app_listening_level.cpp` (7 cases, a fake reader injected through `EngineController::Options::endpointVolumeReader`; the background poll runs headless with `pollEndpointVolumeHeadless`). No core file, preset, parameter, golden or render value changed (the default is off and the chain API is unchanged); the Linux `pactl` read is still tested against a fake `pactl` (`test_app_endpoint_volume.cpp`). Open: the Windows and macOS reads on real systems (compiled only), a contour curve in the UI, the ISO 226 check against the printed tables, the 10-listener panel against Late Night, and (c).
- **Status (p3b3, app).** The contour curve is in the UI: Settings › Processing › Listening level › **Contour now**, read-only, draws the lift the selected strip's contour aims at (ISO 226 G (f) at the 29 one-third-octave points from 20 Hz to 12.5 kHz, capped at `contour.maxLift`, at `contour.level` plus the listening level and clamped as the stage clamps it; `SettingsDialog::contourCurve`), with the line *At −30.0 dB re the reference: +12.1 dB at 50 Hz, +4.4 dB at 12.5 kHz; level trim −9.9 dB ...*; flat and *Contour off* while the contour is off. Tested against the chain's own design (its target lift at 50 Hz within the 0.5 dB fit) in `tests/app/test_app_ui_guards.cpp`; screenshot state `contour-curve`. No sound moves. Open: the Windows and macOS volume reads on real systems (compiled only; gated on the owner's Windows PC and a Mac), the ISO 226 check against the printed tables, the 10-listener panel against Late Night, and (c).
- **Status (p3b4, (c) mechanism).** Partially done: the core of (c), in new `engine/HearingGuard.*` after `MixEngine`'s master limiter, `DeviceProfiles.*` (the sensitivity field) and `MeterBus.h` (`HearingMeters`) ([03 §14.12.1](03-dsp-design.md#14121-hearing-guard-listening-level-estimate-dose-and-cap-mix-bus)). Before: no SPL, dose or cap code anywhere. After: an **estimate** (the word used throughout; non-medical, no compliance claim) = the A-weighted level of the output (IEC 61672-1 sections, within 0.06 dB of the curve to 12.5 kHz at 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz) + the endpoint volume the host passes (the E32 poll) + a sensitivity (dB SPL of a 0 dBFS sine at full volume; the user's figure first, then the device profile's `sensitivity`, `dbSplAtFullScale` or `dbSplPerMw` with `impedanceOhm` and the source voltage); readings Fast / 5 s Leq / session Leq in dB(A), the dose against 80 dB(A) for 40 h a week on the engine's clock (`Dosimeter` on a synthetic clock), today's dose = the host's persisted baseline + the session's; an optional listening-level cap (off, 60 … 100 dB(A), 85 by default). Host settings, not parameters: no layout bump. Done-when rows: **synthetic-clock dose matches a hand computation**, met (2 h at 86 dB(A) = 0.19905; a synthetic day to 1e-9; on the engine's clock 0.0103772 against the hand 0.0103772); **the cap never exceeds its A-weighted Leq over 5 s**, met (every sample-aligned 5 s window of a hostile programme 104.7 dB(A) uncapped → worst 84.25 dB(A) under an 85 dB(A) cap and 74.52 under 75 with the volume moved, no forced step, steady programme 1 dB under the cap); **off or unknown bit-identical**, met (unknown sensitivity with the cap on, a sensitivity with the cap off and a cap above the programme, also through `MixEngine`); **RTSan**: `process()` / `reset()` `FLUB_NONBLOCKING`, in `tests/test_rtsan.cpp`. Cost: 0.06 % of a core measuring, 0.27 % capped (48 kHz stereo). `tests/test_hearing_guard.cpp` (7 cases). No shipped profile carries a sensitivity: Turtle Beach publishes no dB SPL at full scale for its headsets, and a driver's dB / mW needs the headset's own amplifier voltage, so every entry stays unknown (guard off) until the device lab measures one (`labVerified: false` would apply to any manufacturer figure). No sound moves by default: no golden, render-baseline, KnownGap or `contentHash` entry moves. Open: the app (the readings, the sensitivity / calibration entry, the cap setting, persisting the daily dose, the rolling 7-day sum and notifications: 5D U1), the cap limiting Boost and the upward compressors' maxima, and the coupler accuracy row (≤ 3 dB on 5 headsets), gated on the device lab.
- **Status (p3b5, (c) app: Settings › Hearing, the dose kept per day).** Partially done in new `ui/HearingPage.*`, `ui/SettingsDialog.*` (a *Hearing* page), `engine/EngineController.*` (the guard, dose and profile hunks), `settings/AppSettings.*`, `ui/LoudnessPanel.*` (a dose row) and `shell/ScreenshotDriver.*` ([06 §6.11](06-gui.md#611-settingsdialog)). Before: the guard ran only in the core; no sensitivity could be entered, the cap had no switch and no dose was kept. After: Settings › Hearing shows the sensitivity in use, the listener's own figure (per output endpoint, `hearing.sensitivities`, matched as E51 matches endpoints; 60 – 150 dB SPL, typed or dragged) over the device profile's, which the page prefills and marks *manufacturer figure, not lab-verified* unless `labVerified`; the estimate (the level now, over 5 s and this session in dB(A), with the system volume it counts); the listening-level cap (a switch, off, and 60 – 100 dB(A), 85); today's and the last 7 days' estimated dose against the WHO / ITU-T H.870 reference; non-medical wording throughout. While a sensitivity is known the output's volume is read off the audio thread as for the contour (the E32 poll runs for either) and handed to the guard (`setEndpointVolumeDb`; unreadable counts as full volume). The dose is kept per calendar day (`hearing.dailyDoses`, the last 7 days; stored every 5 s and at exit; after a restart the day's stored dose is the guard's baseline, `setDoseBaseline`; at midnight the day is final and the next starts from its own). The loudness panel shows *EST. / DOSE / CAP* only while a sensitivity is known. Done-when rows of this unit: **with no sensitivity the page says *Unknown* and nothing is applied**, met (a Turtle Beach output, the cap switched on at 70 dB(A) under a −6 dBFS tone: guard sensitivity NaN, readings unknown, cap gain 0 dB, the ears 0.00 dB apart, no volume read, no dose stored); **each control reachable and persisted**, met (the page's switches and sliders drive the controller and come back after a restart, plain and under Xvfb). Measured through the whole engine (Music strip, a 1 kHz tone at −20 dBFS, 100 dB SPL, system volume −10 dB): estimate 69.99 dB(A) against the hand figure 70.00 − 0.003 dB (the strip's gain); the cap at 60 dB(A) holds it at 59.37 dB(A) with −10.67 dB of gain within 2 s; a restart continues the day's dose from its stored figure (within the settings file's precision, 1e-6), midnight starts at 0 and the week sums both, a day older than 6 days drops out. Tests: `tests/app/test_app_hearing.cpp` (10 cases, each under 0.2 s). Screenshot states `settings-hearing`, `settings-hearing-unknown`, `hearing-readout`. No sound moves: no shipped profile has a sensitivity, so the guard stays off unless the listener enters a figure; no golden, render-baseline, KnownGap or `contentHash` entry moves. Open: a notification at the weekly allowance (the page says it in words only); the cap limiting Boost and the upward compressors' maxima (core); every real sensitivity figure and the coupler accuracy row (device lab); the Windows and macOS volume reads on real systems (the owner's PC, a Mac).
- **Status (owner-verified, 2026-10-06, Turtle Beach Stealth 600PC Gen 3, USB dongle, Windows 11).** Warmth 0 ↔ 100 (Tube) on the Music strip, music from Microsoft Edge through per-app capture: "seems to work" (it sounds warmer). The equal-loudness half of the check was not reported separately.
- **Status (owner-verified, 2026-10-06, Turtle Beach Stealth 600PC Gen 3, USB dongle, Windows 11).** *Follow the system volume* with the loudness contour on the Music strip: turning the Windows volume down raises the *Contour now* curve and turning it up flattens it, so the Windows endpoint-volume read works on real hardware.
- **What.** (a) Correct the two false preset descriptions now. (b) Read endpoint volume (WASAPI `IAudioEndpointVolume`, CoreAudio `kAudioDevicePropertyVolumeDecibels`, PipeWire/Pulse node volume) and drive an ISO 226:2023 contour relative to a user-set reference volume, inside the governor and headroom accounting, with volume-aware bass protection; it replaces Late Night's static contour. (c) Absolute SPL from device sensitivity/impedance and optional calibration, feeding an ITU-T H.870 / WHO-style dosimeter (A-weighted, rolling 7-day dose, notifications) and an optional "Max listening level" loudness cap that also limits Boost and upward-compression maxima, with a 75 dBA sensitive/kids mode.
- **Why.** Music: at late-night levels 40–60 Hz needs 10–15 dB more to sound balanced, while bass protection withdraws boost exactly when the ear needs it. Gaming and safety: a product built around Boost and upward compression has no SPL or dose awareness, and a −1 dBTP ceiling can mean more than 100 dB SPL.
- **Expected benefit.** The most audible music improvement in this group, and the fix for false shipped wording: `music-late-night-low-volume.json` promises "a loudness contour (adaptive bass lift...)" but is a static bass +3 / eq.9 +1.5 dB (tilt +1.8 / +1.7 dB at −14 and −34 dBFS RMS); `gaming-tournament-clean.json` calls a −1 dBTP limiter "hearing protection". Boost 100 adds +5.45 LU with no warning. The relative mode delivers most of the value without SPL accuracy. Hardware dials on many USB and wireless headsets are invisible to the OS, so uncalibrated SPL error of 10–20 dB is realistic.
- **Approach.** Ship relative mode first. Measure digital level at the master output after strip faders, so only the endpoint and hardware dial remain unknown. Budget the contour against governor headroom and the bass-protection cap. An optional "volume takeover" (hold the OS endpoint, attenuate after the master limiter) must be opt-in, since it breaks the Windows volume flyout and media keys unless mirrored. The dosimeter shows "uncalibrated" rather than a number without a profile or calibration, and never claims H.870 compliance.
- **Depends on.** PlatformServices on 3 OSes; a sensitivity field in `DeviceProfiles.h`; the device lab (roadmap 1.10, [10 §5][10-5]); roadmap 2.11 (the existing hearing-guard slot); the in-flight UI work.
- **Done when.** ISO 226:2023 within 0.1 dB of the tables (20–100 phon). Contour gain at 50 Hz tracks endpoint change within 1 dB from 0 to −40 dB, with no step on ramps. Limiter GR < 1 dB on pink + contour at −30 dB endpoint volume. Synthetic-clock dose matches a hand computation. The cap never exceeds its A-weighted Leq over 5 s. Coupler error ≤ 3 dB on 5 calibrated headsets. A 10-listener bass-balance test at 50/60/70/80 phon against today's Late Night.
- **Evidence.** No `IAudioEndpointVolume`, ISO 226, SPL or dose code in `core/` or `app/Source`; [03 §11.9][03-119] "No automatic loudness compensation"; [DeviceProfiles.h][DPh] (no sensitivity field); [MeterBus.h][MB] (dBFS/LUFS only); [08 F][08-F].

<a id="e37"></a>
#### E37 · Fair comparison everywhere: loudness-matched A/B, preset audition, module and virt toggles, and a blind mode

*UX & workflow · benefit 4 · difficulty Medium · slice 3–5 days, total 4–6 engineer-weeks · Quick win (slice)*

- **Status (Phase 1 batch).** Phase 1 done in `Protection.*` (`ComparisonMatcher`: both trims ≤ 0, 1 s acquire, frozen for the comparison) and `ProcessingChain.cpp`; matched bypass on hot programme: the raise-only match read 2–3 LU short → within 0.5 LU through a 10 s comparison, reference under the ceiling. Open: Phases 2–4 (per-bank A/B, probes, UI, ABX).
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 1, app).** Partially done: the preset comparison in the preset browser ([E40](#e40), [06 §6.1a](06-gui.md#61a-presetbrowser--search-filters-favourites-and-a-matched-preview)), in `ui/PresetAudition.*` and `ui/PresetBrowser.*`. A previewed preset and the current sound are matched by the rule above: only the louder side is turned down, to the quieter (both trims ≤ 0 dB), as an offset on the strip's gain in the mix, so a flip is matched from its first second. The per-preset offsets are chain gains estimated in the background (output minus input integrated loudness of a reference programme rendered with each preset's values through the offline renderer, about 0.2 s each, cached) at the level the strip's programme arrives at, because the gap depends on it (Club Loud − Lo-Fi Chill: 4.5 LU at −13 LUFS, 7.2 LU at −23 LUFS). Done-when row "two presets 4 LU apart are matched within 1 LU 1 s after a flip, and `needsReprepare()` stays false": Lo-Fi Chill → Club Loud on the game scene at about −24 LUFS, 1–2.5 s after the flip, against a run that did not flip: 7.6 LU → 0.6 LU (trim −7.0 dB), no re-prepare; back from Lo-Fi Chill to a current Club Loud: 8.4 LU → 1.3 LU (the estimate is made on music, so it is approximate on other programme) (`tests/app/test_app_ui_preset_browser.cpp`). No sound, preset, parameter or golden changed. Open: the per-bank A/B offsets (the header's A/B is still a plain bank flip), the true-original tap and the live per-side probes of Phase 3 (which would refine a first flip on programme unlike the reference), module and virtualiser toggles, "Processed +x LU" on Bypass and a "matched ±x LU" indicator in the header, the blind A/B/X mode, and RTSan with 4 strips and probes.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, app).** Partially done: Phase 2, Phase 4 and the module and virtualiser toggles, in the app: new `ui/Comparison.*` and `ui/AbxPanel.*`, `ui/HeaderBar.*`, `ui/ModuleRack.*` / `ModuleCard.*`, `ui/PresetAudition.*` (estimator variants), `ui/MainComponent.*`, `engine/EngineController.*` (a comparison trim on the strip's gain in the mix, two slots), `settings/AppSettings.*` (`compare.matched`, default on) and `shell/ScreenshotDriver.*` ([06 §6.1](06-gui.md#61-headerbar--mode-strip-presets-ab-bypass-status), [§6.1b](06-gui.md#61b-abxpanel--the-blind-abx-test), [§6.9](06-gui.md#69-modulerack-and-modulecard--the-processing-modules)). Every comparison follows the rule above: only the louder side is turned down. (1) **Per-bank A/B** (`BankComparison`). Before: the header's A/B was a plain bank flip. After: each bank's chain gain is estimated in the background at the strip's input level (the preset browser's estimator, shared), so a flip applies the playing bank's trim at once; 3.5 s after the flip it is refined once from the strip's meters (short-term out minus the programme's level, averaged over the polls while each bank played unchanged) and then frozen, so an edit is heard at its own level; a preset load releases the trim, identical banks get none. Done-when row "two presets 4 LU apart are matched within 1 LU 1 s after a flip, and `needsReprepare()` stays false", now for the header's A/B: Lo-Fi Chill (A) → Club Loud (B) on the game scene, 1–2.5 s after the flip against a run that stayed on A: **7.9 LU → 0.9 LU** (trim −7.0 dB), no re-prepare (the estimate is made on music: 0.7–1.1 LU on other stretches of the game scene). Refined, on music, 3.6–4.2 s after the flip: 0.7 LU (trim −5.9 dB from the meters; the estimate had −7.0 dB). (2) **Module and virtualiser toggles** (`ListenMatch`): a module card's ear is estimated with and without the module (a render with its audition bypass) when the pointer enters it; while held, a louder "without" is trimmed; a quieter one plays as it is and the "with" side is trimmed from the release until 10 s after the last hold, then back to 0 dB at 2 dB/s (the bypass matcher's session). Club Loud, the maximizer's ear: estimated gap −6.31 LU against −6.30 LU measured (the held side is the quieter: no trim while held, −6.3 dB on "with" after the release). A 7.1 strip is estimated on the 7.1 game scene (the virtualiser's ear on Competitive FPS: gain 2.61 LU with, 3.80 LU without), which also removes batch 1's stereo-only estimate of the preset browser on the Game strip. (3) **Indicators:** a line under A/B (*B −7.0 dB*, *matched*, *matching…*) with the gap and its source in the tooltip, and under Bypass *proc. +x LU* (processed minus input, short-term, as last read before the bypass; +9.5 LU for Club Loud on music at −12 dB). (4) **Blind A/B/X** (`AbxTest`, `AbxPanel`): 10 trials of the two banks, X hidden (seeded random), A / B / X played matched, answers scored with the one-sided binomial p-value; the panel is opaque over the whole window so neither the A/B buttons nor the trim line gives X away; closing puts the bank back. ABX Done-when row: the level half is met, Signature at Boost 0 (A) against Boost 100 (B) on music at −12 dB plays **10.5 LU → 0.5 LU** apart (trim on B −11.1 dB); the "no level-driven preference" half needs listeners ([E60](#e60)) and was not run. Tests: `tests/app/test_app_ui_compare.cpp` (10 cases). No parameter, preset, render or golden changed: the trims are app-side strip gains during a comparison; A/B flips now play matched by default, as this item asks (`compare.matched` switches it off). Open: Phase 3 (the true-original tap and continuously running per-side K-weighted probes in the engine, so a module hold and a first flip on programme unlike the reference are exact rather than estimated; the ear is never refined, a hold is too short to measure), RTSan with 4 strips and probes (no audio-thread code was added), the listening half of the ABX row, and E40's separate audition bank.
- **Status (p3b3, by-ear demo pack).** Tooling done, no product change: `flubsound-cli demo` (`tools/flubsound-cli/Demo.*`, [04 §10](04-project-structure.md#10-continuous-integration)) renders 24 before / after WAV pairs (every macro of both modes 0 → 100 %, Boost 0 → 50 / 100 in both modes, Smoothness, crossfeed, the virtualiser on a 7.1 scene, the contour, Startle Guard, Night, the four maximizer styles) on built-in music / speech / game programmes or the user's file, matched by the rule above (only the louder file is turned down) and listed in `index.txt` with their settings, numbers and what to listen for; CI uploads it from the Linux gcc leg as `Flubsound-demo-pack`. Before: no way to hear a feature matched outside the app. After (10 s programmes): every matched pair within 0.01 LU, e.g. Music Boost 0 → 100 rendered 5.65 dB louder and is turned down by that; the level features are left unmatched and say so (Loudness macro +6.56 LU, Startle Guard −7.15 LU, Night −1.36 LU) (`tests/test_cli_demo.cpp`, ≤ 0.5 LU). No sound, preset, parameter or golden changed. Open: the matched ABX of Boost 0 against Boost 100 itself (a listening test; pair 12 is its material) and the E37 items listed above.
- **Status (p3b5, the feature guide and the demo pack's coverage).** Done: new [12-feature-guide.md](12-feature-guide.md) (task #29's deliverable) describes every control of the Simple and Advanced views, every macro in both modes, every Settings page and every CLI command. Each entry gives what the control does to the sound in numbers (from the Status lines and the pack), what to listen for, its demo pair, a fair A/B, its defaults, ranges, limits and gated items. The guide ends with a 64-row by-ear checklist and the list of what cannot be judged by ear yet. Punch / Impact, the Enhanced renderer, Smart macros and the Linux node are marked *verify after the batch 5 review*. `flubsound-cli demo` (`Demo.*`) grows from 24 to 61 pairs (81 renders; about 30 s on two worker threads at 10 s). The new pairs: Punch and Impact at Boost 100; the shaper's Attack Low / High; Relative presence; the crossfeed types; the Enhanced renderer and its front / back contrast; the five genre presets and the three levelling presets (a side can now load a factory preset, `--preset`); the module cards (the noise gate on a new speech-over-hiss programme, EQ, dynamic EQ, bass boost, harmonics and Tighten, saturation, Tape grit, compressor, the automatic preamp, the latency profile, protection Normal). The app's own settings render through the app's `MixEngine` (a new `DemoHost`): Smart macros on a new loud-master programme, the headset enhancement cap, the safe speaker bass cap, a headphone correction, the per-ear profile, the hearing cap, and the chat duck and ChatMix on a new game + voice scene with a Chat strip. `DemoOptions::only` renders a subset. Examples on the 10 s programmes: Smart's multipliers attack 0.00 / drive 0.25 / bass 0.69 on the loud master; the safe speaker cap's bass as applied +14.0 → +3.0 dB; the per-ear profile's reservation −3.1 dB; the hearing cap at 75 dB(A) −10.2 LU (the loudest 5 s estimated 83.4 dB(A) at 110 dB SPL); ChatMix 50 % towards Chat −6.0 dB on the game; the duck at full depth while the voice talks. Tests (`tests/test_cli_demo.cpp`, 3 → 8 cases, each ≤ 1.2 s): the pair list; the first 24 pairs, the chain pairs and the engine pairs (two cases) rendered, matched, their band deltas equal to `analyze`, and each engine side changing what its setting changes; `--input` with the virtualiser and chat pairs on their built-in scenes; and the guide, where every entry names existing pairs (or "none" with the reason), every pair is named and has a checklist row, and every macro, module card, Settings page and CLI command has an entry. Found while making the bass pairs: on the built-in music at its mastered level (peak −1 dBFS), the bass engine's default *Headroom Protect* (−12 dB) holds a +6 dB `bass.boost` to +0.2 dB at 31.5 Hz, so Boost's bass rows and `bass.boost` do little on loud masters. The pair renders the music 12 dB down, and the guide says so; this is a voicing question for [E02](#e02) and the panel. No DSP, parameter, preset, golden or render-baseline entry moves. Open: the owner's by-ear pass of the checklist; the CLI's `help demo` text (`main.cpp`) and docs/04's `test_cli_demo.cpp` and `Demo.*` lines still describe the 24 pairs (outside this unit's files).
- **What.** Replace the capped, raise-only bypass match with a `ComparisonMatcher` that only attenuates the louder side (processed or bypass, bank A/B, current or previewed preset, module on/off, virt on/off), acquires in about 1 s and freezes during a comparison. Keep per-bank and per-preset LUFS offsets so a flip is right from the first second. Add a true-original tap before input gain and AutoLevel, "Processed +x LU" on Bypass, a "matched ±x LU" indicator, and a blind A/B/X mode.
- **Why.** Every comparison favours the louder setting: Boost 100 is +5.45 LU over bypass on a normal master, presets span 4–7 LU, and A/B is not matched at all. That drives "louder = better" choices and the fatigue risk the roadmap names.
- **Expected benefit.** Fixes a shipped feature that does not do what its label says: the "loudness-matched" bypass is 3.10 LU too quiet on a dense −16 LUFS signal (−11.82 against −8.72 LUFS) and 1.91 LU too quiet on a peaky kick signal, because the dry reference is capped at the ceiling and also limited by `dryLimiter`. A/B is a bare bank flip. The benefit is indirect (better choices), hence 4 rather than 5.
- **Approach.** Phase 1: `wetTrimDb ≤ 0`, `dryTrimDb ≤ 0`, applied only while Bypass or Compare is armed; fast acquire, then freeze the trim for the comparison (continuous tracking audibly rides the reference level). Phase 2: per-bank offsets from `inShortTermLufs` / `shortTermLufs`, applied instantly on toggle and then refined. Phase 3: true-original tap and continuously running per-slot K-weighted probes, so hold-to-bypass knows its delta when the hold starts. Phase 4: UI and ABX. The "latency.profile written into one bank" bug is fixed once, in [E40](#e40).
- **Depends on.** [E40](#e40)'s quick slice first (an A/B flip must never re-prepare the engine). Rebase onto the in-flight engine swap.
- **Done when.** `process --set bypass=on bypass.matched=on` gap ≤ 0.5 LU on the dense and peaky signals, with the dry path ≤ ceiling. New tests in `tests/test_protection_gaps.cpp` with the cap engaged: within 0.5 LU and trim variance ≤ 0.2 dB during a 10 s frozen comparison. Two presets 4 LU apart are matched within 1 LU 1 s after a flip, and `needsReprepare()` stays false. RTSan with 4 strips and probes. A matched ABX of Boost 0 against Boost 100 shows no level-driven preference.
- **Evidence.** [ProcessingChain.cpp][PC] `matchDb = min(matchDb, ceiling - dryPeakHold)` (dry tap after AutoLevel); [Protection.cpp][PR] `LoudnessMatch::getDryGainDb` (3 dB/s slew, ±12 dB); [EngineController.cpp][EC] `setActiveBank` (plain flip); [03 §14.6][03-146] (1.2–1.5 LU shortfall); TRACEABILITY R2.10 note.

<a id="e39"></a>
#### E39 · Progressive disclosure: Simple default view, plain-language tooltips, relevance-ordered rack, compact mode and a lower minimum size

*UX & workflow · benefit 4 · difficulty Medium · quick slice < 1 week, total 4–6 engineer-weeks*

- **Status (Phase 3 batch 1, app).** Partially done: the Simple view, in `ui/MainComponent.*`, new `ui/SimpleStatusPanel.*`, `ui/BoostPanel.*` and `ui/HeaderBar.*` ([06 §3.5](06-gui.md#35-simple-view)). Before: one view, the full rack, for everyone. After: **Simple** is the default for a user without a saved choice and the last choice is kept (`ui.view`, not guessed from user presets): the header as before (mode, strip, preset, A/B, Bypass) with a view button, the Boost dial up to 280 px, the five macros, the [E38](#e38) active-now chips wrapped over up to three rows (Signature at Boost 55: 11 of 11 shown against 3 and *+8* in the Advanced view's single row), the headset status (profile or generic output, connection, safety ceiling, headphone correction, device problems; buttons to Settings › Audio and › Correction) and one loudness meter (output short-term LUFS with the input as a marker and *2.9 LU louder than the input* in words); **Advanced** (the full window, unchanged) is one click away from the header or the footer. The Simple view skips the analyser's FFTs and the rack poll. Done-when rows: a layout test (both views at 1100 × 700 with and without the advice banner and a notice, 1280 × 820, 1920 × 1080, 2560 × 1440) asserts that no child leaves its parent and that the Simple controls are shown and do not overlap: met at the current 1100 × 700 minimum; screenshots of both views at 100 % and 150 % UI scale and in high contrast, rendered with the new `--view` screenshot option, inspected (800 × 560 and 1093 × 614 wait for the reflow). Tested in `tests/app/test_app_ui_simple_view.cpp` (9 cases). No sound, preset, parameter or golden changed. Open: the plain-language hints file (every key, per mode, ≤ 140 characters), the relevance-ordered rack with the gate card hidden outside Quality, the reflow below 1100 px and the lower 800 × 560 minimum (the header alone needs about 1050 px today), the compact tray flyout, and the moderated test with 6–8 non-experts and the SUS comparison.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, app).** Done except the user test, in new `ui/ParamHints.*` and `ui/QuickControls.*`, `ui/ModuleRack.*` / `ModuleCard.*` / `ParamGrid.*`, `ui/BoostPanel.*`, `ui/HeaderBar.*`, `ui/MainComponent.*`, `ui/LoudnessPanel.*`, `shell/MainWindow.h`, `shell/TrayIcon.*` and `shell/ScreenshotDriver.*` ([06 §3.3](06-gui.md#33-layout-rules), [§6.9](06-gui.md#69-modulerack-and-modulecard--the-processing-modules), [§7.1](06-gui.md#71-tray)). (1) **Hints:** one "what you will hear" sentence per parameter key, keyed by `param::Info::key` (`#` for a band number) with a Gaming text where the mode changes the meaning (the five macros and Boost), as the tooltip of the Boost dial and macros, the module cards' keys and power switches and every control of an expanded card. Before: the tooltip was the parameter's name. Row "every parameter key has a hint ≤ 140 characters per relevant mode": met, all 215 keys of the current layout in both modes, the longest 124 characters (`tests/app/test_app_ui_hints.cpp`). The table is C++ data, not yet a JSON resource or read by `flubsound-cli params --json` (the CLI is not this track's). (2) **Relevance-ordered rack** per mode (Music: EQ, Bass, Clarity …; Gaming: Dynamic EQ, Clarity, Stereo & Space …; the virtualiser first on a 7.1 strip in Gaming, last on a stereo strip); row "the gate card is absent outside Quality": met (`tests/app/test_app_ui_reflow.cpp`). (3) **Reflow and minimum size:** `kMinWidth`/`kMinHeight` 1100 × 700 → **800 × 560**; under 1100 px the header folds the strips into a menu and the copy button and readout into an overflow menu, the routing panel becomes a drawer, under 700 px of height the history is left out, the right column narrows to 220 px. Row "a layout test asserts no child exceeds the window and Simple controls are visible": met, the layout test now covers 800 × 560, 1093 × 614, 1100 × 700 (with the banner and a notice), 1280 × 820, 1920 × 1080 and 2560 × 1440 in both views and also checks that the header's controls never overlap (`tests/app/test_app_ui_simple_view.cpp`); row "screenshots at 800 × 560, 1093 × 614 and 1100 × 700 at 1.0 and 1.25": rendered and inspected, with 1.5 and high contrast. Before: 2 of 10 cards at the minimum and a window that did not fit a 1366 × 768 laptop at 125 %; after: the window fits it, and the first cards in view are the mode's most relevant. (4) **Compact tray flyout** (`QuickControls`: Boost, the preset stepper, Bypass, open the window) on a left click of the tray icon (a double click opens the window). (5) The governor and brightness readouts in the Advanced view ([E38](#e38)). Open: the moderated test with 6–8 non-experts and the SUS comparison. *Review (Phase 3 batch 2):* the hint for `auto.preampHot` (E11, added in parallel) was missing and is added; 216 keys now.
- **What.** "Simple" as the default view (Boost, 5 macros, preset and headphone picker, Compare/Bypass, one loudness meter), with the rack as "Advanced". A one-sentence "what you will hear" hint per parameter, per mode where macros differ. Rack cards ordered by mode relevance, with modules the active profile disables hidden (the Noise Gate is currently the first, greyed card outside Quality). A layout reflow below 1100 px and a minimum of about 800x560. A compact tray flyout (Boost, preset stepper, bypass) instead of an always-on-top window.
- **Why.** A listener faces the same 195-parameter side-scrolling rack as an engineer. On a 1366x768 laptop at 125 % scaling (1093x614 effective) the 1100x700 minimum window does not fit, and only 2 of 10 cards are visible at minimum size.
- **Expected benefit.** The Simple view and laptop fit are the value, a hard usability fix for laptop gamers. An always-on-top compact window has little gaming value (exclusive and borderless fullscreen hide it; hotkeys already give feedback), so its effort goes to the reflow.
- **Approach.** Hints in a keyed data file (e.g. `presets/param-hints.json` keyed by `param::Info::key`, with a mode dimension), not English strings in core `param::Info`, which would make localisation ([E41](#e41)) harder; `flubsound-cli params --json` can read the table. A per-mode relevance table in `ModuleRack.cpp`; the gate card hidden unless `gateInChain`. Breakpoints in `MainComponent::resized()` with a header overflow menu. Lower `kMinWidth`/`kMinHeight` only after the layout test passes. Persist the last view rather than guessing from "has user presets".
- **Depends on.** The in-flight UI scale / high-contrast work must merge first (same sizing code). [E37](#e37) for Compare semantics, [E38](#e38) for active-now chips, [E41](#e41) for where hints live.
- **Done when.** Screenshots at 800x560, 1093x614 and 1100x700 at scales 1.0 and 1.25; a layout test asserts no child exceeds the window and Simple controls are visible. Every parameter key has a hint ≤ 140 characters per relevant mode. The gate card is absent outside Quality. Moderated test with 6–8 non-experts ("make footsteps louder", "make it warmer") completed without opening Advanced; SUS compared with the current build.
- **Evidence.** [ModuleCard.cpp][MC] tooltip = parameter name only (~L169/174); [MainWindow.h][MW] `kMinWidth = 1100, kMinHeight = 700`; [06 §13][06-13]; `screenshot-min-1100x700.png` in [docs/images](images/); `flubsound-cli params --json` count 195.

#### E40 · Preset browser with search, tags, descriptions, device and game suggestions, and hover audition

*Presets · benefit 4 · difficulty Medium · quick slice 3–5 days; browser 2–3 weeks (+1 week audition bank) · Quick win (slice)*

- **Status (Phase 1 batch).** Part (1), core side, done in `core/*/io/PresetIO.*`, the 8 factory presets that named a profile (now the `suggestedLatencyProfile` label) and the plug-in's preset import; loading a factory preset changed `latency.profile` (8 of 24 set Quality / Low Latency, 16 reset to Balanced, each a re-prepare) → never. Open: the E42a prompt and part (2) (the app half of part (1) and part (3) are below).
- **Status (wave B2, app side).** Part (1) done in the app: `PresetManager::loadIntoBank` loads through `applyPresetToStore`, so no preset (factory, or a user file that still carries `latency.profile`, `bypass` or `bypass.matched`) writes app state; *Save* writes no app-state key and names the strip's profile as `suggestedLatencyProfile`; `PresetInfo::suggestedLatencyProfile` carries it for the E42a prompt. A user preset saved in Quality, loaded on the Music strip: every strip's profile Balanced → Quality and a re-prepare → unchanged, no re-prepare (`tests/app/test_app_first_run.cpp`, which also loads all 25 factory presets on all 4 strips in Quality and Low Latency: profile, bypass and matched bypass unchanged, `needsReprepare()` false).
- **Status (wave B2, core side).** Part (3) done in `core/*/engine/MixEngine.*`: `StripConfig::syncGroup` (default `kNoSyncGroup`, a group of its own), applied in `build()` only (no live group editor, so no new re-prepare trigger); a strip is padded to the slowest chain of its group, and `getStripLatencySamples()` / `getStripPaddingSamples()` / `getMasterLatencySamples()` report it. Game on Low Latency beside Music on Quality (48 kHz, default layout): Game output 1352 + 68 → 100 + 68 samples (29.6 → 3.5 ms), padding 1252 → 0; Game and Chat in one group still pad Game to Chat's 192 (test *MixEngine: padding only within sync groups, per-strip latency reported*, impulse-verified per strip). Part (2) (gate in the Quality chain only when used) is not done: it adds a run-time re-prepare trigger and waits for the verified engine swap (§5.6); with the gate off, Warm Vinyl in Quality is still 1352 samples. The app's latency readout reports these per-strip figures since the wave B2 review (see [E42](#e42)). Open: part (2), the app assigning groups (every strip is in a group of its own), the browser.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 1, app).** The browser is done, with a plain preview instead of the audition bank (§5.4's drop order), in the new `ui/PresetBrowser.*` and `ui/PresetAudition.*`, `ui/HeaderBar.*` (the preset box opens it; *Browse presets…* in the preset menu), `settings/AppSettings.*` (`presets.favourites`, `presets.recent`, `presets.preview`, `presets.previewMatched`) and `shell/ScreenshotDriver.*` (`--state preset-browser`) ([06 §6.1a](06-gui.md#61a-presetbrowser--search-filters-favourites-and-a-matched-preview)). Before: a combo box of names; the descriptions and tags were never shown. After: a popover over the window with a search over name, tags, category, mode, author and description (ranked by words found, then field: *late night quiet listening* lists Late Night Low Volume first), scope (all, favourites, recent, and *For <headset>*: the device profile's suggestion from `adviceFor`, plus the Bluetooth presets on a Bluetooth link), mode, category and tag filters, stars, the description pane (description, tags, author, the latency profile the preset was made for, the reader's warnings from [E52](#e52)), keyed by the E52 uuids. The selected row plays on the strip: the preset's sound in the active bank through `applyPresetToStore` (no app state, so no re-prepare and no padding), the other bank never written; Cancel, Escape or a click outside puts the active bank back bit for bit (except values someone else moved meanwhile), Load goes through `EngineController::loadPreset`. The preview is loudness matched against the current sound ([E37](#e37): 7.6 LU → 0.6 LU for Lo-Fi Chill → Club Loud). Done-when rows: "audition restores on mouse-out with bank B bit-identical": met in the preview's form (restore on Cancel; B untouched, tested); "users find a late-night quiet listening preset in under 20 s": the search ranks it first (tested); no user test was run. Screenshots (`--state preset-browser`, 1280 × 820 and 1100 × 700, Simple and Advanced, 150 % and high contrast, gaming with a Stealth headset) inspected. Tested in `tests/app/test_app_ui_preset_browser.cpp` (9 cases). No sound, preset, parameter or golden changed. Open: the separate, never-saved audition bank (the preview writes the active bank, so a crash during a preview can leave it in the saved strip state) and hover audition; part (2); the app assigning sync groups; "Recommended for <game>" from the automatic profiles; a timed user test.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, app).** The crash window of the plain preview is closed in `engine/EngineController.*` and `ui/PresetAudition.*` ([06 §6.1a](06-gui.md#61a-presetbrowser--search-filters-favourites-and-a-matched-preview)). Before: a preview wrote the active bank and the strip-state autosave (every 5 s, and at shutdown) saved it, so a crash or kill during a preview restarted with the previewed sound. After: `PresetAudition` registers every write with `EngineController::setPreviewInProgress` (and clears it when the session ends), and while one is registered the autosave stores the bank as *Cancel* would leave it: a value that still holds what the preview wrote is saved as the value before it, a value someone else moved meanwhile (a Boost hotkey) as it is. Tested (`tests/app/test_app_ui_preset_browser.cpp`, *a crash during a preview cannot save the previewed sound*): Signature previewing Club Loud, the saved JSON and the file read back as a restart would are the pre-preview state byte for byte; a Boost change during the preview is saved as Cancel keeps it; after Load the autosave writes the loaded preset. No sound, preset, parameter or golden changed. Open: the separate never-saved audition bank (a user-preset save or an A/B copy during a preview still takes the previewed sound), hover audition, part (2), the app assigning sync groups, "Recommended for <game>", a timed user test.
- **Status (p3b4, app).** The never-saved audition bank done in `engine/EngineController.*` (the preview hunk: `getSavedBankValues`, `copyActiveToOtherBank`, `saveUserPreset`), `presets/PresetManager.*` (`setAuditionFilter`, `savedValues`, `setCurrentPresetIdSaved`; `saveUserPreset` and `saveCurrent` read through it), `ui/PresetAudition.*` (`presetChanged`) and `ui/PresetBrowser.*` ([06 §6.1a](06-gui.md#61a-presetbrowser--search-filters-favourites-and-a-matched-preview)). Before: a *Save As*, a *Save* or a *Copy A → B* during a preview took the previewed sound (only the autosave read around it), and a save ended the preview without restoring. After: the preview's registration makes its values an audition bank that no save or copy reads: every path that saves or copies a bank takes it as *Cancel* will leave it (a value that still holds what the preview wrote as the value before it; a value someone else moved meanwhile as it is), the saved preset counts as unmodified after *Cancel*, and a save keeps the session going with the saved preset as its start. Tested (`tests/app/test_app_ui_preset_browser.cpp`, *the preview is a never-saved audition bank*): Signature previewing Club Loud, *Save As* and *Save* write Signature's sound, *Copy A → B* copies Signature (B = A before the preview, bit for bit), B is untouched until the copy, *Cancel* restores A bit for bit and leaves the saved preset unmodified; without a copy, two previews and *Cancel* leave B bit-identical (the Done-when row in the preview's form: the browser has no hover audition, *Cancel* stands for mouse-out). It is not a third `ParameterStore` bank (`Parameters.*` is shared; the preview still plays in the active bank's slots). No sound, preset, parameter or golden changed. Open: hover audition, part (2), the app assigning sync groups, "Recommended for <game>", a timed user test.
- **What.** Quick slice: presets no longer write `latency.profile` (a suggestion prompt instead); the noise gate is in the Quality chain only when used; a per-strip sync group so only strips that share A/V content are padded together. Browser: a popover with search, tag chips, favourites and recents, Factory/User/Device groups, the description pane, "Recommended for <headset>" from `adviceFor`, and loudness-matched audition in a non-persisted third bank.
- **Why.** Every factory preset ships a paragraph of description and 5–6 tags that the UI never shows. Stepping presets moves latency from 4 ms to 28 ms with a dropout for 5 of 12 music presets.
- **Expected benefit.** The quick slice is a gaming-latency correctness fix. Measured: Warm Vinyl and Podcast & Voice render at 1352 samples (28.17 ms) in Quality against 192 (4.00 ms) in Balanced and 100 in Low Latency. Because MixEngine pads every strip to the largest strip latency and `loadIntoBank` preserves only `BypassAll`, choosing Warm Vinyl on the Music strip silently delays the Game strip to 28 ms. The browser itself is Medium value.
- **Approach.** (1) `PresetManager::loadIntoBank` preserves `LatencyProfile` (and `LoudnessMatchBypass`) like `BypassAll`, recording `suggestedProfile` for an [E42](#e42) prompt. (2) `ProcessingChain::prepare` includes the gate only when Quality and `gate.on`; `needsReprepare` learns that `gate.on` is structural in Quality only. Warm Vinyl in Quality then drops to about 328 samples. (3) MixEngine `syncGroup` (also needed by [E35](#e35)). Browser: a CallOutBox searching name, description and tags; no "Recommended for <game>" until the in-flight auto-profile switcher lands. The audition bank never changes latency or mode-structural state and is excluded from state save, plug-in state and A/B copy. Measure aliasing before moving Warm Vinyl / Lo-Fi to Balanced (they lose the Quality 2x HQ saturator).
- **Depends on.** Quick slice, part (1) (presets stop writing `latency.profile`): nothing. It removes a re-prepare trigger and adds none, so it does not need the in-flight engine swap. Part (3) (sync groups): nothing, provided group membership is applied only when `MixEngine::build()` already runs; a live group editor waits for the swap. Part (2) (gate in the Quality chain only when used) makes `gate.on` structural in Quality, which adds a new re-prepare trigger. It therefore merges only after the swap is verified, and until then the gate stays in the Quality chain as today. Browser: [E37](#e37), [E42](#e42) prompt, [E39](#e39), the in-flight auto profiles. E37's A/B correctness depends on parts (1) and (3).
- **Done when.** `process -p "Warm Vinyl" --profile quality --json` reports about 328 samples with the gate off and 1352 with it on. A Music strip in Quality and a Game strip in Low Latency in separate sync groups leave the Game output at chain latency plus master look-ahead (about 100 + 48 samples), not 1352. Loading each of the 24 factory presets leaves `latency.profile` unchanged and `needsReprepare()` false. Audition restores on mouse-out with bank B bit-identical. Users find a "late-night quiet listening" preset in under 20 s.
- **Evidence.** [PresetManager.cpp][PM] parses description and tags (~L85–87) and `loadIntoBank` (~L238–249); [HeaderBar.cpp][HB] lists names only; [ProcessingChain.cpp][PC] `needsReprepare`, `gateInChain`; [MixEngine.cpp][ME] `build()` padding; [06 §6.1][06-61] "brief dropout".

<a id="e42"></a>
#### E42 · Real end-to-end latency: a loopback measurement tool and a whole-path Low Latency policy

*Latency & performance · benefit 4 · difficulty High · E42a 4–6 days; total 7–9 engineer-weeks*

- **Status (wave B2, host side).** `AudioEngineHost::getLatencyInfo()` now reports per strip (`LatencyInfo::strips`: own = the strip's chain + master limiter, padding = what the slowest strip adds, output), `estimated = true` (driver-reported device latencies, nothing measured), `valid` (false while no device runs, so no stale number) and an optional known graph quantum in `totalMs` (`setGraphQuantumMs`; nothing detects the PipeWire quantum yet); `formatTotalLatency()` gives "12.3 ms (est.)" or "--". Since the wave B2 review the strip figures are the MixEngine's own (`getStripLatencySamples()` / `getStripPaddingSamples()`, core side below). Measured with Game on Low Latency and Music on Quality (48 kHz, offline): default layout Game 168, Music 1420 samples, no padding; Game and Music in one sync group Game own 168 + padding 1252 = 1420 (before: one engine total only; `tests/app/test_app_device_safety.cpp`). Open: the prompt (the header shows these fields, app side below), E42b–E42d.
- **Status (wave B2, app side).** The header shows these fields (`HeaderBar::formatLatencyReadout` / `describeLatency`): the total reads `~12.3 ms` while estimated and `--` without an audible path (before: a bare total, also with no device), and its tooltip adds "estimated" with what is left out and one line per strip with its engine latency and, when padded, its own latency plus the sync padding. Open: the E42a profile prompt and the rest above.
- **Status (wave B2, core side).** In `ProcessingChain.*` and `MixEngine.*`: Quality runs as Balanced below 32 kHz (`getLatencyProfile()` = in effect, new `getRequestedLatencyProfile()` = stored; no pending re-prepare): 8 kHz 1152 → 92 samples (144 → 11.5 ms), 16 kHz 1192 → 112 (74.5 → 7.0 ms). The noise gate's STFT frame is defined in ms (21.3 ms, the nearest power of two per rate; the compressor / limiter look-aheads already were), so Quality is 26.8–30.2 ms from 44.1 to 192 kHz instead of 30.2 → 10.8 ms (96 kHz 1592 → 2616 samples, 192 kHz 2072 → 5144; 44.1 / 48 kHz unchanged); the Balanced / Low Latency spread (2.9–4.1 / 1.3–2.2 ms) is the oversampler FIRs and the 20-sample detector, which stay in samples. Padding within sync groups and per-strip latency in `MixEngine` (see [E40](#e40)): Game on Low Latency with a Quality preset on Music reports 100 + 68 samples at 48 kHz (before 1420). Tests: *Chain: Quality runs as Balanced below 32 kHz, without a re-prepare*, *Chain: each profile's latency in ms is about the same from 44.1 to 192 kHz*, *MixEngine: padding only within sync groups, per-strip latency reported*, each failing on the old code. The host readout uses `MixEngine::getStripLatencySamples()` / `getStripPaddingSamples()` (wave B2 review). Open: a UI note while Quality is clamped, the prompt, removing the master look-ahead (+68 samples), E42b–E42d.
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2, app).** E42a's prompt done in `EngineController` (`takeLatencySuggestion()`, filled by `loadPreset` and next / previous preset) and the `NoticeBar` (`ui/NoticeBanners.*`). Before: loading `music-audiophile-subtle` left the profile unchanged and said nothing (its `suggestedLatencyProfile` was read and never used). After: the profile stays Balanced on every strip and both banks (Done-when row 2 met) and the notice *"Audiophile Subtle" was made for Quality; the engine runs Balanced.* offers **Use Quality**, which calls `setLatencyProfile` (every strip, both banks); Competitive FPS on Quality offers Low Latency; a preset that matches the profile or names none asks nothing; the prompt goes when the profile becomes the suggested one or another preset is loaded (`tests/app/test_app_ui_status.cpp`; screenshot `--state latency-prompt`). Automatic profile loads do not prompt. The master look-ahead is **not** removed: it lives in `MixEngine` (core), outside this track's files, so a strip still carries the master limiter's 68 samples at 48 kHz (1 ms look-ahead + 20-sample detector), before → after unchanged; the Done-when row "two-strip sum at −1 dBTP without master look-ahead" stays open. Also open: a UI note while Quality is clamped below 32 kHz, E42b–E42d.
- **Status (Phase 3, E42d).** Done offline; not yet run on real devices. `flub::latency::LatencyProbe` (`core/include/flub/analysis/LatencyProbe.h`) generates exponential sweeps (default 10 × 1 s, 20 Hz–20 kHz, −12 dBFS, each followed by a gap of the maximum delay + 250 ms). It deconvolves each run with `flub::Fft` (regularised, band-limited division by the sweep's spectrum), takes the largest peak of either polarity, refines it with a parabola, rejects runs under 30 dB SNR and reports the median of the rest (no result when fewer than half pass). `analyseRelative` measures against a reference channel that `locate()` finds, for recordings whose start is not locked to playback. The command is `flubsound-cli latency-probe generate | analyze` (text or `--json`; exit 1 without a valid result). Before: no measurement existed; every end-to-end figure was an estimate. After, offline (`tests/test_latency_probe.cpp`, 7 cases, each under 0.7 s): integer delays at 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz within 0.05 samples (measured error 0.000). A 1234.37-sample fractional delay is found within 0.06 samples when it is also attenuated by 30 dB, inverted and under noise 40 dB down; this meets the Done-when's "±1 sample offline". Through a 60 Hz high-pass and an 18 kHz low-pass it is within 0.1 ms. Three runs buried under noise 30 dB over the sweep read 10–13 dB and are rejected, with the median unchanged. A 1.3 s start offset cancels against a reference channel (700.25 samples, found within 0.25). The processing chain measures 1351.997 / 191.997 / 99.997 samples against its reported 1352 / 192 / 100 on Quality / Balanced / Low Latency. The real-device procedure is in [01 §5.4](01-architecture.md#54-measuring-it-the-loopback-probe) and has not been run. Open: measurements on real interfaces and the RTL Utility comparison (within 0.2 ms on 3 interfaces); storing results per device and showing them in the app; E42b–E42c; the master look-ahead removal; a UI note while Quality is clamped.
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07, E42c and E42d in the app).** Done in the app, tested with fake devices only; nothing has been measured on real hardware yet. (1) **Why the owner's device runs at 480 samples (10 ms).** Two causes. The app never asked for a buffer size, so JUCE opened every device at its default, which for *Windows Audio (Low Latency Mode)* (`IAudioClient3`) is the engine's *default* period: 480 frames at 48 kHz, whatever the device allows. And the owner's headset allows nothing smaller. A read-only query of the owner's endpoints (`IAudioClient3::GetSharedModeEnginePeriod`, `IAudioClient::GetDevicePeriod`; no stream started) reads default = fundamental = minimum = maximum = 480 frames for *Speakers* and *Microphone (Stealth 600PC Gen 3)* (the exclusive-mode minimum is 3 ms), and the same for the three NVIDIA HDMI outputs. Only *Digital Audio (S/PDIF) (High Definition Audio Device)* (Microsoft's HD Audio driver) offers 128 .. 480 frames in steps of 32. Two more findings. JUCE reports a shared-mode device's latency as its buffer (`GetStreamLatency` reads 0 for a shared stream that has not started), so 480 + 480 samples, while WASAPI's own shared buffer is 1056 frames (2.2 periods); that unreported part is what the measurement below is for. And a duplex WASAPI device runs both ends at the larger of their minimum periods, so the Stealth's microphone (480 only) holds even an output that allows 128 at 480. The app opens that microphone as its input although no strip uses it (device input *Automatic* takes only loopback-like inputs). (2) **E42c: the buffer per latency profile** (`app/Source/engine/BufferPolicy.h`, `AudioEngineHost` *DEVICE BUFFER SIZE*, `EngineController`). While *Automatic buffer size* is on (Settings › Audio, on by default, `device.autoBufferSize`), the host asks for one of three sizes. Low Latency gets the smallest offered size of at least 1.33 ms (64 samples at 48 kHz, this item's "≥ 64"). Balanced gets the offered size nearest to 5 ms (ties: the larger). Quality gets the device's default. No size goes above the default, which is what the app used before. The size is applied when the device opens or changes and when the profile is chosen by hand: one device re-open, and the engine is built once for it. The profile the automatic overload response steps to never shrinks the buffer (the controller passes the user's profile). Device types whose buffer is not the app's to choose are left alone: plain *Windows Audio* (shared mode ignores the request), ALSA, JACK and the native PipeWire node (the graph quantum, [E48](#e48) E48a). A size the user picks in the device list wins: Settings › Audio turns Automatic off, and the host then never touches the size. **The safety net.** The existing automatic overload response does *not* raise the buffer: it is the opt-in latency-profile ladder (`AutoLoadReducer`), and the watchdog only reports. So a back-off was added (`buffer::Backoff`, fed by the watchdog's 2 Hz poll): more than 2 glitches (xruns, overrunning or late callbacks) within 10 s, or a sustained overload, raise the buffer one offered size, at most to the default. That size becomes the device's floor (`device.bufferFloors`, per device type and output), and the next step waits 10 s. Turning Automatic off and on forgets the floor. Before → after, 48 kHz, reported figures (not measured): the **Stealth 600PC Gen 3 on its dongle is unchanged on every profile** (480 only): device out 10.0 + engine 5.4 (Balanced) / 3.0 (Low Latency) / 30.6 (Quality) + device in 10.0 ms = 25.4 / 23.0 / 50.6 ms, and a per-app capture adds its FIFO, 964 + 23.5 frames = 20.6 ms at 480-sample blocks. An HD Audio output whose input also allows small periods: Balanced 480 → 256 samples (reported 25.4 → 16.1 ms; capture FIFO 20.6 → 15.9 ms), Low Latency 480 → 128 (23.0 → 8.3 ms; capture FIFO 20.6 → 13.2 ms), Quality 480 → 480. Tests (`tests/app/test_app_latency_measure.cpp`): *App: E42c buffer choice per latency profile on real devices' lists …* (HD Audio 128 / 256 / 480, the Stealth's 480 on every profile, WASAPI exclusive 144 / 256 / 480, ASIO and CoreAudio lists, the default cap, the floor), *App: E42c back-off: more than 2 glitches within 10 s …*, *App: E42c the host asks for the profile's buffer …* (a fake device type: the saved state's 480 → 128 when the device opens, then 256 / 480 by profile, Automatic off keeps 480, the back-off 128 → 160 with the floor remembered and applied when the device opens again), *App: E42c a device with one buffer size (the Stealth 600PC Gen 3 dongle) keeps it on every profile*, *App: E42c the controller persists Automatic and the floors; the profile chosen by hand, never the overload response's, drives the buffer* *App: E42c the controller's back-off raises an automatic buffer after glitches and persists the floor* and *App: E42c Settings > Audio: a size picked in the device list turns Automatic off and wins; the switch turns it back on*. (3) **E42d in the app: the live measurement** (`LatencyMeasurement.*`, `LatencyMeasurer.*`, `ui/LatencyPanel.*`). Settings › Audio › **Measure latency…** (Device only / Through Flubsound (the selected strip) / Both, the default) asks first: turn the volume down, hold the headset's microphone at an earcup or use a cable, microphone monitoring off. Then the device callback plays `LatencyProbe`'s sweeps (5 × 0.5 s, 20 Hz – 20 kHz, −24 dBFS, gaps for round trips up to 500 ms; 6.55 s per pass, never looped) and records the input in the same callback, so the recording is sample-locked and `analyse()` reads the round trip directly. *Device only* replaces the outputs (5 ms crossfades). *Through Flubsound* makes the probe the strip's input (the other strips' inputs fade out) and caps the output at −18 dBFS (a sample-peak cap whose gain only falls). The analysis runs on a worker thread; each recorded input channel is analysed and the best kept. The result gives the round trip (ms and samples), how it splits (the device's reported output and input latency, the engine's on the Through path, and what the device does not report), the playback latency (reported output + engine + half the unreported part, with the bounds all-on-the-input-side .. all-on-the-output-side), and how sure it is. *High* means every run accepted, a spread ≤ 0.25 ms and a median SNR ≥ 40 dB; *medium* means a rejected run, a weak signal or a small spread; *low* means runs more than 1 ms apart, a second arrival within 6 dB of the first (an echo, a headset's sidetone: `LatencyProbe` now reports each run's strongest other arrival, `Run::secondaryDb`, also in the CLI's JSON) or glitches during the pass; *none* means no result, with why (too weak; silence; cancelled; the device stopped). With *Both* the engine's own latency is measured as the difference. The result goes to the log and to Settings › Processing's *Current latency*. Audio thread: no allocation, no lock (`ProbeSession`'s callback functions are `FLUB_NONBLOCKING` and listed in `tests/test_rtsan.cpp`); silent while the feedback-loop guard holds the output; a cancel fades out in 5 ms. Before: no in-app measurement, every end-to-end figure an estimate. After, through a fake duplex device whose input is its output 1512 samples later plus −70 dBFS noise: device only **1512.000** samples, through Flubsound (the Music strip, Balanced) **1771.997** = 1512 + 260 (the engine's 260 measured as 259.997), median SNR 71.4 dB, strongest other arrival about −54 dB, confidence high (*App: E42d live measurement through a fake loopback finds the round trip within 0.1 ms, device only and through Flubsound*). The sweep 80 dB down under −50 dBFS of noise gives no result and says why, silence says "recorded silence", and an echo 2 dB down 20 ms later still finds the direct path (1512 samples) but grades it low with the sidetone advice (*App: E42d a weak or silent input, or an echo …*). Also: *App: E42d the probe allocates nothing on the audio thread …*, *App: E42d a device restart or a cancel during the measurement ends it with a message, never a hang*, *App: E42d no measurement without an input or a running device …*, *App: E42d Settings > Audio latency texts …* and, in core, *LatencyProbe (E42d): the strongest other arrival reports an echo nearly as strong as the direct path* (clean path −55.1 dB; a −3 dB echo −3.01 dB at its lag). Gated (needs the owner): the measurement on the Stealth 600PC Gen 3 (microphone at the earcup) and with a cable loop; the procedure is in [12 › How to measure your latency](12-feature-guide.md#how-to-measure-your-latency-settings--audio). Open: the RTL Utility comparison; storing results per device; not opening an input no strip uses (it holds a duplex WASAPI device at the input's period); macOS / Linux untested (the buffer choice applies to CoreAudio and ASIO as well).
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07, review fixes for E42c and E42d).** Fake devices only, before → after measured by the same test code run against both versions. (1) **Through Flubsound with a programme playing.** The −18 dBFS cap tracked from the session's first sample, while the programme already in the chain still left it for the strip latency, so it locked onto the programme. Music strip fed a 440 Hz sine (−1.1 dBFS at the output): cap −16.94 dB → **0.00 dB**, the sweeps at the output −38.8 → **−21.9 dBFS**, median SNR 62.0 → 76.8–76.9 dB, round trip 1771.997 samples both. The result also said, wrongly, that the strip had raised the sweep. Now an output gate fades the outputs out at the start (5 ms), holds them through the lead-in and opens (5 ms) as the first sweep arrives (lead-in + strip latency, `ProbeSession::setOutputLatency`). The cap tracks only from the gate's opening to where the returning programme can reach the output, then eases back to unity. Its note is written only when it acted on the sweep; a separate note covers sound left over before the first sweep. A sweep the strip leaves above −18 dBFS is still held there (a −6 dBFS probe: output peak −18.00 dBFS, cap −14.15 dB, as before). (2) **Both.** The second pass started from the 2 Hz poll, so the programme came back between the passes: 156.8 ms at −1.05 dBFS with the test's fast polling, up to 500 ms in the app. Now both sessions are handed over together and chained on the audio thread. `AudioEngineHost` plays the first session still playing (one slot each, seq_cst pointers); the device-only pass leaves the outputs silent at its end, and the through pass starts with its gate closed. Result: one quiet stretch of 6202–6203 ms for 2 × 3100 ms of probe, nothing between. The poll now only collects the passes; a glitch between the last poll before the first pass was collected and that collection counts for both passes (a warning too many rather than one missed; untested, the fake devices report no glitch count). (3) **Cancel through Flubsound** with a programme: the cap held the returning programme at −18 dBFS for 100 ms and then let go in one sample (largest step 0.0815 against the 440 Hz sine's own 0.0510). Now the gate closes in 5 ms, stays closed for the strip latency + 5 ms and fades the programme back in, uncapped: largest step 0.0509 (the sine's own 0.0500), the programme back at its level. Device-only cancel unchanged (0.0510), now tested. (4) The device callback loads the probe pointers with `seq_cst`, as the capture slots do (it was `acquire`; `waitForAudioThreadToPass` needs seq_cst on both sides). (5) **Restart.** At start the buffer followed the strip's persisted profile, which keeps the automatic overload response's step. Test: Quality chosen by hand, the reducer steps to Balanced, restart: buffer profile Balanced → **Quality**. The profile chosen by hand is now stored on its own (`device.bufferProfile`). (6) **Upgrade.** *Automatic buffer size* defaulted to on and overrode a buffer size picked in Settings › Audio before it existed. On the first start without `device.autoBufferSize`, the device's open now decides (`setAutomaticBufferSizeFromSavedState`): JUCE saves the size the device ran at, and the app never asked for one, so a size other than the device's default was the user's pick and keeps Automatic off. Test: saved 320 on the HD Audio list stays 320 with Automatic off; saved 480 (the default) turns it on, 128 on Low Latency; the Stealth (480 only) turns it on, unchanged. Release notes (owner): *Automatic buffer size* is new and on by default; a buffer size picked in Settings › Audio before keeps it off. (7) **Back-off.** One size per step took 14 steps from 64 to 512 on a CoreAudio list (32-sample steps, 10 s hold: over two minutes of dropouts). Now each step goes to at least twice the size, at most the default: 64 → 128 → 256 → 512 (3 steps) with a 5 s hold; HD Audio 128 → 256 → 480. The thresholds are still untuned. (8) **The Game strip** (selected by default) measures **7.19 samples** (0.15 ms) more than its reported 260 with the virtualiser on, and 0.00 with it bypassed (strongest other arrival −21.3 / −41.9 dB): the head-related filters start late. Both's result now says so, and docs/12 no longer promises a match on every strip. Tests (`tests/app/test_app_latency_measure.cpp`, 20 cases, each 0.0001 – 0.58 s, 15 repeats with 0 failures): new *App: E42d through Flubsound with a loud programme on the strip …*, *… both passes with a loud programme …*, *… a sweep the strip leaves above −18 dBFS is held there by the cap …*, *… a cancel with a programme playing brings it back without a step …*, *… through the Game strip the virtualiser's head-related filters …*, *App: E42c upgrading …* and *App: E42c after a restart the buffer still follows the profile chosen by hand …*; updated: the back-off (doubling, 5 s hold), the RT test (a chained pair across its boundary: no allocation, silent, the second starts in the next callback). The RT test's no-lock check runs only on Linux glibc (`FLUB_APP_TESTS_COUNT_LOCKS`); on Windows only *no allocation* is tested, so the lock part rests on CI. Still gated: the measurement on the Stealth 600PC Gen 3.
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07, R1.2: ASIO into CI after the licence check).** The licence check is done ([02 §6](02-tech-stack.md#6-licensing-summary-third-party-components)): since 2025-10-15 Steinberg's ASIO SDK (2.3.4) is dual-licensed, the proprietary Steinberg ASIO Licence (no redistribution of the SDK, a signed licence agreement before publishing) or GPLv3. Before: ASIO compiled only with `-DFLUB_ASIO_SDK_DIR`, never built anywhere, no UI note. After: `cmake/FlubAsio.cmake` (`FLUB_ASIO` off by default; `FLUB_ASIO_SDK_DIR` for a downloaded SDK, or `FLUB_ASIO_FETCH` for the official archive with a pinned SHA-256 under the GPLv3 option), the single-client note on Settings › Audio ([08 D10](08-pitfalls-and-solutions.md#d10-asio-specifics)), and CI's non-blocking `asio` job (windows-2022) building the app and `flub_app_tests` with the fetched SDK and running the R1.2 cases (ASIO offered; uploads nothing). The configure paths were checked here with a harness (default off; `FLUB_ASIO=ON` without an SDK stops with the two ways to get one; a folder without `common/iasiodrv.h` is refused; the `ASIOSDK` folder or the folder above it is found; `FLUB_ASIO_FETCH` alone warns); the download and the compile ran only on CI (runs 37582910555 and 37585773116: the pinned SHA-256 matched Steinberg's archive, ASIO offered, 0 drivers on the runner). Open: E42c's smallest ASIO period with xrun step-back, a run with a real ASIO driver, and the owner's licence choice for a published build.
- **What.** **E42a** (do first): `latency.profile` becomes app state with a suggestion prompt; padding only within sync groups, with latency reported per strip; look-aheads defined in ms; Quality clamped to Balanced below 32 kHz; totals labelled "estimated" and including the known PipeWire quantum. **E42b**: an adaptive capture-FIFO target (packet jitter p99 + margin, capped at 612). **E42c**: smallest stable device period on Low Latency (IAudioClient3, CoreAudio, ASIO) with xrun step-back. **E42d**: a loopback probe (exponential sweep, cable or mic at the earcup) with results stored per device. Remove the master look-ahead when a single strip is active or strip ceilings guarantee the master ceiling. Get ASIO into CI after a licence check.
- **Why.** The 10 ms claim is an estimate for an unbuilt driver. Real paths add 12.75 ms (Windows loopback FIFO) or 21.3 ms (PipeWire default) before device buffering. Low Latency saves about 2 ms while costing clipper fidelity, and loading Competitive FPS on the Game strip can leave it at another strip's latency. At Bluetooth hands-free rates Quality reaches lip-sync-breaking latency.
- **Expected benefit.** The high-value part is E42a. Loading `music-audiophile-subtle` on the Music strip moves the Game strip from 192 or 100 samples to 1352 (28.17 ms instead of 4.00 or 2.08 ms); Quality at 8 kHz is 1152 samples (144 ms), Balanced 92 (11.5 ms). Correction: the UI does not under-report the capture FIFO (`HeaderBar` and `SettingsDialog` add `captureBufferMs`); what is missing is the PipeWire quantum, Bluetooth codec delay and any measurement. Shared-mode loopback delivers about 10 ms packets, so E42b realistically saves 2–4 ms; the structural FIFO fix is [E46](#e46) M2. Most players own no loopback cable or measurement mic, so never gate "Competitive FPS" on a measured result.
- **Approach.** E42a: save/restore `LatencyProfile` in `loadIntoBank` and call `EngineController::applyLatencyProfile` from the prompt; `StripConfig::syncGroup` in `MixEngine::build` (default: each strip its own group); ms-based look-aheads and the < 32 kHz clamp in `ProcessingChain::prepare`. E42b: fixed-point analysis against the drift controller before shipping, tau ≈ 10 s with hysteresis. E42c: smallest `getAvailableBufferSizes()` ≥ 64, step up after > 2 xruns in 10 s using [E45](#e45)'s histogram. E42d: `LatencyProbe` with `flub::Fft` deconvolution, parabolic peak, median of 10 runs, reject < 30 dB SNR. The `PIPEWIRE_LATENCY` step belongs to [E48](#e48).
- **Depends on.** E42a: [E40](#e40)'s slice (parts 1 and 3) and nothing else. It sets prepare-time values (look-ahead in ms, the < 32 kHz clamp, padding within sync groups) and labels. Its prompt calls the existing `applyLatencyProfile`, a user-initiated re-prepare that exists today with a brief dropout ([06 §6.1][06-61]), so it does not need the swap to merge. It does need the swap to become click-free. E42b–E42c and removing the master look-ahead at run time: the in-flight crossfaded engine swap (FIFO target, device period and look-ahead changes while audio runs); [E45](#e45); [E46](#e46) M2; [E48](#e48). E42b must also be designed against [E50](#e50)'s larger FIFO target ([§6](#6-updated-risk-assessment-for-the-proposed-enhancements) T8).
- **Done when.** Game strip in Low Latency plus a Quality preset on Music reports 100 samples at 48 kHz (today 1352). Loading `music-audiophile-subtle` leaves `LatencyProfile` unchanged and raises the prompt. Quality at 8 and 16 kHz ≤ Balanced; look-ahead ms constant from 44.1 to 192 kHz. Two-strip sum at −1 dBTP without master look-ahead. 8 h FIFO soak at ±200 ppm with no xruns, target below 612 frames and pitch deviation < 1 cent. Probe accurate to ±1 sample offline and within 0.2 ms of RTL Utility on 3 interfaces.
