- **Evidence.** [TRACEABILITY Known gaps][TR-gaps] R1.1, R1.2; [01 §5.3][01-53] (612 frames = 12.75 ms; 1024/48000 = 21.3 ms); [AudioEngineHost.cpp][AEH] `getLatencyInfo()`, `openDevice()` (device-reported only); [PresetManager.cpp][PM] `loadIntoBank`; [MixEngine.cpp][ME] `build()`; [01 §5.1][01-51] master +44/+68 samples; [08 A1][08-A1].

<a id="e47"></a>
#### E47 · Routing correctness and recovery: honest Automatic method, dry+wet doubling guard, route journal

*Stability & platform · benefit 4 · difficulty Medium · E47a 2–3 days; total 2.5–3.5 engineer-weeks*

- **Status (Phase 1 batch).** E47a done in `PlatformServices.h` (`AppAudioRouter::canList()` / `canMoveEndpoint()` / `cannotMoveReason()`), `PlatformServices_win.cpp` (`WinAppAudioRouter`: lists, moves only with `FLUB_ENABLE_UNDOCUMENTED_ROUTING`), `AppRouting.*` (`isEndpointRoutingSupported` uses `canMoveEndpoint`, `getUnavailableReason()`, `getProcessedAppCount()`) and `RoutingPanel.*` (red *No apps are being processed* notice under the header, also its accessible description); Automatic in a default Windows build: EndpointRouting with every move failing → ProcessCapture, or Disabled with the router's reason, never EndpointRouting (fake router whose `isSupported()` still claims support, `tests/app/test_app_routing.cpp`). With it, the process-capture gate moved 20348 → 19041 (Microsoft documents 20348; OBS gates at 19041), so Windows 10 2004–22H2 get capture instead of Disabled; not yet run on a 19045 machine. The wave B2 review keeps the red state off while nothing is assigned and the device input feeds a strip (a virtual cable as the system output: processed, just not per app); an assigned app that is not processed is red either way. Open: the same-endpoint doubling guard and amber badge, the route journal and its crash tests.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, Windows lane).** Partially done: the doubling guard with its amber badge and fix, and the route journal with its crash tests, in `AppRouting.*`, `RoutingPanel.*` and `PlatformServices.h` / `_win.cpp` / `_common.cpp` ([06 §6.10](06-gui.md#610-routingpanel--strips-and-applications), [06 §8](06-gui.md#8-per-app-routing-ux), [10 §4](10-headset-compatibility.md#4-recommended-setup-all-turtle-beach-models)). *Doubling guard.* Windows sessions now carry every endpoint the process has an active session on, its image path and its creation time; `AppAudioRouter::listOutputEndpoints()` lists the active render endpoints in JUCE's WASAPI order (default first) and `findOutputEndpointId()` undoes JUCE's `" (2)"` duplicate-name numbering, so the JUCE output device name gives the endpoint id the sessions use. In process-capture mode an assigned app that plays to that endpoint is not captured (a running capture stops) and is marked `doublingBlocked`: before, the capture started and the app was heard twice, dry and processed; after, 0 capture starts for it (fake router: cs2 on the headset endpoint held back, Spotify on the speakers captured; the fix applied, cs2 captured; the output moved to the speakers, both held back; an ASIO output the router cannot name, guard off). The routing panel draws its chip with two amber rings (*original also audible*, the *warn* status colour), adds *Original also audible - how to fix…* to its menu, and shows an amber *Original also audible* notice (or, when nothing else is processed, the red state with the same text) naming the device and the fix: set the app's output to a device you do not listen to in Windows' Volume mixer / App volume and device preferences (**Open sound settings**), with the active devices other than the output and Flubsound's own listed (`getSpareEndpoints`), or a virtual cable when there are none; this also covers [E36](#e36)'s "a capture whose session still renders to the output device produces a warning". *Route journal.* Every endpoint move is first written to `route-journal.json` next to the settings (process id, start time, executable and path, target and previous endpoint, `pending`; temporary file + rename) and its entry removed when the move is undone; a move whose entry cannot be written is not made. Before: the moved executables reached disk only through the settings autosave, up to 2 s after the move and via the message thread, so a kill in that window left the app on the Flubsound endpoint. After: the next start reads the journal; a process of a recorded executable is moved back to the system default, the very process (same pid and start time, not pending) stays while it is still assigned, and a reused pid is left alone. Tested with a crash at both points of a move (journal copied at the move and after it), a reused pid, and a real kill: a child `flub_app_tests` process moves pid 4242 to the Game endpoint through a file-backed fake audio service, the parent kills it (`ChildProcess::kill`: SIGKILL, TerminateProcess on Windows) and the restart moves it back (2 moves in the service's log: Game, then default; journal gone). `tests/app/test_app_routing_journal.cpp` and `test_app_routing_doubling.cpp`, 9 cases, 0.35 s together; the Windows code passes a MinGW-w64 `-fsyntax-only` with the warning flags as errors, with and without `FLUB_ENABLE_UNDOCUMENTED_ROUTING`, and is not yet run on Windows. Open: the Linux half (clearing WirePlumber's restore-stream entries and the `pw-play` / `kill -9` test, `PlatformServices_linux.cpp`, on [E48](#e48)'s headless PipeWire CI); a run on a real Windows PC with a Turtle Beach headset (the JUCE-name match assumes WASAPI names; DirectSound names are unchecked); restoring an app's own earlier per-app device instead of the system default (it needs the undocumented `GetPersistedDefaultAudioEndpoint`); a one-click move to a spare endpoint in builds that can move; the `ISimpleAudioVolume` session-mute experiment; an app that no longer has an audio session is moved back only when it plays again; the journal is not `fsync`ed (a killed process is covered, a power cut may lose the last write).
- **Status (p3b5, the Linux half).** Done on a headless PipeWire 1.0.5 + WirePlumber 0.4.17 + pipewire-pulse, in `PlatformServices_linux.cpp` and `tests/app/test_app_route_journal_linux.cpp`; the Linux Done-when row is met there (with a Game sink named per test run, not `flubsound_game` itself). Three changes:
  - **Restore-stream entries.** WirePlumber keeps a stream's move target as the application's restore-stream entry and sends its next streams there. A move back to the default now also deletes the stream's `target.object` / `target.node` in the "default" metadata (`pw-metadata -d`). pipewire-pulse 1.0 already writes -1 for a move to `@DEFAULT_SINK@`, which clears the entry. Older versions write the default sink's id, which would pin the application to that device; the deletion covers both.
  - **Native PipeWire clients.** Before, a stream of `pw-play` (and SDL or OpenAL on their PipeWire back-end) had no pid in pactl's sink-input list, so it was skipped and could never be routed. After, its pid (`pipewire.sec.pid`), binary and name come from its client (`pactl --format=json list clients`, asked only when such a stream exists).
  - **Moves verified.** pipewire-pulse acknowledges a move before WirePlumber applies it. A move that raced WirePlumber's own placement was dropped in 1 of 9 runs of the crash test, right after a default-output change, and the app was taken for routed and never retried. Now the router checks for 300 ms, moves again once, and otherwise reports an error, so the next pass retries. Result: 0 failures in 50 + 20 runs.
  - **Test.** A `pw-play` stream plays on the default output. A child `flub_app_tests` routes its executable (`pw-cat`) to the Game sink through the real `pactl` router and is killed with SIGKILL; the journal names it. WirePlumber then sends the application's next stream to the Game sink too (the precondition). The restart, without the route, moves both streams back to the default and empties the journal, and leaves no target on the stream. With another default output, the application's next stream goes there: neither to the Game sink nor to the old default. It runs on a test server only, since it changes the default output, and in CI's `pipewire` job. Timings: median 0.49 s, at most 1.32 s over 50 runs. Mutation check: without the client lookup the child cannot move `pw-play`, and the case fails.
  - **Open.** WirePlumber 0.5 (another state format; the metadata path is the same) is not tested. A restore-stream entry of an app whose process is gone at the restart stays until that app plays again; it is then moved back, as before. WirePlumber 0.4 keys the entry by `media.role` first, so while an app with a role (`pw-play` sets Music; most PulseAudio apps set none) sits on a strip, every other app of that role also starts on that strip. This was found here and is not fixed. Moves through `target.object` metadata instead of `pactl` belong to [E48](#e48).
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07, R4.5: Flubsound moves a captured app's own output).** Done for Windows: *Move the app's own sound away automatically* (Settings › Routing, `routing.moveOriginalAway`). It is **off by default**, with a one-click switch-on in the amber notice's fix dialog and in the chip menu (*Move automatically to Digital Audio (S/PDIF)*). Off by default because the move uses the undocumented per-app device API, Windows keeps it per executable after Flubsound quits when the app is closed, and Flubsound cannot know whether the chosen device is really silent (an S/PDIF jack may feed a receiver, a monitor may have speakers): the user sees the device name before the first move. Code: `AppRouting.*` (`applySilentMoves`, `chooseSilentEndpoint`, journal kind *away*), `PlatformServices.h` / `_win.cpp` / `_common.cpp` (`canMoveAppOutput`, `getAppEndpoint`, `endpointIdFromInterfacePath`; the adapter is compiled into every Windows build, while `FLUB_ENABLE_UNDOCUMENTED_ROUTING` now only offers endpoint routing to strip endpoints), `RoutingPanel.*`, `SettingsDialog.*` (a new Routing page; the device input and the method moved there from Processing) and `AppSettings.*`.
  - **Before → after (owner's case: Edge or a game on the Stealth 600PC Gen 3, which is both the default output and Flubsound's output, under process capture).** Before: the app is held back (0 captures, amber), and the owner sets its output by hand in the Volume mixer. After, with the option on: 1 move per executable, journaled first, to the silent device. The capture starts only when the next pass, 250 ms later, sees the app off the headset, so there is no moment of doubled sound (asserted: 0 captures at the move, and 0 for an app whose stream stays on the headset).
  - **Silent device.** Chosen in Settings › Routing, or automatic: an S/PDIF / digital output, else an HDMI / DisplayPort output (since the review fixes below: each only while no unassigned app plays to it, and never an endpoint named *Speakers* / *Headphones* / *Headset*). Never Flubsound's output, the system default, a Flubsound endpoint or a virtual cable that feeds Flubsound's input. On this PC the automatic choice is *Digital Audio (S/PDIF) (High Definition Audio Device)*, out of the headset (default) and three NVIDIA HDMI outputs (LEN T23i-10, Odyssey G40B, VG248). If the device goes away, an automatic choice re-points the apps to the next candidate; a chosen one pauses them (held back, *… is not connected*).
  - **Put back.** Each app gets back the per-app device it had before the move, read with `GetPersistedDefaultAudioEndpoint` ("" = the system default; a device that is gone falls back to the default). This happens when the app is unassigned, the option goes off or capture stops being the method; at quit, through a process that a last enumeration finds (not a stale pid); and after a crash at the next start, as soon as a process of the executable is seen. An app that is closed at quit keeps its journal entry until it runs again. Putting the device back does not move a stream that is already open (see the review-fix status below): a playing app may need its playback restarted to be heard.
  - **The user's own choice.** An app that does not play to the output (e.g. Edge set to S/PDIF by hand) is captured where it is, never moved and never "put back". If the user changes an app's device after Flubsound moved it, Flubsound forgets that move (no fight) and offers **Move again**.
  - **Other cases.** A browser's audio comes from its audio process: the session's process is moved, once per executable, and a re-launched process inherits the device (no new move; the record follows the new pid). A failed move shows Windows' error on the chip and is tried 3 times. An app that keeps playing to the headset after the move (it opens the device itself) stays held back with *restart its playback*.
  - **Tests.** `tests/app/test_app_routing_move_away.cpp`: 12 cases with the scripted audio service, 0.0005–0.98 s each here. Also 2 cases in `test_app_routing_move_away_windows.cpp`.
  - **Real Windows check (build 26200, this PC; opt-in `FLUB_TEST_REAL_APP_MOVE=1`).** A child `flub_app_tests` played silence on the default output. The per-app device read '' before the move and the S/PDIF id after it. Its open WASAPI stream stayed on the headset. A second process of the same executable started on S/PDIF and read the S/PDIF id. Putting it back read ''.
  - **Registry.** The per-app PropertyStore (`HKCU\…\Audio\PolicyConfig\PropertyStore`) dumped before and after: all 240 lines of the existing entries unchanged. The only additions are two value-less keys for the test binary's path on the headset and on S/PDIF, which Windows creates for any process that plays there. No other app was touched.
  - **Open.** Not tried with Edge, a game or Flubsound Pro itself on the owner's headset: how fast Edge's open stream follows a per-app device change is unknown (the test stream did not follow). Other Windows builds (19045, 22631, 26100) are not checked; the IID check guards them. The automatic choice cannot tell a silent S/PDIF jack from one that feeds a receiver. A moved app whose process is closed when Flubsound quits stays on the silent device until Flubsound sees it again. In Tournament mode nothing is moved or put back, and the journal waits for the next start.
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07, R4.5 review fixes).** The review of the move-away found one major and seven minor problems; all are fixed in `AppRouting.*`, `PlatformServices.h`, `RoutingPanel.*`, `SettingsDialog.*` and the tests, with 4 new cases (16 in `test_app_routing_move_away.cpp`, 0.0001–0.84 s each over 3 runs here).
  - **Put back while playing (major).** Measured on this PC (build 26200, the opt-in real check): a second test process opened its stream on S/PDIF after the move, and after the put-back its open stream **stayed on S/PDIF** (per-app device '' again). So an app that is playing when Flubsound puts it back (unassigned, option off, method change, quit) keeps playing to the silent device and, no longer captured, is not heard until it restarts its playback. Flubsound cannot move an open stream. Before: the reviewer's probe showed per-app device '', stream on S/PDIF, no capture, no note, and the docs promised *Edge … plays straight to the headset*. After: the passes after a put-back watch the app (`watchPutBacks`, the next pass in 250 ms) and, while it still plays there uncaptured, say so: *Spotify still plays to Digital Audio (S/PDIF), where Flubsound had moved its own sound, so it is not heard. Its own output device is set back: restart its playback (reload, or pause and play) to hear it again.* It shows as an amber *Not heard* notice on the routing panel, or is added to the red / amber one, and in Settings › Routing. It goes when the app plays elsewhere (asserted after a restarted stream), exits, is moved again, or the user sets that device for it. At quit the same case goes to the diagnostic log. The tooltip, the doubling text, `describeMoveAway`, docs/06, docs/10, docs/12 and TESTING.txt now say the device is *set back* and a playing app may need its playback restarted.
  - **Moves per executable file.** Windows keeps the per-app device per executable path, so moves, the journal match and the process acted through are now keyed by the lower-case path (the name where none is known). Before: two programs named `game.exe` got 1 move (the second stayed on the headset), and an app whose path changes on update (Discord's `app-1.0.x`) was taken for *output changed after Flubsound moved it* and held back. After: 2 moves and 2 put-backs for the two `game.exe` files, and the updated Discord is moved afresh (`Moved`, not `LeftToUser`). The old file's record is forgotten once that file is gone and a newer one runs (journal 3 entries, not 4).
  - **Crash during a re-point.** A pending re-point now journals the device it leaves (`"from"`). Before (reviewer's probe): journal {endpoint HDMI 1, pending}, app on S/PDIF → the entry was dropped and the app stayed on S/PDIF for good (0 moves). After: it is still Flubsound's move. The re-point is finished (1 move to HDMI 1), and unassigning puts the device back ('' , 2 moves in all, journal gone).
  - **Silent device rules.** `silentPreference` matches only S/PDIF, SPDIF, optical, TOSLINK and *Digital Output*, and never an endpoint named *Speakers* / *Headphones* / *Headset* / *Earphones*. Before → after: *Speakers (USB Digital Audio)* 2 → 0 (and nothing is picked from {headset, it}), *Digital Audio (USB Audio Device)* 2 → 0. An output an unassigned app plays to is now skipped for digital outputs too: S/PDIF busy picked S/PDIF before, HDMI 1 now. Apps Flubsound moved or put back do not count as busy. One consequence, tested: an app the user parked on S/PDIF and then unassigned makes the automatic choice move on to HDMI 1. The owner's S/PDIF (*Digital Audio (S/PDIF) (High Definition Audio Device)*) is still the automatic choice on this PC.
  - **Settings › Routing.** The input-map line says *Device input is off: the map is not used.* or *Device input is not processed now (Automatic …): the map is not used.* when the device input is not processed (`SettingsDialog::describeInputMap`; before, it said *The map is in use* whenever a map was saved). The fix dialog's Return key is back on **Open sound settings**; the one-click move has no key. The 2 Hz refresh no longer rebuilds the silent-device list or a map row while its list is open.
  - **Linux router.** `AppAudioRouter::canMoveAppOutput()` now defaults to `false`; only the Windows router says true. Before, the Linux `pactl` router inherited `canMoveEndpoint()` (true).
  - **Tests.** New: the *Not heard* note (and its quit log line), moves per executable file, a crash during a re-point, and Tournament mode with an *away* journal entry (0 enumerations, 0 moves, journal kept at quit; the next start puts it back). The real check now asserts that the second process's stream plays on the silent device, prints whether the open streams follow (they did not, in either direction), and puts the device back and stops its children in a scope guard. It took 1.20 s; the registry dump of `PolicyConfig\PropertyStore` was identical before and after (246 lines), and no child was left running.
  - **Docs.** docs/08 D7 and the safety note, docs/02 §4.1, docs/04's option table, docs/06 §8 and §13 and the README now match the split: the adapter is in every Windows build and used only for the opt-in move-away; the flag only offers endpoint routing.
  - **Open.** Whether Edge or a game picks up a put-back without a restart of its playback is not checked (the test process did not). The scope guard's failure path has not run (no check failed). The panel's *Not heard* notice was checked through `RoutingPanel` in a test, not seen on the owner's screen.

- **What.** **E47a**: split the Windows router's capability into `canList` and `canMoveEndpoint`, so without `FLUB_ENABLE_UNDOCUMENTED_ROUTING` Automatic resolves to process capture (Windows build 20348+) or to Disabled with an explanation; add an "unsupported router" test and a red "No apps are being processed" state. Then: block a process capture whose app renders to the device Flubsound Pro outputs to, with an honest fix (open `ms-settings:apps-volume`, list spare endpoints) and an amber "original also audible" badge. A route journal written atomically before every move, restored at startup (including clearing WirePlumber restore-stream entries), with a simulated-crash test.
- **Why.** Every endpoint move fails in Automatic mode today. The working capture path comb-filters music and smears footstep transients by playing dry and wet about 12.75 ms apart (notches at about 78 Hz spacing). A crash can leave games silent in `flubsound_game`, the known Sonar and Wave Link failure.
- **Expected benefit.** A correctness fix confirmed in code: Automatic is the default, `isSupported()` returns true, `setAppEndpoint` fails without the opt-in define, and `AppRouting::getEffectiveMethod` prefers EndpointRouting whenever `isSupported()`. After the fix, most Windows users get an honest "not available, here is why" rather than new processed audio, which is still better than the current failure modes. On Linux the journal fixes a severe case: WirePlumber remembers the move and routes are undone only in `AppRouting::shutdown`.
- **Approach.** `isEndpointRoutingSupported` uses `canMoveEndpoint`. Same-endpoint guard compares the session's `currentEndpointId` with the engine output's MMDevice ID. Treat an `ISimpleAudioVolume` session-mute trick as a gated experiment: ship only if a runtime self-test shows loopback capture survives the mute. Journal keyed on pid + start time + exe path, covering Linux pactl/metadata moves, the undocumented Windows adapter and later [E46](#e46) endpoints.
- **Depends on.** Nothing for E47a. The in-flight auto-profile switching must use the corrected effective method. [E48](#e48)'s headless PipeWire CI hosts the Linux crash test.
- **Done when.** With an unsupported router, Automatic gives ProcessCapture (fake build ≥ 20348) or Disabled with a reason, never EndpointRouting. The red state appears with zero live routes. A capture on the output's endpoint is blocked; another endpoint is allowed. Simulated crash (TerminateProcess / SIGKILL) and restart restore the previous endpoint. Linux: a `pw-play` stream moved to `flubsound_game`, `kill -9`, restart: the stream returns to the default sink and WirePlumber does not re-apply the route.
- **Evidence.** [PlatformServices_win.cpp][PSW] `WinAppAudioRouter::isSupported()` (~L1071), `setAppEndpoint` `#else` branch; [AppRouting.cpp][AR] `getEffectiveMethod` (~L93–107), restore only on clean shutdown (~L60–83); [01 §4.6][01-46]; [PlatformServices_linux.cpp][PSL] restore-stream comment (~L2040); [06 §8][06-8]; [08 D7][08-D7].

<a id="e51"></a>
#### E51 · Device-handling robustness: feedback-loop guard, stable endpoint IDs, auto-recovery, safe fallback profile, visible errors

*Stability & platform · benefit 4 · difficulty Medium · Phase A 4–6 days; total 4.5–6 engineer-weeks · Quick win (Phase A)*

- **Status (wave B2, host side).** Done in `AudioEngineHost`: the loopback-pair guard (`isLoopbackPair`, exact partner pairs; checked at every device start, including JUCE's own `selectDefaultDeviceOnFailure` fallback; per-pair `allowLoopbackPair()` override; only while the device input feeds a strip) holds the output at silence from the first sample (10 ms ramp if tripped while running) and freezes the engine, so the governor and AutoLevel see nothing of the loop; every device start builds a fresh engine (protection state reset on device change); `getDeviceSafetyState()` / `onDeviceSafetyChanged` carry the banner text for a loopback pair, a device error (`audioDeviceError`) or a failed open, cleared by the next successful start; `getLatencyInfo().valid` is false while offline, stopped or guarded. Fake-backend test of the exact case (`tests/app/test_app_device_safety.cpp`: preferred output removed, input "CABLE Output", default "CABLE Input"): output peak over the 200 looped blocks 0 (before: the engine played into the loop), banner kind LoopbackPair, governor scale 1 → 1 after the headset returns. Open: the UI banner with Retry / Choose output / Open sound settings and the override in Settings (the header's DEVICE warning and "--" readout are wired, app side below), explicit selection instead of `initialise(..., true)`, and the later phases.
- **Status (wave B2, app side).** `EngineController` forwards `onDeviceSafetyChanged` as `Change::Device` and exposes `getDeviceSafetyState()`; the header's bottom line turns into a hot DEVICE `muted` / `error` warning with the host's message in its tooltip, and a click opens Settings (`HeaderBar::formatCpuReadout` / `describeDeviceSafety`; tested with a BlackHole pair in `tests/app/test_app_overload.cpp`). Open: the full banner with Retry / Choose output / Open sound settings and the per-pair override in Settings.
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2, app).** Phase A's banner done in `ui/NoticeBanners.*` (`DeviceErrorBanner`), `MainComponent` and `EngineController::retryDevice()`. Before: a device problem showed only as the header's DEVICE `muted` / `error` line with the message in a tooltip. After: a banner under the header while the state is not None (it cannot be dismissed), headline *Output muted: feedback loop* or *Audio device error* plus the host's message, and **Retry** (a device error re-opens the device from the saved state; a loopback pair is checked again with the current device names), **Choose output** (Settings › Audio) and **Sound settings** (`AppRouting::openSystemRoutingSettings`). Tested in `tests/app/test_app_ui_status.cpp`: a device error shows and Retry clears it (headless: dismissed); a CABLE Output / CABLE Input pair feeding the Game strip shows *Output muted*, Retry keeps it while the input still feeds a strip and clears it once it does not; the whole window lays the banner above the notice bar. Screenshots: `--state device-error`, `--state loopback`. Open: the per-pair override in Settings (`allowLoopbackPair` has no UI), explicit selection instead of `initialise (..., true)`, and the later phases.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, app).** The per-pair override has its UI in `ui/SettingsDialog.*` (Settings › Audio), `engine/EngineController.*` and `settings/AppSettings.*` ([06 §6.11](06-gui.md#611-settingsdialog)). Before: `AudioEngineHost::allowLoopbackPair` existed with no UI and nothing persisted, so a deliberate cable monitor was muted at every start. After: a **FEEDBACK-LOOP GUARD** box says what the guard muted (*Output muted: "CABLE Input …" plays back into the input "CABLE Output …"*) or what it does; **Allow this pair** allows the muted pair (or current devices that look like a pair and are not allowed yet), one row per allowed pair has **Remove**. `EngineController::setLoopbackPairAllowed` persists the pairs (`device.allowedLoopbackPairs`) and applies them to the host at once and before every device start; names compare ignoring case. Tested (`tests/app/test_app_night_loopback.cpp`): the CABLE Output / CABLE Input pair feeding the Game strip is muted, allowed → the banner clears and the guard releases at once, still allowed after a restart, another pair (BlackHole) still guarded, removed → muted again at once; blank entries are dropped from the file; the screenshot driver has `--state settings-audio`. Open: explicit device selection instead of `initialise (..., true)`, and the later phases (stable endpoint IDs, hot-plug listeners, the safe-speaker profile, device-lab runs).
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, Windows lane).** Partially done: explicit device selection, stable endpoint identities, event-driven hot-plug and sleep / resume, and the safe-speaker profile, in `AudioEngineHost.*` (DEVICE SELECTION AND RECOVERY), `PlatformServices.h` / `_win.cpp` / `_common.cpp` (`AudioDeviceWatcher`, `OutputEndpointIdentity`) and `NoticeBanners.*` ([06 §6.2a](06-gui.md#62a-deviceerrorbanner-and-noticebar--what-needs-attention), [10 §3](10-headset-compatibility.md#3-what-flubsound-does-automatically)). *Explicit selection:* `openDevice` calls `initialise (..., false)`; when the saved output is missing or fails to open, or JUCE fell back after an unplug, `selectOutput` takes the chosen output, else the chosen one recognised (the same endpoint id: a rename; the same vendor / product id from the adapter's device path and the same name without Windows' `2- ` instance number: another USB port), else the system default unless it is virtual or the input's loopback partner, else the first such safe output, else none (the current device stays and the loopback guard keeps it silent). Before: saved headset missing, system default CABLE Input → JUCE opened CABLE Input and the guard muted it (silence); after: the speakers play (fake JUCE backend). With nothing chosen the output follows the system default, never into a cable. The choice is JUCE's explicit setup (never a fallback) plus its identity (`flubOutputEndpointId`, `flubOutputHardwareId` in the saved device state through `AudioEngineHost::createDeviceStateXml`, which `EngineController::persistDeviceState` uses now; older settings carry the name only and the identity is filled in once the device is seen). *Hot-plug:* the Turtle Beach dongle unplugged → speakers with the safe profile → plugged into another port as `Headset Earphone (2- Stealth 700 Gen 2)` with a new endpoint id → recognised and re-opened (1 device open), profile off; before, the name-based preferred-output tracker never matched the new name and stayed on the fallback. Windows events come from `IMMNotificationClient` (added, removed, state, default output, friendly-name change); the host re-selects at once and again 0.5 s later, and does not rescan JUCE's WASAPI types itself (they would then miss the change). *Sleep / resume:* `PowerRegisterSuspendResumeNotification` (resolved from powrprof.dll); 1.5 s after a resume the output is re-opened unless callbacks have run (a dead device re-opened once, a running one left alone); a device error takes the same path. An output that fails to open (exclusive mode) is skipped, the next safe output plays, and it is retried with backoff (1 → 16 s, 8 tries, then the device error banner until the next device event). *Safe speaker profile:* on an unplanned fallback to anything not known to be headphones (the endpoint's form factor, else its name), every strip's virtualiser and bass engine are bypassed (`ProcessingChain::setAuditionBypass`, their click-free fades, nothing stored) and the output is trimmed by 6 dB over ≤ 50 ms (output peak 0.25 → 0.1253, −6.0 dB; no allocation or lock in the callback); `DeviceSafetyState::outputFallback` shows the banner in amber naming both devices. Tests: `tests/app/test_app_device_selection.cpp`, 8 cases, 1.6 s; the Windows code passes MinGW-w64 `-fsyntax-only` with the warning flags as errors and is not yet run on Windows. Open: a real Windows PC (the dongle on two ports, sleep / resume, [10 §5][10-5] test 5) and the device-lab run of 20 power cycles; DirectSound and ASIO devices are matched by name only; Linux and macOS have no watcher (JUCE's notifications and EngineController's 5 s rescan remain; the selection and the safe profile apply, and with no form factor known the profile goes on for any fallback whose name does not say headphones); the safe profile bypasses the bass engine rather than capping it at +3 dB, and a preset's EQ low shelf stays; the per-output device correction and EngineController's preferred-output tracker are still keyed by name; following the system default has no setting of its own (it applies while nothing is chosen).
- **Status (p3b4, remainder).** Partially done: the three software rows of the remainder are done; the real-PC and device-lab rows stay gated. (1) *Safe-profile bass cap:* `ProcessingChain::setSafeSpeakerBassCapDb` (core; one atomic, `FLUB_NONBLOCKING` and in `tests/test_rtsan.cpp`'s `static_assert`s; carried by `adoptGovernorState`) scales the bass engine's `bass.boost` and the parametric EQ's positive low shelves and positive bells at or below 150 Hz down together until their sum is at most the cap ([03 §14.4](03-dsp-design.md#144-gaming-mode-macros-macro-15)). `AudioEngineHost`'s safe speaker profile uses it at `kSafeSpeakerBassCapDb` = +3 dB instead of bypassing the bass engine (the virtualiser bypass and the −6 dB trim stay); the banner says *virtualiser off, bass at most +3 dB, -6 dB*. Fake-device fallback to speakers (`tests/app/test_app_device_safety.cpp`, the Music strip voiced +9 dB boost, +6 dB EQ low shelf, +4 dB bell at 60 Hz; 40 Hz re 1 kHz): headset render +17.3 dB; fallback with the bass engine bypassed (batch 2's profile) +9.3 dB, because the EQ lift stayed → +2.74 dB with the bass engine running; 1 kHz −6.00 dB (the trim); back on the headset the full +17.3 dB. Core (`tests/test_safe_speaker_cap.cpp`): +17.3 → +2.75 dB, a preset within the cap bit-identical, Bass Head's applied boost 7.9 → 3.0 dB, engaging and releasing the cap mid-programme adds no sample step above the steady programme's. `BassEngine.cpp` is unchanged: the chain writes the capped boost into the effective values and the engine's own smoothing takes it. (2) *Endpoint identity keys:* [E15](#e15)'s correction (`AppSettings::findDeviceCorrection` / `setDeviceCorrection` / `removeDeviceCorrection` by `platform::OutputEndpointIdentity`; `DeviceCorrectionEntry::endpointId` / `hardwareId` beside the name; matched like [E16](#e16)'s `DeviceEndpointEntry`: the same endpoint id, else the same hardware id and name without Windows' "2- ", else that name) and the preferred-output tracker (`EngineController::trackPreferredOutput` stores the host's identity-keyed choice, `AppSettings::getPreferredOutput` with `device.preferredOutputId` / `device.preferredOutputHardwareId`, and no longer switches devices by name, which fought the host's selection; while the choice is missing it rescans non-WASAPI device types every 5 s and asks the host to select again). A curve saved on *Headset Earphone (Stealth 700 Gen 2)* is applied after a re-plug as *Headset Earphone (2- Stealth 700 Gen 2)* with a new endpoint id (before: the name did not match and the headset played uncorrected), not for another model under that name, and by the name without the instance number where the platform lists no ids; a settings file from before E51 (`<ENDPOINT id="<device name>">`) loads, applies after the re-plug and gains the ids in place. (3) *Settings › Audio **Follow the system default output*** (`device.followSystemDefault`, off by default; `EngineController::setFollowSystemDefaultOutput`, `AudioEngineHost::setFollowSystemDefault`, applied before the device opens): the system default plays whatever was chosen and is followed when it moves, never into a virtual cable or the input's loopback partner, with no fallback banner and no safe speaker profile; the choice is kept for when it is off, and choosing another output in the selector ends following (`onFollowSystemDefaultChanged`, persisted). Tests: five new cases in `tests/app/test_app_device_safety.cpp` (0.01–0.15 s each) and three in `tests/test_safe_speaker_cap.cpp` (0.25 s together); `test_app_device_selection.cpp`'s profile check now expects the cap instead of the bass bypass; all 892 core and 226 app cases pass, plain and under Xvfb. No golden or baseline entry moves (the cap is off unless the host falls back to speakers; no parameter; render diff 125 / 125 within 0.1 dB). Open: the owner's Windows 11 run (the dongle on two ports with its correction following it, sleep / resume; [10 §5][10-5] test 5) and the device lab's 20 power cycles; the tracker with a real device runs only in the app (the tests drive the host on a fake backend and the headless controller); choosing the output that is already the choice while following does not end following (JUCE reports no change; the row switches it off); the loudness contour, Warmth and the dynamic EQ's static gains are not counted in the cap, and a freshly built engine reaches the cap through the modules' smoothing on its first blocks.
- **What.** Phase A: never let the output become the loopback partner of the current input (CABLE Input/Output, Voicemeeter In/Out and AUX, BlackHole, `flubsound_*` and `.monitor`); ramp the output to silence, reset protection state, and show a banner that surfaces `getLastDeviceError()` with Retry, Choose output and Open sound settings; show "--" instead of a stale latency while offline. Later: track the preferred output by stable ID, "follow system default (excluding virtual devices)", event-driven hot-plug (`IMMNotificationClient`, CoreAudio and PipeWire listeners) with backoff and sleep/resume handling, and a safe-speaker profile (virtualiser off, bass ≤ +3 dB, −6 dB trim) on unplanned fallback to speakers.
- **Why.** In the Windows cable setup the system default output *is* CABLE Input. When a wireless headset drops, JUCE's `audioDeviceListChanged` re-initialises with `selectDefaultDeviceOnFailure = true`, so the output becomes CABLE Input while the input is still CABLE Output: a closed loop through Boost. Errors show "offline" next to a fake 5.4 ms latency.
- **Expected benefit.** Affects most wireless-headset gamers. The claim that the loop "plays at −1 dBTP when the headset returns" is unproven, but the demonstrated harms are: anything recording CABLE Output (OBS, Discord) captures a howl; governor, AutoLevel and overload watchdog accumulate step-downs during the loop, leaving the returning headset ducked; on non-cable setups, headphone voicing (virtualiser, bass) is sent to laptop speakers.
- **Approach.** `isLoopbackPair(input, output)` from a pair table extending `looksLikeLoopbackDevice`, checked in the device-manager change callback and again after JUCE's own `initialiseFromXML(..., true)`. Stronger: pass `selectDefaultDeviceOnFailure = false` in `openDevice` and select explicitly (preferred device, else default excluding virtual/loopback devices, else silence + banner), which also covers the Linux `flubsound_*` default-sink case. Stable IDs share [E52](#e52)'s settings migration. Provide an override for false positives.
- **Depends on.** Phase A: nothing. The in-flight engine swap for click-free switches. Real Windows validation needs [E53](#e53) step 0 and [10 §5][10-5] test 5.
- **Done when.** Fake-device test of the exact real-world case (preferred output removed; input "CABLE Output"; default "CABLE Input"): output silent within one ramp, banner text shown, governor and AutoLevel state unchanged when the headset returns. Rename, callback error, exclusive-mode busy and fallback-to-speakers cases. Device lab: 20 power cycles of a 2.4 GHz headset with OBS recording CABLE Output show no howl and the returning level within 0.5 dB.
- **Evidence.** [AudioEngineHost.cpp][AEH] `openDevice()` `initialise(..., true)`; [EngineController.cpp][EC] `trackPreferredOutput` (name strings, 5 s rescan), `lastDeviceError` with no retry or UI consumer; [HeaderBar.cpp][HB] "offline"; [FlubsoundApplication.cpp][FA] (prints the error to stdout only); [08 D3][08-D3].

<a id="e56"></a>
#### E56 · In-game control surface and automation API: correct hotkey target, OSD, more actions, local API, Stream Deck and MIDI

*UX & workflow · benefit 4 · difficulty Medium · Phase A 3–5 days; total 7–9 engineer-weeks · Quick win (Phase A)*

- **Status (wave B2).** Phase A done in `AppSettings.*` (`hotkeyStrip`, five new actions), `EngineController.*` (`getHotkeyStrip`, `setFocus`, `setNight`, `setChatMix`, `setStripBypassed`), `HotkeyManager.cpp` and Settings › Hotkeys (*Hotkeys act on*). Hotkeys go to the active auto profile's strip, else the chosen strip (default Game): with Music selected in the window, Boost+ changed Music's Boost → Game's only, feedback "Game: Boost 60%". Focus: Macro 1 → 1.0 and back (both banks, latched, Gaming mode only). ChatMix: ±1.2 dB per press on Game and Chat in opposite directions, ±6 dB at the ends, no other strip. Night (the Night Mode Gaming dynamics, latched) and a per-strip bypass. 6 → 11 actions, all with distinct Ctrl+Alt defaults, none a momentary (hold) action (`tests/app/test_app_hotkeys.cpp`). Open: the OSD, PresentMon, the local API, MIDI / Stream Deck and the later phases.
- **Status (p3b5, OSD, forwarding and `ctl`).** Partially done: the on-screen display, command-line forwarding and `flubsound-cli ctl` are done and tested here; the PresentMon row stays gated on the owner's Windows PC (borderless CS2 / Apex with PresentMon, [E53](#e53) step 0), and the local JSON-RPC API, MIDI / OSC and the Stream Deck plug-in are later units. *OSD* (`app/Source/ui/Osd.*`, [06 §7.2](06-gui.md#72-global-hotkeys)): before, hotkey feedback was only a tray bubble (hidden by Focus Assist; JUCE's tray notification is "Not implemented" on Linux and macOS); after, every hotkey (and `ctl`) action shows its feedback at the top centre of the primary display ("GAME" / "Boost 60%", a level bar for Boost) for 1.2 s, faded out over 0.3 s. The window is a JUCE top-level window with `windowIsTemporary | windowIgnoresMouseClicks` (X11: override-redirect), always on top, never wanting focus, shown with `toFront (false)`; on Windows the HWND also gets `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE | WS_EX_TRANSPARENT | WS_EX_TOOLWINDOW` (JUCE adds `WS_EX_LAYERED` for the translucent window) and `HWND_TOPMOST`. Under exclusive fullscreen (`SHQueryUserNotificationState` = `QUNS_RUNNING_D3D_FULL_SCREEN`) no window is shown; the earcon option (`osd.earcon` off / fullscreen / always, default off) plays two 60 ms blips at −24 dBFS through `OsdEarconVoice`, a second `AudioDeviceManager` callback on Flubsound's own output after the master limiter (never the system sound, which can be a game or stream mix; not while the device guard holds the output silent; registered on the first earcon only). Tournament mode ([E55](#e55)) shows nothing and takes a message on screen away on the `Change::Settings` that turns it on; with the display off (`osd.enabled`) or held by Tournament mode the feedback goes to the tray bubble as before. *Forwarding and `ctl`* (`tools/flubsound-cli/Ctl.*`, `app/Source/shell/RemoteControl.*`, `FlubsoundApplication.*`, `HotkeyManager::perform (action, strip)`): before, `anotherInstanceStarted` ignored the command line and, on Linux, where JUCE's broadcast is not implemented, a second start did nothing at all; after, `flubsound-cli ctl <action> [strip] [value]` and `FlubsoundPro --ctl …` send one line over a per-user AF_UNIX socket (Linux `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, macOS `$TMPDIR`, Windows `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Flubsound`; the POSIX folder must be the user's and not writable by others, a peer of another uid is refused, nothing listens on the network) and print the reply. The actions are the 11 hotkey actions under their `HotkeyAction` names (the hotkey's own code, feedback and display), `Boost [strip] <0-100>`, `LoadPreset [strip] <uuid>`, `Show`, `Osd on|off` and `Earcon …`; exit codes 0 ok, 1 not applied, 2 usage (an unknown action is refused before anything is sent; an unknown strip or value by the app), 3 not running. A second plain start on Linux sends `Show`. Tests: `tests/app/test_app_osd.cpp` (5 cases, 0.5 s under Xvfb): the hold and fade on a fake clock, no window and the earcon in a faked exclusive fullscreen, hidden in and taken away by Tournament mode, the earcon's render (peak 0.063, 140 ms, 0 allocations and 0 locks in the callback), and under Xvfb the real window's style flags with a focused window keeping the X input focus and JUCE's keyboard focus while the display shows and fades (a mutation to `toFront (true)` fails three of its checks); `tests/app/test_app_ctl.cpp` (2 cases, 0.2 s): with the window on Music and the hotkey strip Chat, `ctl BoostUp Game` prints "Game: Boost 60%" and moves Game's Boost 0.5 → 0.6 and no other strip's, the OSD shows it, `Explode` / an unknown strip / Boost 150 exit 2 with nothing changed, a stopped server exits 3, `LoadPreset Game <uuid>` loads on Game only, Focus in Music mode exits 1 with the hotkey's text, and every hotkey action is a `ctl` action with the same id; `tests/test_cli_ctl.cpp` (4 cases, 0.2 s): argument rules, the wire format, client against server, a stale socket file replaced, a group-writable folder refused. End to end under Xvfb with the built app: `ctl BoostUp Game` → "Game: Boost 30%", "40%"; `FlubsoundPro --ctl BoostDown Game` → "Game: Boost 30%", exit 0; after quitting, exit 3. The Windows code (the socket in `Ctl.cpp`; the OSD's style and fullscreen calls, with the style constant `static_assert`ed against the SDK's `WS_EX_*`) passes MinGW-w64 `-fsyntax-only` with the warning flags as errors and has not run on Windows; macOS code is checked by reading only. No DSP, parameter or preset change: no golden or render-baseline entry moves. Open: PresentMon (gated, above); a Settings row for `osd.enabled` / `osd.earcon` (Settings is not this unit's file; today `ctl Osd` / `ctl Earcon` set them), OSD position and size, and a `--state osd` screenshot; on X11 the override-redirect window takes (and ignores) clicks inside its rectangle while it shows (no input shape), and a `SIGKILL`ed app leaves its socket file, which the next start replaces.
- **Status (owner-verified, 2026-10-06, Turtle Beach Stealth 600PC Gen 3, USB dongle, Windows 11).** With per-app capture running (Microsoft Edge on the Music strip, its own output moved to Digital Audio (S/PDIF)), moving the dongle to another USB port: the sound came back by itself, with no action in Flubsound; switching the headset off and on again: the same; sleep and wake with Edge playing: the processed sound came back by itself.
- **Status (CI, 2026-10-06, first macOS app run).** On the GitHub macOS runner the OSD's window reports keyboard focus after it is shown, although it is created without keyboard focus and as a temporary, click-through window; the Windows and Linux runs keep the focus on the window that had it. Open: check on a real Mac whether the OSD takes key-window status (it must not leave a fullscreen game); the test prints the macOS reading instead of asserting it.
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07, R4.4 hotkey conflicts).** Done here, not yet on CI. Before: the owner's log said at every start "Bypass hotkey strip (Ctrl+Alt+B) could not be registered: another application may already use it …", 10 of 11 hotkeys registered, and only the log and a grey-amber row on Settings › Hotkeys said so; two actions on one chord also failed as "in use". Probe on the owner's PC (a throw-away program that registers each chord from its own process and releases it at once; nothing else touched): Ctrl+Alt+B and Ctrl+Alt+R taken by another running program, every other default and all 43 chords of the new alternative lists (the defaults included) free; the owner of Ctrl+Alt+B cannot be named (Windows has no call for it; the readable settings and logs of the running candidates did not name it). After: Bypass hotkey strip defaults to **Ctrl+Alt+Shift+B** (free there, so the probe predicts 11 of 11 registered; a saved chord is kept, and the owner's settings file holds no hotkey keys, so the new default applies at the next start); `Conflict` and `Invalid` are named before anything is sent; a failing row is red with the reason and **Pick a free one** (`HotkeyManager::pickFreeChord` over `AppSettings::getAlternativeHotkeys`, e.g. Bypass: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+B → Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Y → Ctrl+Alt+Y); the notice under the header names each failure once per action and chord (`hotkeys.announced`) with **Fix in Settings**, and the tray menu has *N hotkeys not active - fix…*; chords are recorded by pressing them (`ui/HotkeyCapture.*`, Flubsound's own hotkeys paused meanwhile, Esc / Backspace / reset, a right click types Win / Super chords). Tests: 8 new cases in `tests/app/test_app_hotkeys.cpp` (one of them the real Windows `RegisterHotKey` against a second registrant: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F13 refused → *In use by another app* → picked Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F14) and 2 in `tests/app/test_app_tray.cpp` (every tray menu item acts as listed; the icon images), 0.0002 – 0.29 s each alone on the owner's PC (the Settings page case up to 1.45 s while other builds loaded the machine); flub_tests and flub_app_tests (320 / 320) pass under ctest. No sound change (no golden or render baseline moved). Open: the app's own start on the owner's PC (expect no hotkey line in the log), recording with a real keyboard, the Linux / macOS builds of the new code on CI.
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07, R4.4 review fixes).** Done here, not yet on CI. (1) **Recording and registration** (the review's major finding): the reset button and the *Enable* switch take no keyboard focus, so clicking one while a chord was recording re-registered every hotkey with the field still recording. Before (the reviewer's probe): after the reset click `capturing=1 suspended=0 registered=11`; Flubsound's own chords ran their actions instead of reaching the recorder; a chord pressed then (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+K) was saved but the service still held Ctrl+Alt+Shift+B, while the row said "Registered" and the summary "All shortcuts are registered". After: every control on the page (reset, the switch, *Pick a free one*, typed entry) ends a recording first (`HotkeysPage::stopRecording`), and `HotkeyManager::setSuspended (false)` always registers from the settings; the same sequence gives `capturing=0 suspended=0 registered=11` after the reset, and the chord recorded next is the one registered with the service (tested, also for the switch, the pick and typed entry). (2) **Chords other applications use**: before, the recorder accepted any valid chord, so one stray click and a reflex Alt+F4 (which JUCE hands to the focused field), Alt+Space, F5 or Ctrl+C became a saved global hotkey. After: `HotkeyManager::commonShortcutProblem` refuses Alt+F4, Alt+Space, Ctrl+Alt+Delete, Super+Space / L / D / Q and F1–F12 alone or with Shift (recorded or typed), and, when recorded, one modifier besides Shift (Ctrl, Alt; Cmd on macOS) with a letter, digit, navigation key or F1–F12 (*Not taken: Ctrl+C is a shortcut other applications use …*); typed, such an application chord is taken on purpose. Saved chords are never checked, so nothing that works today stops working. (3) **Alternatives**: before, 11 of the 43 chords of the lists were Ctrl+Alt+letter (AltGr+letter on Windows: Ctrl+Alt+E is the euro sign on German / French / Italian / Spanish layouts, C / L / O Polish letters). After: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F1 … F11 instead (one per action), still 43 chords, every one after the default with Ctrl+Alt+Shift; a second register-and-release probe on the owner's PC: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F1 – F12 and all Ctrl+Alt+Shift letter alternatives free, Ctrl+Alt+B still taken. (4) The row text "In use by another app" is now "Could not register" (the same status also means a chord the system reserves, no hotkey window, or a stopped portal), and the sentence *… could not be registered (another application may hold it, or the system reserves it)*. (5) **The notice**: before, a later failure replaced it with only the new one, and a fixed one stayed listed until all worked. After: it lists every hotkey still failing, the new ones first, refreshed in place at every status change (`NoticeBar::refresh`); a notice the user dismissed is not brought back without a new failure. (6) **Keyboard and layouts**: Space now starts recording (the docs said so, JUCE's button only handles Return); Shift+F10 and a screen reader's *show menu* open typed entry, *press* records; on Windows a character key is mapped through `VkKeyScanW` to its letter / digit key (AZERTY's '&' → 1; tested with a fake AZERTY layout, and the real lookup on this PC: 'a' → A, ',' and '!' → none). Not changed, with reasons: macOS / Linux Shift+digit chords stay typed (JUCE gives no key code there); Wayland keeps the suspension while recording: the portal's shortcuts are handled by the compositor (KWin, Mutter) before the focused window, so without it Flubsound's own chords would still not reach the recorder, and the back-end coalesces the unbind / rebind burst and re-binds known ids without asking (its design notes; not seen on a real desktop). Tests: a new case (*… end a recording first, so a chord recorded afterwards is registered; chords other applications use are refused*, 0.39 s alone), the notice case rewritten (it now opens the real Settings window on the Hotkeys page where a display exists and waits for the page's 4 Hz poll, and checks the tray bubble's text; 0.88 s alone), the recorder case waits for the 4 Hz poll instead of calling `showPage` (0.44 s), the key-press case covers the layout mapping and `commonShortcutProblem` (< 0.001 s). Full ctest in the worktree (MSVC 19.51, Release, warnings as errors): flub_tests passed (271 s); flub_app_tests 320 / 321 in that run, the one failure the E47 route-journal kill test (`REQUIRE (entries.size() == 1)`, a child-process timing race in code this change does not touch), which then passed 5 / 5 alone and in a second `ctest -R flub_app_tests` run with all 321 cases (33 s). The `settings-hotkeys` (720 x 580) and `hotkey-notice` (1280 x 820) screenshot states were rendered again with a temporary user data folder: the new texts fit. No sound change (no golden or render baseline moved). Open: the same as above, plus a check by the owner that Space / Shift+F10 / a screen reader reach the field in the running app.
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07, the OSD non-activating on macOS and click-through on X11; verified on CI).** Done in `app/Source/ui/OsdNative.h` (new), `OsdNative_mac.mm` (new, Objective-C++, macOS only), `OsdNative_linux.cpp` (new, X11 only) and `Osd.*` ([06 §7.2](06-gui.md#72-global-hotkeys)). *Cause* (read from JUCE's source, not measured on its own: the new flag never ran on CI without the panel, so which of the two fixed the runner is not separated): the OSD's peer had no `windowIgnoresKeyPresses`, so JUCE's own window class answered `canBecomeKeyWindow` and its view `acceptsFirstResponder` with YES, and on the macOS runner the OSD took JUCE's focused peer from the window that had it: run [37433326507](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37433326507) failed 6 focus checks (the focus holder lost the focus and the OSD's peer had it), run [37444883054](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37444883054) `CHECK (! peer->isFocused())`; since `51abe53` the test only printed it. *Fix:* the window's flags gain `windowIgnoresKeyPresses` (as JUCE's tooltip window; no effect on Windows; X11 ignores `WM_TAKE_FOCUS`). On macOS the peer is attached to the content view of a panel of its own: a borderless `NSPanel` with `NSWindowStyleMaskNonactivatingPanel`, `canBecomeKeyWindow` / `canBecomeMainWindow` NO, `becomesKeyOnlyIfNeeded`, `ignoresMouseEvents`, collection behaviour `CanJoinAllSpaces | FullScreenAuxiliary | Stationary | IgnoresCycle` (a regular app's window does not show in another app's fullscreen Space), level `NSPopUpMenuWindowLevel` (101, the level JUCE gave the old window), `hidesOnDeactivate` NO (NSPanel's default would hide it whenever a game is the active app), ordered in with `orderFrontRegardless` and out with the message; its frame is the top-centre place in points (JUCE's logical pixels × the UI scale, flipped to Cocoa's bottom-left origin) and the component fills it. *X11:* JUCE's `windowIgnoresMouseClicks` only leaves the mouse events out of the window's event mask, so a click inside the display went to it (and nowhere) instead of the window below; the window now gets an empty input shape (SHAPE 1.1 `ShapeInput`, libXext loaded at run time, through JUCE's own display connection): input rectangles 1 → 0 under Xvfb (an ordinary JUCE window measured beside it keeps 1). *Test* (`tests/app/test_app_osd.cpp`, the display case): the macOS print is gone and the focus assertions run on every OS again (the focus holder keeps JUCE's focus and the keyboard focus, the OSD's peer never has it, while the display shows and after the fade); new checks: `windowIgnoresKeyPresses`, the display's place at the top centre of the primary display (±1 px; on macOS through the panel's flipped frame), on macOS the panel (an `NSPanel`, non-activating, cannot become key or main, not key, no view as first responder, ignores the mouse, joins all Spaces and fullscreen apps, not hidden on deactivate, visible, level 101, ordered out after the fade) and an unchanged active app and key window, on X11 0 input rectangles; on Windows a refused foreground now prints "(skipped: ...)" instead of passing silently. *CI* (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`): the Linux app job ran `flub_app_tests` without a display, so this case returned at once there; it now runs under Xvfb, and every app job prints the whole test output (`ctest -V`), so measurements, known gaps and skips show in green runs. Run [37575468250](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37575468250) (the panel; all 11 jobs green): macOS 310/310 cases, "OSD panel level 101, key no; this app active no, key window none, unchanged by the OSD"; Ubuntu under Xvfb 319/319; Windows 310/310. Run [37577909953](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37577909953) (with the X11 input shape; all 11 jobs green): Ubuntu "X11 input shape: OSD 0 rectangles, an ordinary window 1", 319/319; macOS 310/310, the same panel line; Windows 310/310, the case run in full (not skipped). Both runs were on WIP commits later squashed into `36e2c33` and force-pushed over on `wip/macos-osd` (the same code apart from the route-journal skip wording and the docs); the run on `36e2c33` itself is [37580674924](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37580674924) (all 11 jobs green: macOS 310/310 with the same panel line, Ubuntu under Xvfb 319/319 with "OSD 0 rectangles, an ordinary window 1", Windows 310/310 with the case run in full). The display case takes 0.59 s on the macOS runner, 0.42 s under Xvfb, 0.42 s on the Windows runner and 1.6 s on the owner's PC. Also from the macOS and Ubuntu logs: `PlatformServices_mac.mm`'s hot-key handler compiled with one `-Wdeprecated-anon-enum-enum-conversion` warning (`noErr` and `eventNotHandledErr` are constants of two anonymous enums; in the app and the test target) → 0 (both returned as `OSStatus`), and the Linux route-journal test's skip on the app runner said "this build has no Linux per-app router" although the router is built in and only `pactl` is missing there; it now says so (the `pipewire` job still runs that test). Locally (Windows 11, MSVC 19.51, warnings as errors): build clean, the 6 OSD cases pass (the focus holder got the foreground, so the display case ran in full). No DSP, parameter or preset change: no golden or render-baseline entry moves. What these checks cannot show (review, 2026-10-07): the panel's `isPanel`, cannot-become-key and cannot-become-main readings only read back what `FlubOsdPanel` hard-codes; the unchanged active app and key window compare "not active, no key window" with itself on the runner and `{}` with `{}` on Windows and Linux; and a failed X11 shape query read as 0 rectangles. The checks that can fail on the runner are JUCE's focus assertions, `! viewIsFirstResponder` and the place through the flipped frame (the next Status line makes the other two real). Open: a real Mac with a fullscreen game; a real X11 desktop with a game under the display; Wayland sessions (JUCE runs through XWayland there; not checked).
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07, review fixes: X errors in the shape query, the active app on the macOS runner; verified on CI).** From the review of `36e2c33`. *X11* (`OsdNative_linux.cpp`): the SHAPE request and query now run under a scoped X error trap (as JUCE's own XShm check: sync, a handler that counts this display's errors from the request's serial on and hands any other error to the handler it replaced, sync again). Before, a failed query (BadWindow; the error went to a handler that ignores it) read as 0 input rectangles, the same as click-through (from the code: `count` started at 0 and libXext only writes it from a reply; not run before the fix); after, it returns −1 (unknown), and `setEmptyInputShape` returns false when the server refuses the request. A null rectangle array is not taken as a failure: libXext also returns none for a successful reply with no rectangles (an empty shape), so the trap decides. Measured on CI: the OSD's query, now error-checked, 0 rectangles; an ordinary window 1; a destroyed window −1. *macOS* (`tests/app/AppTestSupport_mac.mm`, new, macOS only): the display case first makes its console process a regular app (`setActivationPolicy: Regular`), asks for activation (`activate`, `activateIgnoringOtherApps:`) and waits up to 0.8 s for the app to be active with the focus holder's window key; then, while the OSD shows and after the fade, it asserts that the app stays active and that window stays key (if macOS refuses, the case prints so and the comparison stays weak). Before, the runner compared "not active, no key window" with itself; after: "this app active yes, key window the focus holder's, unchanged by the OSD". Still only read-backs: the panel's `isPanel`, cannot-become-key and cannot-become-main values (what `FlubOsdPanel` sets); `Osd::getNativeFocus()` is `{}` on Windows and Linux, where JUCE's focus checks (and on Windows the foreground) carry the case. *Test:* the primary display is REQUIREd before its bounds are read. CI run [37585159151](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37585159151) on `3b27287` (all 11 jobs green): macOS app 310/310, the display case 0.77 s (0.59 s before; the activation wait); Ubuntu app under Xvfb 319/319, 0.46 s; Windows app 310/310, run in full, 0.44 s; no compiler warnings in the three app builds (the new `.mm` is built with `-Wall -Wextra -Wshadow -Werror`). Locally (Windows 11, MSVC 19.51, warnings as errors): build clean, the 6 OSD cases pass (the display case in full, 0.69 s alone), full `ctest` passes (flub_tests 307 s, flub_app_tests 35 s). Still open: a real Mac with a fullscreen game (the runner shows that the panel leaves an active app's key window alone, not that it shows over another app's fullscreen Space); a click on the display on a real X11 desktop; Wayland.

- **What.** Phase A: hotkeys target the auto-profile rule's strip (once that lands), otherwise a user-chosen `hotkeyStrip` (default Game), never the GUI selection; new actions Focus (a latched Footsteps override), ChatMix ±, Night and Bypass. Then: a click-through, non-activating OSD fading after about 1.2 s, with earcons for exclusive fullscreen; command-line forwarding to the running instance and `flubsound-cli ctl`; a localhost, token- and Origin-checked JSON-RPC over WebSocket; MIDI/OSC learn; a Stream Deck plug-in.
- **Why.** Boost+ mid-match raises the Music strip if Music was last clicked. Feedback goes to tray toasts that Focus Assist hides and that JUCE does not implement on Linux or macOS ("// xxx Not implemented!"). There is no footstep or ChatMix toggle. Streamers expect Stream Deck and MIDI control (Sonar, Voicemeeter).
- **Expected benefit.** The hotkey fix, Focus, ChatMix and OSD carry the benefit. The strip-explicit overloads already exist (`int strip = -1`), so the target fix is about a day. The API, Stream Deck and MIDI serve streamers, a narrower group (benefit 2–3 on their own).
- **Approach.** `AppSettings.hotkeyStrip`; `HotkeyManager::perform` passes an explicit target; feedback names the strip. Focus is a transient Macro1 override through the smoothed parameter path. ChatMix is one balance value applied to Game and Chat strip gains in MixEngine. On Windows keep momentary (hold) actions off by default: `RegisterHotKey` has no key-up and a `WH_KEYBOARD_LL` hook is anti-cheat-sensitive. OSD with `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE | WS_EX_TRANSPARENT | WS_EX_LAYERED`, measured with PresentMon because a topmost layered window can knock a borderless game out of independent flip. The WebSocket server binds loopback only and checks token and Origin (DNS rebinding, browser CSRF).
- **Depends on.** Phase A: nothing. [E52](#e52) UUIDs for LoadPreset and Stream Deck buttons; [E55](#e55) Tournament mode disables the OSD and hooks; [E53](#e53) step 0 to verify Windows hotkeys; [E54](#e54) to distribute the Stream Deck plug-in.
- **Done when.** GUI selection = Music, `hotkeyStrip` = Game: BoostUp changes only Game's Boost and the feedback names Game. Focus toggles Macro1 to 1 and back. ChatMix moves Game and Chat gains in opposite directions only. PresentMon over borderless CS2 and Apex shows no presentation-mode change or < 1 ms added frame time and no focus loss. API rejects bad token and Origin; 1 h of 30 Hz meter streaming with no audio-thread allocation under RTSan.
- **Evidence.** [HotkeyManager.cpp][HK] `perform` uses `getSelectedStrip()` (~L189–217); [AppSettings.h][AS] `HotkeyAction` (6 actions); [FlubsoundApplication.cpp][FA] `trayIcon->notify`, `anotherInstanceStarted` ignores the command line; JUCE `juce_SystemTrayIcon_linux.cpp` / `_mac.cpp` "Not implemented"; [09 §4][09-4].

<a id="e59"></a>
#### E59 · Sound-quality regression suite with numeric targets (not only safety)

*Stability & platform · benefit 4 · difficulty Medium · slice 1–1.5 weeks; total 6–9 engineer-weeks*

- **Status (Phase 1 batch).** Slice done in `tests/test_known_gaps.cpp` (14 KnownGap and metric tests), `tools/flubsound-cli/OfflineRenderer.*` and `Commands.*` (`render.stats`), `tools/scripts/preset-render-diff.py` and `tests/golden/preset-render-baseline.json`; metric none → every Phase 1 retune judged by a pinned number, `render.stats` limiter GR within 0.1 dB of the hand computation (−3.98 dB). Stats read the chain's `MeterBus` per block instead of a core `ChainStats`. Open: the harmonics statistic in `render.stats` (on `MeterBus::harmonicsDb` since Phase 2 batch 1, [E06](#e06) / [E10](#e10)), IMD, the 3 ms delay meta-test, per-compiler ratchet and `quality_targets.json`, REW cross-check.
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 1).** E59 full partially done (the metric set and CLI analysis the Phase 2 E05 work needs, and the meta-validation Done-when). (a) Definitions in `tools/flubsound-cli/Analysis.*`, shared by the CLI and the tests: sine THD+N, two-tone IMD (products up to 5th order, not harmonics), SMPTE IMD (fHigh ± k fLow, k ≤ 4), multitone MTND (residual after the excited tones), the tone gain track (20 ms Hann / 5 ms) with p95 − p5, dip, lift, time > 1 dB down and its modulation spectrum at k × the kick rate, and the energy centroid; the KnownGap helpers now call them (unchanged values). (b) `flubsound-cli quality [preset / mode / macro / --set options] [--json]` (`Commands.*` `measureQuality`): pinned stimuli (48 kHz, fixed seeds) for THD+N at 40 / 60 / 100 / 1000 Hz, bass-third and SMPTE IMD, 31-tone pink MTND at −24 / −18 / −12 dBFS RMS against output LUFS, a ducking spectrum of 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 kHz probes under 55 Hz kicks (plus the scene's limiter GR), kick onset / body / decay and centroid shift, pink loudness; about 1.3–1.9 s per setting. (c) Meta-validation through a pass-through chain, within ±10 % (Done-when): 1 % cubic → THD+N −40.26 dB (analytic −40.26), bass IMD −39.68 (−39.68), SMPTE −31.66 (−31.66); 6 dB 2 Hz square gain modulation → spread 6.02 dB, 2 Hz component 3.83 dB (4/π × 3.01), 3rd harmonic a third of it, 50 % time down; 3 ms delay → centroid +2.98 ms; noise 40 dB under a multitone → −40.0 dB MTND (`KnownGap metrics: the quality suite reads ...`). (d) `render.stats` gains the bass-harmonics / air-exciter reading (`harmonics.maxDb` / `meanDb`), the governor's time per state and per reason (`governor.statePercent`, `limiterReasonPercent`, `distortionReasonPercent`). (e) It judged [E05](#e05) stage 1 (numbers there). Metric count: 16 KnownGap / metric tests → 19, plus the 6-family suite (about 40 values per setting). Open: `quality_targets.json`, per-profile / per-rate thresholds, the per-compiler ratchet and the nightly matrix, `quality-report.py`, the REW cross-check, the harmonics-generator IMD KnownGap (the suite measures it; no pinned harmonics-generator scene yet), bass-protection onset overshoot, THD+N against 3 / 6 / 9 dB GR, governor drift, sibilance / voice ratio, presence invariance, alias / ultrasonic bounds, LFE level and Music band assertions.
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2).** Targets file, ratchet and report done; the nightly CI matrix is open. (a) `tests/quality_targets.json`: 5 named settings (`flubsound-cli quality` options) and 18 rows (46 once profiles × rates are expanded) from the E05, E10 and E59 Done-whens, each a metric path into `quality --json` with a `<=` / `>=` target, per-profile targets (`byProfile`, e.g. −60 dBc in Low Latency) and per-rate rows (44.1 / 48 / 96 / 192 kHz), tier per-pr or nightly. (b) KNOWN_GAP ratchet: a row with `recorded` values per compiler (gcc and clang recorded; appleclang and msvc fall back to gcc with twice the margin; margins 0.5 dB) fails when it is worse than recorded + margin, and says so when it meets its target; 6 gap rows (kick onset / body and centroid shift at 12 dB drive and at Boost 100, the every-macro alias and ultrasonic rows). `tests/test_cli_quality.cpp` evaluates the per-PR rows (maximizer at 12 dB, Music Boost 100, Warmth 100 at 44.1 kHz) and fails a deliberately regressed render (a 3 ms delay injected: centroid 8.62 → 11.58 ms) on the centroid row. (c) `tools/scripts/quality-report.py` runs every row through the CLI (22 quality runs, 17 s on 4 cores; 46 of 46 pass today), prints met / known gap / REGRESSED / FAIL, and `--update-recorded` re-records the gap rows for the build's compiler (`flubsound-cli version` now prints it). (d) The hygiene family of `quality` at `--rate R` ([E10](#e10)): worst alias of 1 / 5 / 7 / 10 kHz sines, DC of the E10 stimulus, share of the energy ≥ 22 kHz from 88.2 kHz (`Analysis.h` `worstAliasDbc`, `dcDbfs`, `powerShareAboveDb`; meta-validated: an injected −80 dBc line reads −80.0, a −60 dBFS offset −60.0, a 30 kHz tone 40 dB down −40.0). (e) `render.stats`: the governor's scale, state and reasons at the end of the programme (`governor.end`, [E06](#e06)), harmonics and governor states in the text summary, and `--protection off|normal|strict` for process / batch / quality (`tests/test_cli_stats.cpp`). Open: the nightly ~1350-render matrix in CI (the script covers the 46 rows), the REW cross-check, the harmonics-generator IMD KnownGap, and the metrics listed above (bass-protection onset overshoot, THD+N against 3 / 6 / 9 dB GR, governor drift, sibilance / voice ratio, presence invariance, LFE level, Music band assertions).
- **Status (p3b3, by-ear demo pack).** Tooling done: `flubsound-cli demo` pairs the metrics with listening. Each written pair file is read back and measured as `analyze --bands` reads it; `index.txt` gives integrated loudness, LRA and true peak of both sides, the octave-band delta (after − before) and the chain's `render.stats` readouts (limiter, compressor, clipper, Smoothness, governor, Auto Level). Done-when rows met (`tests/test_cli_demo.cpp`, 0.5 s programmes, about 1.2 s): every listed pair written, matched within 0.5 LU unless a level feature, index band deltas equal to `analyze` within 0.1 dB, byte-identical across two runs with 2 and 3 workers; the CI step renders 8 s programmes in about 5 s (71 MB, 16-bit). Examples the pack shows at 10 s: Clarity 100 +1.2 / +1.7 / +1.5 dB at 2 / 4 / 8 kHz, Warmth 100 −2.6 / −4.0 dB at 8 / 16 kHz, the contour at −30 dB +2.5 dB at 31.5 Hz against −5.1 dB at 1 kHz, Startle Guard at 10 LU −13.5 dB at 31.5 Hz on the explosions, Night LRA 3.3 → 6.4 LU on the 10 s game scene (short-programme LRA; worth a listen). No metric, golden or baseline moved. Open: the by-ear judgement itself (the owner, task #29's guide) and E59's rows above.
- **Status (p3b4, presence invariance).** The presence-invariance row is in the suite: *Quality metric (E59): presence invariance over 30 dB ...* (`tests/test_known_gaps.cpp`), the 2.5 – 4 kHz lift over the 200 Hz – 1 kHz lift of pink at −45 against −15 dBFS, protection strength Off, every value pinned: Music Boost 50 1.48 dB with the default Absolute presence, −0.02 dB with [E07](#e07)'s Relative; Gaming Boost 100 + Voice & Score 100 6.00 → 1.46 dB (the rest is the Voice & Score band's fixed threshold, flagged KNOWN_GAP at the 1.5 dB row's edge). The −45 / −12 dBFS rows are E07's two presence cases. No golden, baseline or target moved. Open as listed above (the nightly matrix, REW, the harmonics-generator IMD KnownGap, bass-protection onset overshoot, THD+N against GR, governor drift, sibilance / voice in `quality`, LFE level, Music band assertions); the row is not yet a `flubsound-cli quality` metric in `quality_targets.json`.
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-08, the render-diff baseline on CI).** Done: the all-preset render diff gates CI. The `core` job's gcc leg runs `tools/scripts/preset-render-diff.py --largest 20 --print-moved` against `tests/golden/preset-render-baseline.json` (step *Preset render diff (reference platform)*, after the golden renders, about 30 s, **blocking**): it fails when a value moves by more than 0.1 dB / LU, the latency changes, a percentage moves by more than 1 point or a render is added or missing. The script gains `--largest N` (the N largest differences within tolerance, i.e. the noise floor, with a count of every value that differs at all) and `--print-moved` (each moved render's baseline line as `--update` writes it, so a re-base needs only the CI log); a local run against a deliberately perturbed copy (+0.03 LU, +0.50 dB, one render removed) lists the 0.03 LU in the noise floor, fails on the other two and prints their lines; `--update` writes the same bytes as before the change. Before: CI never read the file, and 48 of its 155 renders were MSVC values re-based by MSVC before → after deltas and never rendered on gcc ([E20](#e20)'s harmonics reserve: the 13 `music` / `tone-kicks` rows of the 7 Impact presets; [E04](#e04) step 5: the 30 rows of the six Quality presets; [E14](#e14)'s Synthwave: its 5 rows). Measured, a non-blocking probe on every `core` leg (run [37722477794](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37722477794), `363350e` on `wip/golden-gcc`; its four `core` legs and `fuzz` green, the other jobs cancelled by the next push): gcc 13.3 (ubuntu-24.04, the reference), clang 18.1.3, MSVC 19.44 (windows-2022) and Apple Clang 15 (macos-14, arm64) each read **0 of 5121 values different** from the committed file, in 0 of 155 renders (the noise-floor table is empty at the file's 0.01 resolution; 28 / 34 / 29 / 29 s); locally MSVC 19.51 also 0 of 5121, two local `--update` runs byte-identical, and `FLUB_GOLDEN_REFERENCE=1 flub_tests "Golden renders"` (the gcc golden renders, 0.05 dB) passes on MSVC 19.51 too. So nothing moved and no row was re-based: the 48 MSVC-derived rows are the gcc values to the last digit (four of them spell a value with a trailing `.0`, `-27.0`, `-47.0` or `0.0`, where `--update` writes `-27`, `-47` or `0`, left by the re-base arithmetic; equal values, left as committed). Decision, blocking: the noise floor is 0.00 dB on five toolchains on x86-64 and arm64 against a 0.1 dB tolerance, and the renders are deterministic (one CLI process per render, fixed seeds, no timing), so the step blocks from `328029e`, like the golden-render step (its first blocking run, [37723453200](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37723453200) on `wip/golden-gcc`: 0 of 5121 again, 33 s; its four `core` legs, `fuzz` and `rtsan` green, the rest cancelled by the next push); every change that moves a factory preset's render now re-records the baseline in the same change (an `--update` on MSVC is expected to pass on gcc; otherwise paste the lines the step prints). Open: the nightly ~1350-render matrix, the REW cross-check and the metrics listed above.
- **What.** A `Quality:` test group and matching CLI scripts with per-profile, per-rate thresholds: two-tone and multi-tone IMD for the harmonics generator; kick alignment (centroid shift < 2 ms, first-10 ms loss < 1 dB); bass-protection onset overshoot; sine THD+N at 40/60/100 Hz against 3/6/9 dB GR; pink-multitone MTND against output LUFS; a tone-under-kick pumping index; governor drift on stationary programme; sibilance/voice ratio; presence invariance over 30 dB; transient onset/body gain at 60 ms spacing; alias, DC and ultrasonic bounds; LFE presence and level; Music-mode band assertions. CLI `--json` gains governor, GR, clipper, saturator and harmonics statistics.
- **Why.** Every test passes (503/503 as recorded in TRACEABILITY at `88c13af`; 519 core cases in the tree at `d096a4d`) while the product holds −21.8 dB MTND, dips a tone under kicks, drops the LFE, turns bass chords into IMD and raises sibilance. Tuning changes are assumed, not measured.
- **Expected benefit.** The precondition for proving any retuning in [E01](#e01)–[E07](#e07), [E19](#e19) and [E21](#e21). The skeptic showed why stimuli and definitions must be pinned: with a 440 Hz tone under 55 Hz kicks, the p95–p5 envelope index is 0.1 dB at Boost 0, 0.4 dB at Boost 50 and 4.1 dB at Boost 100, while max–min is about 13–15 dB, more than half of it *upward* gain between kicks. The earlier "21–22 dB dip" depends on the stimulus, and a "≤ 3 dB" target would nearly pass today on some stimuli and fail badly on others. `DistortionMonitor` already exposes block and smoothed THD and harmonics readings, so part of the telemetry exists.
- **Approach.** `tests/QualityMetrics.h`, `tests/test_quality.cpp`, `tests/quality_targets.json` pinning stimulus and metric definitions; report p95–p5 and max–min with lift and dip separately. Set thresholds from a transparent reference limiter at the same output LUFS plus a margin, not by guessing. A KNOWN_GAP ratchet in `tests/TestFramework.h` with per-compiler baselines and margins ≥ 0.5 dB. `ChainStats` from existing getters with relaxed atomic max/sum accumulators, added to the `test_rtsan.cpp` static asserts. Nightly full matrix (about 1350 renders, 3–6 min), per-PR subset. `tools/scripts/quality-report.py` for tuning sessions. **Slice (Phase 0–1, 1–1.5 weeks):** `render.stats` in `--json`; KNOWN_GAP tests for pumping, 60 Hz THD+N and 7.1 LFE; the five metrics the early retunes (Phase 1, and E04's Tighten removal in Phase 2's carry-in) and E36's first-run defaults are judged by (20/40/80 ms burst lift and step/bed contrast at −14/−24/−40 LUFS for [E19](#e19)'s interim bell; the ambush scene's bed lift and post-event hole for [E21](#e21)'s slice; Punch 100 kick lift at 0–10 ms against 10–30 ms for [E04](#e04); 30 Hz audible-band energy for [E03](#e03); 3 kHz ILD under focus for [E24](#e24)); and a scripted before/after `analyze` of all 24 factory presets on the pinned stimuli, attached to every preset-affecting change until [E52](#e52)'s golden renders gate CI.
- **Depends on.** Nothing. Must land before the bass-IMD, sibilance, pumping and LFE retuning. [E60](#e60) reuses it.
- **Done when.** Meta-validation on a pass-through chain with injected artefacts (1 % cubic, 6 dB 2 Hz square gain modulation, 3 ms delay): each metric reports the injected value within ±10 %. Today's values reproduce with the pinned stimuli. THD+N and IMD cross-checked in REW. `render.stats` GR matches a hand computation. Each later fix flips its KNOWN_GAP, and a deliberately regressed build fails the ratchet.
