- **Evidence.** [test_limiter.cpp][T-lim] (24 cases: ceiling, latency, release, allocation, NaN, block size); `test_maximizer.cpp` (safety and telemetry); [test_bass_engine.cpp][T-bass] (single tone only); `test_clarity.cpp`, `test_dynamic_eq.cpp` (no sibilance test); `test_engine.cpp` (ceiling, finite, RMS); [Protection.h][PRh] `DistortionMonitor` getters; TRACEABILITY sub-gaps R2.8, R2.9, R2.10, R3.2.

<a id="e01"></a>
#### E01 · Fold and calibrate the LFE in every path (downmix, capture FIFO, virtualiser)

*Audio quality · benefit 3 · difficulty Medium · 4–6 engineer-days · Quick win*

- **Status (Phase 1 batch).** Chain and virtualiser done in `core/*/dsp/Bs775Fold.h` (shared `LfeFold`, 4th-order Butterworth kept, see its header), `HeadphoneVirtualizer.*`, `ProcessingChain.*`, `Parameters.*` (`virt.lfe` +6 dB default, range to +16, `virt.lfeFold`) and `PresetIO.*` (schema v2, v1 files keep 0 dB); LFE-only 50 Hz re FL, virt off / on: −240 dBFS (dropped) / −0.03 dB → +6.00 / +5.97 dB. Factory presets are v1 files, so they fold at 0 dB (Tournament Clean 7.1 programme +7.4 LU). Open: the capture-FIFO and test-signal folds (`app/Source`, after round 8), +10 dB after E05, the 5.1 pink ratio test.
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2, host).** Partly done in `AudioEngineHost.*`. Where a surround capture actually becomes stereo in the app today is the host, not the FIFO: a 7.1 capture started on the Game strip and moved to a stereo strip (or kept across a layout change) kept its 8-channel FIFO, and the host read only its first two channels. It now folds it as the chain does (`Bs775Fold` plus an `ActiveChannelDetector` per capture slot, the LFE at the destination strip's `virt.lfe` / `virt.lfeFold`, the surround fold's −3 dB or, for FL / FR-only content, the stereo passthrough per `virt.input` / `virt.ownHrtf`, ramped over 400 ms). Measured on a bypassed Music strip, re the source tone (`tests/app/test_app_host_io.cpp`): LFE-only 50 Hz −∞ (dropped) → +2.97 dB, i.e. +5.99 dB re FL at 50 Hz (the chain's own fold of the same capture: +5.99 dB); FC 1 kHz −∞ → −6.02 dB on both sides; SL −∞ → −6.02 dB on its side; FL alone 0.00 → −3.01 dB (the chain: −3.01); FL = FR on Auto 0.00 → 0.00 dB once detected (2 s, like the chain). No allocation or lock on the audio thread. `DriftCompensatedFifo::convertChunk`'s own BS.775 copy is untouched (its owner's file): it runs only when a capture delivers more channels than it asked for, which the Windows process-loopback capture never does. Open: that copy and `TestSignalGenerator`, +10 dB after E05, the 5.1 pink ratio test.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 1, +10 dB).** Done in `Parameters.cpp` (`virt.lfe` default +6 → +10 dB), `PresetIO.*` (schema 3: a 2 → 3 migration fills an absent `virt.lfe` of a version-2 file with +6 dB, so no saved preset is re-voiced; factory presets are version 1 and keep 0 dB) and `ProcessingChain.cpp` (fold headroom: above +6 dB the LFE of a surround fold arms the maximizer's LF-first limiter at (`virt.lfe` − 6) / 4). Done-when: LFE-only 50 Hz re FL +6.00 / +8.99 → +10.00 / +12.99 dB (virt off / on; per ear with E28a's level match, +10 dB in loudness), within 1 dB of each other; 5.1 pink with a band-limited LFE: 12.74 / 12.75 dB over 25–100 Hz in the BS.775 fold / virtualiser (+10 dB plus 3.01 dB for the LFE on both sides); the host's capture fold +10.00 dB re FL. Headroom (explosion-heavy 7.1, defaults, BS.775 fold): limiter over 1 dB 7.3 % / deepest 3.2 dB at +6 dB, 63.5 % / 6.3 dB at +10 dB alone, 2.7 % / 3.0 dB with the LF-first limiter; through the virtualiser 1.4 % → 0.0 %. Default-parameter 5.1 / 7.1 renders move: the pinned surround-7.1 programme (−30 dBFS pink mains, −12 dBFS 50 Hz LFE) −13.45 → −9.59 LUFS, 63 Hz band −13.54 → −9.55 dB, true peak −6.22 → −2.99 dBTP; the explosion scenes −17.77 → −17.34 LUFS through the virtualiser and −17.82 → −18.30 LUFS through the BS.775 fold (there the LF-first limiter takes back more than the lift adds), 63 Hz band −20.76 → −20.07 / −20.86 → −21.87 dB. No factory-preset render moves. Open: `DriftCompensatedFifo`'s and `TestSignalGenerator`'s own copies, the Gaming +10 / Music 0 dB split of the What, and the listening A/B.
- **Status (p3b3, app copies).** Done in `DriftCompensatedFifo.cpp` (plus the `LfeFold` member and its include in `DriftCompensatedFifo.h`), `TestSignalGenerator.cpp` and `Bs775Fold.h` (`LfeFold::next`, the per-sample form of the shared LFE path), [03 §8.9](03-dsp-design.md#89-known-limitations). The capture FIFO's own surround-to-stereo conversion and the test signal's stereo downmix of its Game71 scene keep their per-frame BS.775 matrices but now take the LFE through the chain's `LfeFold` (120 Hz 4th-order Butterworth, 20 ms gain ramp) inside the fold's −3 dB, at `virt.lfe`'s parameter default (+10 dB; neither knows its strip, and the host already folds a strip's 8-channel capture at the strip's own level); the FIFO sanitises the LFE sample like the others (a NaN there is now muted and counted). Done-when row (`tests/app/test_app_lfe_fold.cpp`): a 50 Hz tone on the LFE re the same tone on one main, each side of the FIFO's 5.1 and 7.1 downmix, −∞ (dropped) → +9.99 dB against the chain fold's +10.00 dB (within 0.01 dB; limit 0.1 dB); the test signal's stereo downmix is now the chain's `Bs775Fold` of its 8-channel render sample for sample, the LFE share −∞ → −7.95 dB RMS on each side, as in the chain. No allocation on the capture thread (asserted). No render, preset, parameter or golden file moves (the CLI and the chain do not use these copies). Two existing tests asserted the dropped LFE and now assert the fold: `tests/test_drift_fifo.cpp`'s 7.1 downmix case (100 Hz LFE tone on both sides at +10 dB through the low-pass) and `tests/test_signal_hygiene.cpp`'s FIFO case (a NaN on the 7.1 LFE is counted: 4 bad samples, not 3). Moved on purpose: the Game71 scene played on a stereo strip is about 10 LU louder (−24 → −14 LUFS at −3 dB in the E37 / E40 app fixtures) and LF-heavy, as it already was on a 7.1 strip; three app tests that played it on a stereo strip against estimates made on music no longer held their rows; the batch review moved them onto the music programme (below). **Owner decision recorded:** the What's split (+10 dB for Gaming, 0 dB for Music) is not applied; `virt.lfe` defaults to +10 dB on every strip (older presets keep their schema's default), and a Music 0 dB default waits for the owner, gated with the preset re-voicing ([§5.5](#55-later-or-gated-on-evidence)). The batch review moved the three E37 / E40 app fixtures (`test_app_ui_compare.cpp` "two banks 4+ LU apart ...", `test_app_ui_preset_browser.cpp` "a louder preview is matched ..." and "going back from a quieter preview ...") onto the test signal's music at −12 dB (about −26 LUFS in, inside the browser case's −20 … −28 LUFS row): unmatched 6.76 / 6.90 / 7.15 LU, matched −0.19 / −0.10 / +0.17 LU (limits 1 / 1 / 2 LU); the game scene at −13 dB would restore the −24 LUFS input, but the music-based estimate then leaves 1.3–1.7 LU and 2.9 LU. Open: the listening A/B (needs a Windows 7.1 path).
- **What.** Stop discarding the LFE: add it to the BS.775 stereo downmix and to the 7.1-to-stereo capture FIFO through an LR4 120 Hz low-pass, from one shared fold helper. Default the LFE to the +10 dB in-band convention (or +6 dB until [E05](#e05)'s LF envelope lands) with that gain inside the headroom budget. Add an "LFE fold" choice (Off / 0 dB / +10 dB), default +10 dB for Gaming and 0 dB for Music, because 5.1 music and film is often bass-managed. Replace the test that asserts "LFE dropped".
- **Why.** Games put most explosion, engine and impact energy in the LFE. Today it is silent whenever the virtualiser is off and 10 dB light when it is on.
- **Expected benefit.** Fixes a correctness defect. Measured (50 Hz at −12 dBFS, max and bass off): LFE only gives −93.3 dBFS with virt off (silent) and −15.0 dBFS with virt on, equal to one main channel and 10 dB under the convention. Reach is narrower than first claimed: 7.1 reaches the Game strip reliably only through the Linux `flubsound_game` sink or an endpoint that exposes 7.1; the Windows 7.1 driver does not exist (R4.6). Virt is on by default, so the silent case is off-default. The claim that users compensate with bass boost is unmeasured and dropped.
- **Approach.** (1) `flub::Bs775Fold` (prepare, reset, per-instance LR4 state, `g_lfe`) used by `ProcessingChain::downmixToStereo`, `DriftCompensatedFifo.cpp` and `TestSignalGenerator.cpp`, so the three copies cannot drift. (2) No all-pass "phase alignment" on the mains: LFE and mains carry different signals, and a 120 Hz rotation on every 5.1/7.1 music file buys nothing measurable. (3) Keep `virt.lfe` absolute; change its default for the Game strip and widen `kMaxLfeDb` to about +16. (4) Migration: `PresetIO` omits values equal to the default, so any default change silently re-voices sparse user presets. Add a v2 schema that fills absent keys of v1 presets from a frozen v1-defaults table (shared with every default change, see [E52](#e52)). (5) Route the worst-case LFE + centre sum through the existing `kTrim`/AutoLevel budget, not a fixed attenuation.
- **Depends on.** Merge after the in-flight engine swap and auto profiles (they touch the FIFO and `EngineController`). The v1-defaults shim. Windows reach needs [E46](#e46).
- **Done when.** LFE-only 50 Hz at −12 dBFS: virt-on and virt-off folds within 1 dB of each other and of +10 dB relative to one main. A 5.1 pink reference with band-limited LFE: LFE-to-mains ratio within 1 dB in the chain fold, FIFO fold and virtualiser. Off is bit-identical to today. A v1 preset without `virt.lfe` still loads 0 dB. No allocation on the capture thread. Listening: an explosion-heavy 7.1 capture A/B'd at matched LUFS against Windows Sonic and Dolby Atmos for Headphones.
- **Evidence.** [ProcessingChain.cpp][PC] `downmixToStereo()` "ITU-R BS.775 downmix (LFE dropped)"; [test_drift_fifo.cpp][T-drift] "DriftFifo: 7.1 capture into a stereo FIFO is downmixed per ITU-R BS.775 (LFE dropped, -3 dB)"; [HeadphoneVirtualizer.cpp][HV] `lfeGainDb` default 0, `kMaxLfeDb`; `gaming-7-1-headphone-surround.json` `virt.lfe −4`; [PresetIO.cpp][PIO] sparse `toJson`; [PluginProcessor.cpp][PP] `NormalisableRange` of `virt.lfe`.

<a id="e36"></a>
#### E36 · First-run onboarding wizard that gets sound flowing and sets a safe, audible starting point

*UX & workflow · benefit 3 · difficulty Medium · default-preset fix 1–2 days; minimal wizard 2 weeks; full 6–9 engineer-weeks*

- **Status (wave B2).** Default-preset fix done in `EngineController::loadFirstRunDefault` (checked first: the E59 burst-contrast and ambush metrics exist in `tests/test_known_gaps.cpp`, E19's interim bell and E27 steps (1)–(3) are in, E23's slice below; E27 step (4) is the Linux channel-order check, which does not change the fold). A strip with no saved state loads Signature (Music, System), Voice Chat (Chat) or "First Run – Game" (Game): Competitive FPS with Boost 0.20, Footsteps 0.30 and Detail 0.15, not the 0.30 cap, which left the beds at +3.55 / +3.87 LU. Measured with the E59 definitions: −50 / −60 dBFS pink beds +4.92 / +9.69 LU (Competitive FPS) → +2.70 / +2.62 LU (CLI; +2.71 / +2.66 LU through the app's Game strip), step/bed contrast change −1.67 … +0.83 → +0.06 … +0.49 dB on the 20 / 40 / 80 ms scenes at −14 / −24 / −40 / −50 LUFS, FL / FR-only 8-channel input on the stereo passthrough fold. A fresh Music strip: parameter defaults at Boost 0 (−16.01 LUFS out against −16.00 in, no audible change) → Signature. Balanced everywhere, saved state never overwritten (`tests/app/test_app_first_run.cpp`). **Review of the Game cap (Phase 2 batch 2)**, measured with the E59 definitions (pink beds as in the app test, the `makeBurstScene` step/bed contrast of `tests/test_known_gaps.cpp` at −14 / −24 / −40 / −50 LUFS, CLI stereo render; the app's 8-channel Game strip gives the same bed numbers). Competitive FPS as shipped now passes both sound rows: beds +2.56 / +2.53 LU at the end of batch 1 → +0.83 / +0.80 LU now (E19's background-relative floor for Detail's upward compressor in Gaming mode), contrast +4.39 … +5.81 dB. First Run – Game: beds +0.49 / +0.47 LU, contrast only +1.59 … +2.24 dB. The Footsteps 30 % cap is what costs the contrast (Boost 0.20 alone: beds +0.50 / +0.47 LU, contrast +4.19 … +5.53 dB), and the Detail cap no longer changes anything (Detail 30 % instead of 15 %: beds +0.50 / +0.47 LU, contrast within 0.02 dB; at the end of batch 1 it was what held the beds down, +2.20 → +0.95 LU). Recommendation: keep only the Boost cap (0.20, below the 0.25 point where Boost switches the maximizer on, which the shipped 0.35 does) and drop the Footsteps and Detail caps (`kFirstRunGameFootsteps` / `kFirstRunGameDetail` in `EngineController.cpp`), which gains about +3 dB of step/bed contrast for +0.01 LU of bed; not applied in this batch (`EngineController.cpp` belongs to another change set), and the test's pin on the shipped preset moves from 2.53 to 0.80 LU. Open: that cap change, the wizard and the rest of the item.

- **Status (p3b3, first-run Game caps).** The review's cap change is done: "First Run – Game" is Competitive FPS with only Boost capped at 0.20 (`kFirstRunGameBoost`); `kFirstRunGameFootsteps` / `kFirstRunGameDetail` are gone, so Footsteps 0.80 and Detail 0.30 play as the preset ships them. Measured through the app's 8-channel Game strip (FL / FR only, the E59 definitions; `tests/app/test_app_first_run.cpp`): step/bed contrast change on the 20 / 40 / 80 ms steps −1.68 … −1.58 / +2.28 … +2.46 / +2.42 … +2.70 dB → +1.37 … +1.50 / +4.79 … +5.03 / +5.22 … +5.60 dB at −14 / −24 / −40 / −50 LUFS (Competitive FPS as shipped, Boost 0.35: +1.56 … +1.74 / +4.95 … +5.31 / +5.28 … +5.86 dB); −50 / −60 dBFS pink beds +0.495 / +0.471 → +0.504 / +0.479 LU (+0.008 LU); the pin on the shipped preset reads +0.81 LU against 0.80 (it was moved from 2.53 in batch 2's review). The new case *First Run – Game keeps Competitive FPS's step/bed contrast* pins the −24 LUFS row (+1.46 / +4.86 / +5.27 dB, at least +1 / +4 / +4 dB), and the fresh-strip case now expects Footsteps 0.80 and Detail 0.30. No factory preset, golden file or render-baseline entry moves (a first-run default is app state). Open: the wizard and the rest of the item.
- **What.** Ship first, separately, and only after the slices that make the defaults safe (see Depends on): fresh strips load a factory default instead of parameter defaults at Boost 0. Music and System load "Flubsound Signature". Chat loads [E23](#e23)'s "Voice Chat" (maximizer off), not Signature, because Signature's Boost 0.35 is above the 0.25 point where Boost engages `MaximizerOn` ([E05](#e05)), which would loudness-maximise voice chat, the misconfiguration E23 describes. Game loads a new "First Run – Game" preset, not Competitive FPS as shipped: Competitive FPS lifts a −50 dBFS pink bed by +5.0 LU and a −60 dBFS bed by +9.8 LU (CLI, this report; the post-report review measured +5.2 / +9.5 LU), cuts step/bed contrast by 6.0 dB ([E19](#e19)), and leaves `virt.on` at its default of true, which folds stereo-in-8-channel game audio through two 30° virtual speakers ([E27](#e27)). "First Run – Game" starts from Competitive FPS with Footsteps and Detail capped at 30 % (the cap [E16](#e16) uses for headsets with on-board processing) and Boost below 0.25 so the maximizer stays off, and it ships only if it meets the Done-when numbers below; `virt.on` stays true only because, after E27 Phase 1, FL/FR-only input is switched to the passthrough fold automatically. Then a re-runnable wizard: (1) output device and transducer type, with headphone-model search feeding [E15](#e15); (2) detect Windows Sonic, Dolby, Nahimic and OEM APOs on the endpoint (reported as "detected", never "none present"); (3) get sound flowing with a live "signal reaching strip" check; (4) choose a preset per strip with matched audition once [E37](#e37) exists.
- **Why.** No OS produces processed audio after install without expert steps. Strips start at Boost 0 % with a 195-parameter console, and stacked OEM HRTFs go undetected, so first impressions fail.
- **Expected benefit.** Music at Boost 0 on the dense signal measures −16.01 LUFS against −16.00 in: no audible change, so the default-preset fix is the highest-return piece in the group. It is also the one change in this report that alters what *every* new user hears, so it falls under rule (2) in [§5](#5-recommended-implementation-order) like any retune, and it must not make the Game strip worse than bypass on the defects this report measures. The wizard cannot deliver a sub-3-minute flow on Windows today: endpoint routing fails ([E47](#e47)), drivers do not exist, and process capture doubles the signal unless the app's output is moved off the monitored endpoint. The Windows step reduces to "install VB-Cable"; macOS has no routing; only Linux can be fully automated.
- **Approach.** `OnboardingWizard` under `app/Source/ui/onboarding/` with an `AppSettings` completion flag. Windows: detect a VB-Cable or Voicemeeter pair and guide through `ms-settings:apps-volume`, checking that the app's session peak on the physical endpoint is silent while the strip input is not. Linux: run `flubsound-pipewire-setup.sh` via `ChildProcess` only with explicit consent, record what it created and offer Undo. macOS: state the limitation. Stacked-processing detection from the endpoint property store and a JSON table of APO CLSIDs. Signal check: `MeterBus` `inPeakDb` above −60 dBFS for 1 s, plus a doubled-audio heuristic. Drop the dose meter (it belongs to [E32](#e32)).
- **Depends on.** [E46](#e46) (the real Windows unblocker), macOS routing ([E49](#e49)), [E37](#e37), [E15](#e15), the in-flight UI scale/theme work, and [E40](#e40)'s latency-profile preservation so a first-run Quality preset cannot pad every strip to 28 ms. The default-preset fix needs none of these, but it does need: the [E59](#e59) slice (its burst-contrast and ambush metrics judge the Game default); [E19](#e19)'s interim bell (so the default's cue band is contrast-neutral); [E27](#e27) Phase 1 (so stereo game audio is not folded through the virtualiser by default); and [E23](#e23)'s slice (the Chat default). It therefore ships at the end of Phase 1, not in Phase 0. No preset it loads sets Quality (`music-flubsound-signature.json` has no `latency.profile`; the Game default inherits Low Latency from `gaming-competitive-fps.json`; Voice Chat sets none), so it does not need [E40](#e40)'s slice.
- **Done when.** `tests/app/test_app_onboarding.cpp`: state machine, persistence, fresh strips load their default preset (Music and System Signature, Chat Voice Chat with the maximizer off, Game "First Run – Game"; Balanced or Low Latency), mocked SysFx/spatial flags; the Game default lifts the −50 and −60 dBFS pink beds by ≤ +3 LU, changes step/bed contrast by ≥ 0 dB on the E59 slice's burst scenes, and leaves a 2-channel-equivalent render for FL/FR-only 8-channel input (E27's Done-when); a capture whose session still renders to the output device produces a warning. Manual matrix: Windows 11 with and without VB-Cable, Ubuntu 24.04 PipeWire, macOS 14; time to processed audio reported per OS (target < 3 min on Linux and Windows with a cable), and the no-cable Windows outcome recorded honestly.
- **Evidence.** [06 §9.2][06-92] "None of it exists in code yet"; [06 §9.1](06-gui.md#91-what-exists-today-implemented) defaults Boost 0 %; [TRACEABILITY Known gaps][TR-gaps] R4.1; [DeviceProfiles.cpp][DP] `adviceFor`; [08 D4][08-D4].

<a id="e50"></a>
#### E50 · Clock-domain correctness: drift compensation for cable inputs and a band-limited polyphase ASRC

*Stability & platform · benefit 3 · difficulty Medium · Phase A 5–8 days; total 2.5–3.5 engineer-weeks · Quick win (Phase A)*

- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, Windows lane).** Phase A done in `DriftCompensatedFifo.*` (`Kernel`) and `AudioEngineHost::getLatencyInfo`: a polyphase Kaiser-windowed sinc, 48 taps × 512 phases (β 11, cutoff at the producer's Nyquist frequency, linear interpolation between adjacent phases, every phase normalised to unity DC gain; phase 0 is the identity), replacing the 4-point Catmull-Rom. The 16–24 taps named below cannot meet this item's own Done-when: flat to 20 kHz with images ≤ −100 dB from 28 kHz up needs about 38 taps by Kaiser's estimate, so 48. Before → after: 20 kHz at phase 0.25 / 0.5 −2.62 / −8.41 → −0.00003 / −0.00005 dB; worst deviation to 20 kHz over phases 0 / 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.75 8.41 → 0.00006 dB; THD+N (images included) at ±200 ppm, −1 dBFS: 1 kHz −91.6 → −115.3 dB, 15 kHz −14.6 → −115.6 dB; kernel stopband from 28 kHz −110.7 dB; group delay 24 − fraction frames, i.e. `fixedDelayFrames()` (23.5) ± 0.5, reported as `Stats::resamplerDelayMs` and added to `LatencyInfo::captureBufferMs` (+0.49 ms at 48 kHz; the Catmull-Rom's 1–2 frames were not reported). Through the FIFO at +200 ppm, 10 / 20 kHz sines keep their level within 0.0002 dB. The kernel's history is kept apart from the ring, so the fill target and the drift loop's set-point do not move ([§6](#6-updated-risk-assessment-for-the-proposed-enhancements) T8's re-analysis is not needed; `test_drift_fifo.cpp` passes unchanged). Cost ≈ 18 ns per stereo frame on the review machine (about 0.1 % of a core per stereo capture at 48 kHz); `test_drift_fifo.cpp`'s first case (16 scenarios, 28 simulated minutes) went 1.6 → 3.1 s. Tests: `tests/app/test_app_drift_asrc.cpp`, 3 cases, 0.4 s (the assertions this item's Done-when puts in `test_drift_fifo.cpp`; that file is outside the Windows lane). Open: Phase B (a Windows machine and its glitch count) and a listening check on real hardware, now that the doubling guard keeps the dry original from masking the top octave.
- **What.** Phase A: replace the 4-point Catmull-Rom interpolator in `DriftCompensatedFifo` with a polyphase windowed-sinc ASRC (16–24 taps x 256 phases, linear phase interpolation, ≥ 100 dB image rejection) and report its fixed delay in `LatencyInfo`. Phase B: measure cable-input glitches first; if they occur, open cable inputs as separate WASAPI shared capture clients feeding a capture slot, and meanwhile warn when input and output endpoints differ.
- **Why.** Every Windows process-loopback capture goes through this FIFO, so its interpolator colours all per-app audio. The cable path runs memcpy in a JUCE duplex device with no drift compensation.
- **Expected benefit.** Exact-weight response loss at fractional phase 0.25 / 0.5: 8 kHz −0.12 / −0.23 dB; 12 kHz −0.55 / −1.07 dB; 16 kHz −1.44 / −3.25 dB; 20 kHz −2.62 / −8.41 dB. With a nominal ratio of 1 the phase is either stuck (a random, session-dependent top-octave shelf) or sweeping slowly with drift (slow HF modulation). That breaks transparency on the main Windows path. Below 12 kHz the error is ≤ 1 dB, and the 6–10 kHz pinna region loses only 0.1–0.6 dB, so the gaming claim ("HF cues smeared") is overstated. The cable "slips every tens of seconds" claim is unmeasured; if confirmed, Phase B rises to benefit 4–5.
- **Approach.** Precompute the Kaiser table in `prepare()`; raise the "+4 interpolation window" term in `computeTargetFrames` to the kernel length; add `fixedDelayFrames()` to `AudioEngineHost::getLatencyInfo`. Scalar code is enough (about 1–2 MMAC/s per stereo slot). Phase B on Windows only (Linux null sinks share one PipeWire clock; macOS is [E49](#e49)), reusing the shape of `WinProcessLoopbackCapture`.
- **Depends on.** Phase A: nothing in code. It is scheduled after [E47](#e47)'s same-endpoint doubling guard, because while the dry original still plays about 12.75 ms beside the capture, the resulting comb filter masks the interpolator's top-octave loss and a listening check cannot isolate it. The larger kernel moves the FIFO set-point, so the drift controller's fixed-point analysis must be redone and [E42](#e42) E42b built on the new target ([§6](#6-updated-risk-assessment-for-the-proposed-enhancements) T8). Phase B: a Windows machine ([E53](#e53) step 0) and its discontinuity detector.
- **Done when.** New `test_drift_fifo.cpp` assertions: flat within ±0.1 dB to 20 kHz at phases 0 / 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.75 and ratios 1 ± 200 ppm; images ≤ −100 dB; THD+N ≤ −100 dB for 1 and 15 kHz at −1 dBFS under ±200 ppm; group delay = `fixedDelayFrames` ± 0.5 sample. Phase B: a 1 h real-rig glitch count before and after ([10 §5][10-5] test 1), target 0.
- **Evidence.** [DriftCompensatedFifo.cpp][DCF] interpolator (~L339–360), `computeTargetFrames`; `DriftCompensatedFifo.h` "the Hermite interpolator does not band-limit"; [AudioEngineHost.cpp][AEH] `processBlock` memcpy of device inputs; [test_drift_fifo.cpp][T-drift] (no response or THD assertion); [driver README][DRV] ASRC spec; [08 D1][08-D1].

<a id="e58"></a>
#### E58 · Distribution readiness and reach: licences, notices, SBOM, C ABI SDK, mobile/ARM builds, CLAP and multichannel plug-in

*Future-proofing · benefit 2 · difficulty Medium · licence part 2–4 days after the owner's decision; recommended scope 6–8 weeks*

- **What.** High part: the owner chooses the business model (flub_core under MPL-2.0 or Apache-2.0 + commercial SDK; app and plug-in under AGPLv3 or a commercial JUCE licence), then LICENSE and LICENSES/ in REUSE layout, SPDX headers, `reuse lint` in CI, a DCO clause in CONTRIBUTING.md, and a hand-maintained THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES with a CI check against new `FetchContent_Declare` entries. A content-licence gate (SPDX `license` and `source` fields) for device profiles, presets, future HRIR/SOFA files and models. Medium part: a stable C ABI (`flub_c.h`, opaque handles, key-based parameters, marked experimental), Windows ARM64 and Linux aarch64 CI, CLAP, a surround-output plug-in variant. Deferred: iOS, Android, Wwise/FMOD.
- **Why.** Every binary carries AGPL obligations that conflict with store and console terms, and there is no LICENSE at all. Without a DCO or CLA, contributions accepted before the decision make relicensing impossible.
- **Expected benefit.** The licence decision is a legal release blocker. Nothing else here is audible. A CycloneDX SBOM adds little while JUCE is the only dependency. The first SOFA set added needs a licence check because Renderer B exists but ships no data. iOS allows no system-wide processing and Android offers only DynamicsProcessing ([09 §3][09-3]), so those builds have no product path.
- **Approach.** As above; the C ABI needs `add_library` SHARED/export rules (flub_core is STATIC with no install rules and no `extern "C"`), exceptions caught at the boundary, `-fvisibility=hidden`, a C89 compile test like `tests/test_driver_shared_c.c`. CLAP via JUCE or `clap-juce-extensions` (JUCE 9.0.2 native CLAP support unverified). The surround variant links the ceiling across all channels including LFE. ASIO stays opt-in at build time and is never redistributed.
- **Depends on.** The owner/legal decision (not engineering work) blocks everything. The C ABI waits for stable parameter keys ([E57](#e57) Phase B, if it happens). VST3 SDK terms matter for any hosting.
- **Done when.** `reuse lint` passes; a PR adding a dependency without a notice fails; a fixture with a missing or non-allowlisted licence fails the content gate. A C11 program linking only `flub_c` renders a WAV that nulls against `flubsound-cli process` at −140 dBFS. ARM runners pass the core suite within a documented float tolerance. pluginval strictness 10 (VST3) and clap-validator pass. A 7.1 bus keeps every channel ≤ ceiling.
- **Evidence.** No LICENSE at the repository root; [README licensing notes](../README.md); [02 §6][02-6]; [FlubJuce.cmake][FJ] (single `FetchContent_Declare`, JUCE 9.0.2 AGPLv3/commercial); `core/CMakeLists.txt` (STATIC, no install/export); [CI workflow][CI] desktop matrices only; `plugin/CMakeLists.txt` "VST3 Standalone"; [PluginProcessor.cpp][PP] `isBusesLayoutSupported` stereo output; [CONTRIBUTING.md](../CONTRIBUTING.md).

<a id="e49"></a>
#### E49 · macOS system-wide and per-app processing via process taps

*Stability & platform · benefit 4 · difficulty High · 6–9 engineer-weeks (HAL plug-in deferred, +6–10 weeks) · Ambitious*

- **What.** Roadmap 3.2: `CATapDescription` with `CATapMutedWhenTapped` in one private aggregate device clocked by the real output, giving per-app processing with no driver and no double signal, plus a global "everything except Flubsound Pro and apps already tapped" tap for the System strip. `NSAudioCaptureUsageDescription` and a permission onboarding screen. A libASPL HAL plug-in for macOS older than 14.2 and the processed mic is deferred.
- **Why.** macOS has no path except BlackHole plus a Multi-Output Device, which loses volume keys and drifts. Boom 3D, SoundSource, eqMac and FxSound for Mac all ship one, so this is basic function, not a differentiator.
- **Expected benefit.** Opens macOS for music listeners (a large Mac audience); gaming value is small. Priority is Medium rather than High because none of the 622 lines of Mac code has run, and Windows, the gaming platform, has not run either ([E53](#e53)).
- **Approach.** (1) Run the existing `PlatformServices_mac.mm` on a real Mac first and fix it. (2) Choose the host design explicitly: (a, preferred) JUCE opens the private aggregate as a duplex device, so tap channels arrive as ordinary inputs through the existing `deviceInputFirst` memcpy path: one clock, no FIFO, no [E50](#e50) dependency, but a route change rebuilds the aggregate and restarts the device, hidden by the in-flight engine swap; or (b) a separate IOProc feeding a capture-slot FIFO at a fixed ratio (the HAL already drift-compensates). (3) Rebuild the global tap's exclusion list on route changes, or routed apps are captured twice. (4) Add the usage string via `PLIST_TO_MERGE` in `app/CMakeLists.txt`; onboarding detects denial and deep-links to Privacy & Security. (5) Keep AppRouting and RoutingPanel unchanged behind the router and capture interfaces. (6) Tap tests run on a self-hosted Mac; hosted CI cannot grant audio-capture TCC.
- **Depends on.** [E53](#e53) step 0 for the Mac, [E54](#e54) Developer ID signing and notarisation (ad-hoc builds lose the TCC grant on every rebuild), [E50](#e50) only for design (b), a Mac on macOS 14.2+ and a self-hosted runner.
- **Done when.** Music.app, Spotify and a game on three strips: null test shows no dry leak above −90 dBFS and volume keys work. Route changes during playback give zero discontinuities (b) or one crossfaded transition < 50 ms (a). A routed app never appears in the System strip. Unplug/replug with taps active. Permission denial shows onboarding, not silence. 8 h tone soak through a tap with zero discontinuities.
- **Evidence.** [PlatformServices.h](../app/Source/platform/PlatformServices.h) ("designed, not implemented... roadmap 3.2"); [PlatformServices_mac.mm][PSM] tap design in comments (~L290–360); [platform/macos/README.md][MAC] "Not yet verified on a Mac"; [08 D8][08-D8]; [TRACEABILITY Known gaps][TR-gaps] R4.4, R4.5.

<a id="e02"></a>
#### E02 · Bass engine dynamics and phase coherence: look-ahead split-band protection and one shared crossover

*Audio quality · benefit 3 · difficulty High · subsonic slice 1–1.5 days; total 3.5–4.5 engineer-weeks · Ambitious*

- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, (a) and the subsonic slice).** Partially done in `BassEngine.*`, `Parameters.*`, `ProcessingChain.*` and five factory presets ([03 §4](03-dsp-design.md#4-bass-engine)). (a) `bass.splitProtect` (toggle, default off, layout version 5): a sub detector (the LR4 low band at 85 Hz, overlapping the punch band so a tone near 60 Hz is not under-read; 25 ms hold) withdraws the shelf; what the classic prediction (with an 8 ms hold, the punch detector) still exceeds is cut by a wide bell in the punch band (Q 0.5 at 0.8 · √(60 Hz · detector LP)), never deeper than the boost the shelf still gives there; both have a program-dependent release (onsets are rises of 3 dB over the band level's recent valley; once they recur ≤ 1.2 s apart, a withdrawal is held for the onset spacing + 1/8, then released with 150 ms; an isolated hit is not held). New metric: a 32 Hz line under 55 Hz kicks every 0.5 + 1/128 s (four kick periods cancel the kicks' own 32 Hz content), the peak-to-peak of the line's gain over the kick period. Protection alone at Bass Head's settings, lines at −24 / −18 / −12 dBFS: 3.54 / 3.74 / 4.65 → 0.00 / 0.00 / 0.00 dB, at a steadily lower boost (mean line gain +4.03 / +3.75 / +2.64 → +1.50 / +0.73 / −0.56 dB: the 55 Hz kick sits in the sub band, so the held withdrawal is the one the kicks need); 100 Hz kicks 3.56 → 0.01 dB at about the classic mean (+3.70 → +3.42 dB); Bass Head's whole engine 3.12 / 3.35 / 4.41 → 1.85 / 1.77 / 1.25 dB; through the chain (the Bass Head preset, line at −18 dBFS) 5.07 → 2.95 dB (`KnownGap: bass-line pumping ...`). The ≤ 1 dB row is not met: Tighten ([E04](#e04)) and the harmonics generator ([E03](#e03)) move the line about 1 dB each, and the chain after them (Punch's shaper, the glue and the maximizer at Boost 45 %) the rest. The cap holds within 0.5 dB for every tone and shelf (the classic 0.21 dB). No factory preset turns it on: that, and its cost in steady boost, is a voicing decision. Not done: the optional 2 ms Quality look-ahead and the explosion-onset GR row. Subsonic slice: `bass.subsonicOrder` (12 / 24 dB/oct, default 24 dB/oct, layout version 5; a 20 ms crossfade between the two orders), and Bass Head, Club Loud, Warm Vinyl, Competitive FPS and Battle Royale moved from a 4th-order subsonic at 25–30 Hz to the 2nd order at the 20 Hz default (the preset change this Done-when asks for). The bass engine at 40 Hz: HP4 at 25 Hz 8.17 ms → HP2 at 20 Hz 3.31 ms. Done-when rows (`KnownGap: subsonic slice ...`): Bass Head's chain 40 Hz group delay 18.68 → 13.82 ms (≤ 11 ms open: the rest is the mono-bass LR4 at 110 Hz, 4.6 ms, and the glue split's LR4 at 120 Hz, which only (b) or [E05](#e05)'s open decision on arming the split would remove); 28 Hz, the subsonic's own cost on Competitive FPS / Battle Royale −4.4 / −3.0 → −1.0 dB (met; re 1 kHz through the whole preset −6.80 → −3.43 dB and −3.46 → −1.45 dB, Competitive FPS's own −2 dB low shelf included). Balanced and Low Latency totals unchanged. Preset render diff: only the five presets move: the 31.5 Hz band +0.3 … +1.3 dB, 1 s level windows +0.2 … +0.6 dB, true peak −1.3 … +0.9 dB, integrated −0.11 … +0.14 LU; Bass Head's tone-kicks clip energy −42.6 → −65.1 dB. E60 scenes (`tests/test_scenes.cpp`): Competitive FPS / Battle Royale metrics move ≤ 0.05 dB; E19's KnownGap step / bed rows: Competitive FPS bed lift at −14 / −24 / −40 LUFS −1.86 / −1.30 / −0.94 → −1.79 / −1.16 / −0.81 dB, contrast within 0.02 dB (all pass). Tests: 4 new cases in `tests/test_bass_engine.cpp`, 2 in `tests/test_known_gaps.cpp`. *Review (Phase 3 batch 2):* the five presets' golden renders re-recorded (third-octave 25 Hz +2.3 … +5.6 dB, 31.5 Hz +0.3 … +1.5 dB; integrated Bass Head +0.23 LU (Tighten's share +0.06), Battle Royale +0.04, Warm Vinyl −0.03, Competitive FPS 0.00, Club Loud −0.08 LU), their `contentHash`, and the render baseline (every move of these five presets is this change: with their old subsonic rows the diff moves only E04's presets). One side effect not listed above: the 2nd-order filter passes more of a kick's onset (its sub-20 Hz transient) into the harmonics generator, which runs after the subsonic, so on kicks Bass Head's generated 2nd / 3rd harmonics rise (golden programme: 100 Hz +4.0 dB, 160 Hz +2.4 dB, 80 Hz −1.3 dB; the same kicks alone +3.9 dB at 100 Hz; a steady 55 Hz tone unchanged) and Club Loud's (harmonics 0.1: 125 Hz +2.1 dB, 200 Hz +1.6 dB). It is a voicing change of the preset move (the harmonics could be fed from the 4th-order output instead), for the E60 panel.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, look-ahead).** (a)'s look-ahead done in `BassEngine.*`, not yet wired into the chain ([03 §4.9](03-dsp-design.md#49-reviewed-design-decisions--known-limitations)). `BassEngine::setLookaheadMs` (structural, 0–5 ms, default 0; it is the engine's latency): the split detectors read the undelayed signal with a 1 ms attack, the classic detector and everything after it the delayed one, and the split withdrawals apply without the 5 ms gain smoothing (releases keep it). Explosion-onset row, bass engine → maximizer as Quality runs it (drive 0, ceiling −1 dBTP), deepest limiter GR over the first 50 ms: [03 §4.3.3](03-dsp-design.md#433-adaptive-low-shelf-with-predictive-headroom-protection)'s onset (40 Hz at −6 dBFS into +12 dB at 70 Hz, cap 0 dBFS) classic 4.58 / split 4.72 → split with 2 ms look-ahead 0.69 dB, onset peak +3.89 / +3.98 → −0.36 dBFS (Done-when ≥ 2 dB lower: met at engine level, −3.9 dB); an explosion (45 Hz + low-passed noise, −6 dBFS, tau 350 ms) 1.43 → 0.00 dB. With the look-ahead the line under kicks keeps 0.00 dB modulation (mean gain within 0.12 dB), the cap holds within 0.5 dB, switching is click-free and block-size independent; with `splitProtect` off the engine only delays (−93.9 dBFS difference: the peak holds' bucket grid does not move with the delay). Not wired because 2 ms moves Quality's chain latency 1352 → 1448 samples at 48 kHz (1332 → 1420 at 44.1, 2616 → 2808 at 96, 5144 → 5528 at 192 kHz), which `tests/test_engine.cpp`, `tests/test_neural_slot.cpp` and [01 §5.1](01-architecture.md#51-algorithmic-latency-per-profile-48-khz-the-only-latency-sources-in-the-chain) pin: the wiring is `bass.setLookaheadMs (2.0f)` in `ProcessingChain::prepare()`'s Quality case (0 elsewhere) with those numbers; Balanced and Low Latency totals stay unchanged. No preset, golden or KnownGap value moves (the chain does not call it). Tests: 2 new cases in `tests/test_bass_engine.cpp`. Open: the wiring, (b), and the ≤ 1 dB line and ≤ 11 ms group-delay rows (above). *Review (Phase 3 batch 2):* confirmed that nothing moves: every measured line of `flub_tests` other than E10's (below) is identical to `fe67dac`'s, and the render diff is unchanged at 0.0 dB tolerance; the two cases passed 50 consecutive runs.
- **What.** (0) A `bass.subsonicOrder` parameter (2/4, default 4) with gaming and music presets moved from HP4 at 25–30 Hz to HP2 at ≤ 20 Hz. (a) Split the shelf-protection detector into sub (< 60 Hz, 25 ms hold) and punch (60–150 Hz, 8 ms hold) bands that withdraw independently, with a program-dependent release and optional 2 ms look-ahead in Quality only. (b) Only when [E04](#e04) needs it: fold mono-bass and tighten onto one shared LR4 split. Publish onset-overshoot telemetry.
- **Why.** Music: sustained bass lines pump in time with the kick. Gaming: 28–30 Hz subsonic filters remove rumble that headphones reproduce well.
- **Expected benefit.** Part (a) has the clear music payoff: Bass Head settings move a 32 Hz line by +3.2 to +6.2 dB with the kicks. Part (b) is much smaller than claimed. Of the 15.9 ms analytic group delay at 40 Hz, HP4 at 25 Hz contributes 8.2 ms, the 110 Hz LR4 4.6 ms and the 150 Hz LR4 3.2 ms; a shared split removes about 3.2 ms, while HP2 at 20 Hz alone removes 4.8 ms. The +2.6 dBFS explosion overshoot was measured with the maximizer off (off-default); with it on it is limiter GR, which is [E05](#e05)'s problem. The stacked-phase issue affects only 7 presets using mono-bass or tighten.
- **Approach.** Look-ahead does not fit in Balanced ([01 §5.1][01-51]: only look-ahead stages have latency; Balanced is at the edge of R1.1). (a) In `BassEngine::processSegment` stage 3 (detector constants `kDetectorAttackMs 10`, `kDetectorReleaseMs 150`, `kHoldMs 25`): two detectors applied as shelf plus 60–150 Hz bell, two-stage release reusing `TruePeakLimiter`'s blend pattern, look-ahead via `latencySamples()` set only for Quality. (b) One LR4 for mono-bass and tighten, subsonic stays a separate HP; no linear-phase low band (an FIR crossover at 60–120 Hz needs tens of ms). Redo the `ParkedStage` engage/disengage paths and their tests.
- **Depends on.** The v1-defaults preset shim ([E01](#e01), [E52](#e52)). Do (b) only with E04's LF band. Subsonic ≤ 20 Hz passes more rumble into the maximizer until E05 lands.
- **Done when.** 32 Hz line + 55 Hz kicks at Bass Head: line modulation ≤ 1 dB. Subsonic slice: 40 Hz group delay ≤ 11 ms for the Bass Head chain and 28 Hz within 3 dB of input on gaming presets. Balanced and Low Latency totals unchanged. `test_bass_engine.cpp` transition/click tests pass. With max on, explosion-onset GR ≥ 2 dB lower. ABX of (b) on/off before committing two weeks to it.
- **Evidence.** [BassEngine.cpp][BE] detector constants, `processSegment` stages 1–4, `ParkedStage`; [03 §4.3](03-dsp-design.md#43-algorithm--maths) and [§4.9][03-49] (3–4 dB onset overshoot accepted); measurements above.

<a id="e04"></a>
#### E04 · Multiband, look-ahead transient shaper with a real punch/tactile control (and fix Tighten)

*Audio quality · benefit 3 · difficulty High · slice 0.5–1 day; Tighten fix 2–3 days; full 3–4 engineer-weeks*

- **Status (Phase 2 batch 1).** Approach step (1) done: `MacroMap.cpp` `kMusicTable` no longer maps Punch (M1) to `BassTighten`. Kick-onset metric (`tests/test_known_gaps.cpp`): Punch 100 lift 0–10 / 10–30 ms 3.92 / 5.47 → 5.38 / 4.70 dB, onset minus body −1.55 → +0.68 dB (target +2 dB still open: the full-band shaper's own smear). Presets using Punch re-baselined: Punchy Pop +0.04 LU, Bass Head +0.01 LU on the golden programme; mode and factory-preset tests unchanged. It also moved [E06](#e06)'s all-Music-macros 50 Hz THD+N KnownGap (Strict −25.5 → −24.5 dB, driven base at Off −15.3 → −14.9 dB; with the Tighten row restored the tree reads −25.5 / −15.2), re-based in review. Open: steps (2)–(5) (Tighten's first-20 ms fix, the 3-band shaper, `attackLow` / `attackHigh`, look-ahead).
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, step 2).** Done in `TransientShaper.*` and `BassEngine.*` ([03 §4.3.5](03-dsp-design.md#435-replace-fundamental-small-speaker-mode-and-tighten), [§5.3.1](03-dsp-design.md#531-transientshaper-level-independent)). Two causes of Tighten's onset cut, both fixed: (1) the sustain pair still read the previous kick's decay through the next kick's first 2–3 ms, so the cut sat on its first half-cycle: the sustain is now gated by the onset (the held level over the slow attack envelope, `setSustainGatedByAttack`, Tighten only; Clarity's sustain is unchanged), and a cut the gate lifts returns in 0.5 ms; (2) the stage's output was the 150 Hz LR4 band sum, an all-pass with about 3 ms of group delay under 100 Hz, which took 1.5 dB off the first 10 ms at any Tighten above 0: the gain is now applied as a dynamic one-pole shelf, `x + (g − 1)·LP1(x)`, exactly x at unity gain (the LR4 bands only feed the detector). Kick-onset metric (`tests/test_known_gaps.cpp`), Tighten 0.5 lift 0–10 ms −2.04 → −0.55 dB, i.e. **−1.69 → −0.20 dB against Tighten 0 (Done-when ≥ −0.5 met)**, 10–30 ms +0.56 → 0.00 dB against Tighten 0; the tail is still cut (60–150 / 150–300 ms −1.79 / −4.98 → −1.65 / −5.11 dB, CLI). Costs: the shelf's lag bounds the cut (−6 dB of gain reads −4.9 / −3.2 / −2.0 dB at 50 / 100 / 150 Hz; Tighten 1 at most about −8 dB at 50 Hz, was −12) and it moves the phase of content above the corner slightly while a cut changes (the E59 suite's 1 kHz sine at Tighten 0.5, THD+N −91.7 → −42.3 dB, a level change under 0.1 dB, during the release after its start transient). Seven factory presets use Tighten (Signature 0.1, Punchy Pop 0.2, Club Loud 0.2, Bass Head 0.1, Racing 0.2, Earbuds 0.15, Bluetooth Headphones 0.1) and move, because the Done-when requires it: golden programme integrated Earbuds +0.09, Racing +0.14, Bass Head +0.06 LU, the others within 0.05 LU (band levels up to 0.3 dB); on the hot master Signature / Punchy Pop limit > 1 dB 17.87 / 49.33 → 18.93 / 50.13 % of the time without the automatic preamp (the kicks' onsets now reach the limiter) and 7.47 / 6.93 → 6.93 / 7.47 % with it; Racing's E60 scene onset jump at −14 LUFS +0.30 → +0.69 dB and step lift after vs before +0.31 / +0.27 → −0.04 / −0.07 dB at −14 / −24 LUFS. Not started: step (3), the 3-band shaper with `attackLow` / `attackHigh` (below [§5.4](#54-phase-3--following-two-quarters-weeks-1540-about-months-49)'s cut line, and the +2 dB Punch target needs it or a new attack kernel: moving the shared shaper's timing would re-voice every preset with Boost above 10 %), steps (4) and (5). *Review (Phase 3 batch 2):* golden renders, `contentHash` and render baseline re-recorded for the seven presets (golden programme integrated Earbuds +0.09, Racing +0.14, Bass Head +0.06, Signature +0.01, Punchy Pop −0.01, Bluetooth Headphones −0.03, Club Loud 0.00 LU; bands ≤ 0.27 dB, Bass Head's and Club Loud's up to 0.6 dB); the E60 scene matrix re-pinned for Racing (`tests/test_scenes.cpp`, the numbers above; −24 / −40 LUFS onset jump 1.56 → 1.63 / 0.15 → 0.16 dB, step lift 0.27 → −0.07 / −0.22 → −0.24 dB).
- **Status (p3b5, step 3).** Done in `TransientShaper.*`, `ClarityEnhancer.*`, `Crossover.h`, `Parameters.*` and the chain's hand-over ([03 §5.3.5](03-dsp-design.md#535-the-3-band-path-11-e04-step-3)). `clarity.attackLow` / `clarity.attackHigh` (layout version 8, −12 … +12 dB, default 0) offset the attack of the band below the split and of the band above 4 kHz over `clarity.attack`, which with `clarity.sustain` stays "all bands"; no preset migrates and plug-in automation stays valid. With both at 0 stage 1 is the full-band shaper, bit-exact (*Transparency (E04 step 3): ...*; the preset render diff reads all 150 renders unchanged, the reference-platform golden renders pass). Any other value runs 3 LR4 bands (a 60–200 Hz split, 120 Hz by default, gliding per sample, and 4 kHz), one `TransientShaper` per band with its own timing (holds 25 ms / split⁄3 / 3 ms, slow attack 10 / 8 / 4 ms, gain smoothing 0.3 / 0.3 / 0.25 ms), a program-dependent release (both attack envelopes release fast once the held level has fallen 6 dB under the slow one, slowly on shallower dips) and a speed control (0.5–2); the split and the speed are `ClarityParams` settings without a parameter. Switching the path on, its shapers warm up for 50 ms with the output unchanged, then a 50 ms smoothstep crossfades to the band sum (an all-pass, so the crossover frequencies dip once; no click: ≤ −80 dBFS above 15 kHz with air off, see 03 §5.3.5 for the air exciter's response). Done-when, before (full band) → after (bands), +12 dB attack: hits 75 ms apart, the shaper's gain peak 5.52 / 7.89 / 9.90 → 12.00 / 12.00 / 12.00 dB and through the module the first 10 ms 3.91 / 6.49 / 7.43 → 10.08 / 11.04 / 10.35 dB in the low / mid / high band (**≥ +9 dB per band met**); gain within 1 dB of its peak 2.33 / 2.23 / 2.19 → 0.75 / 0.75 / 0.60 ms after an isolated onset (**within 1 ms met**; hits 75 ms apart, the low band's 2.5 ms is inside the kick's first quarter cycle); 40–60 ms after an isolated hit 3.29 / 3.28 / 3.50 → 0.34 / 0.33 / 0.04 dB (**≤ 1 dB met**); a steady 40 Hz note with every band at ±12 dB attack and sustain moves 0.002 dB once settled, no sidebands above −80 dB (**≤ 0.1 dB met**; in its first 2.5 s a +12 dB sustain moves it 0.31 dB once, the mid band's −38 dB share settling from its high-pass's onset transient); neutral bit-exact (**met**). CPU (Release, gcc, 48 kHz, another build running): the module 31 → 176 ns per stereo sample at attack +6 / sustain −3 (+145 ns: 7 SVF sections per channel, three more shaper gains); the Low Latency strip, a CLI render of a 60 s stereo drum programme at `clarity.attack` 4, 2.46 → 2.88 s per minute of audio, i.e. +0.7 % of one core (about 4.1 → 4.8 % including file I/O and the loudness analysis). Tests: two cases in `tests/test_transient_shaper.cpp`, four in `tests/test_clarity.cpp` (and the allocation and robustness cases run the bands), one in `tests/test_transparency.cpp`; `tests/test_rtsan.cpp` asserts `setSpeed`, `setHoldMs` and `isBandPathActive`. Golden files: `parameter-defaults.json` gains `clarity.attackLow` and `clarity.attackHigh` (0), and all 30 factory `contentHash` values move (the hash covers every parameter); no render, render-baseline entry, KnownGap or quality-ratchet value moves. Not in this step: (4) the macro remap (Punch / Footsteps / Detail to `attackHigh`, Impact to `attackLow`) with the +2 dB Punch row and the explosion-tail row, and (5) Quality's look-ahead. Known limits (03 §5.9): any non-zero offset also brings the bands' timing and all-pass phase to the other bands, and each band shaper lifts what leaks through its LR4 slope as fully as its own content. *Review (p3b5):* `parameter-defaults.json` re-recorded with the two keys (and E28's two, the same layout version 8, appended after them), every factory `contentHash` re-recorded once for both units.
- **Status (p3b5, app card).** `clarity.attackLow` and `clarity.attackHigh` are on the Clarity card of the module rack (*Attack Low*, *Attack High*), bound by their keys (`ui/ModuleCard.cpp` table: a key the core lacks is left out of its card); `tests/app/test_app_ui_guards.cpp`, screenshot state `module-keys`. No sound moves.
- **Status (p3b5, steps 4–5).** Step 4 done; step 5 done in the module, not yet in the chain. In `MacroMap.cpp` (table rows), `ClarityEnhancer.*`, `TransientShaper.*` ([03 §5.3.6](03-dsp-design.md#536-the-macros-the-one-pole-application-and-the-look-ahead-11-e04-steps-45)). **Remap:** Music Punch keeps its +6 dB attack and adds `clarity.attackHigh` +2.5 dB (0–100 %), so any Punch runs the 3-band path; Gaming Footsteps (engaging Clarity) and Detail add `clarity.attackHigh` +2 dB each (+2.5 / +2.0 tipped Night Mode's −24 LUFS track change over its 1 s settle row, 0 → 2.34 s); Impact's +4 dB moves from `clarity.attack` to `clarity.attackLow` (and its static bass rows became [E20](#e20)'s punch); Boost's rows are unchanged. **The band path's application:** step 3 applied the band gains to the LR4 bands, whose sum is an all-pass with about 3 ms of group delay under 100 Hz; with every gain at 1 it took 2.4 dB off the E59 kick's first 10 ms, so Punch 100 through it read onset minus body −0.96 dB, worse than the full band. The gains now apply to complementary one-pole bands (LP1 at the split, HP1 at 4 kHz of the rest, the remainder), which sum to the input exactly; the low band's detector is LR2 (two one-poles) so its gain is not late on the one-pole band (hits 75 ms apart, 60 Hz: 4.74 dB over the first 10 ms with LR4, 9.81 with LR2); the mid and high band shapers ignore the first 1.5 dB of an onset (steady noise at +12 dB 0.23 / 0.53 → 0.00 / 0.00 dB). **Done-when:** Punch 100 kick onset (`tests/test_known_gaps.cpp`, now *KnownGap closed*): 0–10 / 10–30 ms 5.38 / 4.70 → 5.43 / 2.52 dB, onset minus body +0.68 → **+2.91 dB (≥ +2 met)**; a 20 ms step above 3 kHz 150 ms into an explosion's tail (peak −12 dBFS) at Gaming Boost 100 + Impact 100, isolated / under the tail 11.27 / 6.05 → 7.67 / 7.18 dB, **−5.22 → −0.49 dB (within 1 dB met**; new *KnownGap closed* case; the rest is the maximizer's gain reduction on the tail, −1.41 dB at a −6 dBFS explosion, CLI). Step 3's rows re-measured against the input: hits 75 ms apart 9.81 / 10.56 / 10.06 dB (≥ +9 met), 40–60 ms 0.25 / 0.00 / 0.00 dB, the steady 40 Hz note 0.012 dB, neutral bit-exact; a 1 kHz pluck with only the low band at +12 dB 0.10 → 0.60 dB (the one-pole bands overlap like shelves; 03 §5.9). **Step 5:** `ClarityEnhancer::setLookaheadMs` (structural, 0–5 ms): the detectors read x[n], the gains land on x[n − L] in both paths, the module reports L as its latency (neutral: the input delayed, bit for bit); 1 ms, +12 dB attack, first millisecond of an onset 4.18 → 8.90 dB (full band) and 9.50 → 11.85 dB (high band), first 10 ms within 0.5 dB (*Clarity (E04 step 5): ...*). Not set by the chain: it would raise Quality from 1352 to 1400 samples at 48 kHz, which `tests/test_engine.cpp`, `tests/test_neural_slot.cpp` and 01 §5.1 pin (not this unit's files); wiring it is `clarity.setLookaheadMs (1.0f)` in `prepare()`'s Quality case with those numbers (with E02's 2 ms bass look-ahead, 1496), and the plug-in's PDC then follows `getLatencySamples()`. CPU: the band path 176 → 174 ns per stereo sample (full band 31; Release gcc, a build running), the look-ahead within noise. Tests: *Gaming Impact (M3): no static bass boost ...* (`tests/test_modes.cpp`), *Clarity (E04 step 5): ...*, the two E04 cases above; step 3's cases updated for the application (the band sum reference is now the input). **Sound moved, for the review to re-record** (render diff against the base: 824 values of the 21 presets that use Punch, Footsteps, Impact or Detail; the other 9 bit-identical): music presets with Punch −0.1 … −0.3 LU (the bands read fewer of a dense mix's small onsets than the full-band shaper did), gaming presets up to ±0.9 LU on the ambush programme; golden renders of 20 presets (integrated up to −0.20 LU: Rock & Metal −17.10 → −17.30, Signature −16.67 → −16.83 LUFS) and their `preset-render-baseline.json` entries; the 21 presets' `intent` blocks re-measured (`presets/factory`); the E60 scene matrix re-pinned for every gaming row (`tests/test_scenes.cpp`: the combat's event change at −40 LUFS +0.3 … +0.5 dB where Footsteps / Detail lift the shots' HF onsets, Cinematic Adventure's step contrast +0.2 → −0.4 dB now that Impact's attack is the low band's); KnownGap pins re-based with their cause (`tests/test_known_gaps.cpp`: E19 isolated bursts 5.34 → 5.85 dB, the gunfire lift +0.22 / +0.25 / +0.05 → −0.18 / +0.32 / −0.44 dB, E21 Night Mode ambush event −10.31 → −11.10 dB, E02 bass line with the split 2.95 → 2.26 dB, E10 NaN-burst span 510 → 458 ms, E07's Off tilt 4.06 → 4.39 dB, an artefact of E20: its 200 Hz – 1 kHz reference lost Impact's shelf skirt). Moved within their pins, reported: E21's first combat after 10 s of quiet, Night Mode at −24 LUFS 1.75 → 2.01 dB (a pinned KnownGap, worse by 0.26 dB) and at −14 / −40 LUFS 0.42 / 0.39 → 0.70 / 0.59 dB. Release note: every preset with Punch, Footsteps, Impact or Detail sounds different; the owner tests by ear. *Review (p3b5):* attributed and re-recorded once. A tree with only the E04 / E20 macro rows and the chain's `impactPunch` reverted renders all 150 factory renders bit-identical to the batch 4 baseline (0 dB tolerance), so every move is this remap: 824 values at 0.1 dB (1914 at 0 dB) of the same 21 presets, `preset-render-baseline.json` re-recorded; golden renders of 21 presets re-recorded in `factory-presets.json` (integrated −0.20 … +0.05 LU: Rock & Metal −17.097 → −17.297, Signature −16.674 → −16.832, Racing −16.423 → −16.564, Laptop Speakers −17.216 → −17.337, R&B −16.450 → −16.552 LUFS, the others within 0.08 LU; Night Mode's within 0.001 LU). One pin the unit had not listed, found in review and re-based with its cause: *KnownGap closed: hot master …* (`test_known_gaps.cpp`), Signature limiter > 1 dB with the preamp off / on 18.93 / 6.93 → 17.33 / 7.20 %, clip energy −41.53 / −50.78 → −40.91 / −50.20 dB, Punchy Pop 50.13 / 7.47 → 43.47 / 6.93 %, −38.65 / −54.87 → −36.47 / −50.17 dB (Punch's `attackHigh` runs the 3-band path; the reverted tree gives the old values; the preamp and the hot-programme rows are unchanged, 0.53 / 0.00 % ≤ 2 %). Offline soak (`soak.py --only user`, 2 minutes per row): 12 clicks against batch 4's 10; the two new ones are in `user-night` at 107.34 s (−40.4 dB, read 22 ms after a bypass switch): with the bypass held off the wet path shows it at −31 dB on a kick onset 0.8 s after the automation set Punch 79 %, and it goes away with only Punch's `attackHigh` row removed, so it is the high band shaper's fast onset lift (0.25 ms gain smoothing) read as a discontinuity; left for the soak's click triage (a slower high-band smoothing would move every Punch render again). Step 5 in the chain (Quality's look-ahead and the plug-in's PDC) remains open.
- **Status (local, Windows, step 5 in the chain).** Done in `ProcessingChain::prepare()`: the Quality profile sets Clarity's 1 ms look-ahead (0 in Balanced and Low Latency), so an onset's attack lift is in place when it arrives in Quality, and the plug-in's PDC follows through `getLatencySamples()`. Latency, Quality: 1352 → 1400 samples at 48 kHz (28.17 → 29.17 ms; 44.1 kHz 31.20 ms, 96 kHz 28.25 ms, 192 kHz 27.79 ms; app engine 1420 → 1468); Balanced and Low Latency unchanged. Render diff (`tools/scripts/preset-render-diff.py`, MSVC, before → after on the same build): only the six presets that suggest Quality move (Audiophile Subtle, Classical & Jazz Dynamic, Lo-Fi Chill, Orchestral Film, Podcast & Voice, Warm Vinyl): latency +48 on all 30 of their renders; over 0.1 dB, Lo-Fi Chill on *ambush* true peak −10.95 → −11.84 dBTP, integrated −27.86 → −28.03 LUFS, THD+N max −32.84 → −33.97 dB, Warm Vinyl *music* THD+N max −41.24 → −42.22 dB and *ambush* true peak −7.02 → −7.25 dBTP, Podcast & Voice *ambush* clip energy −27.33 → −26.99 dB; 14 more values 0.11 – 0.17 dB, all others within 0.1 dB. `tests/golden/preset-render-baseline.json`: those 30 renders re-based by the measured change (the gcc values plus the MSVC before → after delta; a Linux gcc re-record should confirm them; confirmed on CI's gcc leg on 2026-10-08, every value equal, [E59](#e59)). The golden renders (Balanced) do not move. The bass engine's 2 ms look-ahead ([E02](#e02)) stays unset: it acts only with `bass.splitProtect`, which no factory preset turns on.
- **Status (local, Windows, owner decision 2026-10-06: Punch at high Boost).** Done in `MacroMap.cpp` (`MacroMap::punchHighBoostScale`, [03 §5.3.6](03-dsp-design.md#536-the-macros-the-one-pole-application-and-the-look-ahead-11-e04-steps-45)). The owner keeps Punch as it is at normal Boost (Boost 40 + Punch 100 on deadmau5 "The Veldt" sounds right) and wants the onset ticks he hears at Boost 100 + Punch 100 on music gone; speech ticks at medium Boost (45 – 60 %) are accepted. What Punch's two attack rows add (`clarity.attack` +6 dB, `clarity.attackHigh` +2.5 dB) is now multiplied by 1 − smoothstep (0.60, 0.70, Boost): exactly 1 up to Boost 60 % (bit-identical), 0.5 at 65 %, 0 from 70 %. Boost's own attack row (+2 dB) and the Smart multiplier are unchanged; Gaming (Footsteps / Detail `attackHigh`) is not scaled. Why that start and floor (the soak's scenes, seed 6, a fresh chain per render, defaults + Boost and Punch only, clicks by `DiscontinuityDetector`): (1) the ticks are the maximizer's gain turning hard on the lifted onsets: with Boost's `max.drive` row off they are gone (Music / Loud at Boost 72 / 92 / 100, Punch 100: 6 / 5 / 4 and 3 / 4 / 3 → 0 / 0 / 0 and 0 / 2 / 0), the clipper is not it (`max.clip` 0 leaves them); (2) the main row is `clarity.attack`: fading only `attackHigh` to 0 by Boost 100 leaves the Music scene's clicks (Boost 100, Punch 30 – 100: 1 / 3 / 9 / 2), while `attack` at 0 with `attackHigh` at 25 – 50 % is clean at Boost 80 – 92; (3) any share left at Boost 100 still clicks (both rows at 5 – 15 %, or `attackHigh` alone at 25 – 50 %: 2 clicks), so the floor is 0 for both; (4) the Loud scene needs the rows at ≤ 50 % by Boost 66 % (100 % / 75 %: 7 / 7 clicks at Punch 77; 50 %: 0) and about 0 by 72 % (15 %: 2), so the fade ends at 70 % (ending it at 75 % leaves 7 clicks at Boost 66 + Punch 100, at 100 % 9 at Boost 66 + Punch 77). Before → after: Music scene Boost 100 + Punch 100 4 → 0, Boost 72 + Punch 77 2 → 0; Loud scene Boost 72 + Punch 77 9 → 0, Boost 100 + Punch 100 3 → 3 (= Boost 100 alone: Boost's own clicks in the Loud scene from 75 %, 2 / 5 / 1 / 2 / 3 at Boost 75 / 80 / 85 / 92 / 100, a known gap that is not Punch's); over the grid Boost 66 – 100 × Punch 30 – 100 the Music scene reads 0 except Boost 92 (1, also with Punch 0) and the Loud scene 0 up to Boost 72, Boost alone's count from 75 %. Speech scene Boost 66 + Punch 53: 8 → 4 clicks (accepted). Kick onset lift of Punch 100 over Punch 0, maximizer on: Boost 60 +2.99 dB (unchanged), 65 +2.07 dB, 100 0.00 dB (Boost 100 + Punch 100 renders as Boost 100 alone). No factory preset sets Boost above 60 %: `preset-render-diff.py` (MSVC, before → after on the same build) reads all 150 renders identical, and the golden renders do not move; nothing re-based. Tests: *MacroMap (E04 / E53, owner decision 2026-10-06): Music Punch's attack rows are bit-identical up to Boost 60 % …* (`tests/test_engine.cpp`), *Punch at high Boost (E04 / E53, owner decision 2026-10-06): Punch 100's kick onset lift …* (`tests/test_known_gaps.cpp`), *KnownGap closed: Music Punch at high Boost - the soak's Music scene …* and *KnownGap: Music Punch at high Boost - the soak's Loud scene …* (`tests/test_soak.cpp`). For the owner: above Boost 70 % the Punch knob no longer changes the sound in Music (Boost's own +2 dB attack stays); a listen at Boost 100 + Punch 100 on "The Veldt" should confirm the ticks are gone.
- **Status (owner-verified, 2026-10-06, Turtle Beach Stealth 600PC Gen 3, USB dongle, Windows 11).** After the high-Boost fade, The Veldt (deadmau5) at Boost 100 + Punch 100: "sounds a little better"; what remains at Boost 100 is the maximizer's own ticks on loud programme (Punch 0 gives them too, docs/11 E53); **owner decision:** leave those as they are (Boost 100 is the extreme setting). Owner observation: Punch lifts the kick more than the snare (the mid band's onset floor holds back a snare inside a busy mix); Punch is kept as is.
- **What.** Rebuild the Clarity transient shaper in 3 LR4 bands (adjustable 60–200 Hz split, 4 kHz), with per-band attack/sustain, a 1–3 ms HF hold (25 ms anti-ripple hold for LF only), program-dependent release and a speed control, plus optional look-ahead in Quality. Add a signed LF attack for tactile punch in the 40–100 Hz chest region. Fix `BassEngine` Tighten so it never attenuates the first 20 ms, and remove Tighten from the Music Punch macro first (Phase 2 carry-in, merged with the [E59](#e59) slice's kick-onset metric, since it re-voices every preset that uses Punch).
- **Why.** Music: Punch smears the attack into the body, and attack boosts arrive late. Gaming: HF step onsets lose lift while an explosion rings, and automatic fire and fast percussion get about a third of the set effect (+12 dB attack gives +11.7 dB on isolated hits but +3.8 dB at 75 ms spacing).
- **Expected benefit.** Re-measured on a synthetic kick (max off): Punch 100 gives +3.7 dB at 0–10 ms but +4.8 dB at 10–30 ms, so onset-to-body gets about 1 dB worse; `clarity.attack=6` alone gives +5.1 / +4.5 / +2.3 dB at 0–10 / 10–30 / 40–60 ms (the smear is the full-band shaper itself); `bass.tighten=0.5` costs −2.2 dB at 0–10 ms. The earlier "−8.9 dB" did not reproduce in size. Gaming benefit is overstated: Footsteps (M1) and Detail (M4) do not touch the transient shaper at all; only Boost and Impact drive `ClarityAttackDb`, so HF-band gaming mappings are new, not remaps. A signed LF "tactile" control is a real feature gap against Razer/Nahimic-style punch sliders.
- **Approach.** (1) Remove the M1 `BassTighten` entry in `MacroMap.cpp`, re-baseline factory-preset and mode tests. (2) Tighten: gate the sustain indicator until the attack indicator has decayed. (3) `ClarityEnhancer` stage 1: 3 bands with per-band `TransientShaper` and `PeakHold`; keep `clarity.attack/sustain` as "all bands" and add `attackLow`/`attackHigh` offsets (no preset migration, plug-in automation stays valid); bypass the splitter when neutral to keep the bit-exact transparency contract. (4) New M1/M4 entries to `attackHigh` (+2–3 dB), Impact to `attackLow`. (5) Quality-only look-ahead.
- **Depends on.** [E02](#e02)(b) if the LF split is shared; the Tighten fix touches the shaper shared by Bass and Clarity.
- **Done when.** Punch 100: 0–10 ms lift ≥ 10–30 ms lift + 2 dB. +12 dB attack at 75 ms spacing ≥ +9 dB. Gain peak within 1 ms of onset, no bump > 1 dB at 40–60 ms. Tighten 0.5: 0–10 ms change ≥ −0.5 dB. Neutral settings bit-exact (`test_transparency.cpp`). Steady 40 Hz modulation ≤ 0.1 dB with bands at ±12 dB. HF step lift under an explosion tail within 1 dB of isolated at Boost 100 + Impact 100.
- **Evidence.** `TransientShaper.cpp` `kHoldMs 25`, `kAttackPairReleaseMs 60`, `kGainSmoothMs 1`, test "detection is linked"; [BassEngine.cpp][BE] `kTightenHz 150`; [ClarityEnhancer.cpp][CE] stage 1 (one linked full-band gain); [MacroMap.cpp][MM] `kMusicTable` M1, `kGamingTable`.

<a id="e09"></a>
#### E09 · Professional EQ toolset: more bands, analog-matched response, and dynamic-EQ knee, relative threshold and sidechain

*Audio quality · benefit 3 · difficulty High · 7–10 engineer-weeks for the kept scope*

- **What.** Phase A: 16 user EQ bands with add/remove in the editor; a per-EQ "Classic (RBJ) / Analog-matched" response switch (Vicanek matched biquads mapped to the SVF); 6 dB/oct and tilt shelves; per-band L/R and M/S targets. Phase B: after [E07](#e07) frees the mode bands, 8 user DynEq bands with a soft knee, RMS/peak blend, relative (versus broadband) thresholds and optional 1–3 ms look-ahead in Quality/Balanced; a per-band sidechain from {self, M, S, Chat strip}. Linear phase and a soothe-style resonance mode are cut.
- **Why.** Music: top-octave air and correction bells land short of nominal above 12 kHz, and 10 shared bands are tight for correction plus taste. Gaming: a Chat-keyed band enables voice-aware unmasking.
- **Expected benefit.** Recomputed from `SvfCoeffs::make`: a 12 kHz Q1 +6 dB bell reads +3.14 / +1.37 dB at 15 / 17 kHz at 44.1 kHz (analog +4.95 / +3.95 dB); a 16 kHz bell reads +1.72 dB at 19 kHz (analog +5.32). The audible payoff is small: the curve editor already draws the exact digital response, the error sits above about 13 kHz, and headphone correction must stay Classic anyway ([E15](#e15)). 16 bands matter mainly if E15 does not ship; with E15, 10 taste bands matches FxSound and Boom 3D. The DynEq knee, relative threshold and Chat sidechain have real gaming value.
- **Approach.** `param::kEqBands` 10 → 16 with `sinceVersion = 2` so AUv2 automation order is stable (plug-in IDs are keyed by `Info::key`); decide ownership of slots 10–15 with [E14](#e14) first. Matched designs mapped to Simper (g, k, m0, m1, m2) with double storage; Classic stays bit-identical (the current bell and shelves are exactly the RBJ cookbook). First-order shelves and tilt as pole-zero-cancelled SVF sections. Type-change crossfade over the existing 5 ms window (low priority). DynEq knee in `computeDynamicGainDb`. Sidechain: `MixEngine::process` already holds every strip's input block, so pass a const pointer to the Chat input with a defined strip order. Look-ahead is profile-gated and structural.
- **Depends on.** [E07](#e07) before 8 user DynEq bands; [E14](#e14) over slots 10–15; [E15](#e15) needs Classic RBJ-exact; the in-flight engine swap for look-ahead changes.
- **Done when.** Matched within 0.5 dB of analog for fc ≤ 0.35 fs and Q ≤ 4 (1 dB elsewhere below 0.45 fs) at 44.1/48/96 kHz. Classic bit-exact against golden coefficients and within 0.01 dB of an independent RBJ implementation. A v1 preset loads into 16 bands with identical sound (null ≤ −120 dB). Relative threshold invariant within 0.5 dB to ±20 dB broadband changes. A Chat-keyed band attenuates Game 3 kHz only while Chat is above threshold. Blind ABX Classic vs Matched on a 12 kHz shelf; if inaudible, de-prioritise Matched.
- **Evidence.** [Parameters.h][PARh] `kEqBands = 10`, `kDynEqBands = 4` against `ParametricEq::kMaxBands = 16`; [Svf.h][SVF] `SvfCoeffs::make()` pre-warps at fc only; [DynamicEq.cpp][DEQ] `computeDynamicGainDb` (no knee); [03 §3.6](03-dsp-design.md) "no look-ahead", §2.9 5 ms dry flash; [MixEngine.cpp][ME] `process`.

<a id="e11"></a>
#### E11 · Automatic headroom management (engine-side OS volume split out)

*Audio quality · benefit 3 · difficulty Medium · 6–10 engineer-days (engine-side volume, if pursued: 5–8 weeks, Low)*

- **Status (Phase 2 batch 1).** Predictor partially done, as the static-boost predictor [E15](#e15) reuses. Before: no prediction and no automatic preamp anywhere. After: `headroom::predictMaxBoost` (`core/include/flub/dsp/DeviceCorrection.h`) evaluates any response on the 1/12-octave grid from 20 Hz to 20 kHz and refines every local maximum by golden-section search, with Flat or Programme weighting (a heuristic envelope: −6 dB/oct below 40 Hz, flat to 1 kHz, −3 dB/oct above) and `preampDb (prediction, allowance)` = −max(0, max − allowance). Measured (`tests/test_device_correction.cpp`): a +6 dB Q 1 bell predicts 6.000 dB at 1 kHz; a Q 12 bell a quarter grid step off the grid is found within 0.01 dB; with the predicted preamp no steady sine through a 10-filter correction grows by more than 0.005 dB. It sets the device correction's preamp (allowance 0). Open: the chain-wide prediction (EQ, bass shelf, presence / air, DynEq static gains, macro contributions after `MacroMap::apply`, the virtualiser trim) and the `auto.preamp` / `auto.preampAllowance` parameters through `inputGain` before AutoLevel, which need `ProcessingChain` / `MacroMap`; "Limiter active x %" in the header and LoudnessPanel; every Done-when measurement (hot masters, the classical LRA, predicted within 1 dB of the rendered pink transfer maximum for every factory preset at Boost 0/50/100, the blind comparison).
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2).** Chain-wide preamp partially done in `ProcessingChain.*`, `Parameters.*` and `headroom::predictMaxBoostWith` (`DeviceCorrection.h`), [03 §14.11](03-dsp-design.md#1411-automatic-preamp-chain). Before: the predictor served only the device correction; nothing in a strip predicted or took off its boosts. After: `auto.preamp` (off by default, so every preset keeps its sound) and `auto.preampAllowance` (1 dB), layout version 3. The chain builds its static-boost model from the effective values after `MacroMap::apply` (EQ bands and output, dynamic-EQ static gains, bass shelf at full boost with the subsonic, presence at full lift, air shelf, saturation make-up, the surround folds' −3 dB trim; the bass boost before the governor scales it) and predicts its programme-weighted maximum on the audio thread without allocation when an input changes (at most every 10 ms, about 45 µs); preamp = −max(0, prediction − allowance), 20 ms ramp. Placement differs from Approach's "through `inputGain`": the preamp sits after the dry reference and the input meters, i.e. after AutoLevel's detector (through `inputGain` AutoLevel would cancel it, and the bypass reference and input meters would carry it); with AutoLevel off it is the same broadband gain. Measured (before → after = preamp off → on, same build): the E59-style hot master of `tests/test_known_gaps.cpp` (pink + 55 Hz kicks, −9.2 LUFS, −0.35 dBTP): Signature (prediction +1.87 dB, preamp −0.87 dB) limiter > 1 dB 17.9 → 7.5 % of the time, mean GR −0.81 → −0.47 dB, loudest clip energy −44.4 → −53.1 dB, −10.24 → −10.72 LUFS; Punchy Pop (+2.91 / −1.91 dB) 49.3 → 6.9 %, −1.17 → −0.44 dB, −36.5 → −48.8 dB, −9.74 → −10.76 LUFS. CLI on a −9.8 LUFS drum-programme master: Punchy Pop 32.7 → 8.2 %, clip energy −20.2 → −28.2 dB; Signature 11.0 → 5.7 %; Bass Head 16.2 → 0.2 %, −24.2 → −73.6 dB; Cinematic Adventure 9.4 → 0.4 %; Bluetooth Headphones 78.3 → 11.6 %; Club Loud 99.8 → 97.3 % (clipper active 47 → 14 %); presets without static boosts unchanged; Classical & Jazz stays at 94 % because that master's −0.35 dBTP peaks exceed its −1 dBTP ceiling, which no preamp for boosts addresses. Prediction against the rendered transfer of −24 dBFS mono pink, every factory preset at Boost 0 / 50 / 100 (maximizer and AutoLevel out): within 1 dB for 15 of 25 presets with the compressor bypassed, 1.1 – 2.2 dB over where presence (modelled at full lift) sets the maximum, −1.6 / −1.1 dB under where crossfeed sums LF on centred content (Classical & Jazz, Earbuds); with the compressor in, fixed make-up reads up to 6.4 dB over (Voice Chat). Preset render diff: all 125 renders unchanged and 100 CLI renders sample-identical with the preamp off; golden files: the five new keys and every factory `contentHash` (the hash covers every parameter) move, the golden renders do not (re-recorded in review). Tests: `tests/test_parameters_headroom.cpp`, *KnownGap: hot master …*. Done-when: limiter active ≤ 2 % not met (7.5 / 6.9 %: the presets' Boost drive, which is loudness on purpose, and the allowance); the within-1-dB row partially met (above); the intro / loud-section delta, the classical LRA and the blind comparison not measured. Open: a compressor-aware (make-up minus expected GR) and level-aware presence term, the crossfeed's mono sum; switching `auto.preamp` on in factory presets (a re-voicing, with the numbers above); "Limiter active x %" in the header and LoudnessPanel and an app control for `auto.preamp` (the plug-in already exposes both keys as host parameters, layout version 3).
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2, app).** Readouts and the app control done in `ui/LoudnessPanel.*`, `ui/SettingsDialog.*` and `ui/BoostPanel.*`. Before: the app showed neither the preamp nor how often the limiter works, and `auto.preamp` had no app control. After: the loudness panel's **LIM** is the share of about the last 10 s with the maximizer's limiter more than 1 dB down (the E11 metric's threshold; an exponential average of the per-frame reading: > 0.95 after 40 s of limiting, < 0.05 after 40 s without, 0.50 ± 0.06 alternating by the second), **PRE** the preamp (`ProcessingChain::getAutoPreampDb`, *off* while `auto.preamp` is off), and an active-now chip *Preamp −1.9 dB*; Settings › Processing has **Automatic preamp on the <strip> strip** (the selected strip's parameter, saved with presets) with the live predicted boost. Open: "Limiter active x %" in the header (its readout column is full), the factory re-voicing with the preamp on, and the Done-when rows listed above.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, hot programme).** The ≤ 2 % row met with a new opt-in term, measured why `auto.preamp` alone could not (`ProcessingChain.*`, `Parameters.*`; [03 §14.11](03-dsp-design.md#1411-automatic-preamp-chain) *Hot programme*). Why not before: on the pinned hot master (−9.2 LUFS, −0.35 dBTP) the static preamp leaves in the 1 dB allowance, the presets' maximizer drive (Boost 35 / 40 → 0.3 / 0.8 dB, and Punchy Pop's Loudness 25 another 1.6 dB) and Punch's onset lift (transient attack), and the master's peaks are already 0.65 dB over the −1 dBTP ceiling, so any of them is limiting (CLI on a −10.1 LUFS copy: allowance 0 took Signature 4.3 → 0.3 % but left Punchy Pop at 4.0 %; with Punch at 0 it read 0.0 %). New: `auto.preampHot` (toggle, off by default, layout version 5, only with `auto.preamp`): the chain holds the input's sample peak (2 s hold, 1 dB/s release) and takes hot = −clamp (peak + gain − ceiling, 0, gain) off as well, gain = what the allowance leaves in + the applied `max.drive` + ½ × `clarity.attack`; programme with room is bit-identical. Before → after (`auto.preamp` → + `auto.preampHot`, the pinned case, renamed *KnownGap closed: hot master ...*): limiter > 1 dB **Signature 6.93 → 0.53 %**, **Punchy Pop 7.47 → 0.00 %** (row met; preamp off 18.93 / 50.13 %), mean GR −0.43 → −0.04 / −0.44 → −0.02 dB, loudness −10.69 → −12.05 / −10.75 → −13.59 LUFS (the Boost loudness it takes back). The intro / loud-section row is not met and this term makes it worse: a copy of the master with an intro 8 dB down (mean-square over 3 s of each) moves loud − intro by −1.52 dB with the preamp off, −1.33 dB with `auto.preamp`, −2.58 dB with `auto.preampHot` (Punchy Pop −2.76 / −2.23 / −4.60 dB): a causal preamp can only lower the loud section once its peaks arrive, and even with the limiter idle the level-dependent stages (glue, dynamic EQ) move the delta by about 1 dB. No factory preset sets either key, so no render moves (125 of 125 identical at 0.0 dB). Tests: `tests/test_preamp_hot.cpp` (layout, bit-identity with room and without `auto.preamp`, a −0.3 dBFS sine: preamp −5 → −8 dB, GR −3.75 → −0.75 dB, hold and release), the KnownGap case. Open: an app control for `auto.preampHot` (the plug-in exposes it as a host parameter, layout version 5), the intro / loud-section delta (needs look-ahead or a per-track learned peak), the within-1-dB row's compressor-aware and level-aware presence terms and the crossfeed's mono sum, the classical LRA check, the blind comparison, and switching either key on in factory presets (a re-voicing). *Review (Phase 3 batch 2):* `auto.preampHot` had no plain-language hint, which failed E39's every-key hint test; added (`ui/ParamHints.cpp`). `tests/golden/parameter-defaults.json` re-recorded with the key (off).
- **Status (p3b3, app).** The app remainders are closed: Settings › Processing has *... also on hot programme (<strip> strip)* right under *Automatic preamp on the <strip> strip*, the selected strip's `auto.preampHot` (saved with presets, off by default), dimmed while `auto.preamp` is off since it acts only with it; *Limiter active x %* is the loudness panel's LIM readout (the header's readout column is full), spelled out in its tooltip (*Limiter active 12 % of the last 10 s ...*, `LoudnessPanel::describeLimiterActive`). Before: `auto.preampHot` only in the plug-in host's parameter list and the generic grid. Tested in `tests/app/test_app_ui_guards.cpp` and `test_app_ui_status.cpp`; the screenshot state `contour-curve` shows both preamp switches on. No sound, golden or parameter moves. Open (DSP / product, unchanged): the intro / loud-section delta, the within-1-dB row's compressor-aware and level-aware presence terms and the crossfeed's mono sum, the classical LRA check, the blind comparison, either key in factory presets (a re-voicing).
- **Status (p3b5, predictor terms and the classical LRA).** Done for the within-1-dB and classical-LRA rows, in `ProcessingChain.*` (the headroom model; the mode bands' parameters factored into `modeBandParams()`, same values), [03 §14.11](03-dsp-design.md#1411-automatic-preamp-chain). New in the static-boost model: the compressor's net gain (make-up minus the reduction its curve gives the programme's held sidechain peak, mixed; upward section included), presence at the programme's level in both laws (Absolute on the band's level, Relative on its balance over the body: level-invariant, as [E07](#e07) step 3 made it), the Bs2b / Meier crossfeed's sum on a centred source, the dynamic EQ's dynamic gains (user and mode bands), the bass protection's withdrawal, the bass harmonics and Small Speaker Mode's high-pass. The level terms read the programme through the stages before them, with crests fitted on 20 Hz-high-passed pink through each module alone; the programme level is the input's loud-part loudness (3 s, held 30 s, released at 0.1 dB/s, −18 dBFS until measured), `getModelProgrammeDb()`. The preamp's own model keeps presence, the dynamic EQ and the bass protection at a quiet programme's full size (their detectors withdraw only after an onset has passed) and takes the compressor at the programme's level. Measured (same tree with and without this change; centred 20 Hz-high-passed pink, maximizer out, Auto Level off, flat weighting, 30 presets × Boost 0 / 50 / 100): predicted maximum within 1 dB of the rendered transfer maximum at −24 dBFS **59 → 90 of 90** (worst 8.11 → 0.88 dB; Done-when row met, one case per preset in `tests/test_parameters_headroom.cpp`), at −30 dBFS 52 → 90 of 90, at −18 dBFS 50 → 89 of 90 (worst 1.08 dB, Orchestral & Film Boost 100 at 25 Hz), at −14 dBFS 21 → 84 of 90 (worst 1.78 dB: the saturator's compression of hot programme, not modelled). Classical row: a −17 LUFS classical stand-in (7 sections over 27 dB, LRA 19.60 LU, −2.5 dBTP) through Classical & Jazz Dynamic keeps **19.59 LU with `auto.preamp` on** (19.56 LU off; row met, *Chain (E11) Done-when: a −17 LUFS classical stand-in ...* in `tests/test_known_gaps.cpp`).
