The preamp at −18 dBFS moves where the new terms act: Podcast & Voice 0 → −2.58 dB and Voice Chat 0 → −1.11 dB (their make-up), Late Night −6.42 → −1.40 dB and Racing −3.11 → −1.78 dB at Boost 100 (the compressor's reduction), Classical & Jazz −4.77 → −6.07 dB and Earbuds −5.53 → −6.83 dB (the crossfeed's centred sum); Signature and Punchy Pop unchanged (−0.87 / −1.91 dB on the hot master, whose ≤ 2 % rows keep their values). `auto.preamp` is off in every factory preset: the preset render diff moves nothing (150 of 150 renders at 0.0 dB); no parameter, golden file or render baseline entry moves. KnownGap pins that move (improvements, cause: the crossfeed's centred sum now in the preamp; `tests/test_factory_presets.cpp`, outside this unit's files, to re-pin in review): *Classical & Jazz ... with auto.preamp on loses 1.42 LU* → 0.72 LU (limiter > 1 dB 59.1 → 22.7 %), *... with auto.preamp + auto.preampHot loses 0.97 LU* → 0.44 LU (45.2 → 0.5 %); both still over E14's ≤ 0.3 LU row. The hot master case's printed prediction at the nominal level moves 1.87 → 1.56 dB (Signature) and 2.91 → 2.27 dB (Punchy Pop), its pinned values do not. Cost: two models while `auto.preamp` is on, 20 – 50 µs each plus about 25 µs per prediction, only when an input changes. Open: the intro / loud-section delta, the blind comparison, `auto.preamp` in factory presets (a re-voicing), the saturator's compression of hot programme. *Review (p3b5):* the two pins in `tests/test_factory_presets.cpp` re-based to 0.72 / 0.44 LU with their cause (the cases renamed: *… loses 0.72 LU …* and *… loses 0.44 LU …*); still KnownGaps against the ≤ 0.3 LU row.
- **What.** Predict the chain's static maximum boost (EQ, bass shelf, presence/air, DynEq static gains, macro contributions after `MacroMap::apply`, the virtualiser's −3 dB trim) on a 1/12-octave grid weighted by a programme-spectrum envelope, and apply a matching automatic preamp before the chain through `inputGain`'s 20 ms smoother (`auto.preamp`, `auto.preampAllowance` default 1 dB). Show "Limiter active x %" in the header and LoudnessPanel.
- **Why.** On hot modern masters the −1 dBTP limiter eats the enhancement, and hot game mixes lose transient detail the same way. Peace/Equalizer APO users set a preamp by hand.
- **Expected benefit.** Narrower than first scored. On a genre-typical classical programme (−17.3 LUFS, LRA 20.2 LU, −0.5 dBTP) Classical & Jazz Dynamic keeps LRA 20.19 LU and the limiter never engages: that preset is already a pure safety limiter. The 1.35 LU loss came from a −10.54 LUFS pop master. The real gain is on hot masters and game mixes, where EQ, bass and clarity boosts drive the limiter (Signature raises a quiet intro +0.79 dB and lowers the loud section −1.09 dB). Moving OS volume into the engine has unclear benefit (on Linux the slider is already pre-chain on the `flubsound_*` sink; Windows is a monitoring path) and a high hearing risk, so it is split out.
- **Approach.** Predictor on the control thread, published as an atomic. With AutoLevel or the loudness target on, the preamp must come before the AutoLevel detector or AutoLevel cancels it. Keep `max.on` and drive 0 on dynamic presets: the CLI and plug-in have no MixEngine master limiter, so `max.on = 0` would remove the only ceiling. Allow a user override (the predictor under-predicts on bass-heavy content; Bass Head already sets `eq.output −1.5` by hand).
- **Depends on.** None for the preamp. [E15](#e15) reuses the predictor; [E14](#e14)'s LRA bounds depend on it.
- **Done when.** On a hot master (−10 to −9 LUFS, −0.3 dBTP), Signature and Punchy Pop show limiter active ≤ 2 % and the intro/loud-section delta within ±0.3 dB of input. The −17 LUFS classical LRA stays within 0.1 LU. Predicted maximum boost within 1 dB of the rendered pink transfer maximum for every factory preset at Boost 0/50/100. Loudness-matched blind comparison on 6 hot masters.
- **Evidence.** [factory presets][FAC] (`eq.output` 0 except Bass Head); [ProcessingChain.cpp][PC] `process` (inputGain before AutoLevel); [MeterBus.h][MB]; measurements above.

<a id="e17"></a>
#### E17 · Bluetooth awareness: transport and codec detection, per-codec policy, latency offset, and a hands-free speech policy

*Headsets · benefit 3 · difficulty Medium · slice 1–2 days; full 3.5–5 engineer-weeks · Quick win (slice)*

- **Status (Phase 1 batch).** Slice partly done in `ProcessingChain.cpp` (band 4 off at ≤ 32 kHz) and `DeviceProfiles.*` (hands-free up to 32 kHz, "wireless" no longer USB, BlueZ / A2DP / HFP names); Footsteps 100 3.2 kHz lift +10.0 dB at every rate (+7.0 dB over 1 kHz at 8 kHz) → −0.02 to −0.03 dB over 1 kHz at 8 / 16 / 32 kHz. Open (KNOWN_GAP): Competitive FPS still +2.4 to +2.5 dB (Clarity presence, voice band), the runtime hands-free override, `pw-dump` / pactl parsers and codec work.
- **What.** Slice: detection fixes (Bluetooth voice profiles at ≤ 32 kHz are hands-free; drop "wireless" from the USB tokens; Bluetooth evidence beats a profile's `typicalConnection`) plus a sample-rate rule in `configureModeBands` that clamps or disables gaming band 4 (3200 Hz) when fs ≤ 32 kHz. Then: a Linux transport and codec query from PipeWire node properties; per-codec ceiling and air policy; codec delay (labelled "about") in the latency readout; a hands-free runtime override (Footsteps, Spatial, Bass and virtualiser off, −3 dBTP); an automated version of [10 §5][10-5] test 6.
- **Why.** A Bluetooth Stealth Pro on Linux is treated as USB with a −1 dBTP ceiling, and on hands-free the Footsteps band adds a large lift into an already harsh narrowband link.
- **Expected benefit.** Mostly correctness at the edges. The wrong cap costs about 1 dB. The Stealth Pro is normally used through its 2.4 GHz base station, where "USB" is correct. On hands-free links the game sound is already poor; removing the +8.7 dB (Footsteps 100) / +13.9 dB (Competitive FPS) lift near 0.8 × Nyquist at 8 kHz makes it less harsh, not good. The slice is small and independent and ships in Phase 1 together with [E19](#e19)'s interim bell, which makes the band-4 lift constant. The runtime hands-free policy and the codec work are worth doing only after E19's redesign and [E21](#e21).
- **Approach.** Use a runtime override rather than loading a preset: it needs no snapshot/restore, survives a crash mid-call and does not fight the auto-profile switcher. Linux: `pactl` sink-input lookup fails under pipewire-jack (the multi-strip path), so resolve the sink through `pw-dump` links from Flubsound's JACK node and read `device.bus`, `api.bluez5.profile`, `api.bluez5.codec`; keep pactl (`bluetooth.codec`) as the PulseAudio fallback. Windows already classifies BTHENUM/BTHHFENUM, so the override works there without a codec read.
- **Depends on.** Slice: nothing. The detection fixes are table-driven in `DeviceProfiles.cpp`, and the band-4 clamp is computed in `configureModeBands` from the sample rate at prepare time. A rate change already re-prepares today, so the clamp adds no new structural transition. Full item: the in-flight auto profiles and engine swap (the runtime 48k/2 → 16k/1 hands-free switch is a stop/re-prepare that must be click-free and must not fight the switcher). [E18](#e18) reuses the hands-free state; [E24](#e24) disables focus under it.
- **Done when.** Table tests: "Stealth Pro" 48k/2 + Bluetooth hint → bluetooth, cap −2; "Stealth 600 Gen 3" 32k/1 → hands-free; "Wireless Headset" without hint → not USB. CLI at 8/16/32 kHz: 3.2 kHz lift from Footsteps 100 / Competitive FPS ≤ +1 dB. Parser tests on captured `pw-dump` and pactl JSON (PipeWire 1.0/1.2, PulseAudio 16) for SBC, AAC, LDAC, mSBC, LC3-SWB. Automated rate-switch test with no NaN and no over-ceiling samples. Manual check on a real headset under Fedora/PipeWire.
- **Evidence.** [PlatformServices_linux.cpp][PSL] `queryOutputTransport` returns Unknown; [DeviceProfiles.cpp][DP] `detectConnection` (hands-free only ≤ 16000 Hz; "wireless" → USB), `Database::match` fallback; [PlatformServices_win.cpp][PSW] BTHENUM classification; [08 A5][08-A5].

<a id="e18"></a>
#### E18 · Microphone strip, virtual mic and sidetone

*Headsets · benefit 3 · difficulty Very high · hardware sidetone 1 week; Linux strip + virtual source 6–9 weeks; Windows virtual mic gated on the driver · Ambitious*

- **What.** First, a hardware sidetone slider (ALSA mixer "*Sidetone*" controls on USB headsets). Then a capture strip (high-pass, gate, EQ, compressor, de-esser, limiter, later neural denoise) whose output goes to a virtual mic: a PipeWire Audio/Source node on Linux first, the driver MIC endpoint on Windows, the HAL plug-in on macOS. Optional low-latency software sidetone after the master limiter (−30 to −10 dB, feedback guard). Mic presets per mic type.
- **Why.** Chat is half of gaming audio, and Sonar ClearCast, NVIDIA Broadcast and Voicemeeter all process the mic. Budget headsets without hardware sidetone make players shout.
- **Expected benefit.** Feature parity for the outgoing voice path; it changes what the player hears only through sidetone. Discord's built-in Krisp, AGC and echo cancellation (and NVIDIA Broadcast on RTX machines) already cover most denoise and levelling value, so the marginal gain for teammates is small. The Windows virtual mic is blocked on a driver that does not exist.
- **Approach.** CaptureStrip excluded from `maxStripLatency` padding (shared with [E23](#e23)); mono chain; second output block; sidetone after the master limiter with a clip guard. A 256-point spectral gate (187.5 Hz bins) gives musical noise on voice, so plan an RNNoise-class model in the neural slot as the real denoiser. On Windows, JUCE's combined WASAPI input/output device allows mic-in to sidetone-out without the driver; only the virtual mic endpoint needs it. The 10 ms software sidetone target needs a PipeWire quantum ≤ 256; show a warning otherwise.
- **Depends on.** The padding exemption ([E23](#e23), [E40](#e40) sync groups), a shipped model ([E35](#e35), R5.3), the Windows driver ([E46](#e46)), [E17](#e17)'s hands-free state, lab data for `micMonitoring`.
- **Done when.** The sidetone slider reads/writes the ALSA control on a real USB headset and persists across reconnects. Game strip latency unchanged with the capture strip on; ≥ 10 dB stationary fan-noise reduction. Sidetone delay = device period + chain latency, ≤ 10 ms at quantum 128/48k, warning at 1024, feedback guard trips on a synthetic loop. `pw-record` from "Flubsound Mic" is silent within 50 ms of engine stop. Blind comparison against Discord Krisp alone; ship software denoise only if rated at least equal.
- **Evidence.** [10 §4][10-4] "Flubsound does not process your microphone in this version"; [TRACEABILITY Known gaps][TR-gaps] R6.1; [driver README][DRV] MIC endpoint (design only); [09 §1.2](09-future-roadmap.md#12-candidate-neural-features-prioritised) N1; [MixEngine.cpp][ME] (render strips only); [08 C8](08-pitfalls-and-solutions.md#c8-voice-chat-problems).

<a id="e22"></a>
#### E22 · ChatMix, voice-keyed spectral ducking and strip-priority master protection

*Gaming · benefit 3 · difficulty High · 6–10 engineer-weeks plus the routing prerequisite*

- **Status (p3b4, core).** Parts (1), (2)'s engine half and (3) done in `MixEngine` with new `VoiceActivity.*` and `ChatDucker.*` ([03 §14.14](03-dsp-design.md#1414-chat-sidechain-and-chatmix-mix-bus-desktop-app)); host settings, not parameters (no layout bump), off by default and bit-identical off (also bit-identical to an engine with no Chat strip, and with the duck on while no voice is held). Strips take their role from their names (Game / Music / Chat). **Voice activity** on the Chat strip's input (300 – 3400 Hz band share, six-band flatness, 9 dB syllable dips, 600 ms hangover): the E60 formant speech at −20 / −35 LUFS 86 % of syllable frames held, the duck in about 0.13 s after a talker starts from silence; false-positive duty on 10 s of music **0 %** (drum music −14 LUFS, pad and legato lead −18 / −40 LUFS; pink noise 0 %). **Duck** (`setChatDuck`, 3 – 6 dB, default 4.5, attack 30 ms, release 300 ms) on Game and Music after their chains: Game 1.25 kHz −4.62 dB and Music 2 kHz −4.50 dB while speech is held, the footstep bands (260 Hz, 3.2 kHz) moved ≤ 0.32 dB (design: ±0.5 dB over 2.8 – 6 kHz and below 370 Hz), no dip in any 100 ms window without speech, bit-identical before and after, no click. The Game chain's 2 kHz Voice & Score lift is **taken back after the chain** by its exact inverse bell, read from the chain's `MeterBus` (4.00 dB lift → −4.00 dB cancel while chat talks); the chain itself is not signalled (a `ProcessingChain` flag was outside this unit's files). **Strip-priority ceiling**: the Game peaks held 3 dB × the duck under the master ceiling by a zero-latency peak limiter. Explosions limited to −1 dBFS under speech at −20 LUFS: Chat short-term level change **0.20 → 0.02 dB** (row met); a teammate at −14 LUFS 1.02 → 0.82 dB (not under 0.5 dB: the sub-limiter of (1) is not built). **ChatMix** (`setChatMix`): complementary amplitude gains (Game 1 − max (0, b), Chat 1 + min (0, b)), 0/0 at centre bit-identical, 50 ms glides, a per-block sweep and a full jump click-free. Latency unchanged; no allocation; RTSan clean (the new entry points in `tests/test_rtsan.cpp`). The engine swap carries the settings, an active talker and the duck amount; a frozen strip's duck keeps gliding. `tests/test_mix_engine_sidechain.cpp` (10 cases). No golden, render-baseline or KnownGap entry moves (the CLI and the plug-in run one chain, not the mix bus). Open: the app half (ChatMix slider on `MixEngine::setChatMix`, the duck switch and depth, the voice dot: done by the E22 app unit below, which also replaced the app's ±6 dB strip-gain ChatMix), the sub-limiter for loud chat, a chain-side Voice & Score flag, (4) Chat-endpoint routing, and the play test against Sonar ChatMix with real Discord captures (gated on the owner's Windows setup).
- **Status (p3b4, app).** The app half of (2) and (3) done in `engine/EngineController.*`, `settings/AppSettings.*` (`chat.duck`, `chat.duckDepthDb`), `ui/QuickControls.*`, `ui/RoutingPanel.*` (the Chat row) and `shell/HotkeyManager.cpp` (unchanged calls, new law), with `MixEngine::setChatDuck` / `setChatMix` now callable from any thread (relaxed-atomic requests taken at the next `process()`, and by any engine configured later; [03 §14.14](03-dsp-design.md#1414-chat-sidechain-and-chatmix-mix-bus-desktop-app), [06 §6.10](06-gui.md#610-routingpanel--strips-and-applications), [§7.1](06-gui.md#71-tray)). Before: the app's ChatMix (hotkeys only) moved the Game and Chat strip gains ±6 dB in opposite directions (+1.2 / −1.2 dB per press), the engine's complementary ChatMix was unused, the duck could not be switched on and nothing showed the voice detector. After: one ChatMix balance, `EngineController::setChatMix` → `MixEngine::setChatMix`, from a slider in the tray flyout (*GAME* ‹slider› *CHAT*, 330 × 176 → 330 × 216 px), a slider on the Chat strip's row and the ChatMix hotkeys (one press: Game −1.9 dB, Chat 0 dB; five: Game muted); the strip gains never move. Measured at the device output (fake 14-input device, a tone per strip, chains bypassed; `tests/app/test_app_chatmix.cpp`): at +0.5 / −0.3 / −0.1 / +0.3 / 0 from the three controls, Game and Chat within 0.05 dB of the law, Music moved < 0.01 dB, the Chat-to-Game ratio up for every step towards Chat and down for every step towards Game, back at the centre within 0.001 dB; a rebuilt engine starts on the balance. *Duck game under voice chat* (off by default) and its depth (3 – 6 dB, default 4.5) on the Chat row, persisted and in the engine of a restarted app. A voice dot on the flyout and the Chat row (`isChatVoiceActive`): dark for a 1 kHz tone on Chat, lit 0.10 – 0.15 s after formant speech at −20 LUFS starts, 28 of 30 50 ms windows held, out 0.35 s after the speech window ends (the 600 ms hangover after its last syllable), the duck fully in. `tests/app/test_app_hotkeys.cpp`'s ChatMix case rewritten for the new law. No sound path, preset, parameter, golden, render-baseline or KnownGap entry moves (ChatMix centred and the duck off are bit-identical, E22 core). Open: the sub-limiter for loud chat, a chain-side Voice & Score flag, (4) Chat-endpoint routing, the Settings dialog row (the switch lives on the Chat row), a `--state` screenshot of the Chat row, and the play test against Sonar ChatMix with real Discord captures (gated on the owner's Windows setup).
- **Status (p3b5, app: the Settings dialog row).** Settings › Processing has a *Voice chat* section: *Duck game under voice chat* and its depth (3 – 6 dB), the same settings as the Chat row (`EngineController::setChatDuck`; the page follows a change made on the row), in `ui/SettingsDialog.*`; tested in `tests/app/test_app_ui_guards.cpp`. No sound moves (off by default). Open as above, less the Settings dialog row.

- **What.** (0) The Linux prerequisite: automated sink-monitor wiring and a strip-mapping UI, or the feature has no input. (1) Strip-priority master protection, first via per-strip ceiling offsets (Game −3 dBTP while Chat is active), then a sub-limiter only if needed. (2) A ChatMix balance with complementary gains (both 0 dB at centre) and hotkeys. (3) Voice activity on the Chat strip drives a 3–6 dB dynamic dip at 1–4 kHz on Game and Music, turns off Game's 2 kHz Voice & Score lift while chat is active, and protects the footstep band. (4) Optional routing of Chat to the headset's physical Chat endpoint, and a warning when the output is a Chat endpoint.
- **Why.** Teammates' callouts are masked by the game, and the shared master limiter ducks chat on every explosion. Sonar ChatMix, Turtle Beach Chat Boost and Discord attenuation all solve this.
- **Expected benefit.** High once chat reaches the Chat strip, which today it mostly does not: Windows per-process loopback cannot mute the original output, so a Discord capture is heard twice; Linux wiring is manual; macOS is unsupported. The master-limiter coupling (one −1 dBTP limiter with 50 ms auto-release over the sum) is a real mixing defect but also needs two fed strips. Realised benefit today is about 3; priority follows the routing work.
- **Approach.** Order (0) → (1) → ChatMix → ducking. Pass the "external voice active" flag as an atomic in the chain's parameter push. `MixEngine::setStripGainDb` is already smoothed, so ChatMix is mostly UI and persistence. Measure master-limiter engagement with the ceiling offsets before building a sub-limiter. A MixEngine-level harness is required, because the CLI renders one chain.
- **Depends on.** Routing ([E46](#e46), [E48](#e48)/R4.6). [E23](#e23), [E21](#e21), [E18](#e18). The in-flight engine swap must carry or reset sidechain state.
- **Done when.** New `tests/test_mix_engine_sidechain.cpp`: −1 dBTP explosions on Game with speech on Chat change Chat short-term level < 0.5 dB; the Game 1–4 kHz dip occurs only while speech is active; false-positive duty < 5 % on 10 s of music. ChatMix sweep: complementary, 0/0 at centre, no zipper noise. Latency unchanged or changed by exactly the documented look-ahead. Play test on Linux against Sonar ChatMix on Windows.
- **Evidence.** [MixEngine.h][MEh] (strip → pad → sum → single master limiter); [MixEngine.cpp][ME] `process()` (no cross-strip signal); [ProcessingChain.cpp][PC] `configureModeBands` band 7; [AppSettings.h][AS] `HotkeyAction`; [02 Tech stack](02-tech-stack.md) (loopback cannot mute the original); [TRACEABILITY Known gaps][TR-gaps] R4.5, R4.6.

<a id="e23"></a>
#### E23 · Voice/Film mode and a real Chat strip default (speech leveller, low-latency denoise)

*Gaming · benefit 3 · difficulty High · slice 1.5–2 days; total 8–11 engineer-weeks · Quick win (slice)*

- **Status (wave B2).** Slice done: `presets/factory/music-voice-chat.json` (Music mode; low cut 110 Hz, de-mud, presence 2.8 kHz, de-esser; Auto Level −19 LUFS + compressor 3:1 at −30 dB with +16 dB makeup + upward 3:1 below −42 dB; Boost 0.15; the maximizer only its true-peak limiter: no drive, no clipper, no glue, since the factory checks keep the limiter on), loaded on the Chat strip at first run (E36). Speech-like programme at −35 / −12 LUFS: 23 LU apart → −20.3 / −17.6 LUFS short-term at the end (2.6 LU; CLI in all three profiles, 2.2 LU through the app's Chat strip), true peak ≤ −1.05 dBTP. Open: denoise, the padding exemption, the Voice / Film mode, real speech captures.

- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07: chat denoise, experimental).** The neural slot can now run a denoiser on the Chat strip: the experimental neural voice cleanup ([E35](#e35), [03 §16](03-dsp-design.md#16-neural-voice-cleanup-experimental)), off by default, 20 ms on the Chat strip only (Balanced or Quality; the Game strip is not padded). On held-out synthetic speech: −19.9 dB between words and +4.7 dB SNR (the gate −6.1 dB / +3.5 dB, Quality only); through the Voice Chat preset the speech-to-pause ratio goes 7.3 → 21.7 dB; clean speech −0.24 dB, 0.55 dB per band. Against this item's done-when: "denoise ≥ 8 dB" is met between words on synthetic data, STOI is not measured, and listening and real Discord captures are open (the owner's). On macOS the model's worker now runs with the time-constraint policy, so its frames meet their 10 ms deadline with margin there too ([E35](#e35), CI 2026-10-07).
- **What.** Slice: a "Voice Chat" factory preset in Music mode (HPF 100–120 Hz, de-mud, presence, speech leveller to about −18 to −20 LUFS short-term, no maximizer drive), assigned to the Chat strip on first run without overwriting saved state. Then chat-only denoise with a padding exemption, a third mode "Voice / Film" (Dialogue, Night, De-ess, Bandwidth, Background macros), and a dialogue path before the virtualiser (centre channel, or centre extraction in Quality).
- **Why.** Incoming Discord/Teams audio gets Music mode with the maximizer on, and no noise reduction in Balanced or Low Latency. Quiet and loud teammates stay mismatched. Streams and films have no dialogue mode.
- **Expected benefit.** The slice fixes a real misconfiguration (Mode defaults to Music, `MaximizerOn` to true), under the same routing precondition as [E22](#e22). The full mode competes in a crowded field (Dolby dialogue enhancer, Nahimic voice, Windows voice clarity), and stereo centre extraction is research.
- **Approach.** The padding exemption must also change the master look-ahead rule in `MixEngine::build` (`allLowLatency` ignores alignment), or Game latency still grows from 0.5 to 1 ms. Level speech with a syllable-scale upward compressor (50–200 ms), with [E21](#e21)'s fixed AutoLevel only as a slow trim (today it slews at 1 dB/s). The third mode touches 73 `ModeValue::` references in 21 files, tray, hotkeys and CLI `--mode`.
- **Depends on.** Slice: nothing but its own preset test (below). It adds a factory preset in the existing Music mode, touches no mode enum and sets no `latency.profile`, so it ships in Phase 1 together with [E36](#e36)'s default-preset fix, which assigns it to the Chat strip. Its audible value still waits for chat to reach the Chat strip (routing, R4.5/R4.6). Full item: routing, [E21](#e21), [E22](#e22), [E18](#e18) (same exemption), the in-flight auto profiles (mode enum), the neural slot for RNNoise-class denoise.
- **Done when.** Preset test: maximizer off; −35 and −12 LUFS speech end within 3 LU short-term; ceiling held. Chat unaligned in Quality with Game in Low Latency leaves Game latency and master look-ahead unchanged. Mode audit covers all three modes. Denoise ≥ 8 dB with STOI drop < 0.05, confirmed by listening. Discord captures from 5 mic types, bypass vs Voice Chat.
- **Evidence.** [Parameters.cpp][PAR] Mode default Music, `MaximizerOn` true; [MacroMap.cpp][MM] (only `kMusicTable`, `kGamingTable`); [ProcessingChain.cpp][PC] `prepare()` `gateInChain` only in Quality; [01 §5.1][01-51] gate "(not in chain)"; [MixEngine.cpp][ME] `build`; [08 C8](08-pitfalls-and-solutions.md#c8-voice-chat-problems).

<a id="e28"></a>
#### E28 · Parametric binaural renderer quality: pinna and front/back cues, diffuse-field timbre, real room, level normalisation

*Gaming · benefit 3 · difficulty High · E28a 1–1.5 weeks; total 8–11 engineer-weeks*

- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2, E28a).** E28a done in `HeadphoneVirtualizer.*` and `Bs775Fold.h` (`LfeFold::addToMono`), on by default (`VirtualizerParams::levelMatch`, `foldHeadroom`; no parameter key), [03 §8.3.8](03-dsp-design.md#838-level-match-and-fold-headroom-e28a). Level match: a per-layout diffuse-field gain (K-weighted pink power of every ear path or HRIR on a 1/3-octave grid, reflections included, recomputed at each layout swap: −2.87 / −3.84 / −4.03 dB for stereo / 5.1 / 7.1 at the defaults) trimmed by a feed-forward servo on the K-weighted powers of the BS.775 matrix and of the render (3 s averages, −70 LUFS gate, ±4 dB around the diffuse gain, ≤ 6 dB/s, linear 16-sample ramps in stream time); the LFE stays outside it. Virt on − off, −30 dBFS pink on every speaker, every other module off (`flubsound-cli process` + `analyze`, whole 6 s file): 5.1 correlated / uncorrelated +0.82 / +3.63 → −0.16 / −0.02 LU, 7.1 +1.41 / +3.83 → −0.15 / 0.00 LU (spread 2.4–2.8 → ≤ 0.16 LU; Done-when ≤ 1 LU met; the item's −3.43 / +2.33 LU were measured on an earlier chain). Module alone over 3–6 s: all four within 0.10 LU, and a synthetic 7.1 HRIR set −2.98 / +0.71 → −0.09 / 0.00 LU. Fold headroom: a linked zero-latency peak gain (instant attack to exactly 0 dBFS, 10 ms hold, 150 ms release; bit-identical below 0 dBFS): full-scale correlated pink on every speaker, pre-limiter peak +11.79 → 0.00 dBFS (7.1) and +8.92 → 0.00 dBFS (5.1), true peak +0.29 / +0.48 dBTP (Done-when ≤ +1 dBFS met). Switching either is click-free (largest sample step ≤ 1.25× steady); block-size exact under random 1–4096 partitions with both toggled. Cost +7–9 ns per 7.1 frame at 48 kHz, about 0.04 % of a core (Approach: < 0.05 %). Moved on purpose: a main channel that reaches both ears sits 3 dB lower per ear at the same loudness, so E01's per-ear "LFE re one main" reads +8.99 dB with the virtualiser (was 5.97; the LFE itself is unchanged) and the forced-surround E27 reference curve sits 2.9 dB lower; the virtualiser → passthrough ramp now starts 2.9 dB below (was 0.8); the render baseline's `surround-7.1` programme moves for 24 of 25 presets (mains 3.7–3.9 dB lower, integrated −3.42 to +0.56 LU and true peak up to −3.1 dB after each preset's AutoLevel and maximizer; Club Loud's clipper no longer engages on it), no other programme moves; `tests/golden/preset-render-baseline.json` re-recorded for it in review. Open: the same headroom for the BS.775 downmix (virt off still hands +6.88 dBFS to the chain on full-scale correlated 7.1, so on such overs virt on is 5.5 LU quieter; its crossfades call the fold at unity in `ProcessingChain::foldToStereo`); a user-facing key and a make-up meter; re-voicing the gaming presets for the quieter virtualised surround; the rest of E28 (pinna and directional bands, diffuse-field inverse, FDN room).
- **Status (p3b3, E28a remainder).** Partially done in `Bs775Fold.h` (`FoldHeadroom`, the virtualiser's law as a shared class), `ProcessingChain.*` (`foldToStereo`), `MeterBus.h` (`virtMakeupDb`, `foldHeadroomDb`) and the CLI (`OfflineRenderer.*`, `Commands.cpp`: `render.stats.fold`), [03 §8.3.8](03-dsp-design.md#838-level-match-and-fold-headroom-e28a). (1) **Fold headroom for the BS.775 downmix (virt off):** the same linked zero-latency peak gain (instant attack to exactly 0 dBFS, 10 ms hold, 150 ms release), on the steady downmix and inside the crossfades on the `0.7071·D` term (per sample, from the same peaks), so the virtualiser ↔ downmix crossfade mixes two held folds; the stereo passthrough has none; bit-identical below 0 dBFS (asserted against the plain fold). Full-scale correlated pink on every speaker, every module off (`flubsound-cli process`, 6 s): pre-limiter sample peak +6.88 → 0.00 dBFS (7.1) and +4.65 → 0.00 dBFS (5.1), true peak +7.14 → +1.00 / +4.91 → +0.92 dBTP (Done-when ≤ +1 dBFS met). Switching `virt.on` and the passthrough on such overs is click-free (largest sample step ≤ 1.25× the steady maximum of the louder side; test). Virt on vs off on those overs: −5.51 → −2.16 LU (7.1) and −3.65 → −2.09 LU (5.1) with every module off, −3.31 → −1.68 / −2.09 → −1.60 LU at the defaults (the maximizer's mean limiter GR with virt off −3.40 → −1.74 dB on 7.1); the unit's ≤ 1 LU row is **not met**: at the same loudness (the level match) the binaural render peaks 3.1–3.2 dB higher than the downmix (+10.05 / +7.76 against +6.88 / +4.65 dBFS), so the same 0 dBFS ceiling takes about 2 LU more from it; closing it needs a loudness-linked law across both folds (e.g. the level match measured after each fold's headroom, in `HeadphoneVirtualizer`). (2) **Meters:** `MeterBus::virtMakeupDb` (the level-match make-up, 0 while the virtualiser does not run) and `MeterBus::foldHeadroomDb` (the block's deepest headroom gain of the fold that ran); `render.stats.fold` reports `virtMakeupMinDb` / `MaxDb` / `EndDb`, `headroomMaxDb` and `headroomActivePercent` (tested: full-scale correlated 7.1, virt off −6.88 dB, 100 %; virt on −10.31 dB, make-up −3.97 … −1.45 dB; stereo input all 0). Cost: one peak and one multiply per sample on the downmix path. Golden files and render baseline: nothing moves (the preset render diff at 0 dB tolerance, 25 presets × 5 programmes, against a build with the headroom disabled: no change; the `surround-7.1` programme stays below 0 dBFS; no parameter added). Open: the ≤ 1 LU row on overs (above); showing the two gains on the virtualiser card (an app change: the MeterBus fields are there); a user-facing key; re-voicing the gaming presets for the quieter virtualised surround (gated, §5.5); the rest of E28 (pinna and directional bands, diffuse-field inverse, FDN room).
- **Status (p3b5, E28 baseline from the E60 stage 2 metrics).** No DSP change here: today's parametric renderer (7.1, defaults), measured with [E60](#e60)'s `SpatialMetrics` on the module's impulse responses and pinned in `tests/test_spatial_metrics.cpp` ([03 §8.9](03-dsp-design.md#89-known-limitations)), against the Done-when rows: centre (FC) early IACC 0.995 at the default room, 1.000 dry (target < 0.6; the "room 0.15 vs 0 changes IACC by 0.01" of Why reproduced: 0.005); FL vs BL at the near ear ≤ 0.35 dB in every 1/3 octave up to 2 kHz, +1.46 / +3.30 / +3.52 / +4.27 dB at 4 / 8 / 10 / 16 kHz (the rear shelf alone: ≥ 3 dB from 8 kHz up, so the "≥ 3 dB in ≥ 2 directional bands" row needs its bands named before it can be judged); 4–8 kHz peak-to-notch of the same impulse on all seven speakers, dry, 28.06 dB at each ear (target < 12 dB; the 28 dB of Evidence reproduced; at the default room the reflections deepen the notches to 43–45 dB); FC against the sides (both ears' power) −3.8 dB at 2.5 kHz (target within 1 dB; Why's 4.3 dB was a single-frequency read); no energy after 80 ms (DRR +22.9 dB FC, +24.8 dB SL; late IACC and RT60 undefined); the seven speakers' diffuse field spans 3.52 dB around its mean (1/3 octave, 100 Hz – 16 kHz). `HeadphoneVirtualizer::parametricHrir` renders the renderer's own speaker path at any azimuth (equal to the module within 1e−7). Golden files and render baseline: nothing moves. Open: the renderer itself (pinna and directional bands, diffuse-field inverse, FDN room), measured with these rows.
- **Status (p3b5, E28 renderer).** Partially done: the objective part of the pinna and directional-band renderer, opt-in. In `HeadphoneVirtualizer.*`, `Parameters.*` (`virt.renderer` Classic / Enhanced, default Classic, and `virt.frontBack` 0 … 1, default 0.5; layout version 8, appended after E04's `clarity.attackLow` / `attackHigh`), the chain's hand-over in `ProcessingChain.cpp` and two hints in `ParamHints.cpp`; [03 §8.3.9](03-dsp-design.md#839-enhanced-direction-cues-e28). **Enhanced** replaces the binary −4 dB rear shelf with six SVF sections per speaker, common to both ears and ahead of the ITD line, whose gains are continuous in the angle from the front φ (c = cos φ): a lateral timbre match (high shelf 3 kHz, 60 % of the sphere's both-ear HF power difference to its circle average: FC +2.1 dB, the sides −1.1 dB), Blauert's directional bands (front: 4 kHz bell +5 dB · c and 13.5 kHz shelf +6 dB · max(0, c); rear: 1 kHz +4 dB and 10 kHz +6 dB bells · max(0, −c)), scaled by `virt.frontBack` / 0.5, and a −10 dB, Q 2 pinna notch at 7.6 kHz · 2^(−0.1 (1 − c)/2) (7.6 kHz in front, 7.1 kHz behind). A change glides (the Enhanced share and the band scale on 30 ms one-poles at the control rate, the rear shelf at (1 − share) of its Classic gain, per-sample coefficient ramps); the level match's diffuse-field gain includes the cues (−3.84 / −4.03 → −4.77 / −5.09 dB for 5.1 / 7.1) and is recomputed when a glide lands; the cue sections of four speakers share one SSE register. Done-when rows, Classic → Enhanced, 7.1 at the defaults, measured with [E60](#e60)'s `SpatialMetrics` on the module's impulse responses (`tests/test_virtualizer.cpp`): **FL vs BL near-ear ≥ 3 dB in ≥ 2 directional bands: met**, with the bands named and signed (front louder at 4 and 16 kHz, rear louder at 1 and 10 kHz): 1 kHz −0.31 → +3.34, 4 kHz +1.46 → +7.15, 10 kHz −3.52 → +4.10, 16 kHz +4.27 → +4.31 dB, one band → four (Classic's 10 kHz difference had the front's sign); `virt.frontBack` 0 leaves none above 1 dB, 100 % doubles them (1 / 4 / 10 / 16 kHz 6.37 / 14.20 / 7.17 / 7.92 dB). **FC vs side tilt within 1 dB: met** as the least-squares line of FC − mean(SL, SR) (both ears' power, 1/3 octave, 100 Hz – 16 kHz, end to end) −2.78 → −0.57 dB; the largest single band −3.82 dB at 2.5 kHz → −3.42 dB at 8 kHz. **4–8 kHz peak-to-notch < 12 dB: not met**, 28.06 → 18.88 dB on the dry same impulse on all seven speakers: the comb is seven near-equal paths up to 0.6 ms apart at one ear (FC against FL alone combs 11 dB at 3.9 kHz), and on a model of the renderer reaching 12 dB needed about 18 dB of HF difference between centre / sides and front / rear, which the centre-timbre row rules out; the constants were chosen by a search on that model with the other rows as constraints and this one as the objective. **Virt on / off within 1 LU still: met**: module alone over 3–6 s, 5.1 correlated / uncorrelated −0.04 / +0.03 LU, 7.1 −0.08 / +0.02 LU; through `flubsound-cli process` with every other module off, whole 6 s, −30 dBFS pink per speaker, virt on − off Classic −0.16 / −0.02 / −0.15 / −0.01 → Enhanced −0.15 / +0.01 / −0.13 / +0.02 LU (5.1 c / u, 7.1 c / u). **CPU < 1.5 % of a core: met**: 7.1 at 48 kHz, 512-sample blocks, gcc Release, the module with the level match 88 ns per frame (0.42 %) Classic → 135 ns (0.65 %) Enhanced (a scalar first version cost 248 ns, 1.19 %). **Classic bit-identical: met**: the preset render diff, 30 presets × 5 programmes at 0 dB tolerance against a build of the same tree without the change, 150 / 150 unchanged; in the chain the default render equals Classic set explicitly with any `virt.frontBack` bit for bit, and the module under random block partitions with renderer switches is bit-exact. Unchanged by design: every speaker's ITD and, frequency by frequency, ILD (within 0.001 dB); FC early IACC 0.995 → 0.998 (the < 0.6 row belongs to the room stage). Costs: the seven speakers' diffuse field is less flat, range 3.52 → 7.19 dB (the notch and the bands are in every direction; not diffuse-field equalised); FC DRR +22.9 → +26.5 dB (the reflections do not pass the cues). Switching Classic → Enhanced → Classic on a 3 kHz tone: largest sample step at the switches 0.113 / 0.111 against a steady 0.112 / 0.071 (ear 0), and 250 ms later the output equals a module started in the target within 1e−4; `virt.frontBack` in Classic changes nothing bit for bit. Tests: five new cases in `tests/test_virtualizer.cpp` (0.04–0.42 s each), the allocation case switches the renderer, and `tests/test_spatial_metrics.cpp`'s `parametricHrir` case covers Enhanced (`parametricHrir` takes the renderer and `virt.frontBack`). Golden files: `parameter-defaults.json` gains `virt.renderer` (0, Classic) and `virt.frontBack` (0.5), and all 30 factory `contentHash` values move (the hash covers every parameter; the same re-record as E04's two keys); no render, render-baseline entry, KnownGap or quality-ratchet value moves. Gated: making Enhanced the default (the 8-direction forced-choice test against Classic and Windows Sonic; ship only if reversals drop), the band gains and notch by ear (no listener has heard them). Open: the 4–8 kHz comb row; the minimum-phase diffuse-field inverse (now also for the Enhanced cues' common part); directional early reflections and the FDN room (centre IACC < 0.6, RT60); E28a's ≤ 1 LU on overs; re-voicing the gaming presets. *Review (p3b5):* `parameter-defaults.json` re-recorded with `virt.renderer` / `virt.frontBack` and every `contentHash` once, together with E04's keys; no render moved (the tree with the E04 / E20 rows reverted renders bit-identical to the batch 4 baseline).
- **Status (p3b5, app card).** The Virtualizer card binds `virt.renderer` (*Renderer*) and `virt.frontBack` (*Front/Back*) by their keys (`ui/ModuleCard.cpp` table): they appear once the core's layout has them and are left out until then (`tests/app/test_app_ui_guards.cpp` checks both ways). Review (p3b5): with the core's layout version 8 both are on the card (screenshot state `module-keys`).

- **What.** **E28a** (split out, High on its own): per-layout diffuse-field power normalisation plus an adaptive virt make-up gain so virt on/off stays within 1 LU for correlated and uncorrelated content, and fold-headroom handling for correlated overs. Then: angle-continuous pinna and Blauert directional-band cues replacing the binary rear shelf, with an optional "Competitive" front/back contrast; a minimum-phase diffuse-field inverse and centre-timbre match; directional early reflections plus an 8-line FDN tail with room presets (Dry/Competitive as the gaming default).
- **Why.** Front and back differ only by a gentle HF tilt (FL vs BL near-ear difference ≤ 0.3 dB below 2 kHz), the classic recipe for front/back confusion, and the default room has no measurable effect (room 0.15 vs 0 changes IACC by 0.01; FC IACC 1.00). Music: FC is 4.3 dB darker than the sides, and the virt toggle shifts loudness.
- **Expected benefit.** Only for discrete 5.1/7.1 input (Linux 7.1 sink, 8-channel cables, multichannel files, the future Windows driver); after [E27](#e27), stereo-in-8-channel content bypasses the virtualiser. Within that reach the defects are real: measured virt on/off −3.43 LU on correlated 7-speaker noise and +2.33 LU on uncorrelated (5.8 LU spread); a 28 dB peak-to-notch range in 4–8 kHz on a correlated 7-speaker impulse.
- **Approach.** A static per-layout gain cannot meet 1 LU because the error flips sign. Run the cheap `downmixToStereo()` on a copy every block (it already runs during crossfades), compute K-weighted short-term power of both paths, and servo a slow make-up gain (3 s, clamped ±4 dB), keeping the extra cost below 0.05 % of a core. Pinna echo taps via the existing fractional delay; diffuse-field inverse table over head radius; image-source ER through `EarPath` pairs; Hadamard FDN.
- **Depends on.** [E27](#e27) first; precedes [E29](#e29) (shared normalisation law); gaming presets re-voiced afterwards; a Windows rig for the Windows Sonic comparison.
- **Done when.** Virt on vs off within 1 LU for correlated and uncorrelated 7.1 and 5.1 pink (CLI `analyze`). Pre-limiter peak of full-scale correlated 7-speaker content ≤ +1 dBFS. FL vs BL near-ear difference ≥ 3 dB in ≥ 2 directional bands. Centre IACC < 0.6 at the default room; RT60 within 20 %. 4–8 kHz peak-to-notch < 12 dB. FC vs side tilt within 1 dB. CPU < 1.5 % of a core. 8-direction forced-choice test against the old renderer and Windows Sonic; ship only if reversals drop.
- **Evidence.** [HeadphoneVirtualizer.cpp][HV] `kRearShelfHz 4000` / −4 dB, `kReflectionMs` mono bus, `kTrim −3 dB`; [03 §8.3][03-83], [§8.9][03-89]; [08 C6][08-C6].

<a id="e30"></a>
#### E30 · Output-aware rendering: headphone/speaker detection, multichannel speaker output, bass management, haptics, crosstalk cancellation

*Gaming · benefit 3 · difficulty High · detection + banner 1.5 weeks; total 14–19 engineer-weeks*

- **What.** Read the endpoint form factor on all three OSes into `DeviceProfiles` and suggest (banner, not silent switch) a render policy: Headphone Binaural, Speaker Downmix, Speaker Transaural or Multichannel. Make the master bus follow the device (2.0/2.1/5.1/7.1): route Game 7.1 straight through with a linked per-channel limiter, LR4 bass management fed by the mains' low band plus calibrated LFE, a haptics aux output (20–80 Hz, own limiter), per-class bass policy, an in-app lowest-audible-tone calibration and, last, a regularised crosstalk canceller.
- **Why.** Users with 5.1/7.1 speakers, AVRs or subwoofers lose discrete surround and the LFE; 2.1 music systems need bass management; haptics is an immersion differentiator (Razer HyperSense, Woojer).
- **Expected benefit.** Smaller than claimed. Stereo content on speakers is never binauralised (the virtualiser runs only for > 2 input channels), and [E27](#e27) removes the stereo-in-8-channel exposure. What is unique here: multichannel speaker users (channels ≥ 2 are zero-filled, MixEngine masters 2 channels, Settings limits outputs to 2, `kMaxMeterChannels = 2`), 2.1 bass management, and form-factor-driven bass policy. Haptics and crosstalk cancellation are niche.
- **Approach.** `PlatformServices_win.cpp` already reads `PKEY_AudioEndpoint_FormFactor` but acts only on HDMI; map all values. Combo jacks misreport (Realtek reports Speakers for headphones), so banner first. Multichannel speaker output must either run DynamicEq and the compressor N-channel with linked detection on the Game strip or state plainly that Gaming mode is stereo-only; today every Gaming-mode stage runs after the fold. Audit every 2-channel assumption (meters, governor, DistortionMonitor, export, plug-in bus, `mixSwap()`). Crosstalk cancellation waits for [E28](#e28)'s head model.
- **Depends on.** [E27](#e27), PlatformServices on 3 OSes, the in-flight engine swap for channel-count changes, [E46](#e46) for discrete game surround, device-lab data before automatic switching.
- **Done when.** Form factor drives the suggested policy; an 8-channel MixEngine uses a linked limiter; LR4 sums flat within 0.1 dB; LFE gets +10 dB; an 8-output fake device gets content on channels 2–7. Protection tests re-run at 2, 6 and 8 output channels. Footstep gain measured per channel of the multichannel Game path, or a documented "Gaming mode off" state. Detection checked on 10+ endpoints. 1 h haptics soak. Crosstalk cancellation tested in a room at 3 speaker spans.
- **Evidence.** [AudioEngineHost.cpp][AEH] `processBlock()` (channels ≥ 2 silenced); [MixEngine.cpp][ME] master loop `c < 2`; [MeterBus.h][MB] `kMaxMeterChannels = 2`; [ProcessingChain.h][PCh] "from here on the chain is STEREO"; [PlatformServices_win.cpp][PSW] `transportFromEnumerator`; [09 §2][09-2], [§4][09-4].

<a id="e34"></a>
#### E34 · Content and scene analysis: classifier-driven mode morphing, material-aware macros, game scene states

*Adaptive & intelligent · benefit 3 · difficulty High · slice 3–4 weeks; total 10–14 engineer-weeks · Ambitious*

- **Status (p3b5, tap + Smart scaling slice).** Slice done in the core and the CLI, synthetic evidence only ([03 §14.16](03-dsp-design.md#1416-content-analysis-and-smart-macros-chain)). **Tap:** `ContentAnalysis` (`core/include/flub/analysis/ContentAnalysis.h`, new) runs in-line in `ProcessingChain::processSegment` after AutoLevel and ahead of the fold (surround read as a stereo mixdown), only while Smart macros or a reader need it, in 100 ms frames counted in samples over the last 3 s of programme (pauses under −70 dB RMS left out, valid after 0.5 s): PLR and crest, octave-band tilt (pink 0.0, white +2.85 dB/oct) and its 8 kHz+ end, the sub-100 Hz share, M/S width and correlation, spectral flux and onsets (kicks + hats at 2 + 2 /s read 3.8 /s); `AnalysisState` goes to other threads through a seqlock (`getContentAnalysis`). In-line rather than on a worker, so live and offline stay identical (P6). **Smart scaling:** `MacroModulation` in `MacroMap::apply` multiplies what the rows add to attack (`clarity.attack`, `.attackLow`, `.attackHigh`: smoothstep 7.5 → 10.5 LU of PLR), drive (`max.drive`, `sat.drive`: 0.25 + 0.75 × attack), bass (`bass.boost`, `bass.harmonics`: down to 0.5 from −3 to 0 dB of sub-100 Hz share) and air (`clarity.air`: down to 0.5 from −6 to 0 dB of high tilt), slewed per governor tick (down 0.25 s, up 2 s), held while not valid; `nullptr` / identity is bit-identical. Per preset: `"smart": true` (`Preset::smart`, in `contentHash` only when true; default off) and `ProcessingChain::setSmartMacros` (`FLUB_NONBLOCKING`, in `tests/test_rtsan.cpp` with `ContentAnalysis::process / reset`, `AnalysisSnapshot::publish`, `setContentAnalysisTap`). **CLI:** every `analyze` prints the readings and `suggest` (the multipliers Smart would apply, with notes; `content` / `suggest` in `--json`). Done-when on a synthetic limited master (PLR 7.4 LU, `tests/test_content_analysis.cpp`): Punch 0 → 100 integrated loudness −0.14 LU static → −0.04 LU Smart, and over Boost 40 + Loudness 60 −0.47 → −0.04 LU; true peak +0.00 dB either way (the maximizer's ceiling; with the maximizer off, Punch 0 → 100 after the first second 2.44 → 2.39 dBTP Smart against 2.44 → 4.31 static, but the onset before the state is valid still reaches 7.07 dBTP either way); open programme (PLR 12.8) Smart within 0.00 / 0.02 LU of static (Punch 100 / Boost 60 + Loudness 60); two Smart renders bit-identical, 64- and 1024-sample blocks bit-identical, Smart off bit-identical to a chain without it. Smart also plays Loudness 60 on the limited master 1.1 LU quieter (its drive at the 0.25 floor): intended, but a product call for the re-tune pass. No factory preset sets `smart`, so no golden or render-baseline entry moves (`tests/golden/*`, `preset-render-baseline.json`: none). Open: handing a preset's flag to the chain in the app (`PresetManager`), the plug-in and the CLI render path (`OfflineRenderer` / `RenderSettings`; outside this unit's files); a Smart toggle in the UI; the re-tune pass; the first 0.5 s gets the static macros (with the maximizer off that onset is not held under a ceiling); the classifier (next unit). Gated: the Done-when on real limited masters and the MUSHRA of Smart vs static on 10 masters (listening panel and music licences, E58).
- **Status (p3b5, app: Smart macros switch).** A per-strip switch *Smart macros on the <strip> strip* on Settings › Processing (`ui/SettingsDialog.*`), persisted per strip name (`strip.<name>.smart`, off) and handed to the strip's chain (`ProcessingChain::setSmartMacros`) at start, on each change and on every engine the host builds (`engine/EngineController.*`); tested in `tests/app/test_app_ui_guards.cpp`. No sound moves (off by default). Open: the switch in the macro area of the Boost panel (`ui/BoostPanel.*`, outside this unit's files); a preset's own `smart` flag applied on load and written by a user preset save (`PresetManager`); the other items above.
- **Status (local, 2026-09-30: a preset's `smart` flag).** Done in the app, the plug-in and the CLI; built and tested on Windows (MSVC 19.51). **App:** `PresetManager::loadIntoBank` / `loadIntoStrip` / `stepPreset` hand back the preset's flag and `EngineController` sets the strip's switch to it (`setPresetSmartMacros`) on a load, next / previous and an automatic profile, whose restore point keeps the switch it found and gives it back when the app leaves; a preset without the flag switches Smart off; `saveUserPreset` / `saveCurrent` write the strip's switch (the HeaderBar's Save too); Export renders with the preset's flag or the strip's switch (`ExportSettings::smartMacros`). Restoring the session at start keeps the persisted switch. **Plug-in:** Import applies the flag (one without it turns Smart off), Export writes it, and the state carries `flubSmartMacros` only while on, so older states keep their bytes and load with Smart off. **CLI:** `process` / `batch` / `quality` take the preset's flag (`ResolvedParameters::smart`, `RenderSettings::smartMacros`, `renderPass`), `--smart on|off` overrides it, the summary prints *Smart macros on* and `--json` `render.smartMacros`. Tests: *CLI Smart macros (docs/11 E34) …*, *App: Smart macros follow the preset (E34) …*, *Plug-in Smart macros (docs/11 E34) …*; each fails with its hand-over removed. No factory preset sets `smart`, so no golden or render-baseline entry moves. Not done: the Smart switch in the Boost panel's macro area, Smart in the preset browser's audition, a Smart change marking the preset modified.
- **What.** A per-strip analysis tap (pre-fold, off the audio path, about 10 Hz) computing modulation energy, spectral flux, onset density, HNR, tilt, sub-100 Hz share, short-term PLR/crest, headroom, correlation and 7.1 spread. First slice: "Smart" macro scaling (e.g. shrink attack and drive as PLR falls below about 9 LU, scale bass with measured LF excess and air with HF tilt) and CLI `analyze` extensions (tilt, crest, M/S width, `suggest`). Later: a Music / Speech / Game-FX / Silence classifier that morphs macro targets over 1–2 s with a user lock. Game scene states and a post-event cue lift go to an experiment branch.
- **Why.** The same macro offsets hit a −9 LUFS brick-walled master and a −23 LUFS classical recording, so Punch on a limited master makes it quieter and flatter, and talk content gets music processing.
- **Expected benefit.** Smart scaling for limited masters is real, but the dense-signal evidence was wrong: the Music Boost 0 crest drop (PLR 12.47 → 10.6) comes from BassEngine alone (bass off restores the input), and Punch 100 merely restores input crest. The valid evidence is the original −11.45 against −11.06 LUFS on a real master. Per-app routing already separates Game, Music and Chat, so a classifier mainly helps the System strip. The 1.5 s post-event lift has no basis in forward masking (which decays in about 100–200 ms); it would be compensating game-mix ducking, which needs per-title evidence.
- **Approach.** E34 owns the tap and `AnalysisState` publisher; [E35](#e35) reuses them. Smart scaling through a `MacroModulation` of slewed multipliers in `MacroMap::apply`, with a per-preset flag and a re-tune pass. Classifier defaults to "suggest" in Music and automatic only on the System strip; morph only across silence or on a strong posterior with ≥ 5 s hysteresis. Note that live and offline renders will diverge once analysis runs on a worker.
- **Depends on.** Genre target curves (data work). The in-flight per-game auto profiles overlap the scene idea. The "Startle Guard" cap does not exist yet ([E21](#e21)).
- **Done when.** On a real limited master (PLR < 8 LU), Punch 100 lowers integrated loudness ≤ 0.1 LU and raises true peak ≤ 0.5 dB, with PLR 12+ material unchanged within 0.2 LU. Synthetic speech/music/silence unit tests; no class flips over 60 s of steady music; ≥ 90 % frame accuracy at 1 s on GTZAN or MUSAN subsets. Offline renders bit-identical twice. MUSHRA of Smart vs static macros on 10 masters from −23 to −8 LUFS.
- **Evidence.** [MacroMap.cpp][MM] `apply()` (no signal input); no classifier or speech-detection code in the tree; `flubsound-cli help analyze` (loudness and peaks only); [09 §1.2](09-future-roadmap.md#12-candidate-neural-features-prioritised) N2/N5.

<a id="e38"></a>
#### E38 · Show what the sound is doing: net tone curve, active-now chips, in-out loudness and governor readouts

*UX & workflow · benefit 3 · difficulty Medium · slice 2–3 days; net curve and chips 3–4 weeks · Quick win (slice)*

- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2, app).** Slice partially done in `ui/LoudnessPanel.*`, `ui/BoostPanel.*` and `ui/MeterSnapshot.*` (no engine change). Before: no in → out loudness figure, the governor chip showed only the scale, no harmonics reading, nothing said which stages act. After: **IN>OUT** (short-term out − in, e.g. `+2.9 LU` on the screenshot scene; `--` at ≤ −70 LUFS), the governor chip with state and reason (see [E06](#e06)), a **Harmonics** row (`MeterBus::harmonicsDb`: the bass harmonics and the air exciter together; the MeterBus has one reading for both, so there are not two rows) next to the Distortion row, and **active-now chips** under the macros from the chain's effective values and meters (`BoostPanel::describeActiveStages`; what does not fit is counted, the tooltip lists all). Done-when rows: Music at Boost 0 on the test programme names the subsonic high-pass (Signature: *Subsonic 20 Hz*, *Bass +1.5 dB @ 60 Hz*, *Tighten 10%*, *Air 5%*, *De-mud 10%*): met; Boost 55 gives ≥ 3 chips (11): met; the coefficient-derived net tone curve (the ≤ 0.3 dB swept-sine row) and the CLI `test_offline_render` JSON keys on Club Loud (the CLI is not in this track's files; E59 added the governor statistics to `render.stats`) are open. Tested in `tests/app/test_app_ui_status.cpp`.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 1, app).** The active-now chips are in [E39](#e39)'s Simple view, its host (Depends on), wrapped over up to three rows (`BoostPanel::layoutChips`): Signature at Boost 55 shows 11 of 11 chips there, 3 and *+8* in the Advanced view's single row (`tests/app/test_app_ui_simple_view.cpp`). Still open: the coefficient-derived net tone curve and its ≤ 0.3 dB swept-sine row, the CLI JSON keys on Club Loud, separate bass-harmonics and air readings.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, app).** The governor and brightness readouts that [E06](#e06) / [E07](#e07) publish are shown, in `ui/MeterSnapshot.*`, `ui/LoudnessPanel.*` and `ui/BoostPanel.*` ([06 §6.3](06-gui.md#63-boostpanel--boost-intensity-and-the-mode-macros), [§6.7](06-gui.md#67-loudnesspanel--loudness-dynamics-and-stereo)). The loudness panel of the Advanced view has a PROTECTION section: the strength, the audible (weighted) residual against its budget, the output PLR against its budget and the brightness lifts (presence / harsh / air) against theirs (Club Loud at Boost 100, Normal, 4 s of music: residual −20.6 dB against −35, PLR 10.7 dB, brightness +1.4 / +1.2 / +1.4 dB). The governor chip names the new reasons (*dynamics*, *harmonics*, *brightness*) and its tooltip quotes the budgets the chain publishes for its strength and mode, where it quoted Off's −6 / −30 dB at Normal too. The main window gives the loudness panel about 20 px more (levels at 36 % of the column instead of 40 %) so the section fits at 1280 × 820; it is left out where the panel is too short (800 × 560). Tested in `tests/app/test_app_ui_reflow.cpp`. Still open: the net tone curve and its ≤ 0.3 dB row, the CLI JSON keys on Club Loud, separate bass-harmonics and air readings.

- **What.** Slice: an in-to-out LU delta, the governor's reason code, bass-harmonics and air-exciter distortion rows, and CLI render-JSON statistics (governor min/mean, limiter GR max and % active, THD per source, safety-clip count). Then a "Net tone" curve summing the actual linear stages at the current level, toggleable against the EQ-only curve, and "active now" chips under the Boost dial from effective values (e.g. "Bass +3.1 dB @ 70 Hz · Width 118 % · Maximizer drive 4 dB"). A brightness meter arrives with [E07](#e07).
- **Why.** Boost 55 % shows a flat curve, five macros at 0 % and GR 0.0, so users cannot tell what changed.
- **Expected benefit.** Trust and tuning. A concrete blind spot: at Boost 0 in Music, BassEngine alone lowers true peak from −3.52 to −5.40 dBTP and PLR from 12.47 to 10.6 at unchanged loudness, and the UI says nothing. Correction: a distortion readout already exists (`LoudnessPanel` draws a Distortion row from `MeterBus.distortionDb`), so R3.5's "not shown" applies only to the harmonics and air readings.
- **Approach.** Do not hand-write response formulas that duplicate each module's filter design and drift from it. Have each linear stage publish its current biquad/SVF coefficients (a small seqlock snapshot per block) and evaluate |H| in the UI; nonlinear stages appear as chips only. Smooth and label level-dependent curves. Keep `EqCurveEditor`'s cached-image strategy at a 10–15 Hz repaint. Remove rather than add readouts where the meter column is dense ([E39](#e39)).
- **Depends on.** [E07](#e07) for brightness, [E39](#e39) as the Simple-view host, the in-flight UI scale/theme work.
- **Done when.** The coefficient-derived curve matches a swept-sine measurement of the linear chain at −30 dBFS within 0.3 dB. Music at Boost 0 on the dense signal shows the subsonic high-pass and a chip names it. Boost 55 gives a non-flat curve and ≥ 3 chips. `test_offline_render` asserts the new JSON keys on Club Loud.
- **Evidence.** [06 §6.5][06-65] (curve = `ParametricEq::responseDb` + `eq.output` only); [LoudnessPanel.cpp][LP] Distortion row (~L183–206); [BoostPanel.cpp](../app/Source/ui/BoostPanel.cpp); [TRACEABILITY Known gaps][TR-gaps] R3.5; CLI `render` JSON fields.

<a id="e44"></a>
#### E44 · Multi-core, reliable real-time audio threads: parallel strips, rtkit, workgroups and visible RT status

*Latency & performance · benefit 3 · difficulty High · Phase A 1.5–2 weeks; Phase B 4–6 weeks*

- **Status (Phase 3 batch 1, Phase A on Linux).** Partially done. The first callback on each new device thread promotes it without allocating (the saved policy is thread-local: 1 allocation → 0, `tests/app/test_app_host_realtime.cpp` and `tests/test_platform_linux.cpp`) and records its kernel thread id. The message thread (`AudioEngineHost::serviceAudioThreadRealtime`, 5 Hz timer) reads that thread's scheduling and, when it is still `SCHED_OTHER`, asks RealtimeKit on the system bus through the portal hotkeys' run-time libdbus-1 (`platform::RealtimeScheduling::requestRealtimeKit`): `MaxRealtimePriority` and `RTTimeUSecMax` are read, `RLIMIT_RTTIME` (soft and hard) is lowered to `RTTimeUSecMax`, then `MakeThreadRealtime (tid, min (20, MaxRealtimePriority))`; rtkit older than 0.11 gets its defaults (20, 200 ms). RT status was invisible (`promotionHandle` written, never read) → `EngineStatus::audioThread`, read through `EngineController::getStatus()`: "real-time (RR 20 via rtkit)", "real-time (FIFO 83, the audio server's thread)" or "NOT real-time: <rtkit's reason>; <fix>". Tested against a mock rtkit on a private `dbus-daemon`, which checks what rtkit checks (the caller's pid from the bus, the thread among its tasks, the priority, the `RLIMIT_RTTIME` hard limit read from `/proc/<pid>/limits` at the call): call sequence, the cap at a maximum of 15, old rtkit, refusal, no rtkit, no bus; the request runs in a forked child so the test binary keeps its limits. Open: the Settings status line and overload notifications (UI), the Realtime portal under Flatpak, the macOS `promoteAudioThread` audit, runs on Ubuntu 24.04 / Fedora 40 / SteamOS with `chrt -p` and the `stress-ng` xrun count, Phase B.
- **What.** Phase A: the rtkit `MakeThreadRealtime` fallback over D-Bus from the message thread (or the Realtime portal under Flatpak), with `RLIMIT_RTTIME` handling; preallocated saved-policy objects; an RT status line in Settings and overload notifications ("Audio thread: real-time (FIFO 20 via rtkit)" or "NOT real-time" with a fix); a macOS audit so `promoteAudioThread` does not overwrite the HAL's own time-constraint policy. Phase B, optional: strips as jobs on a small RT worker pool with a lock-free barrier, a deadline and a serial fallback, workers joined to the device `os_workgroup` on macOS.
- **Why.** On most Linux desktops the audio thread silently stays SCHED_OTHER and competes with the game; four strips run serially on one thread.
- **Expected benefit.** Linux: real, since `promoteAudioThread` leaves SCHED_OTHER on EPERM ("RealtimeKit... not wired up") and allocates on the audio thread. Windows already has MMCSS, so only visibility improves. macOS: the HAL IO thread is already in the device workgroup; only Phase B workers would need to join. `promotionHandle` is written and never read, so RT status is invisible everywhere. Phase B pays off only at 96–192 kHz or heavy multi-strip use (4 strips average 22 % of budget at 48 kHz/128 against 107 % at 192 kHz Quality); games render at 48 kHz.
- **Approach.** Record the audio thread's TID on the first callback; call rtkit at ≤ `MaxRealtimePriority` from the message thread, reusing the libdbus plumbing of the portal hotkeys. Phase B only when [E45](#e45)'s histogram shows serial cost > about 40 % of budget and blocks ≥ 256 frames, with `WaitOnAddress` / futex / `os_sync` waits and a mandatory deadline fallback.
- **Depends on.** [E45](#e45)'s histogram; the in-flight engine swap; on [E48](#e48)'s native PipeWire path Phase A is unnecessary (the data thread is already RT) but stays for the JUCE fallback.
- **Done when.** A first-callback test asserts `promoteAudioThread` performs no allocation; a mock rtkit on the private dbus-daemon verifies the call sequence and RLIMIT precondition. On Ubuntu 24.04, Fedora 40 and SteamOS desktop with default limits, Settings shows real-time and `chrt -p` shows SCHED_FIFO/RR; xruns/hour under a game-like `stress-ng` load are lower than baseline. macOS `thread_policy_get` shows no worse constraint at 64 frames. Phase B: p99.9 callback time ≥ 40 % lower at 4 strips/192 kHz/512 Quality, no regression at 48 kHz/128, 24 h soak with 0 extra xruns.
- **Evidence.** [AudioEngineHost.cpp][AEH] `processBlock()`, `promotionHandle` (written ~L875, never read); [MixEngine.cpp][ME] serial strip loop; [PlatformServices_linux.cpp][PSL] `promoteAudioThread` (~L2563–2595); [PlatformServices_mac.mm][PSM] (~L549); [08 D5](08-pitfalls-and-solutions.md#d5-power-management-windows-11-ecoqos-hybrid-cpus).

<a id="e45"></a>
#### E45 · CPU governance: idle freeze, rate-aware cost, power policy, peak-callback monitoring and a latency-neutral shed ladder

*Latency & performance · benefit 3 · difficulty Medium · 5–6 engineer-weeks*

- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2).** The peak-callback histogram is done; the rest is open. `flub::CallbackTiming` (`core/include/flub/analysis/CallbackTiming.h`) keeps two lock-free histograms, the callback's duration and its interval. Each has 178 log buckets at 1/8 octave from 1 µs, so a percentile is within 12.5 %. There is one writer (`record()`, wait-free, `FLUB_NONBLOCKING`) and any number of readers, which take snapshots and form windows with `since()`. Each snapshot has mean, p99, p99.9, max and load over the period, plus over-budget and late (interval > 1.5 periods) counts. `AudioEngineHost` records every device callback: the duration on the steady clock, the interval on the backend's host time when the context carries one (CoreAudio), else the steady clock. A device start or stop, or a change of clock, starts a new run of intervals. The message thread reads it through `getCallbackTiming()` and `EngineStatus::callbackTiming` (so `EngineController::getStatus()`). The Done-when's spike case (Balanced / 128, 40 % load, a 3× callback every 500 ms, no glitch count): the 2 Hz average the watchdog sees stays at 0.41 of the period; in each 1 s window p99.9 reads ≥ 1.0 and the peak 1.20 (the exact max over the run), and all 19 spikes are counted over budget (`tests/test_callback_timing.cpp`). A backend stall of two periods reads as one late callback, from the host timestamps (`tests/app/test_app_host_io.cpp`). Before, only JUCE's averaged CPU load and the device's xrun count, −1 on backends that report none, were available. Open: feeding p99.9 into `OverloadWatchdog` and a "peak %" readout (`EngineController` / UI, not in this change), the idle freeze, the gate's frame spreading, visibility gating, battery mode, the shed ladder, `tools/flub-bench` and the CI perf gate.
- **Status (p3b3, idle freeze and peak-callback monitoring).** Partially done: the idle freeze (on by default in the app's engine; the −90 dBFS null row is met by the tests' presets and by 20 of the 26 factory presets in stereo, 19 in 7.1, but not by the six that hold a state silence still moves after the 10 s hold, down to −53 dBFS, nor at an unaligned wake's control-grid phase, down to −36 dBFS, both below), p99.9 into the watchdog, "peak %" in the header and late callbacks as dropouts are done; the gate's frame spreading, visibility gating, battery mode, the shed ladder, `tools/flub-bench`, the CI perf gate and the package-power measurement remain.
  - **Idle freeze** (`MixEngine`, core). A fed strip whose input and own output (chain + pad, before the strip gain) stay below −120 dBFS for `kIdleHoldSeconds` (10 s) + its latency is frozen: its chain and pad are skipped and it adds zeros; it scans its input's peak, keeps the last 10 ms and keeps its gain glide running. The first block with a sample at or above −120 dBFS (or a NaN) wakes it in that block: the kept 10 ms run through the chain and the pad (output dropped), then the block, with a 5 ms raised-cosine fade-in capped at the strip's latency, so it never touches the new signal's direct path. The freeze is on by default (`setIdleFreeze`, `setIdleHoldSeconds`, `isStripFrozen`, `getStripFrozenBlocks`); `configureFrom` carries the settings to the next engine. Fed-silence strip CPU: a 7.1 strip fed zeros at defaults, 48 kHz / 256, about 300 → 3–4 µs per block (98.7–99.1 % saved over three runs; median of 1500 blocks against an unfed engine, timed with `CallbackTiming`). Null against continuous processing, wake placed 1 s after the freeze: the default preset bit-exact (−240 dB) for impulse, footstep and music at 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz, stereo; MOBA & Strategy bit-exact on a footstep after a 2 s hold (tests). Measured over the 26 factory presets at 48 kHz with the 10 s hold (not a test; a run takes minutes): 20 of 26 null to ≤ −90 dBFS in stereo (16 bit-exact) and 19 of 26 in 7.1; the others hold a state that silence still moves after 10 s: Competitive FPS −70.5, Battle Royale −74.6, Late Night −78.1, Horror Detail −78.6, 7.1 Headphone Surround −80.5, Night Mode −88.6 dBFS (7.1 adds Lo-Fi Chill −53.2). The hold was chosen on these numbers: at 1 s 12 of 26 null (Late Night −32.6 dBFS: its compressor releases for about 10 s). Two limits of a freeze done in `MixEngine` alone: the chain's control grids (the governor's 10 ms ticks, 25 / 50 / 100 ms meter and loudness steps) count processed samples, so a wake at any other point than a whole number of grid periods ticks at another phase against the new signal (worst unaligned residual −36.3 dBFS, MOBA & Strategy; a phase difference, the same as the signal starting up to one grid step later, not a click), and a state that never settles in silence holds its value from the freeze. Closing both needs the chain to advance its counters over a skipped span without DSP (a `ProcessingChain` API; not this unit's files). No golden or render-baseline entry moves: the CLI and the preset renders run `ProcessingChain` without `MixEngine`. Tests: `tests/test_idle_freeze.cpp` (7 cases, 0.01–1.3 s each; no allocation in the freeze or the wake, checked in every null case), and `MixEngine::setIdleFreeze` / `setIdleHoldSeconds` in `tests/test_rtsan.cpp`.
  - **Peak monitoring** (app). `OverloadWatchdog::Sample` gains `peakLoad` (the p99.9 callback of the poll's window over the period), `overBudget` and `late` (the `CallbackTiming` counts); a poll's load is the higher of the average and the peak, over-budget callbacks count as glitches only when there is no glitch count (−1; JUCE's own count holds them otherwise), late callbacks (gaps > 1.5 periods in the host's timestamps) count whenever they are passed, and `EngineController::updateOverloadWatchdog` passes them only for a device that counts no xruns itself. The Done-when's spike case (Balanced / 128 samples, 40 % load, a 3× callback every 500 ms, glitch count −1): the average alone reads 0.40 and never starts an overload in 20 polls; with the peak, OVERLOAD at the 4th poll (2 s), and every spike after the first poll counted as a dropout (19; `tests/app/test_app_overload_peak.cpp`, 3 cases, 0.04 s together). A 2.4× spike (96 % of the period, no overrun anywhere) is caught the same way through the controller; three stalls of two periods on a device with no xrun count start an overload at 30 % load, and are not added on a device that counts xruns. Header: `42% pk 97%` (the peak of the last poll; amber from 90 %; the compact header and a value too wide beside the caption leave the peak to the tooltip, which gives it as "the slowest 0.1 % of the callbacks in the last half second"). Before: the watchdog saw JUCE's 2 Hz average and the device-plus-overrun count, and the header the average.
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07, R1.2 review: the PipeWire device's intervals).** On Flubsound's native PipeWire device ([E48](#e48)) the interval histogram runs on the driver's clock: the device passes `hostTimeNs` = the cycle's `clock.nsec` + the block's offset, and this item's rule takes the host time when the backend gives one. Its intervals there show the graph's cadence (CI: 2.667 ms at 128/48000, 0 late) and cannot show how late PipeWire's data thread woke; the node's own xrun count (R1.2, `pipewire::XrunCounter`: a cycle finished after its deadline) covers that, and `tests/app/test_app_pipewire.cpp`'s xrun case now measures the wall clock beside the stamps (E48's review-fix Status). Nothing changed in `CallbackTiming` or `AudioEngineHost`.
- **What.** Freeze a strip whose *fed* input stays below −120 dBFS longer than its longest tail, outputting latency-aligned zeros and resuming with pre-roll and a 5 ms crossfade. Time every callback into a lock-free histogram (mean, p99, p99.9, max), show "peak %", feed p99.9 into `OverloadWatchdog`, and detect discontinuities from host timestamps. Spread the spectral gate's frame work across hops (moved here from [E43](#e43)). Gate analysers on visibility; an opt-in battery mode (EcoQoS allowed, lighter oversampling). Replace the profile-stepping overload response with a cost-ranked, latency-neutral shed ladder that recovers with hysteresis and only prompts for profile changes. A committed benchmark tool and CI perf gate.
- **Why.** Silence costs 86–93 % of full processing (73.1 µs against 84.9 µs), power throttling is always disabled, a 2 Hz average hides 3–9x callback spikes (Balanced/128 mean 83.9 µs, max 544.6 µs; gate block 64 mean 87.9 µs, max 579 µs), and the only overload response is a latency-profile step (for example Quality → Balanced, 28.17 → 4.00 ms at 48 kHz) that re-prepares the engine and never steps back.
- **Expected benefit.** Fed-but-silent input is the default Windows setup: `processBlock` marks a strip as fed whenever a device input is mapped or a capture slot is live, so the existing 0.5 s hangover never fires for a VB-Cable or loopback input streaming zeros. That is the main idle battery drain on laptops and handhelds. Correction: the watchdog also reacts to 3 device-reported glitches in 10 polls; the gap is backends that report no glitch count (−1). Battery and robustness work, no sound improvement.
- **Approach.** Freeze at the fed-silence point in `processBlock`; freeze AutoLevel and governor state too so wake-up does not pump; err long on tails (HRIR, gate hangover, auto-release). Order the shed ladder from a measured per-module cost table from `tools/flub-bench`, not by hand. CI gates on instruction counts (cachegrind / perf stat), since wall-clock CI is too noisy.
- **Depends on.** [E10](#e10) for rate-aware oversampling; the in-flight engine swap for shed steps that re-prepare; [E44](#e44) for RT status in notifications.
- **Done when.** Frozen-then-resumed strips null against continuous processing to −90 dBFS for impulse, footstep and music at 44.1/48/96 kHz. Fed-silence strip CPU down ≥ 90 %; package power at idle with a VB-Cable input streaming zeros measured before and after. A 3x spike every 500 ms with `glitchCount = −1` is caught. The ladder recovers fully and never changes profile without a prompt. Gate max/mean < 1.5 at block 64. The CI perf job fails an injected 20 % regression.
- **Evidence.** [MixEngine.cpp][ME] (skips only `in == nullptr`); [AudioEngineHost.cpp][AEH] fed/hangover logic (~L757–788); [PlatformServices_win.cpp][PSW] `disablePowerThrottling()` unconditional; [OverloadWatchdog.h][OW] (90 % x 4 polls at 2 Hz; glitch rule); [03 §15.2][03-152] ("throwaway benchmark"); [CI workflow][CI] (no perf job); TRACEABILITY R1.5.

<a id="e48"></a>
#### E48 · Native, turnkey Linux: PipeWire filter node, auto-linking, node.latency, rtkit, Flatpak, CI

*Stability & platform · benefit 3 · difficulty High · E48a 3–5 days; total 10–14 engineer-weeks · Ambitious*

- **What.** **E48a** (High on its own): set `PIPEWIRE_LATENCY` before the device opens (256/48000 Balanced, 128/48000 Low Latency, never locking the quantum on Balanced) and auto-link sink monitors through the libpipewire registry. Then roadmap 3.5: `flub_core` in a `pw_filter` with one port group per strip, sinks created from the app (no script), registry events instead of `pactl` polling, a strip-mapping UI, Flatpak with the Realtime and GlobalShortcuts portals, a headless PipeWire + WirePlumber CI job, and a headless mode for SteamOS Game Mode.
- **Why.** Linux setup needs a script, manual qpwgraph wiring and a typed map, adds about 21.3 ms of quantum, and breaks under Flatpak (no pactl). EasyEffects is one click.
- **Expected benefit.** Matters for SteamOS handhelds and Linux desktops, a few percent of the Steam audience, hence below [E46](#e46) and [E47](#e47). The biggest SteamOS obstacle was missing from the original: most Steam Deck play happens in Game Mode (gamescope), where a desktop JUCE window and tray are unreachable. E48a captures much of the value for the least effort.
- **Approach.** `pw_filter_new_simple` with `PW_FILTER_FLAG_RT_PROCESS`; Game 8 ports, others stereo; null-audio-sink adapters created by the app; `node.latency` and `node.lock-quantum` only for Low Latency; target.object metadata moves; report the actual graph quantum to [E42](#e42). Keep the JUCE ALSA/JACK path as a fallback. Headless mode autostarted in Game Mode and controlled by hotkeys and auto profiles, with a documented "non-Steam game" launcher for the UI. Support WirePlumber 0.4 and 0.5.
- **Depends on.** [E47](#e47)'s journal for metadata moves; [E42](#e42) consumes the quantum; [E44](#e44) Phase A only for the fallback; the in-flight engine swap inside the pw_filter callback; the in-flight auto profiles for UI-less use.
- **Done when.** The CI job passes 20 consecutive runs. Manual matrix: Fedora 40+ GNOME, Ubuntu 24.04, KDE Neon, SteamOS 3.6 Desktop and Game Mode (processed game audio via autostart, no UI interaction). A fresh install reaches processed game audio with no manual steps. `pw-top` shows the requested quantum with other clients unaffected on Balanced. Loopback-measured ≤ 12 ms at 128/48000 on a USB interface. Flatpak passes the same flow. `kill -9` during a routed session restores default routing.
- **Evidence.** [platform/linux/README.md][LNX] (native node, `node.latency`, RealtimeKit as roadmap; Flatpak "has no pactl"); [TRACEABILITY Known gaps][TR-gaps] R1.2, R4.6; [01 §5.3][01-53]; [EngineController.cpp][EC] `applyDeviceInputPolicy` (text map); [08 D9](08-pitfalls-and-solutions.md#d9-linux-diversity).
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 1).** E48a is partly done, in `app/Source/platform/PlatformServices_linux.cpp` (namespace `pipewire`), a defaulted `AppAudioRouter::connectEndpointInputs` in `PlatformServices.h`, one call per routing-worker pass in `AppRouting.cpp`, and four cases in `tests/test_platform_linux.cpp`.
  - **Quantum.** Creating the router (on the message thread, before the device opens) exports `PIPEWIRE_LATENCY=256/48000`. It is a request, never a lock, and a value the user set is kept. Flubsound's requested quantum goes from the pipewire-jack default of 1024/48000 = 21.3 ms to 256/48000 = 5.3 ms. This is by construction; no PipeWire system was available to confirm it in `pw-top`.
  - **Monitor links.** `pw-dump` JSON gives the graph. Flubsound's node is found by pid (the node's, or its client's `pipewire.sec.pid`). Monitor port *k* of each mapped `flubsound_<strip>` sink goes to input *first + k*, bounded by the next strip and the last input. `pw-link` gets integer port ids only. Links made by others are left alone, the router's own links are removed with `pw-link -d` when the map changes, and the graph is re-checked every 10 s. Missing tools, a missing sink, too few input channels or an input that is not a PipeWire node each give a message that names the fix (qpwgraph or Helvum when the tools are missing), logged to stderr.
  - **No sound change.** `core/` is untouched and nothing runs on the audio thread. The existing tests still pass.
  - **Open in E48a.** The Low Latency request (128/48000 with `node.lock-quantum` through `PIPEWIRE_PROPS`) is planned and tested but not used: `EngineController` would have to export it on a profile change and re-open the device. The link status is not yet shown in the routing panel. JUCE's JACK device has only as many inputs as the chosen input client has ports (8 with "Flubsound Game"), so four strips on one device still need a 14-port input or the native node. A run on a real PipeWire system (`pw-top` quantum, links after a device re-open) is still to do. Linking through the libpipewire registry instead of polling `pw-dump`, and the rest of E48, remain.
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2, app).** The Low Latency request is used, in `EngineController` (`planGraphQuantum` / `applyGraphQuantum`, called from `setLatencyProfile` and before the device opens at start-up). Before: every profile ran with the router's 256/48000 request and the planned 128/48000 lock was never exported. After, on Linux in a device session: Low Latency chosen by hand exports `PIPEWIRE_LATENCY=128/48000` and `PIPEWIRE_PROPS={ node.lock-quantum = true }` (2.7 ms, locked), Quality and Balanced 256/48000 (5.3 ms) and remove only a lock this process exported; a `PIPEWIRE_LATENCY` the user exported before Flubsound started wins for every profile (read during static initialisation, before the router's own export) and the user's `PIPEWIRE_PROPS` is never replaced. When the request changed, a JACK or ALSA device is re-opened (PipeWire reads it when a stream opens; a brief dropout). The automatic overload response does not touch the quantum (a smaller quantum adds callbacks). The values mirror `pipewire::planLatencyEnvironment`, which lives in an unnamed namespace of `PlatformServices_linux.cpp` and cannot be called from the app; exposing it would remove the copy. Tested: the plan for each profile and user values, and the exported environment (`tests/app/test_app_ui_status.cpp`); the re-open is not tested (no fake JACK device) and nothing was checked on a real PipeWire system. Open: the routing panel's link status line needs `AppRouting` to keep the status string `connectEndpointInputs` returns (discarded today in `AppRouting.cpp`'s worker pass), which is outside this track's files; the rest of the open list above.
- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, native node).** Partially done: the node, registry linking, `node.latency` and the link status exist and are tested against a real (headless) PipeWire; the app does not offer the node yet. Code in `app/Source/platform/pipewire/` (built only when `pkg-config` finds libpipewire-0.3; `PlatformServices.cmake`, option `FLUB_WITH_PIPEWIRE`; the app builds and links as before without it) and `NativeAudioNode` in `PlatformServices.h`.
