  - **Node.** One `pw_filter` with `PW_FILTER_FLAG_RT_PROCESS` and a port group per strip: Game 7.1 (`game_FL` … `game_SR`), Music, Chat and System stereo, 14 inputs in the engine's default input map, and `out_FL` / `out_FR`. Before: the JUCE JACK path gave the engine as many inputs as the chosen JACK client has ports (8 with "Flubsound Game"). After: 14 in one node. Missing `flubsound_<strip>` sinks are created by the node (the setup script's names, descriptions and positions) and are not lingering, so they vanish with the process; sinks that exist are used. The process callback hands the port buffers to `pipewire::CycleRunner` (splits a quantum above the prepared block, silence for a port without a buffer); on the data thread, measured over 100 cycles: 0 allocations and 0 mutex locks.
  - **Links through the registry.** Registry events keep a mirror of nodes, ports, links and client pids (`pipewire::addGlobal` / `removeGlobal`) and the "default" metadata's `default.audio.sink`; every change is re-planned 20 ms after the last event of a burst. The node links each sink's monitors to its strip's ports by channel and its outputs to the default output or a named sink, never into its own sinks (a default that is one of them, System made the default, gives way to the real sink with the highest `priority.session`). The JUCE path's router uses the same mirror (`pipewire::RegistryLinker`) instead of polling `pw-dump`: before, a re-opened device's ports were re-linked at the next 10 s check with a `pw-dump` child each time; after, 20 ms after the ports appear (the debounce; the linker test covers mapping, a missing sink and unmapping, not a device re-open), with no child process (`pw-dump` / `pw-link` stay as the fallback without libpipewire or a server). Links are made by the link factory with `object.linger = false`.
  - **Quantum.** `node.latency` is the profile's request, and `setLatency` changes it in place. Measured on PipeWire 1.0.5 + WirePlumber 0.4.17 (headless, dummy driver): the server's default quantum 1024/48000 = 21.3 ms → 256/48000 = 5.3 ms with the node on Balanced → 128/48000 = 2.7 ms after `setLatency (LowLatency)`, with no re-open (same node id, cycles continuing). A lock sent with the new latency froze the old quantum (measured: it stayed at 256), so the lock follows once the graph runs at 128.
  - **JUCE device type and status.** `pipewire/PipeWireDeviceType.*`: "PipeWire" / "Flubsound Engine" (input side "Flubsound Engine inputs", so `AudioEngineHost::isLoopbackPair` does not mute it), channel names `Game:FL` … `System:FR`, a lock-free callback swap, `addDeviceType`, `setDeviceLatency`, `setDeviceOutputTarget`, `getDeviceStatus`. `NativeAudioNodeStatus` counts the links wanted and made each way, the output sink, the quantum and rate at the last cycle and what is missing; `pipewire::describeLinks` makes the routing panel's line.

The latter print "skipped" without a server, so CI never fails for lack of one; 30 consecutive runs green here, 0.5 s for all four. CI's `app` job now installs `libpipewire-0.3-dev`, so it compiles and links the node. No sound change: `core/` is untouched.

Then `target.object` metadata moves ([E47](#e47)'s journal), the strip-mapping UI, Flatpak with the Realtime and GlobalShortcuts portals, the headless Game Mode, a CI job with a headless PipeWire + WirePlumber (the 20-run Done-when), WirePlumber 0.5, a graph running at another rate than `node.rate` asks (reported, not handled), a `kill -9` test (the sinks and links are not lingering, so the server removes them with the connection, but this is not tested), loading libpipewire at run time (a build with it links `libpipewire-0.3.so.0`) and the manual matrix and ≤ 12 ms loopback measurement.

Code in `AudioEngineHost.*` (`openDevice`, `getNativeNodeStatus`, `isNativeNodeDevice`), `EngineController.*` (`applyNodeLatency`, `feedGraphQuantum`, the channel-name map in `applyDeviceInputPolicy`), `AppRouting.*` (`getInputLinkStatus`, `areInputsLinked`), `RoutingPanel.*` (the link line), `pipewire/PipeWireDeviceType.cpp` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (job `pipewire`); [platform/linux/README.md][LNX] and [06 §8](06-gui.md#8-per-app-routing-ux).
  - **Offered and taken.** `openDevice` adds the "PipeWire" type after JUCE's own. With nothing saved (a first start) it opens the node when a PipeWire server answers (only in the app's own type list, JUCE's ALSA type present), else JUCE's default as before; nothing is persisted until the user picks a device. Before: a fresh Linux install opened JUCE's ALSA default and processed nothing until the setup script, qpwgraph links and a typed input map. After, on the headless server: the node's sinks, links and every strip's input with no manual step (the Done-when's "fresh install" row, headless only; the desktop matrix is not run).
  - **Every strip mapped.** The device keeps all 14 inputs active (JUCE asked for its default 8, which cut Music, Chat and System off), and `applyDeviceInputPolicy` maps strips by the device's `Strip:Position` channel names: Game 0, Music 8, Chat 10, System 12 (before: Game only); a typed `Game=0;Music=8` map still wins. Tones into `flubsound_game` and `flubsound_chat` reach those strips (Music stays silent), and processed audio reaches the output sink.
  - **Latency in place.** `setLatencyProfile` calls `pipewire::setDeviceLatency` (also after each open and when the node is picked in Settings) instead of the JACK / ALSA re-open. Measured: quantum 256 → 128 frames at 48 kHz with the same device object and callbacks continuing; before, the node stayed on Balanced whatever the profile.
  - **E42 quantum.** `feedGraphQuantum` hands the quantum of the node's last cycle to `setGraphQuantumMs` twice a second, and the device reports no device latency of its own (the node runs in the same graph cycle as the sinks it reads). Before: the device's latency was read once when it started, as one quantum each way, and was 0 when no cycle had run yet, so the total depended on timing. After, on Low Latency: total 5.67 ms = engine 3.00 ms (144 samples) + graph quantum 2.67 ms, following the quantum as it changes. The JUCE ALSA / JACK paths get no quantum fed (not known there).
  - **Link line.** The routing panel shows `pipewire::describeLinks` for the node above its buttons, e.g. *PipeWire: Linked 14 of 14 input channels, output to … (2 of 2), quantum 256/48000*, amber when a link or the output is missing; with a JUCE device, the status `connectEndpointInputs` returns (discarded before), e.g. a missing sink, and nothing when all is linked. While the node is the device the router is given no mapped inputs (the node links its own sinks; before, both would have linked the same ports).
  - **CI.** Job `pipewire` (ubuntu-24.04, `continue-on-error` until 20 consecutive CI runs are green): a headless PipeWire 1.0 + WirePlumber 0.4 + pipewire-pulse on a private runtime folder and session bus, then the `(E48` and `E47 Linux` cases 20 times in a row; a case that prints *(skipped* fails the step. The same loop here: 20 of 20 runs green (7 E48 cases in ≤ 1.31 s per run, the E47 pair in ≤ 1.32 s).
  - **Tests.** `tests/app/test_app_pipewire.cpp`, new case *the running app offers the PipeWire device and plays through it* (`EngineController` with a device on a fresh settings file; a test server only, since it opens the default output): offered and taken on the first start, not persisted, 14 active inputs and the 0 / 8 / 10 / 12 map, Game and Chat tones, the router given nothing to link, the panel line, Low Latency with the same device, the total = engine + graph quantum, the sinks gone after shutdown. `tests/app/test_app_pipewire_links.cpp` (every OS, a fake router): the router's status on the link line in amber, gone once linked, and the device map handed to the router. `flub_app_tests` 263 / 263 plain, under `xvfb-run -a`, and under Xvfb against the headless server.
  - **Open.** The 20 CI runs (then drop `continue-on-error`), the manual matrix (Fedora, Ubuntu, KDE Neon, SteamOS), the ≤ 12 ms loopback measurement, `target.object` metadata moves instead of `pactl`, Flatpak with the Realtime and GlobalShortcuts portals, the headless Game Mode, WirePlumber 0.5, a graph at another rate than `node.rate`, loading libpipewire at run time (done 2026-10-07, the R1.2 Status below), and a `PIPEWIRE_LATENCY` the user exported, which the node's own `node.latency` does not follow (the JUCE path's request does).
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07, R4.6: the strip-mapping UI).** The multi-strip device input map has a UI now: Settings › Routing › *Input map*. For each strip, *Not fed* or the first input channel, offered for every active input of the open device (16 without one). **Fill in one after another** sets Game 1–8, Music 9–10, Chat 11–12 and System 13–14 (`EngineController::consecutiveInputMap`); **Clear map** goes back to *Input feeds strip*. Before, the map was only the typed `deviceInput.map` key (`Game=0;Music=8`). After, it is written by the page (`EngineController::setDeviceInputMapChannels`, `parseDeviceInputMap` / `formatDeviceInputMap`) and applied at once: the host's map reads 0 / 8 / 10 / 12 after *Fill in*, and Game from 0 again after *Clear*. The Linux router links each mapped sink's monitor to those inputs by itself (E48a, `connectEndpointInputs`), so a JUCE JACK / ALSA device with 14 inputs needs no qpwgraph step and no typed key. Tested in `tests/app/test_app_routing_move_away.cpp` (the page, every OS; the text format and the consecutive layout); built and run on Windows only, not yet on a Linux desktop. Open: as above (the strip-mapping UI is no longer on that list).
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07, R1.2: libpipewire at run time, xruns, driver time, recovery, hot-plug).** Done in `pipewire/PipeWireLibrary.*` and `PipeWireApi.h` (new), `PipeWireNative.*`, `PipeWireCycle.h`, `PipeWireDeviceType.*`, `PlatformServices.h` / `.cmake` and `AudioEngineHost.cpp`. This PC cannot compile Linux code: everything Linux was built and run on CI (`wip/pipewire-backend`; gcc and clang with `-Wshadow -Werror`, the `app` and `pipewire` jobs, PipeWire 1.0.5 + WirePlumber 0.4 headless), see the runs below.
  - **libpipewire at run time.** Before: a build with libpipewire linked it (`NEEDED libpipewire-0.3.so.0`), so that binary could not start where the library is missing. After: the headers only (an imported interface target without pkg-config's link libraries, plus `CMAKE_DL_LIBS`) and `dlopen ("libpipewire-0.3.so.0", RTLD_NOW | RTLD_LOCAL)` the first time a connection is wanted. 30 entry points are resolved into a table typed from the installed headers (`decltype`); `PipeWireNative.cpp` reaches them through one redirect per function placed after the headers, so a call that bypasses the table fails the link. Without the library: no "PipeWire" type, `NativeAudioNode::isSupported()` false with the reason (*PipeWire's client library (libpipewire-0.3.so.0) is not installed: …*), the registry linker falls back to `pw-dump` / `pw-link`, the first-run default stays JUCE's ALSA. On CI: *libpipewire 1.0.5 opened at run time (built against 1.0.5)*; the `app` job's new step checks `NEEDED libpipewire` is gone (1 → 0, the app and `flub_app_tests`) and runs the app and the loading case with the library hidden. A system without the library keeps ALSA (on PulseAudio through its ALSA plug-in); documented in [platform/linux/README.md][LNX], not tested (no such system on CI). That held only without the library: with it, a first start took the node wherever a server answered, until the review fixes below.
  - **Xruns into the app's accounting.** Before: the device returned JUCE's −1 ("not counted"): no `xr` in the header and the overload watchdog counted only the callback timing's late intervals. After: `pipewire::XrunCounter` after each cycle on the data thread: *late* (the node finished after the driver's `clock.nsec` + one quantum: the driver found the graph unfinished, PipeWire's own xrun) or *missed* (`clock.position` skipped ahead on the same driver and rate); a new driver (the output moved to another device), a new rate, a restart or freewheeling re-bases without counting. `NativeAudioNode::getXrunCount()` → `getXRunCount()` → `EngineStatus::xruns`, the header's `· n xr`, the overload watchdog, the diagnostics log. CI run 37585773116, the `pipewire` job's 20 loops, 0.6 s on the idle headless server each: 225 callbacks against 225.03 expected at quantum 128/48000 in all 20, 0 xruns counted in every window; in 9 of the 20 the count was already 1 when the window began, from the device's first cycles while the node joins the graph (the watchdog takes its first reading as the baseline and needs 3 glitches within 5 s, so this starts no overload; the header does show `· 1 xr`; the review fixes below stop judging a run's first cycles). Unit cases with made-up clocks on every OS: in time at exactly the deadline, late 1 ns after it, two missed cycles, late and missed once, a quantum change, a new driver, rate or position, freewheel and an unknown time.
  - **Driver time.** Each callback carries `hostTimeNs` = the cycle's `clock.nsec` + the block's offset (before: no host time, so the E45 timing used the callback's start). A 1024-frame quantum in 256-frame blocks reads 0 / 5.33 / 10.67 / 16 ms after the cycle's start; before, the timing saw three back-to-back blocks and one 4-period gap, i.e. one "late" interval per cycle whenever the quantum exceeds the block. CI (the same 20 windows): mean interval 2.667 ms at 128/48000, 0 late intervals.
  - **Hot-plug and recovery.** The output already followed the default sink and its comings and goings through registry events (batch 2); a live case now checks it: a second sink made the default takes the outputs, and when it goes they move to the remaining sink, with the same device and no re-open (20 of 20 loops: output to the remaining "speakers" sink). New: a removed node or a lost server (the connection drops) reaches the host. The node reports it once per run (`Callback::nodeError`, loop thread), the device hands it to the message thread. **Found here:** JUCE 9.0.2's `AudioDeviceManager` starts every device through `CallbackMaxSizeEnforcer`, which does not forward `audioDeviceError`, so an error a device gives its callback never reaches the manager's callbacks (a cross-platform case pins this: *JUCE 9.0.2's AudioDeviceManager drops a device's audioDeviceError*). The PipeWire device therefore reports to the host it was started for (`pipewire::setDeviceErrorTarget`, registered in `AudioEngineHost::audioDeviceAboutToStart`), and the host shows the device error and runs its E51 recovery, which re-opens the device with a new node. Before: `pw-cli destroy` on the node left the device open and silent with no error and nothing re-opened (CI run 37580473143, the error only given to the device's callback: no error after 3 s, recovery attempts 0). After (run 37585773116, 20 of 20 loops, recovery settle time shortened to 100 ms for the test; the app's default is 1.5 s): the host has the error 12 – 14 ms after the destroy and callbacks run again from a new node 260 – 281 ms after it, with one recovery attempt. The same JUCE gap means no JUCE backend's own device errors reach `AudioEngineHost::audioDeviceError` either; the E51 recovery runs from the device watcher's events instead, which is what the owner's re-plug and sleep checks exercised. Not changed here.
  - **Node properties.** `node.nick` and `application.name` "Flubsound Pro" added; `node.name` stays the identifier `flubsound_engine` (tests, docs and patchbay users match it) and `node.description` "Flubsound Engine". No `media.class` on purpose: a DSP filter (pw-filter's example properties) that links itself; `Audio/Duplex` would let the session manager offer it as a default sink / source and link applications into it.
  - **Tests.** `tests/test_pipewire_cycle.cpp` (2 cases, every OS); `tests/app/test_app_pipewire.cpp`: the run-time loading (no server needed; it also runs in the `app` job), the device's xruns and time, the hot-plug and the recovery (a headless server; in the `pipewire` job's 20 loops); `tests/app/test_app_device_types.cpp` (3 cases, every OS). No sound change: `core/` is untouched and the engine's processing is the same.
  - **ASIO (R1.2, Windows).** See [E42](#e42)'s Status (2026-10-07): `FLUB_ASIO` build option, the single-client note and CI's `asio` job; in runs 37582910555 and 37585773116 it downloaded the official SDK 2.3.4 archive (the pinned SHA-256 matched), built the app and `flub_app_tests` with JUCE's ASIO type and offered it (0 drivers on the runner).
  - **CI.** Run 37585773116 on `wip/pipewire-backend`: all 12 jobs green (the four core legs, RTSan, ASan + UBSan, fuzz, the three `app` jobs with the *Without libpipewire* step, `pipewire` 20 of 20 loops, `asio`); 37582910555 before it, the same set green.
  - **Open.** As above (the desktop matrix and real sound cards, Flatpak, Game Mode, WirePlumber 0.5); a server restart is handled by the same path as a removed node but only node removal is tested; the PulseAudio path is documented, not run (the first start's choice there is unit-tested since the review fixes below, which also settle the start-up `1 xr` above).
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07, R1.2 review fixes).** In `PipeWireCycle.h`, `PipeWireDeviceType.*`, `PipeWireGraph.h`, `PipeWireNative.cpp`, `AudioEngineHost.cpp` and `SettingsDialog.*`. The Linux code was built and run on CI only (`wip/pipewire-backend`: run 37595218829 at commit ca371a5 and run 37605340907 at e37d7a2, the final code, all 12 jobs green in each); the Windows parts locally with MSVC as well.
  - **First start only where PipeWire plays audio.** Before: the node was preferred whenever libpipewire loaded and a server answered, so a PulseAudio desktop with a PipeWire running for screen capture would have opened a node with no output (silence), and the docs' "a PulseAudio-only system keeps ALSA" held only without the library. After: `pipewire::serverPlaysAudio` connects a probe session (a few ms) and `pipewire::playsAudio` asks for an output sink that is not Flubsound's own (the default when it exists, else any `Audio/Sink`); without one the first start keeps ALSA and says why on stderr, and the entry stays listed. Unit case with made-up graphs on every OS (video nodes and a microphone only: no; only Flubsound's own sinks: no; a card's sink, the default or not, or WirePlumber's `auto_null`: yes). On CI's headless server the probe says yes and the app-level case still takes the node on its first start. No PulseAudio desktop has run it.
  - **No stale node error after a re-open.** Before: `close()` left a node error the message thread had not handled yet, and JUCE re-opens the same device object for a new rate or buffer size (`setAudioDeviceSetup`: `open()` on the open device), so the old run's error reached the new run's host (a device banner and a recovery check). After: `close()` cancels the pending update and drops the error once the node has stopped (no `nodeError` can follow: it runs under the loop lock that `stop()` takes). New case: `pw-cli destroy` on the node, the error pending, a buffer change to 128 on the same object, then the message thread runs: CI run 37595218829, 20 of 20 loops: the error (*PipeWire removed the Flubsound node*) was pending before the re-open, none reached the host after it, the same device object ran a new node, and callbacks went on (+150 / 151 in the 0.4 s that followed).
  - **Start-up xrun.** Before: 1 xrun counted within the first 20 cycles after an open in 9 of 20 loops (run 37585773116) and 4 of 20 (run 37588726660) on the idle server, so the header showed `· 1 xr`. After: `XrunCounter` does not judge the first `kSettleCycles` = 2 cycles of a run, nor the first two after the filter enters STREAMING again (`restart()`, from the state callback), which also re-bases a pause on the same driver instead of counting the cycles the graph ran without the node. Unit cases (every OS): two late start-up cycles not counted, the third counted; after `reset()` the same; a 500-cycle pause with `restart()` not counted, without it 1 xrun. CI run 37595218829: no start-up xrun in any of the 20 loops (before: 4 – 9 of 20), and still none in any 0.6 s idle window. The next run (37598480193, a docs-only commit) had 1 in 20, already counted when the host had had no callback yet. A first follow-up counted from the device's `start()` and let a new driver, rate or restarted driver settle like a start (unit case: an output moved to another driver, its first two cycles not judged, even late, the third counted); its run (37602022316) had 5 in 20, again all before the host's first callback: in the cycles after `start()` while the host prepares the engine on the message thread and the node plays silence. So the device now takes its baseline at its first callback, on the data thread, and settles the count there (`NativeAudioNode::settleXruns` → `XrunCounter::restart`: the first callback cycle and the next are not judged, the engine's first blocks run cold); `getXRunCount` is 0 before it. Across those 60 loops all 6 start-up xruns came before the host's first callback, and none in any idle window. CI run 37605340907 (commit e37d7a2): no start-up xrun in 20 of 20 loops and none in any idle window; in the same loops the stale error reached the host in 0 of 20, the wall clock read 2.6664 – 2.6671 ms mean (longest 2.70 – 3.39 ms, each callback done at most 0.60 – 1.30 ms after its cycle's start) and the recovery had the error 12 – 15 ms and callbacks again 261 – 277 ms after the destroy (the host's recovery check runs on a 5 Hz timer, so this moves by up to 200 ms between runs: 215 – 319 ms in run 37595218829).
  - **Wall clock.** The device case's timing checks only restated the stamps: each block's `hostTimeNs` is the driver's `clock.nsec` + the block's offset, and E45 takes the host time when given, so on this device its interval histogram is the driver's cadence (see [E45](#e45)). A second device-manager callback now reads the steady clock (CLOCK_MONOTONIC, the driver's clock) in each callback, after the engine's work, next to the stamp. CI run 37595218829, 20 loops at 128/48000 (period 2.667 ms): 225 – 226 callbacks per 0.6 s, mean wall-clock interval 2.6665 – 2.6670 ms, longest 2.70 – 3.58 ms, none over 1.5 periods; each callback done 0.29 – 0.35 ms (median) and at most 0.33 – 1.27 ms after its cycle's start, well inside the period. The case checks the mean (± 10 %), a median ≥ 0 (the stamps are on the same clock) and the maximum < one period.
  - **Settings › Audio's note (Windows).** Before: the ASIO and Exclusive Mode notes told users to send other apps to "Flubsound's virtual devices", which Windows does not have (R4.6: the driver is a design). After: assign the app to a strip (per-app capture) and move its own output to another device (*Settings › System › Sound › Volume mixer*, the [E47](#e47) double-audio fix, confirmed on the owner's PC with the shared types), a virtual cable chosen as exclusive mode's input, or a shared type; the test checks for that wording and that neither note mentions a virtual device. The note now sits under the selector: before, picking such a type pushed the selector's combo boxes down by the note's height plus 8 px (with these texts at the dialog's minimum width: 92 px for ASIO, 80 px for PipeWire); after, 0 px (the case checks the selector's position with and without a note; it failed on a layout that pushes the selector down). Merged with R1.1 (2026-10-07): the LATENCY panel ([E42](#e42) E42c / E42d) follows the note, or the selector when the type has none, so the order under the selector is note, then LATENCY; the case also checks the panel's place (it failed on a layout that keeps the panel under the selector whatever the note: the panel then started at y 466 inside the ASIO note's 464-548). Polish (2026-10-07): the exclusive-mode note adds that Flubsound can make the move itself, Settings › Routing › *Move the app's own sound away automatically* ([E47](#e47), R4.5, off by default); the ASIO note says that switch cannot do it with ASIO (an ASIO output is no endpoint `AppRouting` can name: no doubling guard, no new moves, as its doubling case checks); the test checks both wordings. Not tried with ASIO or exclusive mode on the owner's PC; whether Windows still lists an app as playing to an output held in exclusive mode (which the automatic move needs) is untested.
  - **Counts.** The CI line above said 13 jobs; the workflow has 12 (the four core legs, RTSan, ASan + UBSan, fuzz, three `app`, `pipewire`, `asio`). platform/linux/README.md said R1.2 added three app cases; it was four (loading, xruns and time, hot-plug, recovery), and the stale-error case is the fifth.
  - **Tests.** `tests/test_pipewire_cycle.cpp`: 2 new cases (the settling, the first start's choice); `tests/app/test_app_pipewire.cpp`: the stale-error case, the wall clock in the xrun case, the probe in the app-level case; `tests/app/test_app_device_types.cpp`: the note's Windows wording and its place. Locally (MSVC 19.51, warnings as errors): the full `flub_tests` and `flub_app_tests` (313 / 313) pass under ctest, last on the final code (e37d7a2), with the 4 core and 3 app R1.2 cases that run on Windows. CI's `asio` job ran the note case with JUCE's real ASIO type built in (its placement then checked with the PipeWire stand-in). No sound change: `core/` and the engine's processing are untouched.
- **Status (local, Windows, 2026-10-07, R1.2 after the merge: idle xruns on CI attributed).** CI run 37634884865 (commit 70a2a98, all seven 2026-10-07 items merged) failed the device's xrun case in loop 1 of the `pipewire` job. In the 0.6 s idle window it counted 1 xrun and 1 late interval (driver clock), with 225 callbacks against 225.02 expected, 5.05 ms on the wall clock between two callbacks (1.9 periods), and each callback done 0.34 ms (median) and at most 0.95 ms after its cycle's start. On `wip/pipewire-backend` the case had no idle xrun in any of its CI runs. Done in `tests/app/test_app_pipewire.cpp` and the `pipewire` job's loop (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`); no product code changed.
  - **Root cause: the runner, not the merge.** Nothing merged since adds work, a lock or a wait to the device's path. `PipeWireNative.cpp`, `PipeWireDeviceType.cpp` and `PipeWireCycle.h` are unchanged (`PipeWireGraph.h`: a comment). `AudioEngineHost::audioDeviceIOCallbackWithContext` gained only atomic loads (R1.1's two latency-probe slots; R1.5's output pin, device signal source and output tap), all null or false outside a measurement or a soak, and `processBlock` one null check per strip. `core/`'s processing chain is unchanged (R5.3's neural runner runs only with a model). The failing loop's callbacks were done no later than on the branch: 0.34 ms after the cycle start (median), against 0.57 – 0.62 ms in run 37605340907. The stamps jumped by two periods while the callbacks on both sides ran promptly in their own cycles. So the graph ran one cycle without the node, whose data thread (no real-time priority on the runner) was not scheduled in time.
  - **The case attributes instead of failing blindly.** No xruns, no late intervals and every callback done within its cycle stay the expectation; each exception in the window must be a scheduling stall. The wall-clock recorder now runs throughout. Per callback it records the device's xrun count, the data thread's CPU time (`CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID`) and its voluntary and involuntary context switches (`getrusage (RUSAGE_THREAD)`), so each xrun, late interval and late-finishing callback is located. A stall is a callback whose cycle's CPU time fit the period and that waited for nothing but its trigger (a voluntary switch beyond the usual one would be a lock or a wait in the engine), yet ended more than 1.5 periods after the one before, or was preempted while finishing after its cycle. The case fails on an exception without such a stall, on one it cannot locate, on more than 2 of a kind in 0.6 s, and on any cycle whose CPU time reaches the period (new: the engine's own work, whatever the scheduler did). The job's loop prints the new `own work:` and `stall attribution:` lines.
  - **Why CPU time.** The first version (commit d4f05a5, run 37640705514) used the wall clock only. Loops 1 – 6 had no xrun. Loop 7 had one of a second kind: no missed cycle (driver-clock gap 2.67 ms), but one callback done 2.87 ms after its cycle's start (the engine's callback over its 2.67 ms period, at most 2.82 ms), 4.96 ms after the one before. The xrun was attributed to that gap, but the case still failed on "every callback finished within its cycle" and "no engine callback over its period": both read wall time, which cannot tell the engine's own work from the kernel holding the thread. That loop's CPU time was not measured yet. The other 11 jobs of that run were green.
  - **CI.** Run 37643504114 (commit 5e04bac): all 12 jobs green, and the `pipewire` job re-run on the same commit (attempt 2) green as well: 40 of 40 loops. No xrun, late interval or late finish in any idle window, and no start-up xrun; 225 callbacks per window. The data thread's CPU time per cycle was 0.42 – 0.63 ms (median) and at most 0.45 – 0.84 ms, against the 2.67 ms period. The thread was preempted in 0 – 3 of 225 cycles, with one voluntary switch per cycle (its wait for the trigger) in every loop. Callbacks were done at most 0.51 – 2.19 ms after their cycle's start. The 2.19 ms (attempt 2, loop 11) came with at most 0.78 ms of CPU and no preemption: a wake-up 1.5 ms late, under one period, so no xrun. Since the merge, 2 of 48 loops had an idle xrun (run 37634884865 loop 1: a missed cycle; run 37640705514 loop 7: a late finish), both at a wall-clock stall of more than 1.5 periods; in the first the callbacks around it were done within 0.95 ms of their cycle's start, in the second the CPU time was not measured yet.
  - **Open.** The tolerance is for CI. A desktop where the node gets real-time priority (RealtimeKit or `rtprio`) should show none; not checked on one.

<a id="e52"></a>
#### E52 · Versioned, migratable, self-contained preset and state format (v2)

*Stability & platform · benefit 3 · difficulty Medium · Phase A 1–1.5 weeks; Phase B 1.5–2 weeks · Quick win (Phase A)*

- **What.** Phase A: the plug-in reads `flubStateVersion` and resets parameters missing from saved state to their defaults; a golden-render regression per factory preset (1/3-octave band RMS and integrated LUFS within ±0.05 dB, on one reference platform); unknown-key and clamp warnings from `fromJson` in CLI stderr/`--json` and as an app toast. Phase B: `version` as major.minor (newer minor loads with warnings, newer major is refused), a migration registry and alias table, full-state user presets, `uuid` and `contentHash` (auto-profile rules keyed by uuid), and settings `schemaVersion` with `isValidFile`, rotating `.bak1–3` and `.corrupt-<ts>` quarantine. The signed `.flubpack`, asset store and Scene export move to [E54](#e54).
- **Why.** Changing any default re-voices every saved preset silently; newer presets are refused; a corrupt settings file silently wipes routing and hotkeys; renaming a preset file breaks auto-profile rules; plug-in project recall is not deterministic.
- **Expected benefit.** Protects voicing and recall, not sound. Verified with the CLI: the typo key `bost` renders with exit 0 at "boost 10 %"; a version 1.1 preset is refused with exit 2. Factory presets store only 8–32 keys. The plug-in writes its state version and never reads it, and missing parameters "keep their current values", a real DAW recall bug. Must land before the first public release, not ahead of audible or safety work.
- **Approach.** As above; coordinate uuid-based rules now, before the in-flight auto-profile rule format freezes (the one time-critical coupling). Factory presets may stay sparse because the golden test protects them.
- **Depends on.** The in-flight auto profiles; [E53](#e53) fuzzers cover `fromJson` and migrations; [E54](#e54) for pack signing.
- **Done when.** `bost` reported with an exact warning; 1.1 loads with warnings; 2.0 refused; v1 → v2 round trip. Truncated or garbage settings restore `.bak` and leave a `.corrupt` file. Plug-in: a parameter absent from an older state equals its default after load. Changing one default in the parameter layout fails CI.
- **Evidence.** [PresetIO.cpp][PIO] (rejects version > 1 ~L39, drops unknown keys ~L57, sparse `toJson`); [PluginProcessor.cpp][PP] (writes the version, never reads it; ~L462–473); `AppSettings.cpp` (no `isValidFile`, autosave after 2 s); [DeviceProfiles.cpp][DP] (rejects version > 1); [PresetManager.cpp][PM] ids `user:<file name>`.
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 1).** Phase A and Phase B core done in `core/*/io/PresetIO.*`, `presets/factory/*.json` (a permanent uuid each), `plugin/Source/*`, `tools/flubsound-cli/CliOptions.cpp`, `tests/test_presets_schema.cpp`, `tests/test_presets_golden.cpp`, `tests/golden/` and a step in CI's `core` job (gcc leg). Measured with the CLI: `bost` rendered silently at exit 0 → `warning: preset: unknown parameter "bost" ignored (did you mean "boost"?)` on stderr (also with `--quiet`) and in `--json` `render.notes`, still exit 0; clamps, bad labels, wrong types and an invalid uuid are reported the same way. A 1.1 preset: refused (exit 2) → loads as version 1 with a newer-minor warning; 2.1 loads with a warning; 3.0 refused ("saved by a newer Flubsound version", exit 2, file untouched). `preset::migrations()` is the registry (1 → 2 fills the frozen version-1 defaults, today `virt.lfe` 0 dB; pure, input untouched); all 25 factory presets round trip v1 → v2 → v2 with identical values, uuid and `contentHash`. `tests/golden/parameter-defaults.json` pins all 198 defaults, so changing one fails CI until the major is bumped with a migration; `tests/golden/factory-presets.json` pins each factory preset's uuid and `contentHash` (every platform) and a golden render (integrated LUFS and 30 1/3-octave bands within ±0.05 dB; gcc Release on Linux x86-64 only, about 3 s). Plug-in: `setStateInformation` resolves every parameter through `preset::resolveSavedState` (absent → default, out of range → clamped, non-numeric → default, each reported); *Import* shows the reader's warnings and *Export* writes a new uuid. Probe against JUCE 9.0.2 (a saved state without `virt.lfe` and `max.drive`, loaded into an instance set to +1 dB / 20 dB): +6 / 0 dB (the defaults) after load, and the same with the previous `replaceState`-only code, so the "keep their current values" above was not reproduced on JUCE 9.0.2 (JUCE appends a default PARAM for a missing one); the rule is now explicit, clamped and independent of JUCE internals. No sound moved: the factory presets' resolved values are unchanged (the version-1 shim predates this item and its values are pinned by `contentHash`). Review: the golden renders had not been re-recorded for the batch's later intended sound changes and failed on the reference leg (8 presets beyond 0.05 dB); `tests/golden/factory-presets.json` was re-recorded for those 8 only - [E05](#e05) stage 1 (Club Loud −11.70 → −12.63, Bass Head −16.09 → −16.39, Punchy Pop −15.59 → −15.74, Cinematic Adventure −15.29 → −15.53 LUFS) and [E12](#e12) Phase A's crossfeed (Classical & Jazz −17.08 → −16.33, Audiophile Subtle −17.17 → −16.77, Earbuds −16.58 → −16.14, Bluetooth Headphones −16.81 → −16.50 LUFS) - and the other 17 entries were kept byte for byte; the step then passes on gcc and on clang. The plug-in read saved values with `strtod`, which follows the host's C locale (a ',' decimal locale set by a Qt-based host would have reset every fractional parameter to its default); it now uses the core's locale-independent JSON number grammar. uuid lookup: `preset::findByUuid` (core). **Phase 2 batch 2 (app half and Phase B remainder), done** in `app/Source/presets/PresetManager.*`, `app/Source/settings/AppSettings.*` and one line in `EngineController`'s constructor. A preset's app id is now its uuid: the 25 factory presets are keyed by their permanent uuid; a user preset without one (or with one another preset has, e.g. a copied file; the file that had it on the last scan keeps it) is given a new uuid written into its file once, every other member kept. `findById` still accepts the legacy ids (`factory:<file>`, `user:<file name>`) and uuids in any case, and `getLegacyIdAliases()` is the one-time id → uuid alias map: `AppSettings::migratePresetReferences` rewrites each strip's last preset and every automatic profile rule through it once and records `settings.schemaVersion` 2 (unknown ids are kept and still reported as missing). Tested with a settings file saved before uuids (`tests/app/test_app_auto_profile.cpp`): its rule and strip presets move to the uuids, and the rule still applies after the user preset file is renamed; before, a rename broke the rule. Hotkeys hold no preset reference (Next / Previous step through the list), so there was nothing to key. *Save As* writes a new uuid, overwriting (*Save*, or *Save As* over the same name) keeps it, and the new `renameUserPreset` keeps it; user presets are saved full state (all 195 sound parameters, app state still out; before, only the values that differed from today's defaults). Import keeps a unique uuid and replaces a taken one (a preset exported and re-imported). Reader warnings reach `PresetManager::onPresetWarnings` on import and on every load into a strip. Settings: `isValidSettingsFile` (a PROPERTIES document, `settings.schemaVersion` a positive integer); a file that fails it is renamed to `<file>.corrupt-<yyyymmdd-hhmmss>` and the newest valid `.bak1`–`.bak3` restored (`getRecovery()`); each start backs a changed, valid file up to `.bak1` (`.bak1` → `.bak2` → `.bak3`). A truncated settings file: loaded empty and replaced by the next autosave 2 s later (routing, rules and hotkeys gone) → quarantined and restored from `.bak1` with its routes, rules and hotkey strip (`tests/app/test_app_settings.cpp`, also garbage with a damaged `.bak1` → `.bak2`, and no backup → defaults). A newer `schemaVersion` is read as it is and never lowered. No sound moved (no golden or render baseline entry changes). Open (the UI toast, the recovery notice and the header's *Rename* through `renameUserPreset` followed, see the UI status line below): E53 fuzzers over `fromJson` / `migrate`; a per-ISA tolerance for the golden renders (T12); and a host / pluginval recall test (the plug-in has no test target; the rule itself is tested in core).
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2, UI).** The app's toast and notices done in `ui/NoticeBanners.*`, `MainComponent`, `HeaderBar` and `EngineController`. Before: `onPresetWarnings` had no consumer (a typo'd key loaded silently in the app), `getRecovery()` had no notice, and the header's *Rename* saved a new preset and deleted the old one (a new uuid: a rule that played it showed "missing preset"). After: reader warnings of a preset imported or loaded into a strip are queued (`takePresetWarnings()`, at most 8) and shown as an amber notice for 12 s, *Preset "Typo": unknown parameter "bost" ignored (did you mean "boost"?) (+1 other warning)*, every warning in the tooltip (an import's warnings, reported after its list change, are announced asynchronously); a damaged settings file shows which backup came back (or the defaults) and where the damaged file was kept, until dismissed; *Rename* calls `EngineController::renameUserPreset`, which keeps the uuid, so the strip's saved last preset and the automatic profile rule keep playing it (a preset that had no uuid gets one and both follow). Tested in `tests/app/test_app_ui_status.cpp`; screenshots `--state preset-warning,recovery`. No sound moved.

<a id="e54"></a>
#### E54 · Release engineering: signed installers, update channel, content packs, logging, crash reporting, diagnostics and opt-in telemetry

*Stability & platform · benefit 3 · difficulty High · diagnostics slice 1–1.5 weeks; total 11–15 engineer-weeks without telemetry · Ambitious*

- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, diagnostics and test installers).** Partially done: parts of (1) and (2) and unsigned installers for (3), in `app/Source/diagnostics/`, *Settings › Diagnostics*, `installer/`, `tools/scripts/package-desktop.sh` and the CI `app` job. Before: no Logger, FileLogger or crash handler (the device error went to stderr only), the Windows test build was a folder, macOS a `.zip`, Linux a `.tar.gz`. After: (a) a rotating log `<user data>/Logs/flubsound.log` (512 KB × 3 files; every line redacted: home folder → `~`, login and computer name → `<user>` / `<computer>`) with a start line (version, JUCE, OS, CPU, memory), a stop line, crash reports the previous session left and the app's stderr messages; (b) engine events without new audio-thread code: `DiagnosticsMonitor` reads, at 2 Hz on the message thread, the counters the audio thread already keeps in atomics (device glitches, per-strip safety clips, NaN / Inf blocks dropped, samples muted beyond +24 dBFS, overload episodes) and logs the device opening, changing, closing and its errors at once and the counters summed, at most one line per 10 s ("Last 10 s: 3 glitches (xruns or late callbacks); Game: 2 safety clips"); (c) a crash handler (`CrashHandler.*`) writes `crash-<unix time>-<pid>.txt`: POSIX `sigaction` for SIGSEGV / SIGBUS / SIGILL / SIGFPE / SIGABRT on an alternate stack, async-signal-safe (paths and header prepared at start, `backtrace` warmed up, then only `open` / `write` / `backtrace_symbols_fd`), with the signal, fault address (or the sending process), the stack and on Linux the executable mappings for `addr2line`, on macOS the main image's load address for `atos`, then the default action is re-raised so the OS still sees the crash; Windows: an unhandled-exception filter (code, address as module + offset, the stack as module + offset lines, and a `MiniDumpWriteDump` minidump from a helper thread, dbghelp loaded at start) and a SIGABRT handler; `std::terminate` records the uncaught exception's `what()` as the reason; the newest 10 reports are kept; (d) *Settings › Diagnostics*: the log folder with *Show*, the crash-report count and *Export diagnostics…*, one zip with `system.txt` (versions, machine, the audio device and its state, the device lists, output-device profile, capture streams, loopback guard, strips and counters), the settings file and route journal, the logs and the newest 3 crash reports, text redacted, no audio or presets. Measured: SIGSEGV sent to the running app under Xvfb → a 5.3 KB report (the sending process, a 9-frame stack, 31 executable mappings), exit status 139 kept, and the next start logged "The previous session crashed: crash-….txt"; a forced invalid write (0x10) and an uncaught exception in a forked child → reports with `Fault address: 0x…10` and `Reason: uncaught exception: …` (`tests/app/test_app_diagnostics.cpp`, 6 cases, 0.1 s; the Windows case writes a synthetic access violation's report and minidump through the filter's own writer and runs in CI only). (e) Test installers, unsigned: Windows `FlubsoundPro-Setup-<version>.exe` (Inno Setup, `installer/windows/FlubsoundPro.iss`; publisher "Flubes and Claude (co-authors)", the app in Program Files, the VST3 in `Common Files\VST3`, Start menu, uninstaller that keeps the user's data) beside the folder in the `Flubsound-windows` artifact, the Windows app job now linking the static C++ runtime so no VC++ redistributable is needed; macOS `Flubsound-macos.dmg` (hdiutil, an Applications link, retried on "Resource busy", the `.zip` kept); Linux a `.desktop` entry and a per-user `install.sh` in the `.tar.gz`. Not run here: the Windows and macOS packaging (their first CI run is the check). Open: the lock-free audio-thread event ring with per-event timestamps (this slice reads existing counters at 2 Hz, so it gives counts, not the instant of each xrun), Crashpad / sentry-native with CI symbol upload and consent-gated upload (reports stay on the machine and are symbolised by hand with the build's symbols, which CI does not archive yet), signing and notarisation (MSIX / pkg / AppImage), the update channel, content packs and telemetry; none of the Done-when rows is met yet. *Review (Phase 3 batch 2, after the restart):* `install.sh` writes the menu entry line by line instead of with `sed`, so a home folder with `&`, `|` or `\` gets the right `Exec` line (checked with `home a&b|c`); the package and `install.sh` / `--uninstall` re-run on the GCC build; the Windows crash writer, the Inno Setup script and `make-dmg.sh` were read against the Windows 10 SDK / Inno Setup 6 / hdiutil documentation, nothing to change (a stack overflow writes the text report on the overflowed thread's remaining guard stack, so that report is best effort; the minidump runs on its own thread).
- **Status (p3b4, notify-only update check and CI symbols).** Part (4)'s software half done, notify only, and the symbol half of (2); signed installers and installing an update stay gated on certificates. *Update check* (`app/Source/diagnostics/UpdateCheck.*`, *Settings › Diagnostics › Updates*, started by `FlubsoundApplication`): off by default (`updates.check`), and while it is off nothing runs (no thread, no request). On, it reads `https://api.github.com/repos/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/releases` over HTTPS once at start (at most once in 24 h), when it is switched on and on *Check now* (`juce::WebInputStream`: WinINet on Windows, NSURLSession on macOS; on Linux, where the app is built without libcurl, the system's `curl` with `--proto =https`), compares semantic versions (semver 2.0: pre-releases below their release, numeric identifiers numerically, build metadata ignored) with the running version on the chosen channel (Stable: releases not marked pre-release; Beta: all), and shows the result in the page with an *Open the download page* link; a newer release is announced once in the tray. It never downloads or installs anything, so no signing key is involved. A feed is refused when it is not JSON, not a list of release objects, over 1 MB, lacks a release's tag, page or pre-release flag or has a field of the wrong type, or points a release page outside `https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/releases/` (another host, `http`, another repository, `..`, `@`); drafts and tags that are not versions are skipped. Tests (`tests/app/test_app_update_check.cpp`, 8 cases, 0.2 s): semver.org's precedence order and 13 malformed versions; a fake feed gives newer / same / older / beta-only on Stable and the pre-release as newer on Beta; 15 malformed feeds refused; off → 0 requests, 0 notices, nothing stored; on → 1 request, the result on the message thread and in the settings, the tray notice once per release, no second request at start within 24 h; a refused feed and a failed request reported, not announced; a hanging request cancelled when the checker goes (< 1.5 s) and its result dropped; the page's line. Not run against GitHub here (the repository has no release yet; the request path is the owner's first run, and on Linux it needs `curl`). *Symbols:* the CI `app` job builds Release with debug information (MSVC `/Z7` with `/DEBUG /OPT:REF /OPT:ICF`, `-g` on macOS and Linux) and uploads `Flubsound-symbols-<OS>` (90 days, `COMMIT.txt` names the commit): the PDBs, moved out of the artefacts so the package does not carry them; a `.dSYM` per bundle (`dsymutil`); on Linux `<binary>.debug` split off with `objcopy`, the packaged binaries stripped of it and given a debug link. A crash report or minidump from a CI test build can now be symbolised by hand (WinDbg, `atos`, `addr2line`) with the matching archive; before, CI kept no symbols. Not run here: the symbol step (its first CI run is the check). Open: the audio-thread event ring, Crashpad / sentry-native with symbol upload (so "a forced crash yields a symbolicated report" is still by hand), signing and notarisation, the signed update manifest with staged rollout and rollback, content packs and telemetry.
- **What.** In this order: (1) a lock-free audio-thread event ring (xrun, underrun, NaN drop, governor and overload steps, device and route changes) drained at 2 Hz to a rotating log, plus "Export diagnostics" (zip); (2) Crashpad or sentry-native with CI symbol upload and consent-gated upload; (3) signed MSIX/MSI, notarised pkg, AppImage (Flatpak later); (4) an ed25519-signed update manifest with stable/beta channels, staged rollout and N−1 rollback (WinSparkle, Sparkle 2, notify-only on Linux); (5) signed, versioned content packs for presets, device profiles, HRTF sets, correction data and models, merged over the compiled-in floor; (6) opt-in, audio-free telemetry with a published schema, last.
- **Why.** Unsigned binaries trigger SmartScreen and Gatekeeper; a new headset or fixed curve needs a full release; field reports of crackles or "no sound after update" cannot be diagnosed.
- **Expected benefit.** Mostly indirect, but it gates any real release. Near-term user impact is diagnosability: there is no Logger, FileLogger or minidump handler, two DBG calls, and the device error is only printed to stdout. The diagnostics slice should be treated as High before any beta. Correction: signing no longer removes SmartScreen warnings for a new publisher (EV certificates lost instant reputation in 2024). Telemetry has no users yet and carries privacy obligations for audio software, so it is deferred.
- **Approach.** Share event counters with the [E53](#e53) soak. Content packs require [E52](#e52)'s schemas; `juce_add_binary_data(FlubsoundPresets)` and the generated `DeviceProfilesData.cpp` remain the fallback floor. Plan certificate lead times (Azure Trusted Signing eligibility, Apple Developer ID enrolment).
- **Depends on.** [E52](#e52), [E53](#e53); [E49](#e49) needs Developer ID; the driver-ABI field only once [E46](#e46) exists.
- **Done when.** The diagnostics zip from a soak run contains an injected glitch. A forced crash yields a symbolicated report. Artifacts pass `signtool verify /pa`, `spctl -a -vv` and `stapler validate`; the SmartScreen result is recorded over time, not gated. Stable → beta → rollback works on a VM and a tampered manifest is refused. A device-profile pack changes a ceiling without an app release.
- **Evidence.** [CI workflow][CI] (build, test, screenshots only); roadmap 1.7, 1.8, 3.3, 3.4, 3.11 in [07][07]; no logging or crash handler in `app/Source`; [02 §1](02-tech-stack.md) Crashpad/Sentry "(planned)"; [EngineController.cpp][EC] `lastDeviceError`.

<a id="e55"></a>
#### E55 · Anti-cheat-safe process handling and a verified anti-cheat matrix

*Stability & platform · benefit 3 · difficulty Low · 3–5 engineer-days plus 1–2 calendar weeks of matrix testing*

- **Status (Phase 3 batch 2, Windows lane).** Partially done (the code half): the process cache and Tournament mode's engine side ([06 §8](06-gui.md#8-per-app-routing-ux)). *Cache:* `platform::ProcessInfoCache` (`PlatformServices.h` / `_common.cpp`), used by `WinAppAudioRouter::enumerateSessions`, keyed by process id and the session's instance identifier (`IAudioSessionControl2::GetSessionInstanceIdentifier`): a known session is served without opening the process, a new session of a known pid is checked with one `OpenProcess` (image path and creation time from one handle), a reused pid with a new creation time is resolved again, a pid without a session for a whole pass is dropped. Before: every 2 s pass opened each session's process twice (image path, then E47's creation time), 3600 opens per process per hour; after: 1 per process (fake OS, 1800 passes: 2 opens for 2 processes, 7200 before). *Tournament mode:* `AppRouting::setTournamentMode` freezes routing (no enumeration, move or link: 0 enumerations while frozen; running captures kept; an app that starts playing is captured only after it is switched off); `AutoProfileSwitcher::setTournamentMode` holds the active rule and ignores samples; `platform::AntiCheatServices::running()` lists the running vgc, BEService, EasyAntiCheat(_EOS), FACEITService and ESEADriver2 services (Windows service manager; empty elsewhere). Tests: `tests/app/test_app_routing_tournament.cpp`, 3 cases, 0.6 s (the Done-when's cache test: a reused pid is re-resolved, a known pid is not re-opened); the Windows code passes MinGW-w64 `-fsyntax-only` and is not yet run on Windows. Open: the setting in `AppSettings`, the tray toggle and header badge, `EngineController` skipping the foreground poll (and E56's OSD) while on, and the automatic switch-on from `AntiCheatServices` (app-lane files); the 1 h ETW / Process Monitor trace; the published matrix.
- **Status (p3b3, app wiring).** Partially done: the software half is done; the 1 h ETW / Process Monitor trace and the published matrix stay gated (a Windows PC with the anti-cheat titles, E53 step 0 and E54's signed builds). *Settings:* `tournament.mode` (the user's switch, default off) and `tournament.auto` (switch on while an anti-cheat service runs, default on) in `AppSettings`; no parameter, preset or layout change. *EngineController:* `setTournamentMode` / `setTournamentAuto` / `getTournamentState` / `isTournamentActive` / `describeTournament`; while active it calls `AppRouting::setTournamentMode` and `AutoProfileSwitcher::setTournamentMode`, and the 2 Hz timer skips `pollForegroundApp` (which also returns at once while active; `isTournamentActive()` is the hook for E56's later OSD). `pollAntiCheatServices` reads `platform::AntiCheatServices::running()` at start (before routing's first pass) and every 10 s: a service switches it on at once, two polls without one (20 s) return it to the user's choice, switching it off while a service holds it on lasts until the service stops. *UI:* the tray menu's "Tournament mode" (named after the anti-cheat while one holds it on) and "Tournament mode when an anti-cheat runs"; one tray bubble when it comes on by itself ("Tournament mode on: Vanguard is running"); the header's TOURNAMENT badge (a pill after the strips from 1400 px, an amber shield on the logo mark below that, so the preset box keeps its width: 184 / 140 / 158 px at 1280 / 1100 / 800 px with and without the badge), whose tooltip names the reason and whose click switches it off or toggles the automatic switch-on. Tests: `tests/app/test_app_tournament.cpp`, 3 cases, 1.1 s: with a fake service list and a router that resolves sessions through `ProcessInfoCache` as the Windows router does, a new game process that starts playing while Vanguard runs costs 0 session enumerations, 0 process opens and 0 foreground queries (off: 1 open for it at the next pass, and foreground queries resume); automatic on / off with the 20 s hysteresis; the user's switch and the automatic switch-on persist across a restart, and a restart with the switch on or a service running makes no routing pass at all; the badge at 1600 / 1280 / 1100 / 800 px and the tray items. No sound change; no golden or render-baseline entry moves. Not done here (files of other units): a Settings > General row and a `--state tournament` screenshot. The service query itself (batch 2's `AntiCheatServices` in `PlatformServices_win.cpp`) is unchanged and has still not run on Windows.
- **What.** Cache {pid + process creation time → exe, display name} across session enumerations so each game process is opened once (optionally parse `IAudioSessionControl2` session identifiers to avoid even that). A Tournament mode that stops enumeration and foreground polling, disables auto profiles, the OSD and overlays, freezes routing and shows a header badge (optionally auto-enabled when vgc, BEService or EasyAntiCheat services run). Run and publish the roadmap 2.12 matrix (Valorant/Vanguard, CS2/VAC, Fortnite and Apex/EAC, R6/BattlEye, FACEIT) with functional as well as kick/warning columns. Plan attestation signing and vendor allow-listing for the driver.
- **Why.** Kernel anti-cheats log or strip handles to game processes; a ban or kick would end the product's gaming credibility.
- **Expected benefit.** Lower than first scored. The foreground half is already fixed (`WinForegroundApp::query` opens only on foreground change). Session enumeration opens every session's process on every 2 s pass (about 1800 opens/hour per game) with `PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION`, the right Task Manager, Discord and Steam use; anti-cheats target VM_READ/VM_WRITE and injection, so ban risk is low. The value is less needless work, a reassurance switch, and the untested functional question of whether loopback capture and routing work at all for Vanguard/EAC/BattlEye-protected games.
- **Approach.** Member cache in `WinAppAudioRouter::enumerateSessions`, creation time from `GetProcessTimes` on the single first open, eviction when a pid's session disappears. Tournament mode in `AppSettings` plus a tray toggle. The matrix records title, anti-cheat version, build hash, hours played, kicks/warnings and functional results, and states that no-kick results are weak evidence (VAC bans in delayed waves).
- **Depends on.** [E53](#e53) step 0 (the Windows code must run first); the in-flight auto profiles (shared foreground path); [E56](#e56) OSD and hooks in the matrix; [E54](#e54) signed binaries; [E46](#e46) for attestation.
- **Done when.** Cache unit test (a reused pid with a new creation time is re-resolved; a known pid is not re-opened). A 1 h ETW / Process Monitor trace with a game running shows ≤ 1 OpenProcess per new pid. The published matrix covers 6 titles and is re-run each major release.
- **Evidence.** [PlatformServices_win.cpp][PSW] `processImagePath()` (OpenProcess + `QueryFullProcessImageNameW`, ~L425) called from `enumerateSessions` (~L1151), `WinForegroundApp::query` (~L1918–1937); [06 §8][06-8]; [01 §4.6][01-46]; [08 C7][08-C7] "nothing here is proven by a test yet"; [driver README][DRV] §9 risk row.

<a id="e60"></a>
#### E60 · Perceptual and real-game validation: scenario corpus, spatial auditory models, listening panels and playtests

*Stability & platform · benefit 3 · difficulty High · 4–6 engineer-months plus participant and licence costs · Ambitious*

- **Status (Phase 2 batch 1).** Stage 1 core done in `tests/test_scenes.cpp`: one seeded, licence-free 12 s programme holds the burst (pink stereo bed, panned 20–50 ms 3.2 kHz steps every 350 ms), quiet → combat (2.5 s of 10 shots/s and an explosion, turned down where the sum would pass −1 dBFS: −17 dB at −14 LUFS, −4.7 dB at −24 LUFS) and ambush (bed and steps after the event) scenes, rendered through `OfflineRenderer` at −14 / −24 / −40 LUFS; nine metrics (cue SNR gain against the rest of the bed, step / bed contrast, bed lift, bed drift, event change, onset jump, hole 1–2 s after, recovery time, step lift after vs before) for the 9 gaming presets, Late Night and the Footsteps module, 297 values pinned as an E59-style ratchet (0.3 dB / 0.4 s), about 18 s. Metric validation: bypass reads 0 ± 0.02 dB on every metric at every level; a static +6 dB, Q 1 bell at 3.2 kHz reads +4.42 dB of cue SNR gain, i.e. its gain on the steps minus its gain on the rest of the bed (+4.27 dB from the two filter responses: the steps' Q 1 band is wider than the bell's top, so "reads about +6 dB" only holds for an EQ flat across the step band); a 6 dB broadband cut reads 0.00; a 20:1, 2 s-release compressor fails the recovery metric (2.08 s against a 1 s target; bypass 0). Before → after for [E19](#e19) is in its Status line. Open: the stage 1 remainder (dialogue over effects, speech → music → silence, track change, `analyze --bands --events`, correlation with 5+ real-game clips), stages 2–4.
- **Status (Phase 2 batch 2).** Stage 1 remainder done in `tests/test_scenes.cpp`, each scene at −14 / −24 / −40 LUFS with K-weighted level metrics, pinned (0.3 dB, 0.5 s) for the presets it is for, bypass reading 0 ± 0.03 dB, about 3.2–3.6 s per scene (in review each scene became one test case per level, 1.0–1.2 s each, so no case runs over 2 s; values and tolerances unchanged). *Dialogue over effects* (6 s: the stationary pink ambience plus a 120 Hz rumble, formant-synthesised dialogue at 0 LU): dialogue SNR gain in the speech band (2 kHz, Q 0.7; the dialogue doubled reads +6.07 dB against +6.02), dialogue and effects lift. Competitive FPS +2.90 / +3.00 / +3.15 dB (its cue enhancer lifts the syllables' onsets). KNOWN_GAP (target ≥ 0, a new finding, no Done-when yet): MOBA / Strategy −5.30 / −4.07 / −1.98 and Night Mode −5.86 / −6.18 / −1.31 dB. Their downward compressor (1.8:1 engaged by Detail; 3:1) turns the dialogue down against the effects, and Voice & Score's band 7, an upward compressor at 2 kHz, lifts the effects between the phrases (MOBA at Voice 0: −4.44 / −3.12 / −0.20 dB; at Detail 0: −1.86 / −2.86 / −1.98 dB). Band 7 keyed to the background like CueLift is the obvious fix. *Speech → music → silence* (speech 4 s, music 2.5 s, 1.5 s of −80 dBFS hiss, speech): balance change, music onset jump, silence lift over the speech's, speech return. [E21](#e21)'s rules read on it: the music onset ≤ +1 dB holds (Late Night +0.95 / +0.80 dB); KNOWN_GAP speech return within ±1 dB (Night Mode +1.78 dB at −40, Late Night +1.01 / +2.45 dB at −24 / −40: Auto Level still rising on the quiet speech) and silence lift ≤ +6 dB (Night Mode +8.33 / +8.60, Late Night +7.47 / +8.35 dB at −14 / −24: the downward compressor turns the speech down while its make-up lifts the hiss in the pause). *Quiet → loud track change* (−15 LU for 3 s, then 5 s at the programme level): step change, overshoot, settling, peak. Peaks ≤ −0.5 dBFS and −24 LUFS pass. KNOWN_GAP overshoot ≤ +1 dB and settle ≤ 1 s at −14 LUFS (Night Mode +2.00 dB / 1.88 s, Late Night +3.70 dB / 1.41 s: the gain reached on the quiet track carries over) and settle at −40 (2.34 s for both, from below: Auto Level rising at 3 dB/s). Scene / event detector for user captures: `analyseSceneEvents` in `core/include/flub/analysis/SceneEvents.h` (10 ms frames, background, onset / loud / silence / level-change events). On these programmes it finds 20 of the 23 steps in the 3.2 kHz band, the combat as one loud event (5.00–7.41 s), the 1.5 s silence and the track change (3.00 s, +17.3 dB median step). The CLI reads user captures with it: `flubsound-cli analyze --events` lists the events of all channels, full band or in one band (`--event-band 3200`), and `--bands --events` adds per octave band of the mid a 100 ms level track, its 10th / 50th / 90th percentiles and the band's own events (JSON `events`, `bandTracks`); on a known 12 s programme (`tests/test_cli_analyze.cpp`) it finds the burst, the −15 dB level change and the silence within 20 ms full band, and the five 3.2 kHz steps as onsets within 20 ms in the 3.2 kHz band and in the 4 kHz octave. Open: correlation with 5+ real-game clips, stages 2–4; the three new scenes cover 2–3 presets each (render time), not every gaming preset.
- **Status (p3b5, stage 2 C++ metrics).** Done in `core/include/flub/analysis/SpatialMetrics.h` / `core/src/analysis/SpatialMetrics.cpp`, `HeadphoneVirtualizer::parametricHrir` (a static, non-RT function; the render path is unchanged) and the CLI (`Analysis.cpp`, `Commands.cpp`: `analyze --spatial`), tested in `tests/test_spatial_metrics.cpp` (5 cases, about 1 s together) and `tests/test_cli_analyze.cpp`. Definitions: the direct-sound onset (either ear −20 dB re the response's peak); IACC as ISO 3382-1 (max \|IACF\| within ±1 ms), early over [onset − 1 ms, onset + 80 ms), late from 80 ms, broadband and per octave 125 Hz – 8 kHz (4th-order Butterworth edges), undefined when a window holds < −60 dB of the response; the ITD (lag of the early maximum); DRR ([onset − 0.5, onset + 2.5) ms against the rest); 1/3-octave levels (mean \|H\|² of the band's FFT bins); the diffuse-field response (power average over every response and ear) and its deviation from its own mean (range, rms). Metric validation: a dry centred impulse reads IACC 1.000 in every octave with ITD 0; a far ear 6 dB down and 10 samples late reads IACC 1 and ITD +0.208 ms; ears more than 1 ms apart read 0; a −20 dB reflection in both ears reads DRR +20.00 dB, in one ear IACC 1/√1.01. Today's weak values reproduced (pinned; the numbers are in [E28](#e28)'s p3b5 Status and [03 §8.9](03-dsp-design.md#89-known-limitations)): FC IACC 1.000 dry, 0.995 at the default room; FL vs BL near ear ≤ 0.35 dB up to 2 kHz; 28.06 dB peak-to-notch on the dry correlated 7-speaker impulse. Synthetic pinna notch (−15 dB, Q 4, on the renderer's HRIRs at 24 azimuths): the diffuse field drops −10.19 dB in the 8 kHz band (−3.5 / −2.9 dB in its neighbours, ≤ 0.1 dB up to 2 kHz); moved to 10 kHz, the dip follows (−9.49 dB at 10 kHz) and the 8 kHz band recovers; the octaves up to 2 kHz keep their IACC within 0.005. `analyze --spatial` splits a stereo capture at impulses ≥ 100 ms apart (`splitImpulses`) and reports per response IACC (early / late, octaves), ITD, DRR and 1/3-octave levels, and the diffuse field over all (JSON `spatial` { `responses`, `diffuseField` }; keys tested). Through `process` at Boost 0 (seven impulses on a 7.1 file) the early IACC equals the module's (0.759 / 0.995 / 0.712 / 0.518 for FL / FC / BL / SL); the chain's own filters lower the DRR to 16–19 dB and leave a correlated tail below 125 Hz after 80 ms (late IACC 1.00 broadband). The AMT Baumgartner 2014 cross-check did not run: Octave 8.4 installs here and AMT 1.6.0 downloads, but the model's auxdata (sofacoustics.org, amtoolbox.org) are blocked by the proxy (403); it stays GPL tooling for a runner with that access. Golden files and render baseline: nothing moves (no DSP change). Open: the nightly run over every factory preset and a `render.stats` spatial block (not in this unit's files), the AMT cross-check, correlation with real-game clips, stages 3–4.
- **What.** Stage 1 (now, CI): a seeded, licence-free scenario generator (ambience + panned steps + explosion; quiet → combat; dialogue over effects; speech → music → silence; quiet-to-loud track change) rendered at −14, −24 and −40 LUFS through `OfflineRenderer` for every gaming and night preset, measuring cue-band SNR gain, step/bed contrast, onset jump, recovery time and bed drift, with the [E59](#e59) ratchet; `analyze --bands --events` for user captures, correlated with 5+ real-game clips. Stage 2 (nightly): IACC, DRR and diffuse-field deviation in C++, plus AMT Baumgartner 2014 in Octave (GPL tooling, never shipped). Stage 3: after a licence-clean HRIR set exists, app wiring of `HrirSet`, an in-app localisation test and HRTF picker. Stage 4: pre-registered MUSHRA panel and playtests.
- **Why.** The R2.9 claims (footsteps, positional audio, explosions) and the night presets were never checked on realistic dynamics; three burst files exposed three major defects. Music voicing and spatial defaults were never validated perceptually.
- **Expected benefit.** Stage 1 is High on its own: it directly tests R2.9 and R3.2. The human studies would be confounded now, because the [E59](#e59) defects are unfixed and the Windows path adds a 12.75 ms FIFO the product means to replace. The picker needs a SOFA loader, app wiring and datasets that do not exist (nothing in `app/` or `tools/` references `HrirSet`; no `.sofa` in the repo).
- **Approach.** Set spatial targets from bypass and a public reference HRTF, not guesses. Loudness-match panel stimuli to ±0.2 LU with the engine's `LoudnessMeter`. Keep competitor captures private (HeSuVi Atmos/DTS impulses derive from licensed products). Change no defaults before results.
- **Depends on.** [E59](#e59); [E58](#e58)'s content gate for HRIR data; [E46](#e46) or another low-latency Windows path and the in-flight auto profiles for playtests; licensed competitor copies.
- **Done when.** Bypass: cue SNR gain 0 ± 0.1 dB; a +6 dB step-band EQ reads about +6 dB; a 20 dB pumping compressor fails the recovery metric; metric rankings over real clips agree with synthetic scenes (Spearman ≥ 0.6). Spatial metrics reproduce today's weak values and respond correctly to a synthetic pinna-notch change. Picker test-retest Spearman ≥ 0.7 over 5 users. Pre-registered human-study criteria (localisation at least on par with Windows Sonic within the 95 % CI; fewer reversals than stereo bypass; no matched music-preference loss at Boost 50), with results in roadmap items 1.4 and 2.7 and TRACEABILITY R2.9 and R3.2.
- **Evidence.** [test_modes.cpp][T-modes] (steady `tone(f, levelDb)` only); [test_virtualizer.cpp][T-virt] (32 cases) and `test_spatializer.cpp` (23 cases), no localisation metric; [HeadphoneVirtualizer.h][HVh] `HrirSet`, `setHrirSet`; [TRACEABILITY][TR-gaps] R2.9, R3.2 notes; roadmap 1.4, 2.7 in [07][07].
