## 4. Ambitious / advanced enhancements

Higher effort, potentially transformative. "Transformative" is conditional: each row names the evidence that must exist before the effort is committed.

| ID | Title | Effort | Why transformative | Gate before committing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [E46](#e46) | Windows endpoint driver / APO Lite | driver 7–10 months, M1 4–6 (APO Lite 8–12 wk) | Turns Flubsound Pro from a monitoring tool into a product on the main gaming platform: whole-system processing, real 7.1 beds, per-app strips, no FIFO at M2 | A Windows driver engineer; E47 honesty shipped; early anti-cheat test of a signed build |
| [E15](#e15) | Per-device correction + AutoEQ | 8–13 wk (MVP 3–4) | Fixes the largest audible error in the chain (the headphone itself); parity with Peace/AutoEQ, Sonar, SoundID | Stable endpoint IDs; E11 preamp; licence review of any bundled data |
| [E05](#e05) | Maximizer redesign | 8–11 wk | Moves the loud end from "cheap enhancer" (THD+N −16 dB, 22.6 dB cross-band ducking) towards Pro-L / Ozone behaviour | E06 slice and E59 metrics in place; loud-end MUSHRA against Pro-L 2 / Ozone |
| [E19](#e19) | Footstep/cue enhancer redesign | 4–6 wk | Makes the flagship competitive feature work on real, transient footsteps across mastering levels | Interim bell shipped; scenario corpus (E60 stage 1); loudness-matched ABX |
| [E06](#e06) | SafetyGovernor redesign | 8–10 wk | Protection that measures what is heard (weighted, whole-stage) instead of clipper THD only; closes R3.5 | Slice shipped; residual fit validated against injected artefacts |
| [E12](#e12) | Headphone imaging for music | 12–18 wk | The main selling point of Dolby Atmos for Headphones, Waves Nx and SoundID; stereo music is today left inside the head | Phase A done; Phase B ships default-on only if a blind MUSHRA beats plain stereo |
| [E48](#e48) | Native, turnkey Linux | 10–14 wk | EasyEffects-class one-click setup and a usable SteamOS Game Mode | E48a shipped; headless mode design |
| [E49](#e49) | macOS process taps | 6–9 wk | Opens the Mac music audience, which today has no path at all | E53 step 0 on a Mac; Developer ID |
| [E60](#e60) | Perceptual and real-game validation | 4–6 months | First evidence that any perceptual claim (R2.9, spatial, voicing) is true | E59 fixes landed; low-latency Windows path for playtests |
| [E34](#e34) | Content and scene analysis | 10–14 wk | Macros that respond to the material instead of fixed offsets | Smart-scaling slice proves value on real limited masters |

| [E02](#e02) | Bass dynamics and shared crossover | 3.5–4.5 wk | Removes kick-locked pumping of sustained bass | ABX shows the shared crossover's ~3 ms is audible before building it |
| [E03](#e03) | Multiband virtual bass | 4–5 wk | Clean psychoacoustic bass on laptops and small speakers | MUSHRA against the *fixed* laptop preset |
| [E24](#e24) | Per-source positional enhancement | 6–9 wk | Frequency-consistent cue sharpening instead of a single bell | Pointing test shows today's focus helps at all |
| [E31](#e31) | Height and object audio | 10–14 wk | Keeps height from Atmos-era beds and VR content | Microsoft provider answer; decoded PCM beds with height actually reach users |
| [E57](#e57) | Extensible graph, hosting, output buses | 7–12 months | Voicemeeter-class routing and user plug-in chains | E57-A use justifies later phases; E58 licence decision |

---

## 5. Recommended implementation order

**Team assumption.** [07 §1][07-1] lists 6.5 FTE: Lead DSP 1, DSP 1, Systems / driver engineer (Windows) 1, C++ / JUCE GUI 1, macOS + Linux 0.5 (1 from roadmap Phase 3), QA / audio test 1, product designer 0.5 and product / project manager 0.5. That table is a plan. This report cannot verify who is employed, and one role is known to be empty: [E46](#e46), Phase 0 and risk S1 all say the Windows driver engineer still has to be hired or contracted. The capacity below therefore counts only the other engineering and QA roles, **4.5 FTE (DSP 2, GUI 1, macOS/Linux 0.5, QA 1), rising to 5 FTE in Phase 3**. Design and PM (1 FTE) are not counted as engineering capacity. Work that needs the Windows engineer runs in a separate **Windows lane**, which starts only once someone is in the seat. If any other 07 §1 role is also unfilled, re-cut the phases with the same method.

**How the totals are computed.** Each item's effort comes from its header or from §3. Where a phase schedules only part of an item and the item gives no figure for that part, this report makes its own split (marked \*). S2 says the skeptic raised most estimates by 20–60 %, so each phase is planned so that the **upper** bound of its committed work fits the capacity. Work that fits only at the lower bounds is listed as stretch, with a drop order.

**Reserve for work already in flight.** An earlier version of this plan booked all 4.5 FTE on report items from week 1. That left no time for two things that are not finished: round 8 (auto per-game profiles, the crossfaded engine swap, UI scale and high contrast), which rule (4) and many items below depend on, and the hardening sweep, whose 51 confirmed findings are only partly fixed (`d096a4d` is "wave A" and covers only the files round 8 does not touch). Matching the findings to the files `d096a4d` changed (a file-level match, so an approximation), 29 of the 51 (12 medium, 17 low) sit in files it did not touch, so at least those 29 are still open: 9 in `PlatformServices_linux.cpp`, 4 in `tests/test_platform_linux.cpp`, 3 in `AppRouting.cpp`, 2 each in `PlatformServices_win.cpp`, `PlatformServices_mac.mm` and `AudioEngineHost.cpp`, and the rest spread over 6 more files. Weeks 1–4 therefore reserve **3–5 engineer-weeks (R)** before any report item: round-8 verification (the swap's click harness, auto-profile rule and foreground tests, the UI-scale and high-contrast screenshot and layout matrix) 1.5–2.5\*, and the remaining hardening waves at about half a day per medium and a quarter day per low finding including its test, 1.5–2.5\*. If R overruns 5 weeks, the overrun comes out of Phase 2 through its drop order ([§5.3](#53-phase-2--next-quarter-weeks-514)). Nothing that relies on the swap, auto profiles or UI scale merges before R has verified them (rule 4).

| Phase | Window | Staffed capacity (eng-weeks) | Committed work, lower–upper (eng-weeks) | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R | weeks 1–4, alongside Phases 0 and 1 | shared with Phases 0 and 1 | 3–5 | Booked first |
| 0 | week 1 | 4.5 | 0.2–0.4 | The rest of week 1 goes to R and to starting the E59 slice |
| 1 | weeks 2–4 | 13.5 | 8.2–12.6 | Weeks 1–4 together: R + Phase 0 + Phase 1 = 11.4–18.0 against 18. Fits at the upper bound with nothing spare |
| 2 | weeks 5–14 | 45 | 28.6–44.6 committed; stretch 5.4–7.7 more | Committed work fits at the upper bound; stretch only while lower bounds hold |
| 2, Windows lane | weeks 5–14, only if staffed | ≤ 10 | 6.9–10.8 | Over by 0.8 at the upper bound; APO Lite cannot start before Phase 3 |
| 3 | weeks 15–40 (about months 4–9, 26 weeks) | 130 | 81.7–126.7 above the cut line, plus lab time | Fits in total. Each lane starts only when its own Phase 2 committed High items have closed (S5). The DSP lane is the binding constraint, and the App / GUI lane also needs its drop order (§5.4) |
| 3, Windows lane | weeks 15–40, only if staffed | 26 | 17–26 (E46 M1) | M1 only; M2 falls after month 9 |

The reserve moves Phase 2's end from week 13 to week 14 and Phase 3's start to week 15. Over the first 14 weeks the plan books at most 5 + 0.4 + 12.6 + 44.6 = 62.6 engineer-weeks against 63.

An earlier draft of this section said Phase 2 was "at the team's capacity". It was not. With this report's splits, its list summed to roughly 60–86 engineer-weeks, against about 50–60 available over its 11 weeks even with the Windows role counted as staffed. The phases below are the corrected plan.

Four rules decide the order: (1) stop harm users hear today before adding features; (2) measure before tuning: nothing that changes the sound of a preset, macro or mode band merges before the metric in its Done-when exists (the [E59](#e59) slice for Phase 1 retunes; E59 in full, [E60](#e60) stage 1 and [E53](#e53) step 0 before the [E05](#e05), [E07](#e07), [E19](#e19) and [E21](#e21) redesigns); (3) honesty and recoverability before reach ([E47](#e47) before [E46](#e46); [E52](#e52) before [E54](#e54) content packs); (4) nothing that relies on the crossfaded engine swap, auto-profile switching or UI scale merges before those are verified. [§5.6](#56-key-dependency-edges) lists which slices do and do not rely on the swap.

### 5.1 Phase 0 — this week (stop the harm that needs no retuning; each ≤ 1 day, no dependencies)

[E32](#e32)/[E21](#e21) wording fixes for Late Night and Tournament Clean (hours) · [E24](#e24) part (i), focus forced to 0 under Flubsound's own binaural lock (≤ 0.5 day) · [E41](#e41) Wayland alias fix (0.5–1 day). Total 1–2 engineer-days.

The wording fixes and the Wayland fix change no sound. E24 (i) does change the sound of presets that drive focus, but only for more than 2 input channels rendered by Flubsound's own virtualiser, where the chain already neutralises width, space and crossfeed because the binaural render "already carries exact interaural cues" ([ProcessingChain.cpp][PC] `applyParameters()`). Its Done-when (focus = 0 under the lock) is a parameter assertion that needs no new metric, so rule (2) is met. Game-rendered HRTF content does not reach this lock; it is covered by [E27](#e27)'s own-HRTF switch in Phase 1.

The rest of week 1 goes to the in-flight reserve R and to starting the [E59](#e59) slice (counted in Phase 1), because it gates every Phase 1 retune. Non-engineering, in parallel: the owner's licence decision ([E58](#e58)), a Windows test PC ([E53](#e53) step 0), hiring or contracting the Windows driver engineer ([E46](#e46), S1), certificate and Partner Center enrolment (S6), and booking the listening panel and device lab (S4).

**Moved out of Phase 0**, each with the reason from its own item text. The [E36](#e36) default-preset fix moves to the end of Phase 1. An earlier version of this plan kept it here on the grounds that it "does not change how any preset … sounds". That was wrong under this report's own rule (2): it changes what every new user hears. The presets it loaded were also unsafe defaults. On the Game strip, Competitive FPS cuts step/bed contrast by 6.0 dB and lifts the bed +10.9 dB ([E19](#e19)); this report measured +5.0 LU on a −50 dBFS pink bed and +9.8 LU on a −60 dBFS bed. It also leaves `virt.on` at its default of true, which is the stereo-in-8-channel fold that [E27](#e27) calls the main Windows gaming defect. On the Chat strip, Signature's Boost 0.35 engages the maximizer on voice chat, the misconfiguration [E23](#e23) describes. E36's fix now ships after the E59 slice, E19's interim bell, E27 Phase 1 and E23's slice, with Voice Chat on Chat and a capped "First Run – Game" preset held to numeric limits (its Done-when). The [E19](#e19) interim bell, the [E04](#e04) Tighten removal, the [E03](#e03) laptop subsonic change and the [E24](#e24) 3 dB cap are retunes whose Done-when needs a metric that does not exist yet, so they move behind the E59 slice (rule 2): E19, E03 and E24 into Phase 1, and E04 into Phase 2's carry-in for capacity. The [E17](#e17) slice takes 1–2 days, so it breaks the ≤ 1 day rule; it moves to Phase 1 and ships with the E19 interim bell. The [E20](#e20) band-6 re-key depends on [E19](#e19)'s redesign and E21's Tame control ([§5.6](#56-key-dependency-edges)). It is no longer a standalone slice: the decoupling from Footsteps ships inside E19's Phase 2 redesign, and the Tame keying ships with E21's guard in Phase 3.

None of them blocks a Phase 1 item. The E06 slice still lands before E05 stage 1, and E52 Phase A's golden renders take over from the E59 slice's scripted render diff.

The E20 band-6 decoupling stays inside E19's Phase 2 redesign (§5.6).

Review fixes: the bed-lift cap's step when switched on after the drive moved, the global bypass's engage click (soak 7 / 6 / 5 / 9 → 0 clicks per minute-long row), an out-of-range read in a new test and a wall-clock assertion that failed under ASan + UBSan (both found by the sanitizer build), scene tests split to stay under 2 s. Not started: the [E53](#e53) step-0 fixes, [E05](#e05) step (6) and the master limiter's envelope.

**E12 Phase A moves up from Phase 3.** It is High (benefit 4), depends on nothing, costs 3–4 weeks, and its item calls it "certain and cheap". It removes three measured defects that every stereo headphone listener hears: a crossfeed ITD of 0.24 samples, up to 9.3 dB of ILD on centred vocals, and −6.0 dB anti-phase in the far ear at width 2. The earlier plan gave Phase 2 slots to Medium and Low slices while this waited, which contradicted the priority definition. The Medium and Low slices that had Phase 2 slots are now stretch or Phase 3 work: [E28](#e28) E28a, [E45](#e45) histogram, [E38](#e38) slice and [E16](#e16) slice; [E55](#e55) moves to the Windows lane. The [E23](#e23) slice (1.5–2 days) moved the other way, into Phase 1, because E36's default-preset fix needs it as the Chat strip's default. The exceptions are [E48](#e48) E48a, which the item rates "High on its own" and which closes the largest Linux latency term (gap 7), and E52 Phase B core, which the High item E01 and the auto-profile rule format need.

**Other High items that are not committed in Phase 2, and why.** [E07](#e07) needs a new chain slot (the in-flight engine swap), [E06](#e06)'s governor framework beyond the slice, and a settled maximizer: its Smoothness stage sits directly before the compressor and E05's clipper, so tuning it while E05 stage 1 is in flux would be redone. It starts Phase 3. [E39](#e39) and the [E40](#e40) browser share sizing code with the in-flight UI scale / high-contrast work, which is unverified, and the browser also needs E37's full matcher and E42's prompt. The latency part of E40 already ships in Phase 1. [E42](#e42) E42b saves only 2–4 ms and must be designed against [E50](#e50)'s new FIFO target, and E42c needs [E45](#e45)'s histogram for its xrun step-back. E42a, the high-value part, ships in Phase 1. For [E15](#e15), the MVP is committed; the item says most of the value comes from the AutoEQ import, and targets need raw measurements and licence review. For [E36](#e36), the default-preset fix ships at the end of Phase 1, and the wizard depends on [E46](#e46), which does not exist. For [E37](#e37), the slice ships in Phase 1 and the full matcher needs per-slot probes that compete with E05 and E19 for DSP time. The [E47](#e47) and [E51](#e51) remainders and [E50](#e50) Phase A need Windows engineering and are in the Windows lane below, with the fallback for E47 stated there. E50 Phase A also follows E47's doubling guard (its Depends on).

**Stretch**, started in this order only while committed work tracks its lower bounds. If the phase slips, drop from the bottom: E52 Phase B remainder first, E58 last. An overrun of the in-flight reserve R is absorbed the same way, first from stretch and then by the committed drop order below.

Each lane starts its queue only when its own Phase 2 committed High items have closed: DSP after the E06 slice, E10 Phase 1, E05 stage 1, E19's redesign, E12 Phase A, E15 MVP and E01; App / GUI after E56 Phase A and its share of the E53 step-0 fixes; QA after E59 full and its share of the E53 step-0 fixes. The macOS/Linux lane has no committed High item in Phase 2 and starts at week 15. Until then the lane keeps working on those items, and its Phase 3 queue shrinks from the bottom by the same amount.

If it overruns, drop in this order: (a) E40's audition bank to §5.5 (frees 1; the browser ships with plain preview), (b) E54's update channel to §5.5 (frees about 2–3\*; installers ship without auto-update, which S6's signing still covers) |

M2 (+3–4 months) falls after month 9, so the loopback FIFO stays until then. APO Lite needs a second Windows engineer or contractor. M1 is chosen over APO Lite because it serves both audiences (a System endpoint and the Game 7.1 endpoint), where APO Lite has no 7.1 bed |

Staffed lanes total 81.7–126.7 engineer-weeks plus lab time, against 130.

**Why E06's residual fit now leads the DSP lane.** [E07](#e07)'s Depends on names "E06's governor framework", and §5.3 says E07 needs that framework beyond the slice: its tonal-balance guard is a new governor rule that scales back ungoverned lifts, and it should be built on the weighted, whole-stage measurement rather than on today's clipper-THD loop and then rebuilt. An earlier version of this table queued E07 first and E06's residual fit fifth, which contradicted that dependency. With two DSP engineers, E06's residual fit and E14 start together at the beginning of Phase 3, the E32 contour follows E14, and E07 starts when E06's framework lands.

**Why [E14](#e14) and the [E32](#e32) relative contour (both High) start only here.** Their prerequisites land in Phase 2, and starting earlier would mean redoing the work. E14's voicing pass depends on E11 (LRA bounds), on E12 Phase A (which re-voices crossfeed and Space), on the E09 slot decision and on a listening panel (S4). Its Warmth remap changes every preset that uses M5, so it also needs E52's golden renders. The E32 contour adds 10–15 dB of low-frequency gain at low volume. It must be budgeted against the governor (E06) and preamp (E11) headroom and would drive the maximizer, whose LF envelope arrives with E05 stage 1. It also reads endpoint volume through Windows and macOS PlatformServices code that first runs in E53 step 0. The false wording that E32 fixes already ships in Phase 0. E14 starts at the beginning of Phase 3 beside E06's residual fit, and the E32 contour follows it.

**Below the cut line** (moved to §5.5 unless a lane runs at its lower bounds; E39's Simple view ships without E38's active-now chips until E38 lands): [E56](#e56) OSD and command-line forwarding with `flubsound-cli ctl` (App / GUI lane, 2–3\*; the OSD also needs E55's Tournament mode and a PresentMon check, A3); [E15](#e15) GraphicEQ, targets and model database; [E22](#e22) (it also needs working chat routing, which Windows lacks until E46 M1); [E42](#e42) E42b–E42c; [E45](#e45) idle freeze and shed ladder; [E38](#e38) net curve and chips; [E36](#e36) wizard; [E17](#e17) runtime hands-free policy and codec query; [E34](#e34) Smart-scaling slice; [E30](#e30) detection and banner; [E16](#e16) slice; [E28](#e28) renderer; [E09](#e09) Phase A; [E04](#e04) multiband shaper; [E13](#e13); [E18](#e18) hardware sidetone; plus any Phase 2 stretch not done.

**Validation.** [E60](#e60) stage 4 game playtests wait for a low-latency Windows path (E46 M2), so this phase runs only the music panel.

**CI after the hand-over (2026-09-30 – 2026-10-07).** Actions billing blocked every job up to run [36695058369](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/36695058369) (the hand-over commit `8e5a11c`); the repository is now public. Jobs start again from run [36783725945](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/36783725945) (`686ce19`, 2026-09-30), where the `flub_tests` cases of batches 3, 4 and 5 ran green on gcc, clang and MSVC, but `core` failed on Apple Clang (from 2026-10-01 on gcc too) and the app jobs were skipped. The fixes of 2026-10-06 made every `core` leg green (`c2d05e5`, run [37427019405](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37427019405), where the app jobs ran again: Linux green, Windows and macOS failed) and the Windows app job (`caccf81`, run [37444883054](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37444883054)); `51abe53` the macOS one (it printed the OSD's focus on the macOS runner instead of asserting it; [E56](#e56)'s NSPanel work asserts it again on all three OSes). **First fully green run after the hand-over: [37572308549](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37572308549)** (`51abe53`, 2026-10-07), all 11 jobs: the `core` legs on gcc (with the golden-render step, which the MSVC-recorded golden rows of [E20](#e20) and the Synthwave preset have passed on the reference platform since run [37427019405](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37427019405)), clang, MSVC and Apple Clang (macos-14, arm64), `sanitizers` (ASan + UBSan), `rtsan`, `fuzz`, `app` with `flub_app_tests` and pluginval on windows-2022, macos-14 and ubuntu-24.04, and `pipewire`. It contains batches 3, 4 and 5 and the local Windows work up to then. Six of the requirement items merged on 2026-10-07 (R1.1, R1.5, R4.4, R4.5 / R4.6, R5.3 and [E56](#e56)'s macOS / X11 OSD) first ran together in run [37598813718](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37598813718) (`055e792`): the Apple Clang `core` leg failed the paced VoiceCleanup test (116 of 196 frames missed their deadline on the runner) and the app jobs were skipped. All seven, with R1.2 (merged after `055e792`), first ran together in run [37615196405](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37615196405) (`b9d9ab7`): the Apple Clang `core` leg failed the same test (160 of 196 frames), and the `app`, `asio` and `pipewire` jobs were skipped. `70a2a98` gives the neural worker the macOS time-constraint policy ([E35](#e35)); its run [37634884865](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37634884865) is the first in which all their jobs ran (on this branch; before it, the `wip/mac-neural` iterations on top of `b9d9ab7` ran all 12 jobs from run [37617044054](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37617044054) and were all green in [37624502399](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37624502399)): 11 of 12 green, and the non-blocking `pipewire` job failed on an idle xrun, which the xrun case now attributes ([E48](#e48) Status). The code at the branch head (unchanged since `3554497`) ran all 12 jobs green, `asio` included, in run [37649158870](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37649158870) on branch `wip/pipewire-xrun` (and in [37643504114](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37643504114) on `5e04bac`, its `pipewire` job re-run green). The branch's own run on that commit, [37654557630](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37654557630), ended as a failure in its first attempt, a GitHub-side failure to create the dependent jobs: its 7 `core`, `sanitizers`, `rtsan` and `fuzz` jobs were all green, the `app`, `asio` and `pipewire` jobs were never created (no failed step or error annotation), and the same code ran all 12 jobs green in [37649158870](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37649158870); its re-run (attempt 2) ran all 12 jobs green. The `pipewire` job stays non-blocking until 20 consecutive CI runs are green. By 2026-10-07 it had 27 green results (26 runs on all branches plus one re-run; 7 on this branch) and 5 failures: two on WIP R1.2 code, a compile error from a test comment on `wip/mac-neural`, and the idle xruns of `70a2a98` and `d4f05a5`. Its longest green streak is 12 runs, the current one 3. On 2026-10-08 the all-preset render diff joined the gcc `core` leg as a blocking step ([E59](#e59) Status): a probe on every `core` leg (run [37722477794](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37722477794), `wip/golden-gcc`) read 0 of 5121 values of `tests/golden/preset-render-baseline.json` different on gcc, clang, MSVC and Apple Clang, and the first blocking run is [37723453200](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/37723453200) (`328029e`).

### 5.5 Later, or gated on evidence

[E02](#e02)(b) shared crossover (only with E04's multiband shaper, and only if the ABX in E02's Done-when shows its ~3 ms is audible) · [E03](#e03) multiband rebuild · [E08](#e08) · [E09](#e09) Phase A and Phase B · [E12](#e12) Phase C · [E13](#e13) · [E15](#e15) GraphicEQ, targets and database (after the device lab) · [E16](#e16) slice and hardware IDs · [E17](#e17) remainder · [E18](#e18) hardware sidetone, software strip and virtual mic · [E20](#e20) Punch · [E22](#e22) (after routing reaches the Chat strip) · [E23](#e23) Voice/Film mode · [E24](#e24) redesign · [E25](#e25) (after E27 and E46) · [E26](#e26) (after R2.9 playtests) · [E27](#e27) title-database flag (after E26) and step (5), the stereo-to-7.1 upmix, as a separate Low item (it fabricates direction and is wrong for competitive play) · [E28](#e28) renderer · [E29](#e29) (SOFA picker first) · [E30](#e30) detection, multichannel output, haptics and crosstalk cancellation · [E31](#e31) · [E32](#e32) absolute SPL and dosimeter (after the device lab) · [E33](#e33) (the per-ear slice may move earlier) · [E34](#e34) · [E35](#e35) (thin slice after E18) · [E36](#e36) wizard (after E37 and E46 progress) · [E38](#e38) net curve and chips · [E41](#e41) translation · [E42](#e42) E42b–E42c · [E43](#e43) · [E44](#e44) Phase B (only on E45 evidence) · [E45](#e45) remainder · [E54](#e54) content packs (after E52 Phase B and E54's update channel) and opt-in, audio-free telemetry (last, after the L4 privacy review), plus the update channel and E40's audition bank if the App / GUI drop order removes them · [E46](#e46) M2 and APO Lite · [E50](#e50) Phase B fix, cable inputs opened as separate WASAPI capture clients, only if the Phase 3 QA glitch count finds slips (it then rises to benefit 4–5) · [E56](#e56) local JSON-RPC API, MIDI/OSC learn and the Stream Deck plug-in (after E52 uuids, E54 distribution and E55's Tournament mode; the OSD and `ctl` are below §5.4's cut line) · [E57](#e57) (E57-A first) · [E58](#e58) C ABI, ARM, CLAP · [E60](#e60) stage 3 and game playtests.

## 6. Updated risk assessment for the proposed enhancements

Likelihood and impact are Low / Medium / High / Very high. The [07 §8][07-8] register covers the original build; these are the risks of the work proposed here.

| # | Risk | Category | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation | Items |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | New holds, envelopes, residual filters, the Startle Guard or export sample-rate conversion open a true-peak overshoot path (R3.1 is measured, not proven) | Technical | Medium | High | Re-run the 11-rate matrix and dense stress set for strip and master limiter on every change; E59 ratchet; no merge without it; re-apply the ceiling check after SRC in export (E13 Approach) | E05, E10, E21, E01, E13 |
| T2 | Structural changes depend on the crossfaded engine swap, which is not verified; clicks or dropouts return | Technical | High | High | Gate merges on the swap's click harness; keep latency-constant bypass; prefer runtime overrides over preset swaps. Only the swap-dependent parts listed in [§5.6](#56-key-dependency-edges) wait for it; E27 Phase 1, E40 parts (1) and (3), E42a and the E17/E24 slices add no run-time re-prepare and are not gated | E07, E09 look-ahead, E40 part (2), E42b–E42c, E45 shed steps, E17 hands-free switch, E49(a), E51 later phases, E29, E30 |
| T3 | Interacting control loops (governor, AutoLevel, AutoDrive, Startle Guard, adaptive FIFO and drift controller, virt make-up gain) oscillate or pump | Technical | Medium | High | Time constants separated ≥ 5x or merged; fixed-point analysis; 8 h and 24 h soaks; oscillation assertions | E06, E21, E28, E42, E50 |
| T4 | CPU growth (multiband, 4x oversampling, FDN, convolution, multiband harmonics, oversampled air) across 4 strips trips the overload step-down on laptops | Technical | Medium | Medium | E45 per-module cost table and CI perf gate; profile-gate heavy stages; idle freeze | E03, E04, E05, E08, E10, E12, E28, E29 |
| T5 | Default, range, macro-map or mode-band changes silently re-voice sparse user presets or remap DAW automation | Technical | High | Medium | v1-defaults shim, golden renders, `sinceVersion`, release notes. Macro-map and mode-band changes (Tighten out of Punch, the interim footstep bell, the focus cap, band-6 decoupling) cannot be shimmed, because user presets store macro values rather than their mapping, so each ships with the all-preset render diff and a release-note line | E01, E02, E04, E09, E14, E19, E20, E24 |
| T6 | Kernel driver bug (BSOD), or Windows / OEM updates remove APO registration | Technical | Medium | Very high | Driver Verifier 72 h, IOCTL fuzzing, staged rollout; APO heartbeat and self-test | E46 |
| T7 | E51 replaces JUCE's `selectDefaultDeviceOnFailure = true` (`AudioEngineHost::openDevice()` passes `true` to `deviceManager.initialise`) with explicit selection plus a loopback-pair guard. The guard misfires on a legitimate setup (a VB-Cable or Voicemeeter chain whose output and input are different buses, or a user who deliberately monitors through a cable) and leaves silence. After sleep/resume or a driver reset, explicit selection reopens nothing where JUCE's fallback would have recovered | Technical | Medium | High | Match exact partner pairs (CABLE Input ↔ CABLE Output, the same Voicemeeter bus), not the vendor tokens `looksLikeLoopbackDevice` uses; a per-pair "allow" override; a fallback chain (preferred → default excluding virtual/loopback → any physical output → silence) that always shows the banner and never goes silent without a stated reason; fake-device tests for sleep/resume, driver reset and Voicemeeter A1/B1 layouts; [E53](#e53) step 0 on real cable and Voicemeeter rigs before explicit selection defaults on; guard trips counted in E54's event ring | E51, E47, E36 |
| T8 | E50's 16–24-tap polyphase ASRC adds a fixed group delay of about half its length (8–12 frames, 0.17–0.25 ms at 48 kHz, derived from the kernel, not measured). It also raises the "+4 interpolation window" term in `DriftCompensatedFifo::computeTargetFrames()` to the kernel length, which moves the drift controller's set-point by 12–20 frames and puts E42b's adaptive target on a different base. Results: wrong reported latency, underruns at small targets, or slow pitch hunting | Technical | Medium | Medium | `fixedDelayFrames()` in `LatencyInfo` with the group-delay assertion in E50's Done-when; redo the drift-controller fixed-point analysis for the new target before E42b is designed; ±200 ppm 8 h soak with the discontinuity detector; keep the 612-frame cap; a Low Latency option to keep the short interpolator if the delay matters more than the top octave | E50, E42, E10 |
| T9 | E52's v2 migration (major.minor, migration registry, alias table, settings `schemaVersion`, `.corrupt` quarantine) loses or corrupts user presets and state. A file migrated in place becomes unreadable by an older build; a registry bug silently changes stored values; a valid newer-major file is quarantined as corrupt; moving from `user:<file name>` ids to uuids orphans auto-profile rules and hotkey bindings. T5 covers default changes; this row covers the format | Technical | Medium | High | Never rewrite user files on load: migrate in memory, write v2 only on an explicit save and keep the original as `.v1.bak`; migrations are pure functions with round-trip tests over every factory preset, test fixture and E53's fuzz corpus; newer-major files are refused with a message, never quarantined; rotate settings backups before any migration write; map old ids to uuids once and keep the alias; a "restore previous settings" action | E52, E01, E09, E56, E54 |
| T10 | E44's parallel strip threads, rtkit promotion and macOS workgroups add a new real-time failure mode. A worker misses its deadline and the serial fallback glitches. A worker waits on a barrier held by a thread rtkit did not promote (priority inversion). Strip-sum order varies between runs, so renders are no longer bit-identical. `RLIMIT_RTTIME` kills the process if an RT thread spins. A worker that fails to join the device `os_workgroup` runs as ordinary work | Technical | Medium | High | Phase B only behind [E45](#e45)'s evidence gate (> 40 % of budget, blocks ≥ 256 frames) and off by default; per-strip output buffers summed serially in a fixed strip order, with a serial-vs-parallel bit-exact test; a mandatory deadline with the serial fallback counted in E45's histogram; every worker promoted through the same path and checked with `chrt -p` / `thread_policy_get`; RTSan and TSan on the pool; the 24 h soak in E44's Done-when | E44, E45, E35 |
| T11 | E23's third mode ("Voice / Film") breaks two-mode assumptions across 73 `ModeValue::` references in 21 files (MacroMap tables, `configureModeBands`, presets, tray, hotkeys, CLI `--mode`, the plug-in choice parameter, auto-profile rules). A missed branch falls through to Music or Gaming behaviour, and a changed choice range remaps DAW automation | Technical | High | Medium | Exhaustive `switch` statements without `default`, with `-Wswitch-enum` as an error; append the new value so existing indices stay stable, with `sinceVersion`; the three-mode audit in E23's Done-when; a v1 DAW project and pluginval pass after the range change; ship behind a flag until the audit is clean | E23, E52, E56 |
| T12 | E43's SIMD kernels and ISA dispatch give per-ISA numeric differences (reassociated sums, FMA contraction), so golden renders and true-peak readings differ between machines; a dispatch bug runs AVX2 code on a CPU without it (illegal instruction at start-up or on the first prepare) | Technical | Medium | Medium | Fixed-tree reductions and `-ffp-contract=off`; CI forces each ISA path, with SIMD against scalar null < −120 dBFS (E43 Done-when); a per-ISA tolerance in E52's golden renders; cpuid dispatch once in `prepare()` with an SSE2/NEON baseline and a forced-scalar override | E43, E52, E59 |
| T13 | Readouts and accessibility mislead or cost. E38's net-tone curve or "active now" chips disagree with what is rendered, and its coefficient snapshot adds audio-thread work. E41's portal alias table reports "Registered" for a hotkey that really was reassigned. Translated strings overflow tuned layouts, and screen-reader live values flood announcements | Technical / UX | Medium | Medium | Curve derived from published coefficients and checked against a swept sine within 0.3 dB (E38 Done-when); the snapshot is a wait-free seqlock write with no allocation, under RTSan; unparseable portal descriptions report "Bound (desktop shows: X)", never "Registered"; pseudo-locale and DE/JA screenshots in CI; live values throttled to 1–2 Hz | E38, E41, E39 |
| T14 | E27 changes default Game-strip behaviour, and its active-channel detector or channel mapping misfires. Real 7.1 whose rear and side channels are quiet or sparse for long stretches (a calm scene, a title that uses the rears only for occasional effects) stays under −60 dB relative to FL/FR for 2 s and drops to the stereo passthrough fold, so surround collapses. The 300 ms / 2 s hysteresis then flips between the virtualiser and passthrough mid-match, and each flip is an audible spatial jump and, because the two folds differ by up to 3.01 dB before matching (Approach step 2), a possible level step. On Linux, a wrong channel permutation sends dialogue (FC) to a rear virtual speaker, and a permutation fix applied to the wrong ALSA/PipeWire layout creates that bug on systems that were correct | Technical / perceptual | Medium | High | The item's own asymmetric hysteresis (surround above −50 dB for 300 ms, stereo only below −60 dB for 2 s), extended by a session latch: once surround is confirmed, stay in surround until the device or the routed process changes, unless the user chooses "auto" explicitly. The 300–500 ms loudness-matched ramp, with the "no level step above 1 dB" and "−45 dB ambience does not trigger it" assertions in E27's Done-when, plus new sparse-rear cases (rear bursts every 10 s over silent rears, a 30 s rear-silent stretch in a 7.1 capture). A visible "receiving 2 / 6 / 8 channels" readout and a manual override (Auto / Force surround / Force stereo / own-HRTF switch) persisted per auto-profile rule. The Linux per-channel tone test before any permutation ships, the permutation taken from the backend's reported channel map rather than a fixed table, and FC-on-centre asserted by the round-trip check. Detector transitions counted in E54's event ring and reviewed in E60 stage 1 scenes | E27, E25, E28, E30 |

| P3 | Parametric spatial features are judged phasey or distant against Dolby, Waves Nx or HeSuVi | Perceptual | High | Medium | Default off; panel go/no-go; do not market without significant results | E12, E24, E28 |

E27 Phase 1 does not depend on them; its own risks are T14 | E01, E07, E26, E27 database flag, E39, E40 browser and part (2), E52 uuid rule key |
| S4 | Listening panel, playtests and device lab cannot recruit or lack hardware | Schedule | High | Medium | Book the lab and panel now; pre-register protocols; objective gates meanwhile | E12, E14, E60, E15, E16 |
| S5 | Ambitious items (E57, E29, E31, E35) absorb time from High fixes | Schedule | Medium | High | Evidence gates in §4; E29, E31, E35 and E57 are all in §5.5, not in Phase 3; each Phase 3 lane starts only when its own Phase 2 committed High items have closed (the start condition in §5.4), and its queue shrinks from the bottom if that is late; per-lane capacity check and drop orders in §5.4 | E29, E31, E35, E57 |
| S6 | Certificate, notarisation and Partner Center lead times | Schedule | Medium | Medium | Start enrolment in Phase 0 | E46, E49, E54 |

Listening tests and real-hardware validation are still required before any perceptual, device, latency or platform claim in this report is used in the product or its marketing.
